# Magna Carta — Decision Log

**Status:** Living record · **Last updated:** 2026-06-17 · **Owner:** Trevor Goldsmith (chief worker)

The authoritative register of architectural decisions (ADRs) for Magna Carta. The [white paper](./Magna-Carta-White-Paper.md) holds the narrative design and full rationale; this log is the durable list of _what was decided_ and its _status_. Each entry is the seed of an ADR — terse by default, expandable to the full template when a decision is contested, revisited, or superseded.

---

## Standard

**Identity.** Each decision has a stable ID `ADR-NNNN`, assigned once in sequence and **never renumbered or reused**. IDs are independent of white-paper section numbers, so reorganizing the paper never disturbs a decision's identity. The `· <area>` label on each entry names the white-paper area that carries its detail — **by name, not section number**, because section numbers shift whenever the paper is reorganized (see the cross-reference policy in the root [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md)).

**Status lifecycle.** `Proposed → Accepted → (Superseded | Deprecated | Rejected)`. Accepted entries are **immutable** — to change a decision, add a _new_ ADR that supersedes it (mark `Supersedes ADR-NNNN` on the new one and `Superseded by ADR-MMMM` on the old), never edit the original. Rejected alternatives are kept inline; they record roads not taken so they are not relitigated.

**Adding a decision.** On a branch, add `docs/decisions/pending/<slug>.md` (no number yet; reference it as `ADR-<slug>` until merged). The number is assigned at merge by `vp run decisions-build` — once and never changed. Per-file decisions in `docs/decisions/` are the canonical home; the register in the section below is generated by that command and must not be edited by hand. One line suffices for a clear-cut call; promote to the full template when the decision is weighty or contested.

**Entry template (lightweight — the default):**

> `**ADR-NNNN** · <Status> · <white-paper area> — <decision, naming the key rejected alternatives>.`

**Full template (contested / superseding decisions):** Status · Date · Context · Decision · Considered options (pros/cons) · Consequences (positive / negative / risks) · Related ADRs. Keep it under two pages; link back to the white-paper area for detail rather than duplicating it.

> **Numbering history.** The original seed list mislabeled three consecutive decisions as "26" (Encryption, Archive handling, Design system). They are now assigned distinct stable IDs **ADR-0026/0027/0028**, shifting the later entries; this is a one-time correction. IDs are frozen from here on.

## Register

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED REGISTER -->

- **ADR-0001** · Accepted · Architecture — Web-first, **API-first** architecture (Workers API + Svelte SPA).
- **ADR-0002** · Accepted · Architecture — **Tauri 2** desktop after web MVP; Rust surgical; mobile path open.
- **ADR-0003** · Accepted · Tenancy & isolation — Tenancy: **control-plane D1 + per-firm D1 + per-firm R2**; firm-scoped data-access layer.
- **ADR-0004** · Accepted · Auth & access control — **First-party auth (Better Auth)**; passkeys/TOTP primary, email/SMS secondary; RBAC + ethical walls.
- **ADR-0005** · Accepted · Architecture — Cloudflare **Workers static assets** for hosting (Pages retired).
- **ADR-0006** · Accepted · Directory — **Directory** SSOT — a **graph** of entities + typed edges: Person/Organization (equal depth, distinct types) with **categories + custom fields**; structured names + aliases/former-names + history; affiliations; **entity-to-entity relationships** (family/guardian/beneficiary, parent/subsidiary/successor); representation edges; matter role-links. **Field-level encryption** for sensitive identifiers; **duplicate detection + merge/unmerge + soft-delete**; temporal (valid-from/to) field history + full audit.
- **ADR-0007** · Accepted · Matters — **Matter-centric** navigation; configurable numbering; flexible matter types.
- **ADR-0008** · Accepted · Financial Core — **Append-only double-entry ledger** for trust + billing + costs + operating GL.
- **ADR-0009** · Accepted · Trust accounting — **Jurisdiction-configurable trust engine**; no hard-coded rule numbers; interactive three-way reconciliation.
- **ADR-0010** · Accepted · Documents — **Collabora on Containers** for `.docx` editing; Microsoft/WOPI rejected.
- **ADR-0011** · Accepted · Document Automation — **Conditional document assembly** + PDF form-filling.
- **ADR-0012** · Accepted · Discovery — **Discovery:** Tantivy + Vectorize, full production workflow, defensibility; large-scale designed-out-but-deferred.
- **ADR-0013** · Accepted · Conflicts — **Conflicts:** firm-wide, configurable strictness/depth, immutable log.
- **ADR-0014** · Accepted · Custodian — **Custodian:** location hierarchy + custody log + labels + scans + reminders.
- **ADR-0015** · Accepted · Plugin system — **Uniform plugin model:** Worker-per-integration behind typed ports.
- **ADR-0016** · Accepted · Maintenance agents — **Maintenance agents** propose-only: security, deps/upgrades, Cloudflare-watch, compliance-watch, competitive-watch, diagnostics.
- **ADR-0017** · Accepted · Observability — **Observability & diagnostics** pillar: redact-by-default, correlation-over-content, per-firm scoped, no replay, Cloudflare-native to start.
- **ADR-0018** · Accepted · Subsystem map — Added subsystems: Calendar & docketing, Tasks & workflows, Client portal, Payments + e-signature, Communications layer, Intake/CRM, Operating accounting/GL.
- **ADR-0019** · Accepted · User documentation — **User docs:** public help center + in-app help + admin docs.
- **ADR-0020** · Accepted · Dev guidelines — **Dev guidelines:** layered CLAUDE.md + ADRs; inherited conventions (no shell for files; docs-in-commit; autonomous+terse implementation).
- **ADR-0021** · Accepted · LEAP migration — **LEAP migration** deferred; legacy IDs baked in; trust reconciliation as the cutover gate.
- **ADR-0022** · Accepted · API — **API style:** REST + OpenAPI 3.1 on Hono; **code-as-contract** (zod → typed TS client + emitted spec), with CI spec tooling (Spectral, Prism, SDK-gen, oasdiff). Implementing library decided in a Phase 0 spike. tRPC and GraphQL rejected.
- **ADR-0023** · Accepted · Architecture / parity — **Web/desktop parity (enforced):** no desktop-exclusive or web-exclusive features; desktop = same bundle/API + optional local accelerations falling back to server paths. Only asymmetry: the direct _local_ (desktop-attached) scanner trigger (browser sandbox). *(Framing flagged for revision — networked copy-room MFP/copier capture is a receiving/routing concern with a complete web path, not a true asymmetry; see the scan-to-matter note in the white paper *Delivery model*.)*
- **ADR-0024** · Accepted · Architecture / Rust — **Rust scope:** Tauri shell + Tantivy + local desktop processing; a scoped **shared Rust core (WASM + native)** for the document engine and discovery extraction; native Rust in Containers for the heaviest server work; TypeScript for everything else.
- **ADR-0025** · Accepted · Architecture / caching — **Web caching:** service-worker **app-shell caching only** (no client data, cleared on logout); the web app is **not** an installable PWA — Tauri is the sole installable app.
- **ADR-0026** · Accepted · Encryption & keys — **Encryption & key management:** envelope encryption with two stored keys — a single **root KEK** (Worker secret, outside D1) wraps a **per-firm key** (stored wrapped in the control-plane firm registry), which **HKDF-derives** purpose keys; enforced structurally by a **`protected()` field type in the firm-scoped DAL** (encrypt-on-write, role-gated + audited decrypt, HMAC **blind index** for Conflicts matching), with the same module handling per-firm plugin credentials. **Encrypt only the narrow secret set** (SSN/DOB/DL/financial IDs, plugin credentials, TOTP); everything else relies on D1/R2 at-rest encryption. **Secrets Store and a separate crypto service both rejected** as unnecessary. **Per-firm crypto-shred** by destroying the wrapped firm key; external-KMS-rooted anchor deferred (Cloudflare has no managed KMS for app data). _Amended 2026-06-12 (slice 1·2 design, ADR-0084): the TOTP open line is closed — seeds stay under **Better Auth's own at-rest encryption** (empirically verified against the pinned version; `encryptionKeys` rotation configured; auth secret in the managed-secrets inventory), not routed through the per-firm chokepoint: they are user-owned auth credentials read at login before firm context exists, and firm crypto-shred must not break a user's login. The concrete envelope/AAD/blind-index layer is ADR-0084._
- **ADR-0027** · Accepted · File handling — **Archive (zip) handling:** uploaded zips stored **opaque** (versioned/hashed, never expanded server-side — no extraction code, hence no zip-bomb/password/nested-archive surface); **bulk download** builds zips by **streaming R2 → store mode → ZIP64**, version-pinned, access-checked, audit-logged, with an async-job→R2→signed-URL seam for very large bundles (live stream ships first). Reading/extracting archive _contents_ for discovery is **deferred but not designed-out** (likely client/desktop-side extraction → loose-file upload, original archive kept as as-received evidence).
- **ADR-0028** · Superseded by ADR-0076 · Design system — **Design system (UI consistency):** four enforced layers — **DTCG tokens via Terrazzo → CSS vars**, **Tailwind v4** (`@theme`, token-bound), **shadcn-svelte** component library as the sole primitives (Lucide icons, no emojis), and **full enforcement** (lint blocks off-token values + Storybook/Histoire catalog + visual-regression CI). Drift prevented by construction, caught in CI. vanilla-extract rejected (maintenance); Panda CSS runner-up.
- **ADR-0029** · Accepted · File preview — **Inline file preview (video-first):** stream originals from **R2 via authorization-gated signed URLs** into a **Vidstack** player (Lucide), with an `inlinePlayable` verdict computed at ingest and rolled up in Reports; download always available. **Cloudflare Stream rejected** (≈10x storage + metered delivery); transcoding deferred to **self-hosted FFmpeg (Queues + Container)**, the player already HLS-capable.
- **ADR-0030** · Accepted · File conversion — **In-app file conversion:** general any-format→any-format utility in the file manager, **in-house** for confidentiality (R2 → own Container → R2, no third-party egress) + margin + integration. A unified **conversion orchestrator** over a data-driven **format registry** (with multi-hop chains) dispatches to engines already run — LibreOffice, libvips, discovery OCR, FFmpeg — plus a small email→PDF worker. **Hybrid** execution (small inline, large/batch as a per-job Durable Object with progress + completion notification); output is the **user's choice** — versioned save-to-matter or download, single or batch. Third-party converters (CloudConvert/Zamzar) and bundling an all-in-one OSS converter (Stirling-PDF/ConvertX) rejected — redundant with engines already running, plus egress/attack-surface.
- **ADR-0031** · Accepted · Locations — **Multi-location firms:** a firm may operate multiple offices, modeled as a first-class **Location** entity — a _dimension inside the per-firm D1_, not a new isolation tier. The **firm stays the tenancy/isolation/crypto-shred/conflicts boundary**; conflicts impute across all offices. Matters **optionally** reference a home Location (driving number prefix, letterhead/remit-to, reporting); **trust jurisdiction profiles bind per account**, each account associated with a Location (a firm runs several jurisdictions at once); **Custodian hierarchy is rooted at Location**; users belong to one+ Locations with optional per-location role scoping; reports gain per-location roll-ups. Rejected: office-as-tenant (breaks shared Directory + firm-wide conflicts), office-as-Directory-`Organization` (conflates internal/external), office-in-control-plane (wrong tier).
- **ADR-0032** · Accepted · Payments — **Payments — lowest-fee, build on a raw processor:** payments as a port; **ACH / "direct deposit" is the default rail** (no interchange — the real cost lever), **card a convenience fallback**. **Interchange is the network floor** no processor beats; minimize fees by steering off cards + interchange-plus pricing + **surcharge on operating-only where jurisdiction-legal** (never trust, never debit). **Fees never touch trust** — trust deposits land gross, fees debited from operating, enforced by separate deposit routing. **Launch adapter: Helcim** (interchange-plus, no monthly/PCI fees, ACH 0.5%+25¢ capped $6); swappable to Stripe/Dwolla (cheaper ACH), Adyen (card volume), or Confido/Gravity Legal (trust-compliance fallback). PCI minimized via hosted-fields tokenization (SAQ A). **LawPay-style turnkey processors rejected** as the primary (markup), kept only as fallback.
- **ADR-0033** · Accepted · Time tracking — **Time tracking — the daily driver, built deep:** speed/keyboard-first capture via a **single active live timer** with **server-side state that persists across tabs/devices and offline (Tauri) then syncs**, plus manual entry, a **weekly timesheet grid**, and one-click **calendar-derived** drafts. Entries carry resolved **effective-dated rates** (matter → client → timekeeper → role; multiple rate cards), **configurable increment/rounding** (default 0.1 h), narratives with snippet/template assist, and optional **UTBMS codes**. **No approval workflow** — Draft (editable) → Billed (locked), append-only corrections after. **Time is WIP, not a ledger posting** (converts to A/R at invoicing). Daily-driver features: "My Day" totals, **missing-time detection**, billable **targets**, utilization/realization into Reports. **Passive/automatic capture stays backlogged.** **LEDES e-billing promoted to v1:** export **LEDES 1998B + XML 2.0/2.1** with UTBMS task/activity/expense codes and **client-billing-guideline validation**; expenses carry UTBMS **E-codes**.
- **ADR-0034** · Accepted · Testing & fixtures — **Synthetic data & fixtures:** mock matters generated **through the real domain write-paths, never raw inserts** (valid by construction; doubles as write-path coverage). One **seeded, deterministic** engine (fixed clock + seeded IDs) with three entry points — asserted **scenarios**, a curated **demo-firm bundle**, and a snapshot-replayable **load profile**; layered content (faker + real `.docx` templates). Confined to a **demo tenant**, **never runs in production**. Raw-SQL seeding rejected (bypasses ledger/trust/conflicts invariants, drifts from schema).
- **ADR-0035** · Accepted · Document versioning — **Document versioning & storage tiering:** revision history is **app-managed version objects**, not R2 native versioning (native lifecycle has **no noncurrent-version action** and gives no per-version storage-class control). Decouple a churning **`current`** key (overwritten on every save, Standard) from **immutable `vN` snapshots** cut deliberately — driven by Collabora's **`X-COOL-WOPI-IsAutosave`** header: version on **explicit Save + session-close**, **not per-autosave** (optional debounced checkpoint). Superseded versions transition **Standard → Infrequent Access** in place via S3 **`CopyObject`** (`x-amz-storage-class: STANDARD_IA` — server-side, no retrieval fee, ~33% storage cut on the coldest segment). **Tiering, never deletion** — every version kept forever, IA equally durable/available, safe under legal hold and append-only. **No compression** (corpus already compressed; would break the inline-preview range-stream path). WOPI `current`-overwrite is timestamp-guarded (`X-COOL-WOPI-Timestamp` → **409 / `COOLStatusCode 1010`** on mismatch). Native R2 versioning rejected.
- **ADR-0036** · Accepted · Client portal — **Client portal — unified grant sharing model:** every share (matter / folder / file) is one first-class **`PortalGrant`** (recipient = Directory entity, scope + optional R2 version-pin, capability subset, per-share **assurance level**, optional expiry, status + full audit); a recipient's portal is the **aggregation** of their active grants. Recipients are **always Directory entities** (no shadow user list; conflict-checkable). **Opt-in folders:** folder shares are live for files but **child folders stay private until shared**; "share whole matter" grants only currently-shareable folders; shareability is **visible** in the File Manager. Capabilities: view / download / upload / comment / esign, with **uploads quarantined** for staff acceptance (never silently mutate matter files). **Per-share assurance** (magic-link | account | account+2FA); portal accounts are a **distinct external principal type in the control-plane D1**, never staff RBAC. **Instant revocation + optional expiry + metadata-only audit.** Enforced in the firm-scoped **DAL**: **per-firm isolation** and an **ethical-wall / least-privilege invariant — a grant never exposes more than the sharer's own current access** (walled-off staff cannot share a matter). Reuses the bulk-download engine, inline preview, and the portal/payments/observability surfaces scoped by grant. Deferred: per-recipient watermarking, ad-hoc non-Directory recipients, per-file in-folder exclusions. Rejected: **per-matter-only sharing config** (no file-level or cross-matter aggregation) and **link-first/Drive-style sharing** (breaks Directory-recipient + per-share-assurance constraints, weakest audit).
- **ADR-0037** · Accepted · Documentation & process — **Living docs, plus documentation-as-done and a standard writing language.** A root [`CHANGELOG.md`](./CHANGELOG.md) (Keep a Changelog, `[Unreleased]` updated per PR) and a living root [`ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) (execution tracker — Now/Next/Shipped/Backlog — **distinct** from the white paper's stable phase strategy) are maintained in the **same commit** as the work. Documentation is a **definition-of-done** gate for **both** developer and user audiences (standard in `docs/conventions/documentation.md`). A single **writing language** standardizes change types via **Conventional Commits → Keep a Changelog** categories, commit/changelog phrasing, bug-fix/feature templates, and a **canonical glossary** (`docs/conventions/documentation-style.md`). Cross-references into the white paper use **document + topic name or `ADR-NNNN`, never `§` numbers** (numbers shift on reorganization). Release-time changelog aggregation and roadmap roll-up are **automated in Phase 0** — tool (Conventional Commits + git-cliff / release-please / changesets) picked then and logged as its own ADR; a propose-only changelog/roadmap **maintenance agent** can later draft entries from merged PRs for human approval. Chose hand-written `[Unreleased]` entries as the reliable baseline over fully-generated changelogs.
- **ADR-0038** · Accepted · Observability — **Error logging & user-driven diagnostics — in-house, Cloudflare-native.** Two layers: **operational telemetry** always-on (redacted, Error-ID-keyed traces) vs. developer-visible **incidents** that are **user-initiated only**. An **in-house client reporter** (Svelte error boundary + global handlers + breadcrumb ring buffer — actions/IDs, never content) surfaces a short **Error ID** with a one-click **"Send to developer"** on crashes and an always-available **"Report a problem."** A report = Error ID(s) + redacted breadcrumbs + user description + **opt-in/redactable screenshot** + context → bug-report Worker → **firm-scoped D1 incident** (+ R2 screenshot), access-gated + audited. The **diagnostics agent** acts on submitted reports, **propose-only**: triage → root-cause draft → **draft PR** (money/trust/conflicts human-reviewed) → acknowledge the reporter. A **diagnostics setting** (per firm, per-user override) selects Off/strict (default) · Standard · Verbose/auto, where verbose/auto is the standing-consent **beta** posture — one setting, one pipeline. **No third-party error SaaS** (privileged stacks never egress — same logic as in-house file conversion); **self-hosted Sentry deferred**. Rejected: third-party error SaaS (Sentry SaaS/Highlight) and auto-capture-everything (surveillant, against the user-initiated stance).
- **ADR-0039** · Accepted · Performance — **Performance & concurrency model — deliberate compute placement + measure-first discipline** (not generic multi-threading). **Workers** = lean request handlers (stream, never buffer; concurrency is the platform's); **heavy/CPU work** = native Rust in **Containers** + **Queues** + per-job **Durable Objects**; **Durable Objects** = single-threaded coordinators; a **shared Rust core (WASM + native)** for compute-heavy parity features; the **browser** keeps the UI main thread free (Web Workers, list virtualization, code-splitting); **Tauri** runs real multi-threaded local Rust. Resource discipline: stream / paginate / no-N+1 / bounded Worker memory. Optimize against the **fixtures load profile**; promote hot-path logic to Rust→WASM only on profiling evidence. **Concrete per-tier budgets and CI perf gates deferred** until there is code to measure — model & discipline are foundational now. **Build in vertical slices.** Rejected: speculative pre-threading / premature micro-optimization.
- **ADR-0040** · Accepted · Settings — **Settings & configuration framework — a declarative registry that generates the UI.** Each setting declared once (key, type, **scope**, default, **view/edit role**, validation, audited/effective-dated); the Settings page is **generated from the registry** — no bespoke per-setting pages (same data-driven discipline as the format registry and design tokens). **Scope resolution:** entity override → user → location → firm → system (per-setting opt-in), mirroring rate resolution. **Access by RBAC role**, enforced **server-side in the firm-scoped DAL**, not UI-only. **Storage** per the tenancy model (control-plane user prefs / `member` row / per-firm D1); Settings is a **surface over data the subsystems already own** (trust profiles, rate cards, numbering, locations, plugin credentials, diagnostics), never a copy. Categories shown per access; financial/security/firm settings audited + effective-dated. Rejected: bespoke per-setting pages (unscalable, drift-prone).
- **ADR-0041** · Accepted · Testing — **Test layers & tooling.** **Vite+ integrated testing** (the Vite+ toolchain's runner, Vite-native) is the **unit + Svelte component (behavior)** layer — components test against the same SvelteKit pipeline with no extra build config (props/events/state + accessibility). **Playwright** for end-to-end + **visual regression** (Chromatic screenshots over the **Storybook/Histoire** catalog). **Integration** tests run against **Miniflare** (local D1 + R2). A component is covered by **catalog entry + behavior tests + visual-regression** — appearance _and_ behavior. Test data is always the **write-path fixtures** (ADR-0034): real domain APIs, never raw inserts; seeded + deterministic. TDD expectations, CI gates, and coverage targets are detailed in Phase 0 (`docs/conventions/testing.md`). Rejected: catalog + visual-regression _only_ (verifies appearance, not behavior); a non-Vite test runner (would duplicate the SvelteKit build pipeline).
- **ADR-0042** · Accepted · Toolchain — **Commit to Vite+ as the unified JS/TS toolchain; Oxc for lint/format; Biome dropped.** Vite+ (VoidZero) is the single entry point for **dev server (`vp dev`), build/pack (`vp build` / `vp pack`, Rolldown), test (`vp test`, Vitest), the one-shot quality gate (`vp check` = Oxfmt + Oxlint + tsgolint), and the monorepo task runner (`vp run`)** — one toolchain, one config in `vite.config.ts`. Lint/format = **Oxlint + Oxfmt**; **Prettier** is the interim formatter until **Oxfmt is GA** (not yet released). Vite+ is **free at our tier** (individual/small-firm; tiered, source-available — paid only for startups/enterprises), and its underlying tools (Vite, Vitest, Rolldown, Oxc) are **MIT**, so every command has an OSS fallback. **Alpha maturity accepted** (public preview targeted early 2026), risk bounded by that fallback. Investigated against **Biome** (mature, GA lint+format) — **rejected** because it would fork the toolchain family and duplicate Oxc; using the OSS tools without the Vite+ CLI also rejected (loses the unified entry point + task runner the owner wants). Pairs with ADR-0041 (testing).
- **ADR-0043** · Accepted · Code management — **GitHub for source control + GitHub Actions for CI/CD.** Repositories, branch protection, **pull requests** (the maintenance and diagnostics agents open _draft_ PRs), code review, and issues live on GitHub. **GitHub Actions** runs the quality gate (`vp check`), tests (`vp test`, Playwright, visual-regression), and the OpenAPI spec toolchain (Spectral/Prism/oasdiff/SDK-gen), then **deploys to Cloudflare via Wrangler** (auth via OIDC / encrypted secrets). **Cloudflare Workers Builds** considered but GitHub Actions chosen for flexibility and because the propose-only agent PR workflow already centers on GitHub.
- **ADR-0044** · Accepted (amended 2026-06-17) · Community — **Public community & feedback board — native build on Cloudflare (supersedes the deferred-Fider lean).** A wanted feature: cross-firm public discussion, **feature voting**, known-issues, and roadmap/changelog, tied to bug reporting (_Observability and diagnostics_) and the help center (_User-facing documentation_). **Decided now:** the forum is **platform-level, public, cross-firm, opt-in/user-authored, and never touches per-firm or privileged data**; integration is native **SSO** + a one-way **escalation bridge** from private _Observability and diagnostics_ reports. **Per-tracked-user pricing (Canny) rejected** — reproduces the per-seat cost-scaling the platform exists to escape. Caveat: a public forum is a permanent moderation / spam / security-patch commitment regardless of host. **Amended 2026-06-17 (delivery decided — native build):** a 2026-06-17 changelog/feedback-tool survey made the Fider lean the weakest part of the plan — Fider needs **Postgres + mandatory SMTP**, is **AGPL-3.0**, and ships **no changelog and no roadmap/kanban UI**; none of that fits the Cloudflare per-firm D1/R2/Workers + Cloudflare-email stack, and its actual surface is small enough (posts → votes → comments → tags → 6 statuses → 3 roles) that a **native build is less work than operating an off-Cloudflare host**. **Decision:** build natively as a clean, extractable **`@magna-carta/community`** package (the government-links / property-links pattern) on **control-plane D1 + Cloudflare email**, isolated from the per-firm DAL so the isolation _is_ the confidentiality boundary. **Board now** (feature requests + upvote + comments + status pipeline + roadmap-as-filtered-view-by-status), with a **dormant `discussion` seam** for an open forum later; **display-name identity** (real name/firm never shown publicly, aggregate vote counts only, admins see the real account); the loop is **subscriber-carry + Cloudflare-email notify on ship**, never auto-authored prose (preserves ADR-0048 version-accuracy by construction). Hosted options (UserJot/Featurebase) and Fider all rejected for the Cloudflare/confidentiality mismatch. **Implementation deferred until Fable is available again.** Design spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-17-community-feedback-board-design.md`](specs/2026-06-17-community-feedback-board-design.md).
- **ADR-0045** · Proposed · Documents — **In-house PDF services + e-signature (provisional).** A unified **PDF service** (fill/draw/stamp/merge/split/rotate/flatten/secure/redact/Bates/seal-sign), **client-side by default** (privileged bytes never leave the device; the server touches a PDF only for the platform seal, no-client automated generation, server-only engines like DOCX↔PDF, and web batch/ingest). Composed from permissively-licensed engines — **@cantoo/pdf-lib, PDFium, qpdf, @signpdf** — behind a registry, _not_ a bundle. **In-house e-signature** built on it: the **ESIGN/UETA** electronic-signature model (intent / consent / association / attribution — **not** per-signer PKI), a **PAdES platform seal** + server-observed **audit trail** + **Certificate of Completion**, attribution as a configurable spectrum (client-portal signers strongest); a **port seam** is kept for the PKI/notarial long tail. E-signature is now a **Documents** capability — the _Payments_ section points here, resolving the earlier overlap. Rejected as primary: **Stirling-PDF** (redundant JVM runtime), **Documenso/DocuSeal** (AGPLv3 + stateful, break the Workers/D1/R2 isolation model). **Deferred:** true content-level redaction (AGPL engines — carried to Phase-4 discovery). **Status Proposed** — the area is explicitly a first design pass; every library license/maintenance, the Worker-signing constraint, the ESIGN/UETA specifics, the Certificate-of-Completion scheme, and all cross-references **must be independently verified before build**. Extends ADR-0011 (PDF form-filling) and reuses the ADR-0030 engine-composition approach.
- **ADR-0046** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Encryption & keys — **Post-quantum cryptographic posture — agility-first; the symmetric corpus is already quantum-safe; signatures deferred behind seams.** **Context:** the encrypted secret set (PII, plugin credentials, TOTP seeds) and the decades-long confidentiality lifetime of legal data (estate/sealed matters) raise the **harvest-now-decrypt-later (HNDL)** question. **Decision:** treat post-quantum readiness as primarily an _agility_ property, not a cipher swap. (1) **Keep AES-256 as the bulk cipher** — symmetric/hash crypto (AES-256-GCM, AES-KW, HKDF, HMAC) is not broken by Shor and only halved by Grover (AES-256 → 128-bit, infeasible; CNSA 2.0 approves AES-256), so the at-rest corpus needs **no migration** and there is no "more quantum-resistant" bulk cipher to adopt. (2) **HNDL is a transport threat**, met by Cloudflare's hybrid **X25519MLKEM768** TLS — on for inbound and, by default, Worker→origin egress (Phase-2 "PQC-only" downgrade protection targeted mid-2026); third-party plugin endpoints that won't yet negotiate PQC TLS are an inherited gap that is **logged**, not silently accepted. (3) **Public-key signatures** (passkeys/WebAuthn, the PAdES e-signature seal) are forge-able only once a quantum computer exists — a _migrate-before-then_, not retroactive, risk — so they are **seamed but deferred**, riding upstream standards (FIPS 204 ML-DSA / 205 SLH-DSA / 206 FN-DSA; COSE algorithm IDs for the WebAuthn path; ETSI/CA support for PAdES ML-DSA, est. 2026+). (4) **Crypto-agility is the mechanism**: every ciphertext, wrapped key, and signature carries an explicit **algorithm-version identifier**, making a future hybrid **ML-KEM** key-wrap or **ML-DSA/SLH-DSA** signature a versioned rotation behind the existing `protected()` DAL chokepoint, the envelope-rotation machinery (ADR-0026), and the e-signature port (ADR-0045) — never a rewrite. (5) **Prefer symmetric where there is a choice** — session signing stays HMAC (already quantum-safe); AES stays **256-bit everywhere** and KDF/HMAC hashing moves to **SHA-384** for CNSA-2.0 margin where free; the deferred external-KMS-rooted KEK (ADR-0026 _Honest limits_) gains "supports PQC-wrapped keys" as a selection criterion. **Considered & rejected:** swapping the bulk cipher to **ChaCha20-Poly1305** or **AES-256-GCM-SIV** (equally quantum-safe — a performance / nonce-misuse-robustness choice, not a quantum one, and not natively in Workers Web Crypto); adopting app-layer **ML-KEM now** (the app KEK is a symmetric Worker secret wrapped by AES-KW — there is no asymmetric key-establishment to harden until an asymmetric KEK is introduced); **doing nothing** until standards settle (forfeits the cheap agility seam and leaves transport HNDL unaddressed). **Consequences:** at-rest data is quantum-safe today; transport HNDL is mitigated by the platform; the only real migration work (signatures, an asymmetric KEK) is bounded, standards-gated, and reduced to adapter / field-version swaps; the cost is the discipline of carrying algorithm identifiers and the AES-256 / SHA-384 mandate. Extends **ADR-0026** (encryption & keys); touches **ADR-0004** (auth/passkeys) and **ADR-0045** (e-signature seal).
- **ADR-0047** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Communications layer — **Messaging (SMS/MMS/RCS) + voice/telephony via a CPaaS behind plugin ports; Telnyx launch adapter; transcription in-house on Cloudflare Workers AI; self-hosted gateway rejected.** **Context:** the _Communications layer_ was email-only, but notifications, the SMS 2FA factor (ADR-0004), e-signature/portal access codes (ADR-0045/ADR-0036), the formerly-backlogged "client texting," and a new **receptionist call-intake** workflow (transcribe inbound calls → call log + intake capture) all need text and voice channels. The owner's instinct was to self-host for control. **Decision:** add two typed **category ports — `messaging` and `telephony`** — under the uniform plugin model (ADR-0015): each adapter is its own isolated Worker with per-firm scoped secrets, signed inbound webhooks, delivery receipts, and opt-out state. (1) **CPaaS, not self-hosted.** Self-hosting the carrier is rejected — SMS/MMS require a registered aggregator, **RCS exists only through Google's RBM + carrier agreements (cannot be self-hosted)**, and an SMPP gateway (Jasmin, Apache-2.0) is an off-Cloudflare persistent Python/Twisted+Redis+AMQP host that fights the Workers/D1/R2 model (same reasoning that rejected Documenso/self-hosted-Sentry and hesitates on Fider's host). Control comes from the **swappable adapter + isolation**, not from owning the carrier; Jasmin is kept only as a documented fallback. (2) **Launch adapter: Telnyx** — owns its private IP network (voice quality/latency for the receptionist line), lowest published SMS cost (~$0.004/segment), one number inventory spans **SMS/MMS/RCS + voice**, exposes **bidirectional WebSocket media streaming** (clean fork into transcription), and signs webhooks with **Ed25519** (public-key verification in a Worker, no shared secret). Swappable behind the port: **Twilio** (turnkey fallback, most mature RCS GA), **Bandwidth** (owned Tier-1 US voice + E911 strength), **Plivo** (cost at scale). Same "launch adapter, swap in days" posture as Payments' Helcim (ADR-0032). (3) **Transcription in-house on Cloudflare Workers AI** (Whisper / Deepgram Nova-3), **not** the carrier's hosted transcription — privileged call audio never egresses (same confidentiality logic as in-house file conversion, ADR-0030, and no-third-party-error-SaaS, ADR-0038); one capability also serves the deferred transcript-sync (ADR-0029) and discovery media (ADR-0012). (4) **OTP stays in Better Auth** (ADR-0004); the port only _delivers_ the code — no vendor "Verify" product (one OTP path, no lock-in). (5) **Compliance invariants live in the ports:** **A2P 10DLC / toll-free / RCS-brand registration** is a procurement prerequisite (TCR fees, weeks of lead time — same class as the legal-research connector entitlement, ADR-0015); **TCPA consent + STOP/HELP opt-out** enforced in the messaging port; **call-recording consent** gated by the existing jurisdiction-profile machinery (ADR-0009 trust rules / ADR-0032 surcharge legality — Nevada one-party, ~12 states all-party), reused not rebuilt; **E911** seamed (inbound intake line sidesteps it; a real outbound office line triggers it — Bandwidth's strength). **Considered & rejected:** **self-hosted SMPP gateway (Jasmin)** — off-Cloudflare host, cannot do RCS, no real independence (kept as fallback only); **vendor Verify/OTP products** — duplicate the Better Auth OTP path and add lock-in; **carrier-hosted transcription** — egresses privileged audio. **Infobip** dropped from launch (sales-gated, no self-service eval); Sinch/Vonage kept as later port-swap options. **Consequences:** the _Communications layer_ becomes three channels (email + messaging + voice) behind one trigger engine; "client texting" leaves the backlog; intake (_Client intake / CRM_) gains a call-log/transcript input; carrier choice is reversible in days. Cost: A2P/RCS registration lead time and per-message carrier surcharges, and a media-stream→Workers-AI path to build for live transcription. Extends **ADR-0015** (plugin model); touches **ADR-0004** (SMS 2FA), **ADR-0009**/**ADR-0032** (jurisdiction-config reuse), **ADR-0029**/**ADR-0012** (transcription consumers), **ADR-0036**/**ADR-0045** (access codes).
- **ADR-0048** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Product updates — **In-app + public product-updates surface (changelog, release notes, roadmap) + live status; bundle the versioned content, centralize only the live content.** **Context:** users want in-app access to the changelog, release notes, and roadmap, and the question was raised whether to serve this from a central SQL database or bundle it with the app — and how it relates to the deferred _Community & feedback forum_ (ADR-0044), which also lists changelog/roadmap/known-issues. **Decision:** (1) **Split by mutability, not by surface** — versioned content (changelog, release notes, roadmap) **ships with the build** as a structured `product-updates.json` static asset (the in-app read path); only the **live** known-issues / service-status banner lives in **control-plane KV**, written via an owner/admin RBAC-gated endpoint (and propose-only by the diagnostics/maintenance agents), no deploy needed. (2) **Repo stays the single home** — `CHANGELOG.md`/`ROADMAP.md` (ADR-0037) are the authoring source; a tool-agnostic build step derives the artifact (parser now, or the Phase-0 release tool — git-cliff/changesets — emitting it later; the _schema is the contract_, malformed input fails CI). **No CMS**; authoring stays in git/PR via the changelog/roadmap maintenance agent (ADR-0016). This gives **version-accuracy by construction** (a build can't advertise features it lacks) and **offline-native parity** (web + Tauri). (3) **KV, not D1**, for the live content — platform-level, read-mostly, edge-replicated config; keeping status off the off-Cloudflare forum host means the banner survives an incident that takes the forum down. (4) **Public-by-default** read surfaces rendered as **Workers static pages** from the same artifact + KV status (transparency + acquisition, SEO), so the canonical public changelog/roadmap does **not** depend on the unchosen forum tool; the forum is scoped to login-gated requests/votes/discussion and **links to** our pages / **mirrors** our status. (5) **Single system of record per content type** — we own changelog/roadmap/status, the forum owns requests/votes/discussion — wired by **seams** (native SSO deep-links, optional read-only vote-count enrichment on roadmap items, the one-way private-report→feature-request escalation bridge of ADR-0038), built when the forum tool is chosen; the surface is fully functional standalone until then. (6) **Per-user `lastSeenProductUpdate`** marker registered in the Settings framework (ADR-0040, control-plane storage) drives the "What's New" unread badge. (7) The internal **roadmap is shown as-is** now (sole tenant); the artifact reserves an optional `audience` field as a non-breaking seam for an internal/public filter at multi-firm. **Considered & rejected:** **central SQL (D1) + a CMS** as the system of record (creates a second home for the changelog against _one home per fact_, can drift ahead of the deployed build, abandons the git/agent authoring flow, and is the most infra for the worst fit); **pure-bundle with no central seam** (forecloses the live status banner the owner wants and a shared public/forum source); **delegating the public changelog/roadmap to the forum tool's own modules** (weak/inconsistent presentation, ties canonical content to an off-Cloudflare, not-yet-chosen tool). **Consequences:** a small, mostly-static surface that ships early and works offline; the only authored-without-deploy content is the live status; the forum integration is reduced to thin seams. Extends **ADR-0037** (living docs) and **ADR-0044** (community forum); reuses **ADR-0040** (settings), **ADR-0016** (maintenance agents), and the _Cloudflare primitive mapping_ (KV). Distinct from per-entity audit/temporal history (ADR-0006) and not a per-firm activity feed.
- **ADR-0049** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Document versioning / storage lifecycle — **Deleted-file lifecycle: trash → Infrequent Access → policy-driven destruction; capability built, default indefinite.** **Context:** ADR-0035 tiers _superseded versions_ to IA but left _deleted files_ undefined; deletion in a legal system is not a synonym for purge (retention schedules, legal/discovery/Custodian holds, and privacy-law erasure all bear on it), and a multi-tenant "keep everything forever" default carries unbounded cost and discovery exposure. **Decision:** deletion is a **soft-delete** (a firm-scoped DAL state change that hides the record; bytes are not immediately purged), and the R2 object moves through three stages — (1) **trash window** on **Standard**, recoverable in place with no copy/retrieval fee, **default 30 days**, sized to IA's 30-day minimum-storage charge so delete-then-undo churn pays no IA penalty; (2) **cold retention** via **Standard → IA** in place using the **same `CopyObject` (`x-amz-storage-class: STANDARD_IA`)** path as version tiering (server-side, no bytes through the Worker, no retrieval fee, ~33% cheaper on a never-read segment); (3) **destruction** — a per-record **retention/destruction date** can trigger a true **hard-delete** (the one place file bytes are deleted), **blocked by any legal/discovery/Custodian hold** and audited like any irreversible act. The **destroy machinery is built but retention defaults to indefinite** — keep-by-default behavior today (matching version history's _tiering, never pruning_), with a per-matter/jurisdiction switch for when a bar rule, client purge instruction, or GDPR/CCPA erasure _requires_ destruction, no re-architecture. **Cost finding:** IA storage is cheap at single-firm scale either way (~$0.12/GB-yr, egress free) — cost does not force destruction per firm — but at platform scale "never destroy" trends to a multi-petabyte, $1M+/yr-and-climbing line, while IA tiering of the cold/deleted segment is worth doing regardless (~$300k/yr saved at 1,000 firms × 5 TB; current R2 rates Standard $0.015 vs IA $0.010/GB-mo, IA $0.01/GB retrieval, 30-day minimum). **Considered & rejected:** **soft-delete → IA, never destroy** (simplest and safest against spoliation, but cannot satisfy a must-destroy obligation and grows unbounded — preserved as the _default behavior_ via indefinite retention, not as the _only_ path); **trash → hard-delete, no IA** (cheapest, but discards the retained-but-cold archive legal/discovery/Custodian may require and ignores the IA tier). Extends **ADR-0035** (version tiering — shared `CopyObject` path); touches **ADR-0026** (per-firm crypto-shred is the separate whole-firm destruction path), **ADR-0012** (discovery/legal holds) and **ADR-0014** (Custodian retention/destruction dates) as destruction blockers. D1 backup/snapshot strategy is the open second half of this design and not yet decided.
- **ADR-0050** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Tenancy & data isolation — **Database backup & disaster recovery — two layers (Time Travel + export snapshots), on-Cloudflare, bucket-lock-immutable, GFS 7+yr; separate backup account declined, off-Cloudflare seam deferred.** **Context:** the tenancy model (control-plane D1 + per-firm D1 + per-firm R2, ADR-0003) had no documented backup/DR strategy; D1's **Time Travel** gives free always-on 30-day point-in-time recovery but nothing longer, no protection against database/account loss, and no multi-year legal archive of DB state. **Decision:** two layers — (1) **Time Travel** is line-of-defense #1 for operational recovery (bad migration, fat-finger write, accidental delete) within 30 days, free, no setup; (2) **export snapshots** are line-of-defense #2 — a **Cron-triggered Workflow** fans out across firms, calls the **D1 export REST API** per database, and streams each SQL dump into **that firm's own R2 bucket** (`backups/` prefix), preserving per-firm isolation; **control-plane D1 backed up the same way** as a first-class target; restore = SQL import into a fresh D1, validated by **periodic restore drills**. Backups land in the same at-rest-encrypted per-firm store; `protected()` field ciphertext stays encrypted inside the dump, so **crypto-shred** (ADR-0026) still covers backed-up secrets. **Retention: GFS ~7+ yr** (daily→30d, weekly→1yr, monthly/yearly→7+yr, aligned to matter-retention windows), aged snapshots **tiered to IA** via the same lifecycle as versions/deleted files (ADR-0035/ADR-0049). **Immutability via R2 bucket locks** (prefix-scoped retention per GFS tier) blocks accidental/malicious deletion before age-out — the primary defense against keeping primary + backup in **one Cloudflare account**. **Considered & rejected / deferred:** **Time Travel only** (no DR past 30 days, no account-loss protection — kept only as layer 1); **separate Cloudflare backup account** (cleanest blast-radius isolation, but **declined** for operational simplicity — bucket locks carry the deletion-defense instead); **off-Cloudflare cold copy** (true provider-independent DR + certified-WORM path — **designed-but-deferred** as a seam for the residual tail: Cloudflare-wide catastrophe, account loss, or a firm's SEC-17a-4-grade compliance need). **Honest limits:** R2 bucket locks are retention policies, not certified WORM, and lock rules are account-managed — hence the retained off-Cloudflare seam. Extends **ADR-0003** (tenancy); reuses **ADR-0035**/**ADR-0049** (IA tiering lifecycle) and **ADR-0026** (envelope encryption / crypto-shred); uses **Workflows + Cron + R2** from the _Cloudflare primitive mapping_.
- **ADR-0051** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Architecture / coherence — **Architectural integrity & coherence — coherence _by construction_ for an AI-built codebase: chokepoint seams + registry-first + four-tier fitness functions + generated read-first indexes; spec→plan→execute as session memory; bespoke memory infrastructure rejected.** **Context:** the platform is large and built largely by AI across sessions that start with **no memory of the last**, so the dominant long-run risk is accumulating _incoherence_ — reinvented utilities, features that skip a cross-cutting concern, relitigated decisions, and work no successor session can resume. The _Design system & UI consistency_ model (ADR-0028) already proves the cure on one axis (CSS): **structure + CI, not discipline.** **Decision:** generalize that model to the **whole architecture**, ordered by how little each mechanism depends on the builder choosing right — (1) make the wrong thing **impossible** (structure/types), (2) make it **fail loudly** (CI/tests), (3) make the right thing **cheap to discover** (read-first artifacts); memory/discipline is never the strategy. Four mechanisms: **(a) Chokepoint seams** — every cross-cutting concern is a property of an _unbypassable path_, generalizing the firm-scoped **DAL** (ADR-0003) and **`protected()`** field type (ADR-0026) to the audit log, conflicts-re-check on _add-party_ (ADR-0013), and the comms ports (ADR-0047); the test of a seam is "can the dangerous thing be done without touching it?". **(b) Registry-first** — anything that is "a growing list of similar things" is a **declarative registry** (extending the settings registry ADR-0040, the conversion format registry ADR-0030, design tokens ADR-0028, plugin ports ADR-0015), making each addition uniform, discoverable, and machine-introspectable. **(c) Architectural fitness functions** — CI checks whose subject is the _architecture itself_, in four tiers of decreasing determinism: **boundary/dependency rules** (dependency-cruiser / ts-arch — only the DAL imports D1, no cross-firm import, UI never imports an adapter), **custom AST lint rules** (Oxlint JS plugins, ADR-0042 — alpha-accepted under the toolchain's ESLint/OSS fallback), **conformance tests over the registries + the emitted OpenAPI contract** (ADR-0022) asserting structured metadata not source text (every mutating route audited + role-gated; every `protected()` field has a role; every port has an adapter), and **LLM-as-fitness-function** (the ADR-0016 maintenance-agent pattern pointed inward, **propose-only**) for fuzzy rules. **Seams prevent; fitness functions prove no path escaped** (belt and suspenders). **(d) Generated read-first indexes** — a capability map **generated from registries/module surfaces, never hand-maintained** (a stale index lies — worse than none), regenerated in CI with **fail-on-drift**, surfaced read-first by per-area `CLAUDE.md`; the non-UI analogue of the Storybook/Histoire catalog (ADR-0041). Carried across sessions by **spec → plan → execute** with a persisted plan file per vertical slice (ADR-0020 / ADR-0039). **Considered & rejected:** **bespoke memory infrastructure** (vector DB / knowledge graph / custom context server) — rots, needs babysitting, and duplicates what the **codebase + git history + generated indexes** already are; **discipline / convention alone** (the very failure mode this ADR exists to escape — same reasoning as ADR-0028); **hand-maintained architecture/capability maps** (drift silently, then mislead). **Consequences:** coherence is enforced structurally and in CI rather than trusted to memory; the scariest failure — missed wiring on money/ethics paths — becomes **unmergeable** rather than hope-based; cost is the discipline of routing concerns through seams, preferring registries over bespoke code, maintaining the fitness-function suite, and the fallback-bounded alpha risk of Oxlint JS plugins. Generalizes **ADR-0028**; builds on **ADR-0003** / **ADR-0026** (seams), **ADR-0040** / **ADR-0030** / **ADR-0015** (registries), **ADR-0042** / **ADR-0022** / **ADR-0041** / **ADR-0016** (fitness functions), and **ADR-0020** / **ADR-0039** (workflow & vertical slices). A full subsystem spec (seam catalog, four-tier fitness-function suite, generated-index contract, per-area `CLAUDE.md` template) is the planned next step.
- **ADR-0052** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Architecture / URL & navigation — **URL & navigation architecture — matter-number-canonical permalinks by default (firm-configurable, opaque fallback), firm-from-session (never in the URL), host split (app/api/portal/marketing), tabs-nest/entities-flat, and server-side-only access enforcement (404-not-403).** **Context:** the _Delivery model_ committed to a deep-linkable URL per view but left the addressing scheme, host topology, identifier strategy, and confidentiality behavior unspecified — and matter numbers/names are explicitly **mutable** (renumbering, location prefixes, LEAP-preserved numbers, name/alias history), so the scheme must survive identifier change without breaking links, while staff want to read the matter number in the address. **Decision:** (1) **Every matter carries an immutable opaque handle** (always resolvable plumbing), but the **canonical human-facing URL is the matter number by default** (`/matters/8181-3`) — staff think in numbers and a number leaks **no client identity** (unlike a party-name caption, so **name slugs are rejected**). The scheme is **firm-configurable** (_Settings & configuration_, ADR-0040): matter-number default, **opaque** selectable for maximum confidentiality, and a **per-matter force-opaque override** for sealed/sensitive matters (the settings scope chain: matter-override → firm-default). Resolution is **firm-bound** — a number need only be unique within the firm, so two firms may share `8181-3`. (2) **Link stability is engineered, not free:** prior numbers are **kept as aliases that 301-redirect** to the current one, and the opaque handle always resolves, so renumber/rename never breaks a saved link; an **un-numbered prospective matter** uses its opaque handle until _Open_. Matter-numbering config is constrained to **URL-safe characters** (no `/`/space, else percent-encoded). Entities with **no human number stay opaque** (documents, Directory entities, calendar events); numbered records (matters, invoices) use their number. (3) **Firm comes from the session, never the URL** — no firm segment to spoof; hosts split into `app.` (staff SPA, same-origin `/api`, cookie auth + CSRF), `api.` (**reserved**; public/SDK OpenAPI surface, token auth, no cookies/CSRF), `portal.` (client-portal external principals), and `magnacarta.esq`/`www` (marketing/help/intake/public product-updates). (4) **Path = addressable state, query = ephemeral refinement**; canonical, no trailing slash. (5) **Tabs nest, entities stay flat** — sub-views nest under the parent (`/matters/8181-3/documents`), entities with their own movable identity stay shallow (`/documents/{id}`); **the last identifier resolves and earlier segments are advisory** (a stale parent or superseded number 301-redirects to canonical). (6) **A maintained reserved route-namespace list** prevents feature/system route collisions and stops any user-influenced segment (a matter number, an intake-form path) shadowing a system route. (7) **Access enforcement is server-side on every API request (session → firm → RBAC + ethical walls); the SPA route guard is UX only**, never the boundary — logged-out → **401** → login-with-return-to; a **cross-firm matter is absent from your firm's separate D1 → 404 with no cross-database path to forget** (structural isolation, ADR-0003); unauthorized-but-existing resources (incl. ethical-walled matters) return **404 not 403** to avoid an existence oracle. Matter numbers are **enumerable by design and that grants nothing** (every request is gated; guessing reveals only volume to someone already inside the firm, never a name or access — a firm that must deny even that picks opaque); **`Referrer-Policy: same-origin`** keeps any URL out of third-party `Referer`. **Considered & rejected:** **opaque-only with no firm choice** (most confidential but staff lose the readable matter number; offered as the configurable/forced option instead of mandated); **name/caption slugs** (leak the party caption into history/referrer/shoulder-surf — disqualified, though the _number_ is kept because it carries no client identity); **matter number as the sole key with no opaque handle** (would break links on renumber and has no handle for un-numbered prospective matters — solved by keeping the opaque handle underneath + redirect aliases); **per-firm vanity subdomains** (`firm.magnacarta.esq` — reintroduce a mutable firm slug for branding that white-label custom domains deliver better; the _custom-domain_ seam is kept); **firm-in-path / tenant-in-URL** (a redundant, spoofable second source of truth over the session). **Consequences:** the default URL is readable (`/matters/8181-3`) yet stable across renumber/rename and leaks no client identity; confidentiality remains **structural** (separate-DB 404 + 404-not-403) independent of the chosen scheme; cost is the alias/redirect + URL-safe-number machinery and a per-firm/per-matter setting. Builds on **ADR-0001** (SPA/API-first), **ADR-0003** (tenancy/isolation — the separate-DB 404), **ADR-0004** (RBAC/ethical walls), **ADR-0005** (Workers static assets), **ADR-0007** (configurable matter numbering — now URL-safe-constrained), **ADR-0022** (OpenAPI surface on `api.`), and **ADR-0040** (the scheme setting); the white-label custom-domain path and the reserved-namespace registry are carried-forward seams.
- **ADR-0053** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Design system / UI quality — **UI design quality & the design process — distinctiveness and usability engineered into a foundation, not improvised per screen; a human-set design language + page archetypes that AI agents conform to, enforced by a design-critic + accessibility + visual-regression pipeline.** **Context:** the design-system enforcement of ADR-0028 prevents UI _drift_ (the "jumbled together" look) but not the _generic, AI-made_ look (default-styled primitives, flat hierarchy, toy-data layouts, vague microcopy) — a problem of design _judgment_, which AI performs poorly and which is a live risk for a platform built largely by AI. **Decision:** treat design quality as a **foundational step that precedes feature UI**, mirroring the prevent-by-construction-then-verify shape of ADR-0028 / ADR-0051. (1) **Judgment is set once, up front, by a human** (agent-assisted) and conformed to thereafter — AI applies an opinionated design well but invents one badly. (2) **Two foundation artifacts:** a _distinctive, restrained design language_ (deliberate type/scale, palette-with-intent, legal-power-user density, functional motion, signature elements, anchored to best-in-class references) and _page archetypes_ (List/Index, Record Detail, Editor, Form/Wizard, Dashboard — each fixing layout, hierarchy, and all states), **composed** rather than assembling raw primitives. (3) **A staged UI build pipeline:** interaction design (task/flow → archetype + states) → implementation (against the design system) → **design critique** (an anti-generic + usability rubric) → accessibility (keyboard-first + WCAG) → visual-regression — the critic being the judgment gate a linter cannot be. (4) **Built and reviewed on the load-profile fixtures** (ADR-0034), never toy data, with **domain-grounded microcopy** from the canonical glossary (ADR-0037). **Considered & rejected:** **consistency-enforcement alone** (ADR-0028 — necessary but yields a consistent-yet-generic UI); **per-screen customization of primitives for distinctiveness** (reintroduces the drift ADR-0028 prevents); **one-shot UI generation with no foundation or critic** (the direct cause of the generic AI look). **Consequences:** distinctiveness and usability become structural rather than per-screen luck; the cost is the up-front human design-language / archetype work and maintaining the critic rubric + pipeline. Extends **ADR-0028** (design system); reuses **ADR-0034** (fixtures) and **ADR-0037** (glossary/voice); shares the philosophy of **ADR-0051** (prevent by construction, then verify — applied to design).
- **ADR-0054** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Architecture / frontend — **Client data & state architecture — a three-way state split (URL / server-cache / local) with TanStack Svelte Query for server state, cross-tab BroadcastChannel sync, deliberately-minimal per-feature realtime on the DO-per-job primitive, and Tauri-only offline persistence; a local-first sync engine rejected as the default.** **Context:** the SPA (ADR-0001) committed to deep-linkable routes, multi-tab/pop-out with BroadcastChannel sync, and Tauri offline+sync parity, but the client data/state layer itself was unspecified. **Decision:** split client state **three ways, one owner each** — **URL state** (router, ADR-0052), **server state** (cached API responses) in **TanStack Query (`@tanstack/svelte-query` v6**, verified Svelte-5-runes-native; caching, dedup, refetch-on-focus, **optimistic mutations + rollback** surfacing the Observability Error ID, structured invalidation; query keys/hooks derived from the code-as-contract client, ADR-0022), and **local/UI state** in **Svelte 5 runes**. Cross-tab + pop-out coherence via **BroadcastChannel** cache-invalidation messages. **Realtime is deliberately minimal** — Magna Carta is not live-collaborative at the data layer (document co-editing is Collabora's own channel, ADR-0010); freshness = refetch-on-focus + cross-tab broadcast + **per-feature live channels on the Durable-Object-per-job primitive** (ADR-0039) only where genuinely needed (long-running job progress; the cross-device live timer, ADR-0033). **Offline follows parity** (ADR-0023): the web cache is **in-memory only, cleared on logout** (ADR-0025 app-shell-only service worker), while **Tauri** adds a cache **persister + an outbound mutation queue** behind the same call sites. **Considered & rejected:** a **global server-push / presence layer** for v1 (significant infra + cost before a feature needs it — kept as a seam: extend the DO-channel primitive to a per-matter/per-firm channel later); a **full local-first sync engine** (TanStack DB / Zero / Triplit — heavier, opinionated, and at odds with the server-authoritative ledger + per-firm-D1 model — seam only); a **bespoke runes data layer** (reinvents caching/dedup/invalidation against reuse-first). **Consequences:** a mainstream, typed, reuse-first client data layer meeting the multi-tab/offline/freshness needs without premature realtime infrastructure; cost is the TanStack Query dependency and the Tauri offline adapter. Builds on **ADR-0001** (SPA), **ADR-0022** (typed client + the open OpenAPI-library spike), **ADR-0052** (URL state), **ADR-0023** / **ADR-0025** (parity / caching), **ADR-0039** (DO-per-job realtime primitive), and **ADR-0033** (timer). Recorded in _Delivery model and application architecture_ (Client data & state architecture).
- **ADR-0055** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Time, dates & timezones — model time by kind on the Temporal API: instants in UTC, civil dates/deadlines as PlainDate + jurisdiction zone, IANA zones never fixed offsets; zone homes on Location + per-user display; enforced by one formatting layer + a coherence fitness function.** **Context:** legal work is deadline-driven and multi-jurisdiction; storing everything as one universal timestamp invites off-by-one-DST defects in docketing and inconsistent rendering across offices and timezones; this is the first entry in the new _Foundational data & runtime conventions_ section. **Decision:** adopt the **Temporal API** (TC39 Stage 4 / ES2026 — native in current Chromium/Firefox/Edge, polyfilled elsewhere and in the Workers runtime) and model time **by kind**: **instants** (audit, `created_at`, ledger posting, message sent, login) stored **UTC** (`Temporal.Instant`), never local; **civil dates / deadlines** (filing / statute-of-limitations / hearing dates, reconciliation periods) stored as **`Temporal.PlainDate` + an explicit jurisdiction zone**, never a UTC timestamp, with docketing (_Calendar and rules-based docketing_) computing in the **court's zone** against its business-day/holiday calendar; **wall-clock recurrences** as local-time + zone, materialized per occurrence; **durations** (time entries, ADR-0033) as quantities (minutes). **IANA zone identifiers, never fixed offsets** (offsets break across DST). **Zone homes:** each **Location** carries an IANA zone (ADR-0031; surfaced via settings, ADR-0040), each **user** a display-zone preference; a deadline keys off the court/jurisdiction zone, not the viewer's. **Enforced, not trusted:** a single UI formatting layer always renders in an explicit zone, and a **coherence fitness function** (ADR-0051) forbids a naive local datetime stored where an instant (UTC) or a civil date (`PlainDate`) belongs; rides the fixtures fixed-clock discipline (ADR-0034). **Considered & rejected:** **Luxon / date-fns-tz** (works today without a polyfill, but pre-Temporal idioms and the instant/PlainDate distinction is by convention, not type); **a single firm-level timezone** (wrong for multi-state firms and for court-zone deadlines); **user-timezone-only** (unsafe for docketing — a deadline must key off the court's zone). **Consequences:** DST and cross-zone-deadline bugs are prevented by type; cost is the Temporal polyfill where unsupported and the discipline of carrying zone context on Locations/users. Reuses **ADR-0031** (Locations), **ADR-0040** (settings), **ADR-0034** (fixtures), **ADR-0051** (fitness function); feeds **ADR-0033** (time tracking) and the Calendar/docketing subsystem.
- **ADR-0056** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Observability — **Telemetry analytics tier — Iceberg-on-R2 (R2 Data Catalog) evaluated and deferred; Analytics Engine is the MVP path; design the Logpush→R2→Iceberg seam now, adopt on named triggers.** **Context:** the observability pillar's always-on operational telemetry (ADR-0017, ADR-0038) ships to **Workers Logs/Logpush → R2** and **Analytics Engine**, but Analytics Engine has known ceilings — **~92-day retention**, **adaptive sampling** under high write volume, and a **limited SQL surface** (no rich joins / external engines) — that a long-horizon, exact-count, or BI-grade telemetry query workload would eventually hit. Cloudflare's **R2 Data Catalog** (managed **Apache Iceberg** REST catalog on R2, zero-egress, with managed **compaction** + **snapshot expiration** table maintenance) is a natural fit for the redacted logs **already landing in R2**. **Decision:** **evaluate and defer** — keep **Analytics Engine as the MVP telemetry-query path**, and record R2 Data Catalog as the **designed-but-unbuilt analytics tier** with its insertion point fixed: register the Logpush-in-R2 logs as **Iceberg tables** → query via DuckDB/Spark/external BI at zero egress. It sits on data already written, so adoption is configuration, not re-architecture. **Scope is narrow by construction** — only **redacted, cross-firm aggregate operational telemetry** (the class the pillar already rolls up globally), so it **never touches per-firm privileged data** and stays clear of the ADR-0003 isolation boundary; any future per-firm Iceberg use would require strict per-firm table scoping and is **out of scope**. **Adopt-when triggers:** (1) **retention** beyond ~92 days; (2) **sampling fidelity** — exact/unsampled counts; (3) **query power** — joins / external engines / BI; (4) **cost at scale** — cataloging existing Logpush-in-R2 beats scaling Analytics Engine writes. **On adoption,** enable **compaction** (small-file problem → sustained query performance) and **snapshot expiration**, and treat the open-beta limits (**Parquet-only**, **~2 GB/table/hour** compaction throughput, **orphan-file cleanup not yet automated**) as **verify-before-build** (same provisional posture as ADR-0045). Reuses the **Cron-triggered Workflow → per-firm R2** fan-out established in ADR-0050. **Considered & rejected:** **adopt now** (premature — Phase 0 unscaffolded, the custom report builder / live dashboards are themselves deferred, and Analytics Engine covers the MVP query workload); **reject outright** (forfeits a cheap, well-aligned seam on data already in R2 — re-litigating it later costs more than recording it now). **Consequences:** the telemetry analytics path is captured with explicit flip conditions so it is neither built prematurely nor re-debated; the cost is carrying the seam and re-verifying the beta limits at adoption. **Status Accepted (2026-06-18)** — a trigger bound: product-usage analytics (`ADR-0106`) needs full-fidelity retention/BI, so this tier is **adopted and realized** by that work, with the path fixed as **Logpush → Pipelines → R2 Data Catalog**. R2 Data Catalog now **automates compaction + snapshot expiration (incl. data-file cleanup)** — the open-beta "orphan-file cleanup not yet automated" caveat above no longer binds; Analytics Engine retention is **~90 days (verify current)**. The Logpush→Pipelines provisioning step itself is the remaining deferred build (that slice's sub-step). Extends **ADR-0017** / **ADR-0038** (observability); reuses **ADR-0050** (Cron-Workflow/R2 fan-out) and the _Cloudflare primitive mapping_; bounded by **ADR-0003** (tenancy/isolation).
- **ADR-0057** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Durability on close / graceful shutdown — no close is trusted to be graceful: durability is engineered _before_ the close (acknowledged-write source of truth + idempotency + transactional outbox + on-disk desktop queue + DO durable storage + at-least-once idempotent Queues), and shutdown hooks are best-effort flush-then-warn only, never the correctness mechanism.** **Context:** the cloud is the source of truth (ADR-0003), so "losing data on close" reduces to one class — **client-held state the server has not yet durably accepted** — across four surfaces (web tab/window close, desktop quit, server Workers/DO lifecycle, logout/session end); a design that leans on a clean shutdown hook firing loses data on a crash, kill, or power cut, so the convention must make loss impossible independent of the hook. Cross-cutting and expensive to retrofit, so settled here in _Foundational data & runtime conventions_ alongside the time model (ADR-0055). **Decision:** adopt the **prevent → loud → discipline** ordering (ADR-0051). **(1) Loss made impossible:** an acknowledged write is durable server-side; **idempotency keys** + the **transactional outbox** (queued §9 members) make replay-on-reconnect safe; the **desktop (Tauri) outbound mutation queue is on-disk** (ADR-0054), surviving quit _and_ crash and replaying next launch; **Durable Object state — the cross-device live timer (ADR-0033), job orchestration (ADR-0039) — lives in DO durable storage, not memory**, so hibernation/eviction/deploy are lossless; **Queues are at-least-once with idempotent consumers** so an interrupted job (OCR/conversion/discovery/export) retries; long local desktop jobs **checkpoint** to resume rather than corrupt. **(2) Loss made loud:** a close/logout carrying unacknowledged state trips a guard — web `beforeunload`/`visibilitychange`, Tauri `close-requested` — that **flushes first, then warns only if loss is genuinely unavoidable**; editor/form input **autosaves as server-side drafts** so it is never purely tab-local. **(3) Discipline last, per surface:** web close → guard + autosave (the timer is DO-backed, survives the tab); desktop quit → intercept, drain/persist the queue + confirm checkpoints (offline quit safe by construction); server → stateless handlers complete-or-fail under idempotent retry, no correctness on clean teardown; logout → cache-clear (ADR-0054) **gated on the outbound queue being drained or persisted**. **Enforced, not trusted:** the close/logout guard is a **single shared client primitive** (not per-screen), and a **coherence fitness function** (ADR-0051) asserts live-timer/job state is written through DO durable storage, not held only in memory. **Considered & rejected:** **shutdown-hook-as-durability** (loses on crash/kill/power-loss — hooks are best-effort UX only); **warn-only / "you have unsaved changes"** with no durable queue (data still lost when the user is forced to quit or the OS kills the app); **a full local-first sync engine** as the close-safety mechanism (rejected platform-wide in ADR-0054 — fights the server-authoritative ledger; the on-disk outbound queue is the bounded subset that suffices). **Consequences:** loss-on-close is prevented by construction across all four surfaces; cost is carrying the on-disk queue + draft-autosave plumbing and holding the discipline that DO/job state always lands in durable storage. Per-tier flush/checkpoint/autosave **intervals are deferred to the build** — the convention binds now, the numbers arrive with the code (the same intentional seam as ADR-0039). Reuses **ADR-0003** (source of truth), **ADR-0054** (desktop queue + cache model), **ADR-0033** (live timer), **ADR-0039** (job DOs / compute placement), **ADR-0051** (fitness function); depends on the queued **idempotency** and **transactional-outbox** §9 members.
- **ADR-0058** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Maintenance agents — **Docs-maintenance agent — a propose-only §25 agent for mechanical doc drafting + coverage-gap flagging; authoring stays in-commit, deterministic hygiene goes to CI.** **Context:** ADR-0016 named a "changelog/roadmap maintenance agent" only in passing and never made it first-class, while the documentation model (golden rules 5/9, _Product updates_, _Development guidelines_) is **docs-in-commit / one-home-per-fact** — so an agent that _authors_ feature documentation as a downstream step would reintroduce exactly the drift "version-accuracy by construction" exists to prevent (the running app could advertise what it does not contain). The need is real but must be scoped so automation never becomes the authoring path. **Decision:** add **one propose-only docs-maintenance agent** to the _Maintenance agents_ (§25) family, scoped to two jobs: (1) **mechanical drafting** — draft `[Unreleased]` CHANGELOG entries from merged PRs and roll ROADMAP status up from the changelog (the work ADR-0016 promised in passing, now owned); (2) **docs-coverage-gap flagging** — a user-visible PR with no `docs/user/` guide, a design change with no ADR, a referenced `ADR-NNNN` that does not exist. It **never originates design narrative or user-guide prose** (that ships in-commit with the change author) and **never auto-merges** (trust/ledger/conflicts paths always require human review, per ADR-0016). **Deterministic doc hygiene is CI, not the agent:** the cross-reference rule (a `§N` reference from _outside_ the white paper), dead/broken links, and an empty-`[Unreleased]`-on-a-user-facing-PR soft gate are **`vp check` lint rules** — cheaper, faster, and more reliable than an LLM; the agent carries only the judgment-bearing residue. This is the **LLM-as-fitness-function** pattern (ADR-0051) pointed at the docs corpus: structure/CI first, agent for the fuzzy part, discipline never the strategy. **Considered & rejected:** a **docs-authoring agent** (writes feature docs as a separate step — breaks docs-in-commit, manufactures the very drift the model prevents); a **maximal docs auditor** that also does prose/style review and cross-reference integrity _itself_ (overlaps the deterministic CI checks and risks the agent re-authoring docs — the integrity checks are deterministic and belong in CI); **leaving it as scattered prose** (the current half-specified state across the white paper, `documentation.md`, CHANGELOG, and ROADMAP — relitigated each time it is read). **Consequences:** the changelog/roadmap automation and coverage-gap safety net are captured as a first-class agent with an explicit boundary, so docs automation gains leverage without ever becoming the authoring path or sacrificing version-accuracy-by-construction; cost is building the agent plus the CI doc-lint rules, and holding the line that the agent proposes and never authors narrative. Extends **ADR-0016** (maintenance agents — does not supersede it); reuses **ADR-0037** (living docs), **ADR-0051** (LLM-as-fitness-function, propose-only), **ADR-0022** (PR/CI surface, `vp check`), and the _Product updates_ design (ADR-0048).
- **ADR-0059** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Notifications — **Notifications subsystem — an orchestration layer above the Communications transport: one notification-type registry owns the _decision_ (event → who → which channel → wanted? → read?) and the in-app center; external delivery is delegated to the Communications ports; realtime stays deliberately minimal (durable rows + refetch/poll, per-user push a seam).** **Context:** notifications were anticipated everywhere but designed nowhere — a _Delivery model_ parity row (web vs. native), a _Communications layer_ "trigger engine" (deadline/payment/report-ready reminders), a _Settings_ "My Account → notifications" category, a job-completion "completion notification" (ADR-0030), a _Tasks and workflows_ "notification/escalation" Needs-development note, _Calendar/docketing_ reminders, _Custodian_ release reminders, and the diagnostics-agent "acknowledge the user in-app/portal" (ADR-0038) — plus _Architectural integrity & coherence_ (ADR-0051) explicitly naming "notification templates" as an anticipated registry category, while ADR-0054 **rejected a global server-push layer for v1**. Eight subsystems depend on a thing with no home, no event model, and no ADR. **Decision:** make Notifications a first-class subsystem that **separates the decision from the delivery** and owns the decision. (1) **Boundary — orchestration above transport.** Notifications owns the event model, recipient resolution, per-recipient routing preferences, and the in-app notification center; it **delegates external delivery (email/SMS/voice) to the _Communications layer_ ports** (ADR-0047), which becomes pure channel transport — the trigger-engine _brain_ relocates here (**ADR-0047 amended**). Rejected: _fold into Communications_ (overloads external/client transport with internal/staff concerns and telecom-compliance domains it does not share) and _in-app-center-only_ (splits routing across two homes — the registry anti-pattern). (2) **Registry-first** (ADR-0051/ADR-0040): every notification is a declarative **type** (`key`, `category`, **audience** internal-`member`/external-_Directory_-entity, `severity`, `mandatory` vs opt-out-able, eligible `channels` + default routing, channel-aware `templates` from the glossary, `cadence` immediate/scheduled/digest, `audited`), the single `audience` field unifying the staff bell and the client appointment-reminder under one model; conformance is a **fitness function** (every declared channel has a template; mandatory categories declare no opt-out; a notifiable event not routed through the registry is flagged). (3) **Preferences** are a _Settings_ My-Account category resolving user → firm → system (ADR-0040), with **mandatory categories (security, trust-compliance) non-silenceable** and external routing additionally gated by the ports' **STOP/HELP + TCPA** state. (4) **Delivery is always durable** — a **per-firm D1 row** the instant the event fires (IDs + non-sensitive fields only, never privileged content; a canonical deep-link per ADR-0052) — so the realtime question governs only how fast an _already-stored_ row surfaces. **v1 channels:** in-app (the center), email + SMS via the Comms ports; **seams:** web push (VAPID/Push API on the app-shell SW), desktop-native/tray (rides Tauri, in-app fallback — parity per ADR-0023), digest rollup. (5) **Realtime stays minimal (honors ADR-0054):** v1 freshness = **refetch-on-focus + cross-tab BroadcastChannel + a cheap unread-count poll** (no global push); **job-completion rides the existing per-job Durable Object channel** (ADR-0030/ADR-0039) for instant delivery; a **per-user DO push channel is the documented seam** (extends the named DO-per-job primitive), adoptable later behind the same call sites. Rejected: _build the per-user push channel now_ (the infra ADR-0054 deliberately deferred; no sub-second need justifies it). (6) **Raise as a chokepoint seam** (ADR-0051): one `notify(type, context)` port, but the high-value raises are **co-located with an existing unbypassable path** — money/sensitive notifications raise from **the same write that lands the audit-log entry**, task assignment from the task write path, conflicts/portal/deadline/job from their own seams — and fan-out runs **off the hot path** via Queues (ADR-0039). **Scheduled** reminders are materialized by a **Cron-triggered Workflow** sweeping due reminders per firm (the ADR-0050 fan-out), deadline dates computed in the **court's zone** (ADR-0055).
