(7) **Confidentiality** inherits the observability discipline (ADR-0038): external channels carry **no privileged content** (reference + deep link, allowlist-assembled), in-app may show more behind RBAC/ethical-walls but **re-checks access at read time** in the firm-scoped DAL (a stale/walled row is filtered, never an existence oracle — the 404-not-403 logic of ADR-0052); rows are per-firm + recipient-scoped; preference changes and audited-category dispatch are audited (metadata only). **Considered & rejected:** _fold into the Communications layer_; _in-app-center-only_; _build the global server-push layer now_; _embed content in notifications_ (a leak surface — references only). **Consequences:** the scattered triggers and the _Tasks_ escalation / _docketing_ reminder Needs-development notes gain one home and become notification types; the Communications layer simplifies to transport; v1 ships durable in-app + email/SMS + scheduled reminders without premature realtime infra; cost is the registry + in-app center + the on-raise seam discipline. **Amends ADR-0047** (Comms = transport); reuses **ADR-0040** (settings/preferences), **ADR-0051** (registry/seam/fitness), **ADR-0054** (freshness model + per-user-push seam), **ADR-0030**/**ADR-0039** (DO-per-job reuse, off-hot-path), **ADR-0050** (Cron-Workflow fan-out), **ADR-0055** (court-zone deadlines), **ADR-0038** (redaction), **ADR-0003**/**ADR-0052** (isolation, access-gated read, deep-links), **ADR-0023** (parity). A full subsystem spec (the type catalog, the raise-seam inventory, and the in-app-center UI archetype) is the planned next step.
- **ADR-0060** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Discovery — **OCR engine — PP-OCR (PaddleOCR PP-OCRv5) _models_ consumed as ONNX through a Rust `ort` pipeline, never the PaddlePaddle framework; one engine + one pinned, versioned model set compiled native for both the server Container and the Tauri desktop, so the web/desktop "split" is a deployment _location_, not a divergent implementation.** **Context:** OCR was named as a pipeline stage in four places — _Discovery_ (`ingest → R2 → queue-driven extraction/OCR in Containers → index`), _PDF services_ (scanned-PDF→searchable-PDF, web server-side as a fallback to desktop-local), the _Delivery model_ parity table (Container OCR on web vs. on-device OCR on desktop), and the _Performance & concurrency model_ (heavy CPU work → native Rust in Containers + Queues + DO-per-job) — but **no engine was ever chosen** and no ADR owned it; the parity table read as if web and desktop might run _different_ OCR, which would fork results and double maintenance. **Hard constraints** narrowed the field decisively: **self-hosted / no third-party egress** (confidentiality — privileged bytes never reach a cloud OCR API, same logic as in-house conversion ADR-0030 and observability ADR-0038); **CPU-only** (Cloudflare Containers and user laptops have no GPU); **deterministic** (OCR text feeds both the Tantivy/Vectorize index _and_ discovery evidentiary hashing/chain-of-custody — same input must yield same output regardless of where it ran); **permissive license** (commercial multi-tenant SaaS — the AGPL/OpenRAIL wall of ADR-0030/ADR-0012); and **Rust-native** so the _scoped shared core_ parity story (ADR-0024) holds. **Decision:** (1) **Engine = PP-OCRv5 models via ONNX/`ort`.** Best accuracy of the permissive CPU-class engines (PP-OCRv5 ≈ top of non-VLM benchmarks; ~94.5% OmniDocBench v1.5 for the model family), 100+ languages incl. strong CJK, word-level bounding boxes for redaction — consumed as Apache-2.0 ONNX weights on neutral ONNX Runtime, which **sheds the PaddlePaddle runtime entirely** (the heavy, awkward, provenance-questionable part) and runs from one Rust crate native in **both** the web-path Container and the Tauri desktop. (2) **One engine, two locations, versioned model artifact.** The same crate + the **same pinned model version + config** run server-side and on-device; each produced text layer records the **model version** that produced it, and a model upgrade triggers re-OCR rather than silently diverging — this is what makes the desktop-local path (privacy: bytes never leave the device; speed: no upload of large scan sets; cost: offload off the Container bill; offline) a pure _location_ optimization with reproducible output, not a second implementation. Interrupted jobs retry under the at-least-once idempotent Queue/checkpoint model (ADR-0057/ADR-0039). (3) **Crate pick is a build-time spike:** `oar-ocr` leads (document-focused — bundles orientation detection + rectification that scanned exhibits need), with `retto` (proves desktop + WASM on `ort`) and `rust-paddle-ocr` (MNN backend, 100+ langs) as alternatives; the spike runs them head-to-head against **docTR/OnnxTR** on representative legal scans on the **one axis docTR could win — structured layout output** — the open question being whether docTR's block/line/word JSON beats `oar-ocr` enough to justify forfeiting the Rust-native desktop path and accepting a Python/C++ sidecar (same posture as the ADR-0022 library spike). (4) **Fallback = Tesseract 5** (Apache-2.0, ~10 MB, fastest, deterministic) as the conservative floor for clean typed scans; we rejected the _Stirling-PDF bundle_ (ADR-0030), not Tesseract itself. (5) **Escape hatch = `ocrs`** (RTen; MIT/Apache, **pure Rust, zero native deps, clean WASM**) — the only path to _browser-side_ OCR or a C++-free build, carried for that future but early-preview/Latin-only today. **Considered & rejected:** **the PaddlePaddle framework** (heavy second runtime, provenance — consume the models via ONNX instead); **Surya** (excellent + layout-aware, but model weights are **OpenRAIL-M with a commercial-revenue threshold** — a poison pill for commercial SaaS, the same wall as AGPL); **EasyOCR** (Apache-2.0 but 2026 benchmarks say skip for accuracy-critical work; heavy); **docTR as primary** (Apache-2.0 and best structured output, but Python/PyTorch with only partial ONNX export, weak multilingual, and **no Rust crate → breaks one-engine-two-targets parity**; kept only as the spike challenger for layout-heavy work); **VLM OCR** (olmOCR / dots.ocr / GLM-OCR / PaddleOCR-VL — the 2026 accuracy frontier, but **GPU-required** and **non-deterministic / prone to hallucinating plausible-but-absent text**, disqualified twice over: the CPU-Container/laptop constraint _and_ evidentiary determinism — classic det+rec OCR fails _visibly_, which is a feature for chain-of-custody; recorded as a possible **future web-only high-accuracy tier via Workers AI**, never on the evidentiary path); **any cloud OCR API** (egress — forbidden by confidentiality). **Consequences:** the OCR stage gains a concrete, permissive, CPU-friendly, deterministic engine and the parity table's "split" is reframed as one engine in two locations with a versioned model artifact — accurate search + reproducible hashes regardless of where a doc was processed; cost is carrying the ONNX-model artifacts, the model-version stamping/re-OCR discipline, and running the crate-vs-docTR spike before the Phase-4 build. OCR folds into the _scoped shared core_ "discovery extraction" already named in **ADR-0024**. Reuses **ADR-0012** (Discovery pipeline), **ADR-0024** (Rust scope / shared core), **ADR-0023** (web/desktop parity), **ADR-0030** (conversion orchestrator dispatches scanned-PDF→searchable-PDF to _this_ engine), **ADR-0039** (Containers + Queues + DO-per-job compute placement), **ADR-0057** (idempotent retry / checkpoints for interrupted jobs); bounded by **ADR-0003** (per-firm isolation) and the confidentiality discipline of **ADR-0038**. The specific crate and the docTR comparison resolve in the **Phase-4 Discovery** subsystem spec.
- **ADR-0061** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Money & currency representation — integer minor units at two fixed scales (amounts 2dp / rates 4dp), ISO-4217 currency-tagged USD-only (no FX), arithmetic via Dinero.js v2 (`Dinero<bigint>`) behind a thin in-house `money` seam, rounding named per boundary (half-up tax/invoice, banker's GL, largest-remainder splits); floats forbidden.** **Context:** money binds the ledger, trust, time/rates, billing, and payments and is the most painful convention to migrate after rows exist; a §9 member queued alongside the time model (ADR-0055). The crown-jewel requirements are exactness (trust three-way reconciliation must reconcile to the cent), sub-cent precision where calculations need it (rate×quantity, tax, contingency splits, mileage at $0.655/mi), and a representation that survives a future move of ledger math to Rust→WASM (ADR-0024/0039). **Decision:** (1) **Two storage scales, both integer minor units in D1:** amounts (balances, ledger postings, invoice-line totals, payments, trust entries) at **2dp/cents**; rates and unit prices at **4dp**, the extra precision kept so products don't lose money before rounding — the established billing-system pattern (more precision in calculation, round late, at the transaction boundary). (2) **ISO-4217 currency on every value** (always USD now; ledger accounts single-currency, postings inherit), the field present so multi-currency is a later evolution but **no FX machinery is built** ("design for the superset, ship the subset"). (3) **One arithmetic engine, not two systems** — **Dinero.js v2** (now stable, v2.0.2 Mar 2026; widely adopted; `Dinero<bigint>` gives float-free, ceiling-free amounts, `transformScale` across the 2dp/4dp split, and largest-remainder `allocate`) **wrapped by a thin in-house `money` module** owning the branded `Money`/`Rate`/ratio types, the scale convention, currency tagging, the formatting layer (`Intl.NumberFormat`, explicit currency — mirroring the ADR-0055 time formatter), and DAL enforcement. The wrapper is the swappable seam (same ports posture as ADR-0032/ADR-0047), so the library can later yield to an in-house or Rust→WASM core without touching a stored row — because the **representation** (integer minor units), not the library, is the expensive-to-migrate part. (4) **Percentages/ratios are a dimensionless decimal, never `Money`.** (5) **Rounding named per boundary, never one global default** — `money` exposes intent-named helpers: **tax → round-half-up** (Streamlined Sales & Use Tax §324 statutory rule; Nevada is an SSUTA member), **invoice-line totals → half-up** (client expectation), **GL/financial-statement aggregation → banker's/half-even** (GAAP-accepted, removes half-up's cumulative upward bias), **splits/allocations → largest-remainder** (sums to the whole exactly — contingency client/fee/cost/lien, payment application). Raw rounding requires an explicit mode; rounding only at boundaries, never mid-calculation. **Enforced, not trusted:** `Money`/`Rate` are the only types the DAL accepts for money columns; a coherence fitness function (ADR-0051) bans float math on money and `REAL` money columns in D1, and flags rounding outside the named helpers; fixtures (ADR-0034) mint money only through these types. **Considered & rejected:** **float/decimal-string money** (precision loss — disqualified); **one scale everywhere (cents only)** (rounding rates to whole cents loses real money on volume and drifts reconciliation); **single 4dp scale everywhere** (stored balances at sub-cent disagree with penny-exact bank/trust statements); **build the arithmetic in-house from scratch** (reuse-first, ADR's golden rule #1 — and Dinero now meets every requirement incl. bigint + multi-scale + allocate, so the build isn't earned; the wrapper keeps the irreversible storage decision ours regardless); **full multi-currency now** (no current need — a Nevada firm bills USD; FX revaluation is real complexity for zero benefit); **one global rounding default** (the correct mode is context-specific and tax is legally fixed). **Consequences:** money is exact where it settles and precise where it calculates; the ledger sum-to-zero (ADR-0008) and no-negative-trust (ADR-0009) invariants stay penny-exact and reconciliation is correct by construction; cost is the Dinero dependency (behind a seam) and the discipline of the two-scale convention + named rounding. Reuses **ADR-0034** (fixtures), **ADR-0051** (fitness function), the ADR-0055 formatter pattern; binds **ADR-0008**/**ADR-0009**/**ADR-0032**/**ADR-0033** (ledger, trust, payments, time-rates) and LEDES export (ADR-0033). Implementation detail in the _Foundational data conventions_ spec. **Amendment (2026-06-08):** the `@magna-carta/money` implementation uses Dinero.js v2's **default number calculator** (`Dinero<number>`, JS safe-integer amounts), **not `Dinero<bigint>`**. Two findings drove this: (a) amounts never approach 2^53 ($90T at cents / $900B at a 4-dp rate) in legal practice management, so JS-number integer arithmetic is exact across our range; (b) Dinero 2.0.2's bigint build is broken for `multiply`, `transformScale` (all rounding), `allocate`, and `toDecimal` — it mixes `number` and `bigint` inside its own compiled `multiplyFn`. The number calculator runs the entire surface correctly. The branded-seam discipline, the two storage scales, and the named rounding helpers are unchanged; storage columns (`*_minor`/`*_rate_e4` `INTEGER`) are unchanged. The documented escape hatch — swap `src/dinero.ts` to a `decimal.js`-backed custom calculator via `createDinero({ calculator })` — is a one-file change behind the seam if amounts ever exceed 2^53 (verified working end-to-end on 2026-06-08).
- **ADR-0062** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Identifier strategy — one opaque, type-prefixed, CSPRNG-random, URL-safe handle per row that is also its primary key; purely random (never time-encoded), ~126-bit guess-resistant, UNIQUE+retry for guaranteed non-overlap, branded TS id types; integer-PK-plus-handle split rejected at per-firm scale.** **Context:** ADR-0052 established that matters carry an immutable, always-resolvable opaque handle (and that documents/Directory/events "stay opaque"), but the id format, its relation to the database key, and the generation/crypto details were unspecified; fixtures (ADR-0034) require seeded deterministic id generation, and per-firm D1 (ADR-0003) means ids need only be unique within a firm. The handle is security-sensitive — it is the enumeration/IDOR surface the server-side-only access gate (ADR-0052, 404-not-403) assumes is closed. **Decision:** (1) **One handle per row, serving as both primary key and public id** — a **type-prefixed, CSPRNG-random, URL-safe token** (`mat_`, `doc_`, `dir_`, `inv_`, `evt_`, `pgr_`, `usr_`, `firm_`…) — this _is_ ADR-0052's opaque handle. (2) **Purely random, never time-encoded** — **UUIDv7/ULID/Snowflake rejected** because their embedded timestamp leaks record-creation time, growth rate, and predictability, a real confidentiality leak; generation through a **CSPRNG** (`crypto.getRandomValues`, available in Workers), with a **seeded PRNG injected in fixtures** so ADR-0034 determinism survives. (3) **Entropy sized for guess-resistance (~126 bits, nanoid-21 class)**, not merely collision-avoidance — the IDOR/unguessability bar. (4) **Non-overlap is a correctness guarantee, not a probability:** type-prefix space partitioning + per-firm-D1 scoping + a `UNIQUE` constraint with **retry-on-collision** in the generator. (5) **Prefixes are a registry** (ADR-0051) — self-describing in logs/Error-ID trail (ADR-0038), defense-in-depth against id-type confusion — reinforced by **branded TypeScript id types** (`MatterId` ≠ `DocumentId`). (6) **Cross-database references** (control-plane `usr_`/`firm_` referenced by per-firm `member` rows, ADR-0003) use the same handle scheme, since random handles cross DB boundaries where integer keys cannot. (7) The **matter number** stays a separate human/mutable alias with redirect aliasing (ADR-0052/ADR-0007), never the key. **Considered & rejected:** **integer-PK + separate handle column** (the textbook SQLite max-performance pattern, but its edge is negligible/speculative at per-firm scale where bulk content is in R2 — _measure before optimizing_, ADR-0039 — while it taxes every API boundary with handle↔key translation and an N+1 risk; if scale ever demands it, the handle being the stable external ref makes it a pure internal migration that breaks no URL/contract); **integer rowid PK + handle** (loses self-describing ids and still needs the handle layer); **UUIDv7/ULID as the handle** (time-leak — the decisive security rejection); **the SQLite "random PK fragments the B-tree" fear** (a myth — D1's hidden rowid is the clustered index). **Consequences:** one id per row, enumeration/IDOR-safe by randomness, self-describing, debuggable, type-safe; the confidentiality story (ADR-0052) holds and the id itself leaks nothing; cost is slightly larger TEXT keys/FKs (negligible at per-firm scale) and the prefix-registry + branded-type discipline. Reuses **ADR-0034** (seeded RNG), **ADR-0051** (prefix registry/fitness), **ADR-0038** (Error-ID); underpins **ADR-0052** (opaque handle), **ADR-0003** (cross-DB refs), and the _Idempotency_/_Outbox_ UNIQUE-insert idiom (ADR-0063/ADR-0064). Implementation detail in the _Foundational data conventions_ spec. **Amendment (2026-06-13, slice 1·1 build):** the type prefix is now the **entity type spelled out in full**, in camelCase, identical to the registry key — `matter_`, `document_`, `ledgerAccount_`, `fileVersion_`, `idempotencyRecord_`, `auditLog_`, … — replacing the original abbreviations (`mat_`/`doc_`/`acct_`/`usr_`/…). The owner's call: golden rule 12 (no shorthand) governs identifiers too, and the abbreviated prefixes were an unflagged exception to it. The handle shape `<prefix>_<token>` and the single-`_` separator are **unchanged**; the prefix is **camelCase, not snake_case**, deliberately — our token alphabet already contains `_`, so a snake*case prefix would make the prefix/token boundary ambiguous, whereas camelCase keeps exactly one separating `*`(the recognized TypeID standard assumes an underscore-free suffix, which we do not have). The prefix validator was relaxed from`^[a-z]+$` to camelCase; the registry is now an identity map (prefix === key). No production rows carried handles (only the control-plane schema is live, with no seeded handle rows), so this was a code + committed-seed-literal + test change, not a data migration.
- **ADR-0063** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Idempotency — client-generated IETF `Idempotency-Key` with Stripe semantics; atomic dedup via a UNIQUE idempotency-row insert committed in the same D1 transaction as the effect; chokepoint-enforced and required on side-effectful routes; processor pass-through.** **Context:** exactly-once money movement under retries/double-clicks/network failure is a crown-jewel correctness property binding the append-only ledger (ADR-0008), payments (ADR-0032), and the client's optimistic-mutation/retry behavior (ADR-0054); ADR-0057 (durability on close) already names idempotency as a dependency. A §9 member. **Decision:** (1) **Standard surface** — mutations accept a client-generated **`Idempotency-Key`** header (IETF `draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header`, the WG draft standardizing exactly this; Stripe semantics): the SvelteKit client (TanStack Query, ADR-0054) mints one key per _logical_ operation and reuses it on retry; the public API (ADR-0022) takes the same header from third parties. (2) **Mechanism** — a per-firm D1 `idempotency_key` table stores key + request fingerprint (method+path+body hash) + status (`in_progress`/`completed`) + captured response (status+body). Repeat resolution: **completed+same-fingerprint → replay stored response, no re-execute**; **in-progress+same-fingerprint → `409`**; **same-key+different-fingerprint → `422`** (client bug surfaced). (3) **Atomicity** — the idempotency row and the effect (ledger postings) **commit in one D1 transaction**, and the row's `UNIQUE` key **is** the concurrency guard (two simultaneous identical requests race to insert; one wins, the other is served as a duplicate) — exactly-once with no separate lock, reusing the UNIQUE-insert idiom of ADR-0062. (4) **Chokepoint-enforced** (ADR-0051) — idempotency lives in the API/DAL mutation middleware; each mutating route declares `idempotency: required | optional` in metadata (**required** on money/trust/payment/outbound-comms routes), a conformance fitness function asserting every side-effectful route requires it. (5) **Scope/lifecycle** — per-firm; the fingerprint includes the endpoint so cross-endpoint reuse trips the mismatch; **~24h TTL**, a Cron job purges expired rows (ephemeral dedup state, not the record of truth — the append-only ledger is). (6) **Processor pass-through** — the same key is passed to Helcim/Stripe (which honor idempotency keys) so the external charge dedups too; reliable _outbound_ delivery is the transactional outbox (ADR-0064). Inbound dedup + outbox + idempotent consumers = end-to-end **effectively-once**. **Considered & rejected:** **server-generated keys** (idempotency is inherently client-keyed across retries); **require a key on all mutations** (uniform but taxes every trivial write — required only where irreversible); **a separate distributed lock for concurrency** (the UNIQUE-insert in the single-DB transaction already serializes — simpler, no extra infra); **storing only success responses** (Stripe stores success _and_ failure — a failed-then-retried op must replay the failure, not silently re-run). **Consequences:** money posts exactly once under any retry; double-submit and network-uncertainty are safe; cost is the per-firm idempotency table + TTL sweep + the route-metadata discipline. Reuses **ADR-0062** (UNIQUE-insert idiom), **ADR-0051** (chokepoint/conformance), **ADR-0054** (client mutation keys); binds **ADR-0008**/**ADR-0032** (ledger, payments); paired with **ADR-0064** (outbox) and depended on by **ADR-0057** (durability). Implementation detail in the _Foundational data conventions_ spec. **Amendment (2026-06-09):** D1 has **no interactive transactions** (`BEGIN` is rejected at runtime; the upstream feature request is closed-not-planned; the 2025 Sessions API is read-replication consistency only — no transaction semantics), so "the same D1 transaction" is realized as **one `D1Database.batch()`** — Cloudflare executes a batch as a single all-or-nothing transaction. The `@magna-carta/data-access` write path is: pre-check `SELECT` (fast replay/409/422), **speculative** handler effect (it produces statements and outbox messages, committing nothing — safe because handlers never call external services directly; that is the ADR-0064 chokepoint), then one batch ordered `[INSERT idempotency record, …business statements, outbox rows]`. The UNIQUE(key) insert remains the concurrency guard: a losing duplicate's **entire batch rolls back**, and it re-reads and replays the winner's stored response. The record is written **directly as `completed` with the captured response in the same batch as the effect**, so crash-between-effect-and-record is impossible by construction; `in_progress` is retained as a reserved state (a future long-running/external-call path) and still yields 409 when encountered. All primary keys are minted client-side (ADR-0062), which is what makes up-front batch binding possible.
- **ADR-0064** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Transactional outbox — write the intended effect to an `outbox` row in the same D1 transaction as the business change; a Cron-Workflow relay (DO-poke latency seam) publishes to at-least-once Cloudflare Queues; idempotent consumers; chokepoint-enforced; sagas via Workflows for multi-step.** **Context:** a committed D1 change often must trigger an external effect (payment capture ADR-0032, outbound email/SMS/voice ADR-0047, conflicts re-check ADR-0013, search-index/projection updates), but **a D1 transaction spans a single database only** (no D1+Queues/R2/external atomicity — Cloudflare-confirmed), the classic dual-write problem; ADR-0057 (durability on close) names the outbox as a dependency. Cloudflare's own docs endorse the outbox pattern (publish to Queues from a Durable Object). A §9 member. **Decision:** (1) **Atomic write** — the effect is written as an **`outbox` row in the same D1 transaction** as the business change (and the audit row): `id` (ADR-0062 handle) + topic + payload + status + attempts + aggregate*key + seq + timestamps — recorded iff the change commits. (2) **Chokepoint** (ADR-0051) — a handler that wrote to D1 may **not** enqueue/call-external directly; it goes through the outbox, a boundary fitness function failing CI on any bypass (genuinely independent async work whose job row \_is* its state may enqueue directly). (3) **Relay** — a **Cron-triggered Workflow** sweeps each firm's outbox and publishes pending rows to **Cloudflare Queues** (the cross-firm fan-out of ADR-0050) as the **correctness floor**, with a **post-commit poke to a per-firm relay Durable Object (alarm-driven)** as a **latency seam** on top — prompt drain, but correctness never depends on the poke. Ship the sweep first (measure-first, ADR-0039). (4) **Consumers** — Queue-consumer Workers perform the effect (payment, comms ports, conflicts, indexing/projections) **idempotently**, deduping on the outbox id (reusing ADR-0063); since Queues are **at-least-once and unordered**, ordering-sensitive topics apply by `aggregate_key`+`seq` (skip-stale); max-attempts → **dead-letter** to observability (ADR-0038). (5) **Scope** — the outbox is a **reliable async-dispatch primitive, not event-sourcing**; D1 tables + ledger + audit stay the system of record. **Multi-step external flows** (charge → post → receipt) compose as **sagas via Workflows** with compensating actions (Cloudflare's recommended path), built on the outbox + idempotency primitives, not expanded until a flow needs it. **Considered & rejected:** **direct enqueue/external-call after the D1 write** (the dual-write bug — an effect lost or duplicated when one side fails); **Cron-sweep only with no poke** (correct but adds latency to money/comms — the poke is the bounded enhancement); **DO-storage outbox** (business data is in D1, so the outbox must be in D1 to share the transaction — a DO can't co-write with D1); **event-sourcing as the persistence model** (out of scope — no ADR mandates it; the outbox is delivery, not the source of truth). **Consequences:** state changes and their side effects are consistent without distributed transactions; with inbound idempotency (ADR-0063) the system is effectively-once end to end; cost is the outbox table + relay + idempotent-consumer discipline and at-least-once redelivery. Reuses **ADR-0050** (Cron-Workflow fan-out), **ADR-0039** (Queues/DO compute placement), **ADR-0063** (consumer dedup), **ADR-0062** (ids), **ADR-0051** (chokepoint/fitness); serves **ADR-0032**/**ADR-0047**/**ADR-0013** and depended on by **ADR-0057** (durability). Implementation detail in the _Foundational data conventions_ spec. **Amendment (2026-06-09):** "the same D1 transaction" is realized as the single-`batch()` write path of the ADR-0063 amendment — outbox rows ride the same batch as the business change and the idempotency record, so the row exists iff the change committed. Per-aggregate `seq` is computed **inside the INSERT** (a `MAX(seq)+1` subselect — batch statements cannot share JS-side results but run sequentially in one transaction). The relay **floor shipped as a library** (`sweepOutbox`: publish-**then**-mark for at-least-once, per-row failure isolation, `attempts` tracking, dead-letter callback at max attempts); the Cron-Workflow sweep wiring and the per-firm relay-DO latency poke land with `apps/worker`. **Amendment (2026-06-26):** the "Cron-Workflow" / "Cron-triggered Workflow" wording above is a loose label, **not** a literal use of Cloudflare Workflows (the durable-execution product): the relay shipped — and remains — a plain Cron `scheduled` handler (`apps/worker/src/scheduled.ts`) invoking the `sweepOutbox` library, wired through `triggers.crons`; no `WorkflowEntrypoint` or `[[workflows]]` binding backs it. Cloudflare Workflows the product is adopted separately and narrowly — for the multi-step external flows this ADR names ("**sagas via Workflows** with compensating actions"), once a flow needs it — per ADR-`adopt-workflows-saga-orchestration` (the saga step-rollback primitive Cloudflare shipped 2026-06-25 is what makes that path first-class, with firm provisioning as the pilot). The "sagas via Workflows" intent stands unchanged; only the relay's labeling is corrected.
- **ADR-0065** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Schema migration & the database fleet — Drizzle + drizzle-kit as the single TS source of truth for types and migrations (under the firm-scoped DAL); a Cron-Workflow rollout orchestrator across the per-firm-D1 fleet with control-plane version tracking, per-firm failure isolation, eager + lazy application, and expand-contract zero-downtime discipline; built now though one firm runs as a fleet of one.** **Context:** one firm = one D1 (ADR-0003), so schema evolution is a distributed fleet operation across thousands of databases + the control-plane D1, not a single migration ("add a column" = thousands of tracked `ALTER TABLE`s with a mixed-version window); Better Auth already constrained the query layer to a Drizzle or Kysely adapter (ADR-0004); Cloudflare's guidance is explicit — "build this [fleet migration] tooling before you need it." The heaviest §9 member. **Decision:** (1) **Tooling = Drizzle ORM + drizzle-kit** — schema defined once in TypeScript is the single source of truth for **both** the DAL's generated types **and** the migrations (`drizzle-kit generate` from schema diffs), so types and migrations never drift (the Kysely failure mode where they are separate); Drizzle sits **under** the firm-scoped DAL, which still owns firm-scoping/`protected()`/audit/outbox/idempotency; two independently-versioned lineages (control-plane, per-firm). (2) **Fleet state in the control-plane** — each per-firm D1 records applied migrations; the **firm registry (ADR-0003) mirrors each firm's schema version** (it already tracks which DBs exist / which firm they serve — version is one more column), giving the fleet-wide visibility Cloudflare requires. (3) **Rollout orchestrator** — a **Cron-triggered Workflow** iterating firms (the recurring fleet-fan-out primitive shared with ADR-0050 backups and ADR-0064 outbox relay): applies pending migrations per firm, updates the registry version, with **bounded concurrency, per-firm failure isolation** (a failed firm quarantined + alerted to observability ADR-0038, the fleet continues), **idempotent/resumable** application (current firms skipped), and a migration-state report. (4) **Eager + lazy** — active firms migrate in the fan-out; **dormant firms catch up lazily on next access** (the DAL applies pending migrations on connect) — Cloudflare's "migrate active tenants, dormant ones when they return"; lazy is a clean seam over the eager floor. (5) **Expand-contract discipline** (zero-downtime, the part that makes a non-atomic fleet safe) — every migration backward-compatible with deployed code, ordered _expand → deploy tolerant code → backfill → contract_, so running code never meets an incompatible schema; a fitness function (ADR-0051) flags a destructive change (DROP COLUMN, NOT-NULL-without-default) shipped with the same release as the code that needs it. (6) **Provisioning & safety nets** — a new firm's DB is created at the current schema (runner's "v0 → apply all"); per-DB **Time Travel** (ADR-0050) is the instant-PITR net; migrations tested against the **load-profile fixtures** (ADR-0034) + dry-run in CI; restore drills (ADR-0050) exercise the path; control-plane migrations apply first. **Built now as a seam** — single-firm today exercises the same runner as a fleet of one (ADR-0003: the fan-out is the only real cost of per-firm isolation, cheaper before firm #2). **Considered & rejected:** **Kysely + manual migrations** (type/migration drift — "nasty bugs"; no schema-diff generation); **raw SQL + Wrangler d1 migrations** (no type-safety, hand-written types and migrations separately; Wrangler's per-DB apply isn't fleet-aware); **a heavy ORM** (Drizzle is SQL-first/lightweight with raw escape hatches, fits the control-first stance); **per-DB manual migration / no orchestrator** (unmanageable past a handful of firms — Cloudflare's explicit warning); **deferring the tooling until multi-firm** (Cloudflare's "build before you need it"; retrofitting fleet migration mid-flight is far costlier). **Consequences:** schema evolves safely across the fleet with drift-free types, visibility, failure isolation, and zero-downtime; cost is building the orchestrator now (exercised on one firm) and holding expand-contract discipline. Reuses **ADR-0003** (tenancy/registry), **ADR-0050** (Cron-Workflow fan-out + Time Travel), **ADR-0034** (fixtures), **ADR-0051** (fitness function), **ADR-0004** (Better Auth adapter); underpins every per-firm schema in the platform. Implementation detail in the _Foundational data conventions_ spec.
- **ADR-0066** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · API — **API-definition library — `@hono/zod-openapi` (`OpenAPIHono` + `createRoute`, Zod 4, OpenAPI 3.1 via `doc31`) with a spec-first codegen client (Hey API → typed client + Svelte Query v6 hooks; `openapi-generator`/Speakeasy for non-TS SDKs), deliberately NOT Hono `hc` RPC inference; resolves the ADR-0022 Phase-0 spike.** **Context:** ADR-0022 fixed REST + OpenAPI 3.1 code-as-contract on Hono but left the implementing library to a Phase-0 spike (oRPC vs ts-rest vs @hono/zod-openapi vs chanfana). The contract layer underpins every subsystem, so the choice is high-leverage; June-2026 research drove it. **Decision:** (1) **Library = `@hono/zod-openapi`** — first-party Hono (Hono is Cloudflare's internal Workers framework; its creator is a CF Developer Advocate), `OpenAPIHono` extends `Hono` (zero adapter), first-class OpenAPI 3.1 (`doc31`/`getOpenAPI31Document`), Zod 4. (2) **Spec-first codegen for the client, not `hc` inference** — the emitted 3.1 spec is the single source of truth; **Hey API** generates the typed TS client + Svelte Query v6 hooks for the first-party app, `openapi-generator`/Speakeasy generate non-TS SDKs from the same spec. By **not** building an `hc<AppType>` client we avoid Hono RPC's documented `tsc`/IDE blow-up on large route unions entirely, while keeping per-route handler typing (cheap). (3) **CI spec gate** — the emitted spec is snapshotted/validated (Spectral, Prism, oasdiff) so code-as-contract stays honest regardless of library. **Considered & rejected:** **oRPC v1** (best single-library capability — spec + typed client + Svelte/TanStack from one definition — but single-maintainer **bus factor** and a ~6-month-old v1; the portable spec lets us adopt it later with no contract break if Hono disappoints); **ts-rest** (effectively **dormant** — no commits since Jun 2025, **OpenAPI 3.0 only**, no official Hono adapter, no Svelte Query); **chanfana** (Cloudflare-authored and 3.1-default, but **spec-only** — no typed client — and its headline **D1 auto-CRUD endpoints bypass the firm-scoped DAL** (ADR-0003), making its main advantage unusable here; `@hono/zod-openapi` is a superset that also keeps inference as an escape hatch). **Consequences:** native Hono, OpenAPI-3.1 code-as-contract, fast/flat type-checking (no RPC-union tax), one uniform client-generation mechanism for first-party + third parties; cost is `createRoute` verbosity (tamed by 1.3.0 `defineOpenAPIRoute`/`openapiRoutes` + per-subsystem modules), Zod-4 lock-in (current/correct), and a codegen build step. **Extends ADR-0022** (resolves the spike it named — does not supersede); reuses **ADR-0003** (DAL), **ADR-0017/0038** (Error ID), **ADR-0026** (`protected()`); binds the route-metadata model (**ADR-0067**). Detail in the _API contract & route conventions_ spec. **Build amendment (2026-06-13, slice 0·5b — tooling deviations forced by 3.1/codegen reality):** (a) the committed snapshot is emitted through a programmatic **Vite SSR pipeline** (`scripts/emit-openapi.mts`), not the plan's plain-Node assumption — the worker route graph is bundler-targeted (extensionless imports, `.sql` text modules, attribute-less `.json`) and never loads under plain Node ESM; (b) **Spectral 6.16/nimma crashes** traversing the document because the contract emits `null`-valued `x-mc-permission` extensions, so `.spectral.yaml` disables `duplicated-entry-in-enum` (our enums are trivial single-value literals — near-zero loss); (c) the generated Hey API client is **formatter- and lint-exempt** (its bytes are the generator's, its barrel/codegen idioms trip our rules), so its "compiles" gate is a dedicated `tsc -p` wired into the root `check`, not vp's lint-coupled typecheck; (d) the document's root `tags` are **auto-derived from the operations** (shared emit helper feeding both the snapshot and the live `/api/openapi.json` route) to satisfy Spectral's `operation-tag-defined` without per-module maintenance. Operational detail in [`docs/conventions/api.md`](conventions/api.md).
- **ADR-0067** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · API — **Route-metadata model + enforcement chokepoint — every route declares machine-readable metadata (`operationId`, `surface`, `authenticated`, `permission`, `resource`, `audited`, `idempotency`, `rateLimit`, `tags`, `deprecated`; reserved seams `cache`/`planTier`/`metered`); one chokepoint middleware enforces tenancy → authn → RBAC + ethical-wall → idempotency → rate-limit (404-not-403); metadata is emitted as OpenAPI `x-mc-*` extensions so conformance fitness functions assert over the contract; the route table is a registry.** **Context:** _Architectural integrity & coherence_ (ADR-0051) requires cross-cutting concerns to be properties of a traversed path, not steps to remember, and anticipates a route registry; ADR-0063 (idempotency) and ADR-0017/0038 (audit) already assume per-route declarations exist; the RBAC follow-up (ADR-0004) needs a seam to plug into. **Decision:** (1) **Core metadata** per `createRoute` (the field set above); the filter for inclusion is "consumed by the middleware, a fitness function, or codegen" — nothing else. (2) **Reserved seams** declared-not-enforced (design-for-the-superset): `cache`, `planTier`/`featureFlag`, `metered`. (3) **One chokepoint middleware** runs a fixed order: resolve session → firm (ADR-0003) → authn (401 if required+absent) → **RBAC permission + ethical-wall** (404-not-403, ADR-0052; matter-scoped only when `resource` says so) → idempotency (ADR-0063) → rate-limit → dispatch; audit + outbox ride the DAL write. (4) **Emitted as `x-mc-*` extensions** so **conformance fitness functions** (ADR-0051, robust tier) assert structurally: every mutating route is `audited` + names a `permission` that exists in the RBAC registry; money/trust routes require idempotency; no route silently public; surfaces never mix; `operationId` unique. (5) **Deliberately out** (owned by a better-placed chokepoint, to stop the registry rotting): PII (`protected()`, ADR-0026), async external effects (outbox, ADR-0064), notification raises (audit/write seam, ADR-0059), CSRF (derived from `surface`). **Considered & rejected:** **per-handler discipline** (the memory-tier failure ADR-0051 forbids); **a kitchen-sink metadata bag** (rejected via the consumed-by filter); **403 for unauthorized** (an existence oracle — 404 per ADR-0052). **Consequences:** the API's whole security/coherence posture is declared, enforced at one seam, and provable in CI; the `permission`/`resource` fields are the clean handoff to the RBAC spec (capability set, role→permission matrix, ethical-wall mechanics, `portal` external-principal assurance). Reuses **ADR-0051** (registry/chokepoint/fitness), **ADR-0003** (tenancy/DAL), **ADR-0052** (404-not-403), **ADR-0063** (idempotency), **ADR-0017/0038** (audit/Error-ID), **ADR-0026** (`protected()`), **ADR-0059** (notifications), **ADR-0064** (outbox); built on **ADR-0066** (library). Detail in the _API contract & route conventions_ spec.
- **ADR-0068** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Auth & access control — **Access-control model — a trusted-insider RBAC: anyone logged in is an authorized firm member, so information is open among insiders and RBAC governs _actions_ not _visibility_; permissions are a per-subsystem registry, roles are editable data (defaults seeded, role-editor UI a later seam), effective permissions = roles ∪ direct grants, action-denials are request-able (403 + access request → direct grant); location is a view/reporting filter (not a gate); 404 is reserved for cross-firm isolation; ethical walls and external-principal enforcement are deferred (named seams, no design yet).** **Context:** ADR-0004 named "RBAC + ethical walls" but left the model thin, and the API route-metadata chokepoint (ADR-0067) needs the `permission` set defined; the owner's working assumption is a small, mutually-trusted firm where every login is an authorized member who may access and assist. Captured at the **white-paper level — no separate spec** (owner's call; a subsystem spec is written only when walls or portal-enforcement is actually built). **Decision:** (1) **Trusted-insider posture** — information is open among insiders; RBAC gates _actions_, not _reading_; the only hard boundary is firm isolation (ADR-0003), so **within a firm every denial is 403** and **404 is reserved for the cross-firm/other-database case** — clarifying ADR-0052 (its 404-not-403 now applies to cross-firm isolation, not to within-firm screening). (2) **Permissions = fixed registry, one namespace per subsystem** (`<subsystem>:<action>`, mirroring the per-subsystem route modules + frontend areas, ADR-0067); the `Permission` union is generated from it and a conformance fitness function (ADR-0051) cross-checks every route's `permission` and that a route's namespace matches its module. (3) **Roles = data, not enums** — `role→permission` + `member→role` rows seeded with editable defaults (Owner/Admin, Attorney, Paralegal, Billing, Read-only); role-editor UI is a later additive seam (design-for-the-superset); **effective permissions = roles ∪ direct grants**. (4) **Access requests** — a visible-but-action-denied 403 routes to a `users:`/`roles:` approver who writes an optionally time-boxed **direct grant**; reuses Notifications (ADR-0059) + audit. (5) **Location is a filter, not a gate** — **supersedes** the white-paper "per-location scoping = access control" text (_Locations_); data is firm-wide, users filter by office; **hard per-location separation is a reserved seam**. (6) **Deferred (named seams, no design):** **ethical walls** (future content-screening overlay — visible header, locked contents — built only when a real use case appears; the resolver's `resource` field is the reserved seam), the **external-principal/portal enforcement** detail (the zero-trust grant model of _Client portal_), and dual-control / sealed-matter variants. **Considered & rejected:** **need-to-know / assignment-based matter access** (wrong for a small trusted firm — overhead without benefit; reserved seam); **per-location access restriction now** (owner wants office as a filter, not a barrier); **designing ethical walls now** (no concrete use case — building unused mechanism violates YAGNI; the seam is preserved); **a full RBAC subsystem spec now** (the model fits at white-paper level; a spec is earned when walls/portal-enforcement are built). **Consequences:** RBAC ships as action-gating over open information — cheap and fast for a single trusted firm — with the expensive-to-change parts (permission registry, roles-as-data, the route seam) future-proofed; walls and portal-enforcement attach later as overlays on the same resolver with no retrofit. Refines **ADR-0004** (does not supersede), clarifies **ADR-0052** (404 = cross-firm), builds on **ADR-0067** (route `permission`/`resource` seam), **ADR-0003** (isolation/`member`), **ADR-0026** (`protected()` reuses the same permission check), **ADR-0059** (access-request notifications), **ADR-0051** (registry/fitness); revises the _Locations_ per-location-scoping text.
- **ADR-0069** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Architecture / Delivery — **Worker architecture — the `@hono/zod-openapi` Worker is the deployment entry point and owns `/api/*`; the SvelteKit SPA is built to pure static assets (`adapter-static`, `ssr=false`) and bound to that Worker via Cloudflare Workers Static Assets (`not_found_handling: single-page-application`, `run_worker_first: ["/api/*"]`); one origin, one deploy. Refines the white paper's `adapter-cloudflare` note.** **Context:** the white paper fixed Workers static-asset hosting + a SvelteKit SPA + same-origin `/api` on Hono (ADR-0066/ADR-0022) but named the adapter as `adapter-cloudflare` without resolving which framework _owns the Worker_. June-2026 doc verification settled it: `adapter-cloudflare` emits its own `_worker.js` and its docs require SvelteKit endpoints to own all routes, so a Hono/OpenAPI surface could only live nested inside SvelteKit's request lifecycle. Slice 0·1 (the Phase-0 walking skeleton) must settle the request-handling shape the platform inherits. **Decision:** (1) **Hono owns the Worker** — the `OpenAPIHono` app is `main`; the SPA is built with **`adapter-static`** (`ssr=false`, SPA `fallback: index.html`) to plain files and bound as Workers Static Assets (`assets.directory`, `binding: ASSETS`, `not_found_handling: "single-page-application"`, `run_worker_first: ["/api/*"]`). `/api/*` always hits Hono (billable); static files and SPA navigations serve from assets (free, off the Worker); unmatched paths rewrite to `index.html` (200). This is Cloudflare's documented full-stack pattern and makes the OpenAPI app _the_ server (the code-as-contract assumption of ADR-0066/0067), keeping the frontend a dumb static bundle that also loads trivially inside Tauri (ADR-0002/0023). (2) **One deployable, one origin** (`app.magnacarta.esq`); the reserved public `api.magnacarta.esq` token surface (ADR-0022) is not built here. (3) **Build ordering:** `apps/worker` build/dev/deploy `dependsOn apps/web#build`. **Considered & rejected:** **`adapter-cloudflare`, SvelteKit owns the Worker, Hono nested** (the literal white-paper note — but it subordinates the OpenAPI surface to SvelteKit's lifecycle, drags unused SSR machinery, and fights the adapter's "endpoints own all routes" model); **two Workers (web + api) same-origin via routing** (clean separation and mirrors the future `api.` split, but two deploys + routing config — premature for a login-gated app whose public API is reserved-for-later). **Consequences:** a single-origin, single-deploy app where Hono is unambiguously the server and SPA navigation stays off the Worker bill; the SPA is portable to Tauri unchanged; cost is one deliberate refinement of the white paper (`adapter-static` over `adapter-cloudflare`). **Refines** the white-paper _Delivery model_ adapter note (does not supersede its SPA-mode/Workers-static-assets intent); builds on **ADR-0005** (Workers static assets), **ADR-0066**/**ADR-0067** (Hono contract server), **ADR-0001** (API-first), **ADR-0002**/**ADR-0023** (Tauri reuse / parity). Detail in the _Phase 0 · walking skeleton_ spec.
- **ADR-0070** · Accepted · 2026-06-04 · Architectural integrity — **Module-reference policy — no convenience barrel files; reference modules by their specific source path within a package, and import other packages only through their `package.json` `exports` map (a curated, side-effect-free public surface + explicit subpaths, `"sideEffects": false`); enforced structurally by the Oxlint no-barrel rule + dependency-cruiser boundary/no-cycle rules, not discipline.** **Context:** the import convention starts at the first package (slice 0·1) and is expensive to retrofit across a large monorepo. Barrel files (`index.ts` re-export hubs) are a verified perf/correctness sink on this exact stack: Vite's dev server crawls/transforms every module a barrel re-exports for a single import (Vite's own perf guide says avoid them), mixed inline+re-export barrels break tree-shaking (Rolldown can pull a whole barrel + its heavy deps into a shared chunk), and barrels breed circular deps and slow `tsc`/IDE. Rolldown 1.0's lazy-barrel optimization helps at _build_ time but does not fix the dev-server crawl or every tree-shaking edge case, so the guidance remains "avoid." A specific case of the _Architectural integrity & coherence_ boundary-rules category (ADR-0051), not new machinery. **Decision:** (1) **No convenience barrels in source** — intra-package imports reference the specific module (`./trust/rounding.ts`), never an intermediate `index.ts`. (2) **A package's public API = its `package.json` `exports` map** — one small, **curated, side-effect-free** entry and/or explicit subpaths (`@magna-carta/money`, `@magna-carta/money/rounding`), with **`"sideEffects": false`** so tree-shaking still drops unused re-exports; this curated boundary file is the _only_ acceptable barrel-like module, kept flat (no nested re-export chains). (3) **Cross-package imports touch only another package's declared `exports`**, never its internals. (4) **Enforced, not trusted** — the **Oxlint no-barrel rule** on source (already in `vp check`) + **dependency-cruiser / ts-arch** boundary + `no-cycle` rules in CI (the ADR-0051 fitness-function tier), so the wrong import fails CI rather than relying on memory. **Considered & rejected:** **convenience barrels relying on Rolldown's lazy-barrel optimization** (keeps the dev-server crawl, tree-shaking edge cases, and circular-dep risk — optimizes a problem better avoided); **zero barrels anywhere incl. package entries** (maximally safe but loses the curated, discoverable package surface and makes consumer imports more verbose for no real tree-shaking gain once `sideEffects:false` + `exports` are in place); **deep cross-package imports into internals** (defeats encapsulation and the boundary fitness functions). **Consequences:** clean package boundaries with no barrel perf/tree-shaking tax, circular deps caught structurally, and a fast dev server as the module graph grows; cost is slightly more explicit import paths and the per-package `exports`/`sideEffects` discipline (a scaffold makes it uniform). Practitioner detail in [`docs/conventions/typescript.md`](conventions/typescript.md); a one-line nod in the white-paper _Architectural integrity & coherence_ boundary-rules text; first applied in the _Phase 0 · walking skeleton_ spec. Reuses **ADR-0051** (boundary rules / fitness functions / coherence ordering) and the Vite+/Oxc toolchain; binds every future `packages/*` and `plugins/*` module.
- **ADR-0071** · Accepted · 2026-06-08 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — **Foundational-data package layout — the five slice 0·2 conventions are realized as four workspace packages split on the structure-vs-access seam: `@magna-carta/money` (all monetary math), `@magna-carta/identifiers` (identifier generation + prefix registry + branded id types), `@magna-carta/database` (how the database is defined and evolved — Drizzle schemas + fleet migration runner), and `@magna-carta/data-access` (how the database is safely reached — idempotency middleware + transactional outbox + relay; firm-scoping, `protected()`, and audit added as later layers); dependency graph is strictly acyclic: `data-access → database → identifiers`, `data-access → money`.** **Context:** the five §9 conventions (ADR-0061–0065) interlock across three concerns — pure arithmetic, schema definition, and safe runtime access — and were designed together but must be built and tested independently; the build order (money → identifiers → database → data-access) follows the dependency graph. The split needed a clear seam so each package could be a plain-Vitest, Cloudflare-runtime-free leaf where possible, with hard dependency edges enforced at module resolution (ADR-0070). **Decision:** (1) **`money` and `identifiers` are pure leaves** — no Cloudflare runtime, no repo inter-dependencies (only their external libraries: Dinero.js and nanoid respectively) — so they split cleanly, admit plain Vitest tests, and their boundaries are hard at module resolution. (2) **`database` vs. `data-access` splits on a genuine acyclic seam:** `database` owns _structure_ (Drizzle schema definitions, migration files, the fleet rollout orchestrator); `data-access` owns _access_ (all runtime queries go through it, carrying firm-scoping, `protected()`, audit, idempotency, and the outbox chokepoint). (3) **Idempotency and the transactional outbox are _not_ split into their own packages:** the idempotency row and the outbox row both commit in the same D1 transaction as the effect they guard (ADR-0063/ADR-0064), so separating them would require each to import the other's transaction handle — a genuine circular dependency. They live together inside `data-access`, separated by strict internal module boundaries and the fitness functions of ADR-0063/ADR-0064 rather than package edges. **Considered & rejected:** **one package for all five conventions** (no hard boundaries — the whole point of the split is enforcing the seam at module resolution per ADR-0070); **idempotency and outbox as separate packages** (forces a circular dependency through the shared D1 transaction — not a design smell, a real cycle); **`database` absorbing `data-access`** (mixes schema definition with runtime access, destroying the clean `database → data-access` edge and making it impossible to import schemas without pulling in the DAL). **Consequences:** four packages with hard, acyclic dependency edges; each can be independently versioned, tested, and evolved; `money` and `identifiers` are Cloudflare-runtime-free and admit plain Vitest (fast CI); `data-access` is the single runtime boundary that enforces all the cross-cutting invariants (firm-scoping, idempotency, outbox). Implementation structure detail in the _Foundational data conventions_ spec ([`docs/specs/2026-06-04-foundational-data-conventions-design.md`](specs/2026-06-04-foundational-data-conventions-design.md) §7). Builds on **ADR-0061–0065** (the conventions these packages implement), **ADR-0070** (no-barrel / exports-map package boundaries), **ADR-0051** (fitness functions at boundaries), **ADR-0003** (firm-scoped DAL).
- **ADR-0072** · Accepted · 2026-06-09 · Financial Core — **Append-only ledger core — posting model: direction + always-positive amount lines, a structural account model (no CoA product), materialized per-account balances with bounds-CHECK as the D1 batch-abort mechanism, dual entry dates (civil `effective_date` + UTC `recorded_at`), first-class linked reversals; realized as `@magna-carta/ledger` (first domain package) with the `@magna-carta/fixtures` harness calling the domain write paths directly.** **Context:** ADR-0008 fixed the append-only double-entry ledger as the Financial Core's foundation but left the posting model, line encoding, balance mechanism, and correction semantics undesigned; slice 0·3 builds the ledger as the first real consumer of the slice 0·2 primitives, under D1's no-interactive-transactions reality (ADR-0063/0064 amendments: every write is one atomic `batch()`), and must leave the no-negative-trust invariant (ADR-0009, Phase 3) enforceable with zero write-path retrofit. The line encoding is permanent-storage representation — the expensive-to-migrate part (the ADR-0061 logic). **Decision:** (1) **Line encoding = direction + always-positive amount** (`direction TEXT 'debit'|'credit'` + `amount_minor INTEGER CHECK (> 0)` — the Modern Treasury shape): structurally eliminates the silent sign-flip (a flipped sign still sums to zero; direction-as-data must be _stated_, negative/zero lines are impossible at the schema), keeps permanent rows journal-legible for bar audits/exports, and keeps amounts in the domain `allocate()` (ADR-0061) operates in; the balance aggregate is one CASE expression written once in the domain layer. (2) **Structural account model, no CoA product:** `ledger_account` carries type (asset/liability/equity/income/expense; normal balance derived in code, never a column), single currency (lines inherit — no currency column on lines), a parent/subledger seam (a Phase-3 client trust ledger is just a child account row), and soft-close `active` (accounts are never deleted); chart-of-accounts management UI/numbering is Phase 3 on this unchanged schema. (3) **Materialized `account_balance` now, bounds seam included:** one row per account in **normal-balance terms** (positive = normal, so trust bounds read as the rules are written), updated in the same batch as every posting, with nullable `min`/`max` bound columns and a CHECK — **a failed CHECK is D1's only mechanism for aborting a batch on a business condition**, so when Phase 3 sets a trust child's min to 0 an overdraw rolls back the entire posting atomically with zero retrofit; a fitness query proves SUM(lines) = balance. (4) **Dual dates by kind (ADR-0055):** backdatable civil `effective_date` (`YYYY-MM-DD` — what statements/reconciliation key off; no zone column, zone resolution is a boundary concern) + immutable system-set UTC `recorded_at` (audit truth; the gap between them is audit signal); no future-dating beyond the injected clock's today. (5) **First-class reversals:** `reverses_entry_id` UNIQUE FK + `reverseEntry` composing the exact mirror; reversed status derived from the link, never a flag; double-reversal structurally impossible under race (the loser's batch rolls back); the Phase-3 adjustment workflow composes on these primitives additively. (6) **Validation = ledger invariants only:** balanced ≥ 2-line single-currency entries, active leaf accounts only (parents are roll-ups), non-empty description; consumer rules (trust bounds values, period close, type-posting warnings) defer to their consumers atop existing seams. (7) **Packages:** `@magna-carta/ledger` is the first domain package (`ledger → data-access → database → identifiers`, `ledger → money` — acyclic per ADR-0070/0071), validating in memory and composing statements into the `composeEffectBatch` chokepoint; `@magna-carta/fixtures` realizes ADR-0034 — one seed → `FixtureContext` (seeded faker + fixed clock + the ADR-0062 seeded identifier source; byte-identical replay), layered factories with **the domain service as the canonical write path forever** (HTTP, arriving slice 0·5, is just another caller), named scenarios with baked-in assertions (`retainerDeposit`, `trustCorrection`, `boundsRejection`, `multiLineSettlement`) doubling as write-path integration tests, a tiny demo CoA explicitly bounded as demo-only, and a production guard (explicit opt-in + environment assertion + package never imported by production code). **Considered & rejected:** **signed single-column lines** (prettiest SUM but the sign _is_ the direction — a `negate()` in the wrong branch posts a balanced, wrong, undetectable entry on the table where that is most catastrophic); **two-column debit/credit** (journal-familiar but a per-row XOR CHECK, two-column aggregates everywhere, and a column-swap is still silent); **derive balances by SUM until trust needs them** (forces Phase 3 to retrofit materialization into every posting batch right when stakes are highest); **convention-only corrections** (lineage in prose, double-reversals possible, Phase 3 builds the linked version anyway); **full adjustment workflow now** (Phase-3 product surface with no consumer; composes later on these primitives); **ledger inside `data-access`** (mixes domain rules into the generic access layer — the exact seam ADR-0071 split on); **a full timestamp for `effective_date`** (drags in a zone; a check "dated June 27" renders June 26/28 by viewer — the precise bug ADR-0055 forbids). **Consequences:** the Financial Core's foundation is posting-correct by construction — sign-flips impossible, balance enforcement atomic, corrections linked, audit dates honest — and Phase 3 trust/billing/GL land on seams (bounds, effective-date, reversal links, subledger accounts) that already exist; fixtures are valid by construction and double as integration coverage; cost is the materialized-balance consistency discipline (fitness-checked), the leaf-only posting rule, and two new packages. The ROADMAP's slice 0·3 API-spike side-task is corrected as already resolved (ADR-0066 + the empirical spike suite). Extends **ADR-0008** (does not supersede); reuses **ADR-0061** (money/rounding), **ADR-0062** (client-minted ids enable up-front batch binding; seeded source), **ADR-0063/0064 as amended** (single-batch write path, outbox `ledger.entry.posted`), **ADR-0065** (migrations), **ADR-0070/0071** (package boundaries), **ADR-0034** (fixtures), **ADR-0055** (time by kind), **ADR-0051** (fitness functions); prepares **ADR-0009** (trust bounds) and Phase 3. Design: [`docs/specs/2026-06-09-ledger-core-and-fixtures-design.md`](specs/2026-06-09-ledger-core-and-fixtures-design.md).
- **ADR-0073** · Accepted · 2026-06-09 · Auth & access control — **Better Auth integration shape & firm-provisioning model (slice 0·4) — Better Auth mounts in the worker against control-plane D1 via the Drizzle adapter with `firm_id` as an additional user field joined to the firm registry (the organization plugin rejected: it would centralize membership join tables the tenancy model deliberately keeps per-firm); membership is invitation-only (no open signup) so every user creation rides our idempotent write path; the per-firm `member` mirror syncs through a new control-plane `outbox` + `idempotency_key` pair (reusing ADR-0063/0064 machinery — no second consistency mechanism); factors this slice are email/password + TOTP (seeds under Better Auth's built-in encryption until the `protected()` crypto module lands), passkeys deferred to the auth UI slice because WebAuthn ceremonies require a browser; firm provisioning is an idempotent, resumable orchestration behind a `FirmResourceProvider` port (dev adapter claims pre-declared Miniflare bindings; Cloudflare REST adapter creates real D1/R2, with the new database's binding accepted as a config-commit + deploy step for a fleet of one); user ids are `usr_` handles via the custom-id hook (ADR-0062).** **Context:** slice 0·4 realizes ADR-0004's first-party auth and ADR-0068's RBAC skeleton on the slice 0·2 primitives; the white paper fixes the model (control-plane identity, per-firm member mirror, roles-as-data) but leaves the library integration shape, the cross-database sync mechanism, and the provisioning seam to this design. **Consequences:** the access-control resolver lands as a pure package (`@magna-carta/access-control`) the ADR-0067 chokepoint consumes in slice 0·5; the worker cron handler gains the relay-sweep + TTL-purge wiring slice 0·2 deferred; a platform-admin secret guards `POST /v1/firms` until a real platform-admin principal model exists (named seam). **Rejected:** Better Auth organization plugin (membership centralization); open signup (uncontrolled user-creation path would bypass the outbox sync); synchronous dual-write for the member mirror (reinvents consistency the outbox already provides); building passkeys UI-less (dead, unexercisable surface); dynamic D1 binding dispatch now (fleet of one). Spec: `docs/specs/2026-06-09-slice-0-4-auth-rbac-provisioning-design.md`. **Amendment (2026-06-12, build findings):** (1) **No Better Auth CLI exists for the 1.6.x line**, so the decided "CLI generate as reference" route was impossible — the control-plane schema was derived empirically from the pinned package's `getAuthTables()` under our exact config; the schema-sync fitness test is the version-bump tripwire as designed. (2) The Worker requires the **`nodejs_compat` compatibility flag**: better-auth 1.6.16's workerd build needs `node:async_hooks` (the AsyncLocalStorage transaction context) — without it every mutating auth route 500s. (3) **Invite activation: Path A confirmed** — `resetPassword` on 1.6.16 creates the missing credential account; the build additionally added an `onPasswordReset` hook flipping `email_verified` (completing a reset proves mailbox possession — without the flip, `requireEmailVerification` would lock every invitee out). (4) Signup-disabled surfaces as `EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_UP_DISABLED` (HTTP 400). (5) **`generateId` hardened to fail loud:** an unknown Better Auth model throws instead of the planned silent `verification` fallback — a silent wrong-prefix handle would defeat ADR-0062's self-describing ids. (6) **Idempotency is auth-gated and tenant-scoped** (security-review finding): the route middleware chain runs session + permission checks _before_ the idempotency pre-decision, and stored keys are scoped `${firmId}:${clientKey}` (the control-plane convention; platform-admin routes use `platform:${clientKey}` — the namespaces cannot collide because every firm scope starts with the `firm_` handle prefix) — closing an unauthenticated replay/key-existence oracle and cross-firm replay/poisoning; the data-access middleware gained an optional `deriveStoredKey` hook, and the run-idempotent-effect contract now sanctions an effect performing its own guarded resumable commits provided the response-bearing write rides the final batch (the firm-provisioner pattern). (7) **Firm-provisioning resume identity:** `firm.name` UNIQUE with `database_identifier` nullable (the row exists before its database); same name + same owner email = idempotent no-op, a different owner = 409. (8) **Audit hook wiring as landed** (decided #10 allowed shrink/grow): `session.create.after` → `login.success` (also fires on the session rotation Better Auth performs when 2FA is enabled mid-session — accepted as a fresh proof of the same principal); no dedicated twoFactor plugin hook exists, so `two_factor.enabled`/`two_factor.disabled` is transition-exact via paired user.update.before/after hooks; no password-reset databaseHook exists, so `password.reset` rides `onPasswordReset`. Hook audit writes are guarded (they never fail the auth flow they describe); request-path audit writes ride the atomic effect batch — a deliberate split documented at its one home, `auth-audit-statements.ts`. (9) **Runtime migration bundling:** per-firm migration SQL ships in the Worker via plain `.sql` imports under a wrangler Text rule + a vitest plugin (Vite `?raw` does not survive wrangler's esbuild). From the post-build adversarial review (2026-06-12, 14 confirmed findings, all fixed): (10) **`firm.owner_user_id` records the owner** — the already-active idempotency check joins on it; deliberately no FK, `user.firm_id` already owns the edge. (11) **Concurrency abort fence:** the activation batch carries an INSERT-SELECT fence so a concurrent same-name run cannot double-commit owners. (12) **Firm names are normalized at the route boundary** (trim + NFC) — the name is the resume identity. (13) **The REST resource adapter is find-first** (probe by name, create only when absent); the already-exists message regex is demoted to a race-window fallback. (14) The data-access idempotency middleware gained a **`conflictResponse` hook** so pre-decision 409/422s carry the same RFC 9457 problem bodies as the handler path. (15) **Deactivated members lose all permissions at the read path** (member-status join); `/v1/me` answers 404 for a deactivated member per the spec's absent semantics. (16) **Per-firm queue messages carry `firmId`** — tenant-tagged before any consumer exists. (17) **Dead-lettered outbox rows are revived via the documented requeue statement** (operator seam). (18) **Executed provision/invite arms schedule a best-effort request-path sweep** (`waitUntil`) so activation doesn't race the 5-minute cron; the cron stays the at-least-once guarantee.
- **ADR-0074** · Accepted · 2026-06-09 · Observability / Communications — **Telemetry-baseline realization, transactional-email provider & dependency automation (slice 0·6) — the golden-rule-4 logging path lands as `@magna-carta/telemetry` (allowlist event builders that make redaction structural, a scrubbing-backstop tripwire, `cor_` correlation + `err_` Error IDs minted via the identifiers package, a pure `Logger` seam with a Workers adapter emitting structured JSON to Workers Logs + counters to Analytics Engine), enforced by a console-ban fitness function; the white paper's "bug-report Worker" is realized as `POST /v1/incidents` in the single Worker (ADR-0069 — one deployable) writing a per-firm `incident` row and an `incident.created` outbox→Queue message with no consumer yet (the diagnostics agent's future feed); transactional email is **Cloudflare Email Service** behind the existing `sendEmail` port via the native `send_email` binding (public-beta caveat accepted: DKIM/SPF runbook + the port seam are the mitigations; Resend and AWS SES rejected); dependency automation is **Renovate** (pnpm-catalog-aware, pin strategy, grouped weekly, propose-only per ADR-0058) + an OSV/audit CI gate + GitHub secret scanning.** **Context:** slice 0·6 builds the backend of the observability pillar (ADR-0017/0038) on a paid Workers plan (this slice's first paid dependency, owner-confirmed); the slice's roadmap scope was reshuffled during design because the client-reporter UI, diagnostics settings, and visual-regression CI have nothing to attach to before the design-system/auth-UI slice, and the diagnostics agent has nothing to consume before incidents flow. **Consequences:** ad-hoc logging fails CI; every error path carries a user-visible Error ID joined to its server trace by construction; Logpush→R2 and DKIM/SPF/Email Routing are deploy-time runbook steps; the UI slice inherits the reporter surfaces, breadcrumb library, screenshot capture, diagnostics setting, and visual-regression CI; incident descriptions move under `protected()` when the crypto module lands (named seam). **Rejected:** a separate bug-report Worker (second deployable against ADR-0069 for no present payoff); console-only minimal wiring (builds the exact habit golden rule 4 forbids); Dependabot (weak pnpm-catalog support); auto-merge dependency updates (upgrades stay deliberate). Spec: `docs/specs/2026-06-09-slice-0-6-telemetry-baseline-design.md`. _Amended 2026-06-13 (build, Opus during the Fable-access gap):_ (a) **Email Service API confirmed** — the public-beta Workers binding is a simplified `env.SEND_EMAIL.send({ to, from, subject, text, html })`, no mimetext; the adapter is selected only when both the binding AND an `EMAIL_FROM` var are present, with `EMAIL_FROM` the activation switch the operations runbook flips after `magnacarta.esq` is onboarded — so dev/test and a not-yet-onboarded production keep the logging adapter and no send fails. (b) **Audit gate is `pnpm audit --audit-level high`** with a documented `pnpm.auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` allowlist for two pre-existing TRANSITIVE dev/build-tooling highs (esbuild GHSA-gv7w-rqvm-qjhr, lodash GHSA-r5fr-rjxr-66jc), each dropping when Renovate upgrades its carrier. (c) The catalog grew beyond the original six: **`auth.denied`** (the platform-admin console.error denial-cause seam migrated onto the pipeline — the allowlist is the redaction), **`operation.failed`** + **`outbox.deadLettered`** (the background counterparts of `error.captured`, replacing the scheduled/sweep/auth-audit console.error seams so the console ban is honest), plus a `reconciliation.unprovisioned` counter. (d) The error boundary is `app.onError`, not an outer try/catch — Hono's compose routes a thrown handler there. (e) **`firm:reportIncident`** permission seeded onto every default role gates the incident route (`resource: firm` — no new resource type). (f) A 7-angle adversarial review (11 low-severity findings, zero high) fixed nine in-pass; two deferred as documented seams (the email per-send correlation id; the conformance resolver's allOf/oneOf handling). Runbook: `docs/conventions/operations.md`.
- **ADR-0075** · Accepted · 2026-06-09 · Architecture / Delivery — **Cloudflare resource naming, environment model & deploy path — resources are named `magna-carta-<environment>-<scope>[-<purpose-or-slug>]` (environment baked in from day one because D1/R2 cannot be renamed; `platform` scope = the handful of shared resources, `firm` scope = the per-tenant fleet, so the two sort as adjacent blocks at any fleet size); per-firm resource names are NEVER derived from firm handles (R2 names allow only lowercase/digits/hyphens; `frm_` handles contain uppercase/underscores) — the provisioner mints a dedicated lowercase slug per firm, one slug naming both its D1 and its R2 bucket, recorded in the firm registry and never parsed; queues grow by PURPOSE, never per firm (queues are shared transport carrying firm-tagged pointers; isolation lives in per-firm D1/R2 + the firm-scoped data-access layer, ordering in the outbox `seq` — per-firm queues rejected as a consumer-config explosion buying no isolation); production deploys go through Cloudflare Workers Builds (the dashboard GitHub connection) rather than a GitHub-Actions-held API token, with GitHub Actions remaining the test/quality gate and PR preview deploys disabled until an isolated `staging` environment exists (previews would bind production data).** **Context:** first real deploy required creating the production resources, which forced the permanent naming decision; the owner chose the Workers Builds path over token custody. **Created 2026-06-09:** `magna-carta-production-platform-control` (D1, `a0982ab7-97d9-471b-8dd7-19f18aa53f04`), `magna-carta-production-platform-files` (R2 — documented walking-skeleton placeholder until per-firm buckets land in slice 0·4), `magna-carta-production-platform-jobs` (Queue). **Environment model:** local dev/tests are Miniflare-only (no cloud twins); the demo tenant is a firm inside production, not an environment; `staging` is the probable second environment (returns PR previews), `loadtest` a possible ephemeral third (the golden-rule-10 harness against real infrastructure). **Consequences:** binding names (`DB_CONTROL`, `R2`, `QUEUE`) stay the code-facing contract; Workers Builds deploys what reaches `main` independent of Actions, mitigated by PR-only merges with green CI; the known manual step from ADR-0073 (new firm D1 binding = config commit + deploy) is unchanged. **Rejected:** environment-less names (unrenamable resources would force a parallel-rename migration later); deriving resource names from firm handles (charset mismatch + names-as-data is the wrong coupling); per-firm queues; the GitHub-Actions API-token deploy path (workable, declined by the owner for custody simplicity).
- **ADR-0076** · Accepted · 2026-06-10 · Design system — **Design-system foundation realization (slice 0·7a) — supersedes ADR-0028, keeping its architecture and replacing one layer: tokens are spec-pure DTCG 2025.10 with the Resolver module for theming (curated theme gallery × light/dark as resolver contexts — the legacy `$extensions.mode` pattern is dead), generated by Terrazzo 2.x (pinned exact) to CSS custom properties + the Tailwind v4 `@theme` plugin; the component layer is an in-house styled layer built directly on Bits UI v2 (shadcn-svelte replaced — its look lives in vendored class strings, not CSS variables, so escaping it converges on headless-build cost while adding permanent fork maintenance, and its fixed `--primary`-pair vocabulary conflicts with the richer semantic token tier; its source stays an uninstalled reference corpus); the catalog is Storybook 10 (Histoire dead — resolves ADR-0028's "Storybook/Histoire"), whose stories triple as Vitest browser-mode behavior tests and per-story axe checks; visual regression is Playwright screenshots across every theme×mode in the pinned Docker image (Argos CI the named upgrade).** **Theming model:** the firm picks the theme from the gallery (launch: one flagship + two-three showcase themes, every theme shipping light + dark), each user picks light/dark/system; applied as `data-theme` + mode class on `<html>` — themes are pure data, adding one is a new resolver input. Single launch density with `density.*` semantic aliases as the future-axis seam; arbitrary per-firm brand palettes designed-for via the Culori OKLCH generation script (built to author the gallery) but not productized; `contrast` resolver axis reserved. **Token gates:** golden-file CSS snapshots (generator-swap insurance), a WCAG 2.2 contrast gate over every theme×mode (APCA advisory only), an every-theme-defines-every-token completeness check, and a spec-purity check — these conditions are what make Terrazzo's bus-factor-1 acceptable (abandonment = rewrite one emission layer in days; Style Dictionary v5 is the named escape hatch, re-evaluated when its unshipped resolver support lands). **Component-layer discipline:** semantic component classes in a CSS layer fed exclusively by token custom properties (wrappers carry structure + `data-[state]` styling — "no off-token values" becomes mechanically lintable); variants as plain typed functions (tailwind-variants churned three breaking majors in nine months; class-variance-authority frozen — any DSL adopted later lands pinned behind one internal module); one internal floating/presence wrapper (Bits v2 forceMount transition pattern + the unstyled-wrapper Floating UI rule); z-index as a token scale (raw `z-*` numerals banned); tailwind-merge extended with every token namespace. **Satellites:** svelte-sonner (toast; Bits ships none), virtua (virtualization — TanStack Virtual's Svelte adapter abandoned), TanStack table-core behind a thin in-house Svelte 5 adapter (official v9 adapter adopted when stable; AG Grid reserved), paneforge (panes), `@internationalized/date` (its `CalendarDate` = the ADR-0055 civil date; feed it, never `ZonedDateTime`), `@lucide/svelte` deep imports only, superforms + formsnap with Standard Schema as the field-layer contract. Bits UI's maintainer gets a recurring sponsorship (load-bearing dependency, half-met funding goal); bits-ui + svelte pinned and upgraded as a pair; week-1 spike: Dialog scroll-lock vs the platform's strict CSP `style-src` (open bits-ui #1995). **Slice split:** 0·7a = this foundation; 0·7b = auth UI + passkeys + the client-reporter/diagnostics surfaces, designed separately as the first consumer. **Rejected:** Ark UI Svelte (credible Zag architecture, but one year old, ~3k weekly downloads, missing Command/Menubar/TimeField — revisit 12–18 months, à-la-carte adoption open); Style Dictionary today (resolver support unshipped; `$themes.json` interim = deeper lock-in); hand-rolled generator (2–4k lines of color-science correctness owned forever); no-DTCG hand-authored `@theme` CSS (the theme×mode matrix becomes unvalidated by construction); Skeleton/Flowbite/Carbon/daisyUI/Melt next-gen (own design language / weak a11y / wrong stack / no behavior / stalled pre-1.0); per-user theme choice (theme is firm identity; the user keeps mode); density toggle and broad firm customization at launch (the homogenization-pain generators this slice exists to prevent — each left a named seam). Spec: `docs/specs/2026-06-10-design-system-foundation-design.md`.
