- **ADR-0077** · Accepted · 2026-06-10 · Document versioning — **Version-history delta storage — designed-but-deferred: superseded `vN` snapshots may later be stored as binary deltas instead of full bytes; slice 1·1 reserves the seam now (version metadata carries a `storage_encoding` discriminator — `full` today — and nothing outside the data-access layer may assume a version object is raw file bytes); the delta pipeline itself builds in Phase 2 after Collabora editing ships, gated on real version-churn numbers from the load-profile harness (golden rule 10).** **Context:** ADR-0035 already prevents the autosave-explosion failure mode (autosaves overwrite the churning `current`; versions are cut deliberately, never per-autosave), but deliberate versions still accumulate as full snapshots in Infrequent Access forever (tiering, never pruning — ADR-0035/0049), and successive versions of the same document are highly redundant. The catch: `.docx`/`.xlsx`/`.pptx` are deflated ZIP containers, so a one-word edit scrambles the compressed byte stream and defeats any naive binary delta — deltas require a normalization step first. **Decision (the deferred design, recorded so the seam is cheap):** when a version is superseded, an **async Queue/Container job** (never the Worker request path) re-packs the ZIP container with entries _stored_ rather than deflated (the ReZipDoc insight — ~30 lines against any zip library, not a dependency), computes a **zstd `--patch-from`** delta against a base version (one tool for every format: normalized OOXML, PDF — incremental-save PDFs are append-mostly — images, generic binaries; solid Rust bindings fit the planned shared Rust doc core), **verifies a roundtrip content hash**, then swaps the IA object. The **latest version always stays a full object** and a **full keyframe every K versions** bounds reconstruction chains — no corrupted base may strand unbounded descendants, and the recorded content hash keeps every version independently verifiable (the legal-hold/discovery defensibility bar). Recorded trade-off: a delta-encoded old version cannot be range-streamed for preview — history preview reconstructs first (acceptable at ~once-a-year read rates). ADR-0035's "no compression" stands for the active corpus (`current` + the latest version, preserving the inline-preview range-stream path); this ADR scopes it: superseded versions may delta-encode behind the seam. **Why deferred, not built:** IA is $0.01/GB-mo with no minimum object size — a firm's full snapshots must reach ~100 GB before costing $1/month, so the saving (real, compounding, multi-tenant) does not justify storage-layer complexity before measured churn exists. **Considered & rejected:** **building the pipeline now** (optimizing ahead of measurement — golden rule 10); **ReZipDoc itself** (a Java git clean/smudge filter — the normalization insight transfers, the tool does not); **content-defined chunking + chunk store** (FastCDC/restic-style: O(1) reconstruction and cross-document dedup are attractive for templated legal work, but it multiplies object counts and operational complexity — revisit at Phase 4/discovery scale if measurement justifies); **per-autosave delta versions** (already solved differently — ADR-0035's deliberate version-cutting is the first-order fix to save-churn). Extends **ADR-0035** (version tiering — the supersede-to-IA `CopyObject` hook is exactly where the delta job attaches) and **ADR-0049** (deleted files tier through the same path); touches **ADR-0010** (Collabora — the Phase 2 churn source) and the slice 1·1 File Manager spec (carries the seam).
- **ADR-0078** · Accepted · 2026-06-10 · Documents / Tenancy & data isolation — **Storage-foundation follow-ups to ADR-0077, governed by the owner-set build-when-in-the-area principle: when a slice is already open, the cheap-from-the-getgo / expensive-to-retrofit parts (seams, schema columns, instrumentation) are built up front — "build when measured" gates are reserved for heavy machinery, never for foundations.** Three decisions + one parked question: **(1) Content hash on every stored object from day one (slice 1·1):** every file/version object records a SHA-256 content hash computed at write time — retrofitting means re-reading every byte ever stored. Serves evidence-grade fixity (chain-of-custody / discovery defensibility), the ADR-0077 roundtrip verification, duplicate-detection UX, an upload short-circuit ("these bytes are already held"), malware-scan verdict caching keyed by hash, and a no-op version skip (an explicit Save with unchanged content cuts no version). **Cross-matter blob dedup is explicitly not decided** — sharing bytes across matters collides with ethical walls (existence leakage), per-matter destruction (refcounting), and hold semantics; its own design if measurement ever justifies it. **(2) Backup dumps compressed from the first build (extends ADR-0050):** the per-firm SQL export snapshots are **zstd-compressed as they stream** — SQL text compresses ~85–95%, and neither of ADR-0035's no-compression rationales applies (a dump is not pre-compressed media and is never range-streamed); the ADR-0050 restore drills exercise the compressed path; `zstd --patch-from` deltas against the GFS weekly/monthly fulls as keyframes are the named later option. **(3) Per-firm storage metering built in-area (slice 1·1):** a rollup of bytes + object counts by storage class and key prefix per firm, feeding the existing Analytics Engine path (ADR-0074) — the instrument that fires every storage adopt-when trigger (ADR-0077's ~100 GB/firm delta threshold, ADR-0056's Iceberg cost-at-scale, ADR-0049's destruction economics) and doubles as the per-firm billing/capacity number; without a measurer, "adopt when measured" gates never fire. _Amended 2026-06-11 (slice 1·1 design cycle, ADR-0081 research): Analytics Engine is sampled, single-index, and 3-month-windowed, so it cannot be the authoritative figure — the **billing/capacity number and every adopt-when trigger read from an exact incremental rollup in per-firm D1** (counters by storage class × artifact role × prefix, maintained on the create/tier/delete write paths, trued-up by a scheduled R2 LIST reconciliation); Analytics Engine stays the trend/dashboard feed._
- **ADR-0079** · Accepted · 2026-06-11 · Matters — **Matter-numbering engine: an ordered segment template whose counter scope is derived from the template itself — any segment may be marked _scoping_ and the sequential counter resets per distinct combination of rendered scoping values, allocated from one `matter_number_counter(scope_key, next_value)` table inside the matter's own atomic write batch; numbers freeze as text at assignment; manual entry/renumber are permission-gated actions; numbers are drawn at Open, not at Prospective creation.** **Context:** ADR-0007 fixed "configurable numbering" without an engine design; the owner's requirement (design cycle, 2026-06-11) is that firms reproduce their existing proprietary formats from day one and survive scheme changes without renumbering old matters; a format survey (Clio, esilaw, Dye & Durham, Lawmatics, federal-court formats — research doc section E + design-cycle survey) found year/sequence, client-matter (`10034-00012`), office and practice-area prefixes, attorney initials, date-opened, and court-assigned numbers all in active use. **Decision:** segment kinds `literal` · `counter` (pad width, configurable start — migration continuity) · `year` (2/4-digit) · `date_opened` · `location_code` (ADR-0031) · `matter_type_code` · `client_code` · `staff_initials` · `client_name`; rendered numbers are URL-safe (white-paper constraint) and UNIQUE per firm (bounded-retry idiom, ADR-0062, backstopping the in-batch counter increment); scheme edits affect only future matters; one active scheme per firm stored as per-firm data surfaced through the ADR-0040 Settings registry with a live-preview renderer shared between settings UI and assignment; per-matter-type scheme overrides and per-client scope (needs stable client identity, slice 1·3) are named seams. **Considered & rejected:** fixed scope enums (`{firm}` / `{firm,year}` / `{firm,location}` tables — every new scope a migration; the derived scope_key buys all scopes with one column); renumber-on-scheme-change (firms keep historic numbers — owner-stated); numbering at Prospective creation (dead consultations would burn numbers and wrong-year them — see ADR-0080). **Consequences:** every surveyed format reproduces; the counter table never changes shape; Bates numbering stays explicitly separate (Phase 5). Refines **ADR-0007**; builds on **ADR-0062** (bounded retry), **ADR-0040** (settings surface), **ADR-0031** (location segment).
- **ADR-0080** · Accepted · 2026-06-11 · Matters — **Matter shell, lifecycle & consultations: status `prospective → open → closed` where Prospective = an unnumbered consultation surfaced only in a per-attorney virtual Consultations folder (a filter, not a container); Open draws the number; Closed = archived-but-additive (leaves active lists, still accepts documents/notes) behind a named close-precondition hook — empty in slice 1·1, Phase 3 registers "all bills paid, no remaining balance"; pre-Directory client reference = display-only fields (`client_display_name`, `client_code`) with the `matter_party` role-on-the-link shape sketched but not created; matter types = per-firm data rows (name, code, active), custom-fields engine deferred to a shared design with Directory.** **Context:** slice 1·1 needs the matter shell before Directory (slice 1·3) exists; the owner's practice (design cycle, 2026-06-11) batches consultations in a special per-attorney matter and assigns no number until retention, and corrected the white paper's "Closed/Archived (read-only)" — closed matters must still accept late-arriving items and may close only with a zero balance. **Decision:** everything is a matter (one data model); consultations are individual Prospective matters so their documents never move on retention and (Phase 4) their parties feed conflicts permanently; the Consultations folder is `status = prospective AND responsible_member = X`; stale consultations (per-firm age setting, default 1 year) get a filter + bulk-close at launch, automation later; `reopen` exists permission-gated; closed prospectives are retained forever as conflict history; no link table until Directory designs the entity side (1·3 migrates the display fields into real links). **Considered & rejected:** a placeholder party/client table now (pre-decides Directory's shape, heavier 1·3 migration); creating the link table empty (foreign-key target doesn't exist; shape may shift in 1·3 design); a special consultations-matter entity (the virtual filter gives the same UX with zero extra model); read-only closed status (contradicts practice reality). **Consequences:** dense sequences, retention-year numbering, conflicts-complete consultation history, and a Phase 3 close gate that needs no schema change. Refines **ADR-0007**; revises the white paper _Matters and File Manager_ lifecycle line (amended same commit); builds on **ADR-0068** (permission-gated transitions). _Amended 2026-06-12 (slice 1·3 design, ADR-0087): the "custom-fields engine deferred to a shared design with Directory" line is resolved — the engine is promoted to its own **slice 1·4** (shape designed in the 1·3 spec)._
- **ADR-0081** · Accepted · 2026-06-11 · Documents / File handling — **Inbound upload path & quarantine ingest: uniform direct-to-R2 (bytes never transit the Worker — it authorizes, mints single-key short-TTL presigned URLs via one R2 S3 token held as a Worker secret, and records metadata); two transfer modes behind one pinned client library (Uppy AwsS3 — presigned PUT below a config threshold, resumable S3 multipart above it); quarantine is a status gate on `file_version` (`pending`/`quarantined` vs `clean`), not a separate key — bytes land at the final key and promotion is a status flip; one ingest consumer (R2 event notification → `files-ingest` queue) does everything needing the bytes in a single read: magic-byte/size/bomb gates, SHA-256, the malware-scan port (engine deferred — launch adapter records an explicit `unscanned` verdict), no-op-skip check, commit + metering + outbox.** **Context:** the white paper's named open item ("likely its own ADR"): Worker body caps (100 MB plan-bound) and 128 MB memory make through-Worker ingest impossible for legal files (discovery video, large scans); research (slice 1·1 research doc, section A) verified R2 presign/multipart mechanics, limits (parts ≥ 5 MiB, ≤ 10,000, 7-day auto-abort), and event-notification throughput on primary docs; the owner chose both-modes-now over presign-only after full briefing (design cycle, 2026-06-11). **Decision (additional):** per-firm max-size/content-type policy enforced twice (refused at presign; re-verified against real bytes at the gate — the client controls the bytes); `FirmResourceProvider` gains idempotent `configureBucketNotifications` (prefix `files/` → `files-ingest`, purpose-named queue per ADR-0075); stale pending sessions/versions expire via the 0·4 cron plumbing; presigning via `aws4fetch` (pinned); the ingest consumer is idempotent under queue redelivery; quarantined files are never auto-deleted (evidence; hold-blocked destruction). **Considered & rejected:** tus (R2 has no native endpoint — a tus-shim Worker is extra infrastructure for a protocol nicety; reserved); through-Worker small-file path (two code paths, no benefit — direct-to-R2 composes with quarantine identically); pending-key-then-copy quarantine (promotion re-copies multi-GB objects; R2 single CopyObject caps ~5 GiB; the data-access layer already mediates every read, so the status flag is structurally enforced — golden rule 3); per-firm presign tokens (secret sprawl, no isolation gain — the signed key already pins the firm's bucket); hand-rolled upload client (re-hardens retry/resume/parallelism Uppy has solved). **Consequences:** large/flaky uploads work from day one; portal (Phase 6) and Discovery ingest (Phase 5) reuse the path unchanged; the malware engine drops into a ready port (verdict + engine-version columns, re-scan-sweep seam) — engine choice stays its own deferred ADR, flagged for future research. Implements the white paper _Matters and File Manager_ upload item; builds on **ADR-0075** (resources), **ADR-0078** (hash-on-write), **ADR-0068** (`files:upload`), **ADR-0027** (zips stored opaque).
- **ADR-0082** · Accepted · 2026-06-11 · File handling — **Bulk-download implementation: adopt `client-zip` (MIT, pinned) as the streaming store-mode ZIP64 writer — per-entry CRC-32 via streaming data descriptors, async/stream entries fed directly from R2 `get()` bodies, memory-flat at any archive size; version keys resolved at request time (consistent snapshot); only `clean` versions eligible; single-file selections download directly; v1 ships the live stream only, with the ADR-0027 async-job seam (build-to-R2-artifact → signed URL past a config threshold) documented and flagged for future research (job lifecycle, artifact TTL, identical-bundle dedup — no timeline home yet).** **Context:** ADR-0027 fixed the approach (Worker streams R2 → store mode → ZIP64, version-pinned, access-checked, audit-logged); research (slice 1·1 research doc, section B) confirmed `client-zip` implements exactly the required mechanics and that streaming store-mode zips are I/O-bound (CRC-32 the only CPU work) — fine on a Worker at multi-GB sizes. **Considered & rejected:** a ~200-line in-house ZIP64 writer (re-implements a small, focused, pinned library; control gain not worth the maintenance); compression (ADR-0027 — legal corpus already compressed); building the async-job path now (no measured need; the live path ships first and the seam is documented). **Consequences:** bulk download lands with the File Manager at near-zero marginal design cost; the dropped-connection-wastes-the-stream failure mode is accepted until the async seam is researched and built. Implements **ADR-0027**; builds on **ADR-0068** (open reading, audit-logged exports). **(4) Parked for research (resolve in the slice 1·1 design cycle):** a **derived-vs-original artifact class** — recomputable artifacts (OCR text, conversion outputs, preview/CDR renditions, indexes) are caches, not archives, and arguably should not inherit keep-forever tiering by default; owner flagged needs-more-research before committing. **Recorded constraint:** any future per-firm envelope encryption of R2 objects (extending ADR-0026 beyond D1 fields) must sit _below_ the delta/dedup layer — encrypting first destroys the redundancy ADR-0077/0078 exploit. Extends **ADR-0077** (delta seam), **ADR-0050** (backup snapshots), **ADR-0035/0049** (tiering lifecycle); touches **ADR-0026** (encryption ordering), **ADR-0074** (Analytics Engine path).
- **ADR-0083** · Accepted · 2026-06-11 · Performance / Fixtures — **Load-profile harness realization (cross-cutting slice) — the white paper's third fixtures entry point builds as harness machinery inside `@magna-carta/fixtures` (no new package; a separate export path keeps it off the scenario/demo import surface): declarative named+versioned volume profiles (counts and distributions as parameters, never generator constants; one scale multiplier spanning CI-smoke to stress scale), generation through the real domain write-paths at volume (valid by construction, one-seed deterministic), snapshot artifacts (SQLite file + JSON manifest of profile version/seed/schema state/package versions) in a gitignored content-keyed local cache — reproducible by construction, never committed, manifest mismatch regenerates — and a query-level measurement runner (warmup + timed iterations → median/p95 per named domain-service/data-access operation, plus `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` capture flagging full scans on measured paths) writing small committed JSON baselines for human diffing.** **Context:** golden rule 10 and `performance.md` name the load profile the standing performance/scaling harness, and the slice DoD ("perf-tested against the load-profile fixtures as built") was unfulfillable without it; slice 0·3 built the other two entry points and deferred this one (§5.5); a growing gate list (the 0·3 efficiency candidates first) had no measurer, and the owner's per-gate rule is that every gate needs a named measurer. V1 ships the machinery + the ledger volume profile (the only domain with schema today); the **accretion rule** (ADR-0078's build-when-in-the-area principle) binds every subsequent domain slice to extend the profile and the measured set while the slice is open — 1·1 adds matters/files + the R2 blob layer of the artifact, 1·3 the Directory graph, Phase 3 financial volume. **Honesty clause:** local `node:sqlite` is not production D1 — numbers are relative (regression deltas, growth curves, plan shapes), never absolute latencies; the `loadtest` environment (ADR-0075) running the same harness on real infrastructure is the named absolute-numbers seam. **Rejected:** a new `@magna-carta/load-profile` package (golden rule 1 — the fixtures engine already owns determinism, the node-sqlite database, and the production guard); committing snapshot artifacts (reproducible derived data; repo + OneDrive bloat); CI timing gates now (`performance.md` defers budgets until stable numbers exist — CI gets a tiny-scale smoke only, no timing assertions); request-path HTTP measurement in v1 (slice 0·5's chokepoint stack would be measured mid-change; named seam, builds after 0·5); fleet-scale simulation (per-firm D1 isolation makes per-firm volume the unit that scales). **Consequences:** the 0·3 efficiency candidates become decidable with evidence; the DoD perf-test binds from the next slice to land; UI work (0·7a catalog, 1·1 screens) gains real-density data per the white paper's UI multiplier; the build is phase-independent and parallel-safe (next free session). Realizes the white paper _Synthetic data & fixtures_ load-profile entry point; builds on **ADR-0034** (fixtures), **ADR-0062** (seeded identifiers), **ADR-0051** (fitness-function pattern for the plan-scan check), **ADR-0078** (build-when-in-the-area), **ADR-0075** (`loadtest` seam); serves golden rule 10. Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-11-load-profile-harness-design.md`](specs/2026-06-11-load-profile-harness-design.md).
- **ADR-0084** · Accepted · 2026-06-12 · Encryption & keys — **`protected()` concretization — envelope, context binding & blind-index policy: every protected value is stored as the versioned binary frame `[algo_version | key_version | iv(12) | AES-256-GCM ciphertext+tag]` (base64 TEXT); every encrypt/decrypt is context-bound via AAD = the canonical string `mc:aad:v1|firm|table|field|row_pk|algo_version|key_version` so a ciphertext moved between rows/fields/firms fails authentication; HKDF-SHA-384 purpose keys derive under frozen versioned info constants (`mc:v1:pii`, `mc:v1:secrets`, per-field index subkeys `mc:v1:index:<table>:<field>:<index_name>`) with all keys non-extractable end to end; blind indexes are version-prefixed truncated HMAC-SHA-384 over frozen normalization transforms, with low-entropy fields (DOB, SSN last-four) never standalone-indexed — matched by decrypt-and-compare on a higher-entropy candidate set; TOTP seeds stay under Better Auth's own at-rest encryption (verified + rotation-configured), closing ADR-0026's open line.** **Context:** ADR-0026 fixed the architecture (envelope encryption, per-firm keys, the data-access chokepoint, HMAC blind index for Conflicts) and ADR-0046 the agility mandate, but neither specified the bytes, parameters, or per-field policy — the cheap-now/corpus-rewrite-later layer; research ([`docs/research/protected-crypto-module.md`](research/protected-crypto-module.md)) verified the primitive set on the Workers runtime and surfaced the policy math (CipherSweet's coincidence formula `C = R/2^min(L,K)`, target `2 ≤ C < √R`). **Decision (additional):** module = pure `@magna-carta/encryption` + enforcement in `data-access` (`protectedField` declarations carrying the ADR-0068 permission required to decrypt; `readProtected` = permission check → per-firm `audit_log` row → AAD decrypt, inseparable; `blindIndexValue` matching needs no permission and writes no audit — nothing is revealed); per-field policy table: SSN standalone-indexed at L=16 over digit-stripping, account numbers L=24, driver's license L=16 state-prefixed, DOB/last-four never standalone, plugin credentials + incident descriptions encrypted unindexed; normalization transforms are frozen contracts (a change = full decrypt-reindex, documented as a deliberate maintenance job); firm keys minted at provisioning (idempotent `FirmProvisioner` step + a one-off mint sweep for existing firms); `firm_secret` table + `getSecret`/`putSecret` under `firm:manageSecrets`; `incident.description` upgraded by expand-contract with a code backfill sweep (SQL migrations cannot encrypt); crypto-shred ships as the audited `shredFirmKey` registry function, offboarding route a named seam. **Considered & rejected:** routing TOTP seeds through the module via Better Auth hooks (permanent coupling to library internals, zero gain against the actual database-exfiltration threat — owner decision 2026-06-12, option 1 of the research's three); per-firm keys for TOTP (layering inversion — login precedes firm context; shredding a firm must not break a user's login); standalone DOB/last-four blind indexes (~15/~13 bits — fingerprintable by frequency analysis at any truncation); `deriveBits` (raw bytes in the JS heap; `deriveKey` keeps everything non-extractable); a separate crypto Worker (re-rejected per ADR-0026); wide-nonce ciphers for IV safety (outside the FIPS-friendly set — IV uniqueness is enforced in code with a fitness test instead). **Consequences:** Conflicts gains its exact-identifier channel with quantified leakage (equality + frequency only, accepted knowingly); rotation seams are one byte each instead of corpus rewrites; the module ships exercised (secrets store, incident upgrade, fixtures) before Directory declares its fields in 1·3. Extends **ADR-0026** (amended: TOTP line closed) and **ADR-0046** (the version bytes realize the agility mandate); builds on **ADR-0068** (decrypt permissions), **ADR-0073** (provisioning step), **ADR-0071** (package seam); slice 1·1's `audit_log` is the decrypt-audit home. Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-2-protected-crypto-module-design.md`](specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-2-protected-crypto-module-design.md). _Amended 2026-06-16 (slice 1·2 build): two build-time verifications. **(a)** The `firm_secret` row-id prefix is the full-name form **`firmSecret`** (not `fse`), per the slice-1·1 ADR-0062 amendment full-name convention — the scaffold commit's `fse` placeholder was reconciled to the registry's full-name scheme. **(b)** The Better Auth at-rest 2FA-secret encryption keyset is verified and used, but in the pinned **better-auth 1.6.16 the option is named `secrets`** (an array of `{ version, value }`), **not `encryptionKeys`** — wired as a single-element rotation array with version 0 = the existing `AUTH_SECRET` (so already-stored TOTP seeds still decrypt; a future rotation prepends a version). All else built as designed._
- **ADR-0085** · Accepted · 2026-06-12 · Directory — **Directory model realization + conflicts seams (slice 1·3): the SSOT graph built as two equal-depth node tables (`directory_person` / `directory_organization`, type-prefixed handles, distinct standard fields — not a polymorphic table) + one typed `directory_edge` table (endpoints are entity handles; the global prefix discriminates, no kind column); a **category is a per-firm classification label, not a schema variant** (all field variability is slice 1·4 — ADR-0087); all names (incl. the current one) live in `directory_name` as queryable rows (role + temporal validity + frozen normalization), name changes append-and-flip never edit-in-place; the write-time search substrate = scheme-keyed `directory_name_phonetic_code` rows (Double Metaphone via pinned `words/double-metaphone` at launch — the scheme key makes Beider-Morse additive-by-sweep at Phase 4, no schema change) + an FTS5 trigram table synced by triggers; sensitive identifiers are `protected()` rows declared against the ADR-0084 policy table with `directory:viewSensitiveIdentifiers` as the decrypt permission, **EIN added to that policy table** (standalone blind index L=16, digit-strip — a tax-ID exact-match channel); edge types include the conflicts seam-4 set explicitly (affiliation/principal-of-org, spouse/family relationship, corporate parent/subsidiary/successor, representation, role-assignment); temporal validity on names/edges/identifiers/`matter_party`/contact rows, core identity untimed; soft-delete, never hard-delete.** **Context:** ADR-0006 fixed the SSOT model and the conflicts research ([`docs/research/conflicts-matching-engine.md`](research/conflicts-matching-engine.md) §F) named six cheap-now/expensive-to-retrofit Directory seams for the Phase-4 engine; this slice realizes the model and builds the seams (recall side only — the scorer, severity bands, strictness/depth, graph traversal, and immutable log are Phase 4). The phonetic feasibility ([`docs/research/directory-mechanics.md`](research/directory-mechanics.md) §C: Double Metaphone is Workers-safe today, Beider-Morse has no mature JS impl) drove the minimal-go gate. **Decision (additional — firm personnel, flagged revisitable):** firm personnel are **not** modeled as Directory entities — staffing is the 0·4 `member` axis; intra-firm imputation is a Phase-4 logical join (`member` ↔ matter ↔ `matter_party`), not a personnel node; prior-firm lateral-conflict history is a documented seam built in Phase 4 (self-reported, unreliable, no consumer until the engine exists); a lawyer who is genuinely a party is an ordinary entity (optional `member_id` link). **The owner reserves this firm-personnel decision for reconsideration in the Phase 4 Conflicts design cycle** — the 1·3 position adds no personnel-entity data to retrofit, so revisiting is cheap. **Considered & rejected:** a single polymorphic `entity` table (loses equal-depth distinctness, needs a kind column the handle prefix already gives); category-specific standard columns (sparse real columns = per-firm DDL churn; JSON = breaks merge relink + field audit — both are exactly the 1·4 engine's job); fixed phonetic-code columns (the scheme-keyed table grows without migration); Beider-Morse now (no production-grade JS implementation; it rides the Phase-4 Rust/WASM core); modeling personnel as entities for imputation (conflates the assigned-to/about-them axes and creates a third identity to reconcile). **Consequences:** Phase 4 Conflicts builds on a ready graph + exact-identifier channel; merge's relink surface stays bounded to real indexed columns (§7); the Directory volume profile + a blind-index/protected-read/graph-expansion measured set join the load harness (accretion rule). Refines **ADR-0006**, serves **ADR-0013**; builds on **ADR-0084** (protected fields + EIN policy row), **ADR-0031** (address/Location reuse), **ADR-0062/0071** (handles, package seam). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-3-directory-design.md`](specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-3-directory-design.md).
- **ADR-0086** · Accepted · 2026-06-12 · Directory / Conflicts — **Duplicate handling (slice 1·3): entry-time prevention + non-destructive merge now; automated unmerge deferred to the LEAP slice as a guarded operation. Prevention = a recall-biased "possible duplicate?" candidate list (phonetic-block ∪ trigram ∪ blind-index equality) surfaced on entry. Merge is non-destructive — the subordinate is archived (`status = merged_into` + master id, never deleted), all inbound references (matter_party, edges, categories, contact/identifier rows) are repointed to the master because every reference is a real indexed column, and an append-only `entity_merge` event records the survivorship choices + the exact repoint list (table/row/old→new); the subordinate's names + identifier blind indexes stay master-resolved in the matching corpus (a merged-away alias must still trigger a conflict hit). Field-level survivorship for standard fields (prior values recorded for restore); multi-valued child rows are unioned.** **Context:** duplicates arise in ordinary operation, not only at LEAP (the same person entered as a beneficiary in a second matter), so 1·3 must merge; the research ([`docs/research/directory-mechanics.md`](research/directory-mechanics.md) §B) found no mainstream true unmerge and offered three tiers. **The unmerge tenability finding (the deferral rationale):** unmerge is well-defined only as "reverse a specific, recent, still-clean merge." If A and B merge then C is added against the unified entity, deterministic replay cannot re-attribute C to a former side — that information was never captured (one entity to everyone who touched it). So **unconditional deterministic unmerge is rejected** (it silently misattributes post-merge data — a footgun); whenever unmerge is built it **must be guarded** (offered only while the merge is clean — no post-merge references/edits — then locked to a manual path); and because merge is non-destructive with the full `entity_merge` event, the data to reverse always exists, so the guarded automated button is a deferral, not a retrofit over lost data. **Considered & rejected:** prevention-only with merge deferred to LEAP (duplicates are real pre-LEAP — owner scenario); merge + true unmerge built in 1·3 (the post-merge-attribution problem makes a general split ill-posed; the safety net is non-destructiveness, not an undo button); hard-delete-the-subordinate merge (industry norm — destroys the conflicts corpus + audit). **Consequences:** the white-paper "unmerge/split safety net" line is amended to non-destructive merge + guarded unmerge (defer-with-seam; nothing irrecoverable now); the conflicts-corpus rule binds the merge path; reference-kind custom-field values (slice 1·4) extend this relink set in-area. Serves **ADR-0006/0013**; builds on **ADR-0085** (the model), the slice-0·2 atomic batch, and the 1·1 `audit_log`. Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-3-directory-design.md`](specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-3-directory-design.md).
- **ADR-0087** · Accepted · 2026-06-12 · Matters / Directory — **The shared custom-fields engine is promoted to its own slice 1·4 (after Directory, before Phase 2·6 document automation) — not built in slice 1·3 and not deferred to LEAP. Slice 1·3 ships fixed standard fields only (a category is a classification label, ADR-0085); the shared typed-EAV design is settled in the 1·3 spec as 1·4's seed: per-firm `custom_field` definitions (the Clio-12 kind catalog as a CHECK; target matter | entity; attachment to matter-type/entity-category) + picklist-option child rows + `custom_field_value` with typed value columns (text/number-minor/date/option-FK/reference) exactly one non-null, reference kinds getting real indexed columns. 1·4 extends slice 1·3's merge relink set to repoint reference-kind values (build-when-in-the-area).** **Context:** ADR-0080 deferred the custom-fields engine "to a shared design with Directory"; the 1·3 design cycle found it is the largest single component, is a genuinely shared subsystem (matter types from 1·1 + entity categories from 1·3), and is built against known field _kinds_ (not a firm's unknown field inventory — so it need not wait for LEAP). Sequencing it after 1·3 (needs entity categories as an attach target) and before 2·6 (which consumes custom merge fields) puts it where its consumers are. **Considered & rejected:** building it inside 1·3 (unbalances the slice; the largest component bolted onto Directory); deferring to the LEAP slice (risks landing after 2·6; leaves the build unscheduled — and the kinds are knowable now); JSON-column storage (breaks merge relink + field-level audit/temporal history — the §A.3 platform invariants); descoping firm-defined fields entirely (a white-paper promise). **Consequences:** 1·3 stays lean with a fixed schema; 2·6 document automation has a custom-merge-field home; ROADMAP gains slice 1·4 and the Backlog custom-fields expectation points there. Amends **ADR-0080** (custom-fields-deferral line → slice 1·4); builds on **ADR-0085** (categories, merge relink). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-3-directory-design.md`](specs/2026-06-12-slice-1-3-directory-design.md).
- **ADR-0088** · Accepted · 2026-06-13 · Documents / Licensing — **PDF settles on one PDFium-WASM engine across every runtime (via EmbedPDF, MIT), an AGPL/SSPL prohibition is made a hard repo-wide rule, and content-level redaction is un-deferred onto that engine's off-the-shelf true-redaction plugin targeting Adobe parity — refining ADR-0045's provisional design.** **Context:** ADR-0045 (Proposed) sketched an in-house PDF service hand-composed from permissive engines and **deferred true content-level redaction** to Phase-4 citing AGPL engines. A first pass this cycle proposed a five-library spread (PDFium + pdf.js + pdf-lib + qpdf); further research (owner-pushed — "fragmented vs. Collabora; there should be a real one out there") found the integrated SDK that pass missed and a **spike empirically confirmed server-side execution**. Analysis + spike evidence on file at [`docs/research/pdf-tooling.md`](research/pdf-tooling.md). **Decision:** (1) **One engine across all runtimes — EmbedPDF over PDFium-WASM.** The ecosystem has only two comprehensive engines — **MuPDF (AGPL, excluded)** and **PDFium (BSD-3)**; every permissive option is a binding over PDFium. **EmbedPDF** (MIT; PDFium bundled Apache-2.0) is the integrated, plugin-composable SDK over it: `@embedpdf/pdfium` (low-level WASM) → `@embedpdf/engines` (`PdfEngine`, 100% TS) → framework packages incl. **first-class Svelte** + `@embedpdf/snippet` UI. It **runs in browser, Node, and serverless** — so the _same_ PDFium WASM engine serves the interactive Svelte viewer, server/Container batch work, and (size-permitting) light Worker ops, realizing the ADR-0024/ADR-0039 one-engine-WASM+native model off the shelf rather than approximated. **Spike (2026-06-13, on file):** `@embedpdf/pdfium` v2.14.4 runs headless in pure Node (init ~41 ms, load+parse ~4.3 ms, correct page count/dims); `pdfium.wasm` is 4.6 MB raw / **~2.05 MB gzip** — under the Workers paid 10 MB bundle limit (ADR-0074), so Worker _size_ is fine and CPU placement (edge vs. Container) is a load-harness measurement (ADR-0083), not a guess. Capabilities are **plugins** (install only what is used): Viewer, Annotation, **Redaction**, Forms, Stamp, Search, Selection, **Metadata** (read+edit), **Export/Save**, Signature, Thumbnail, Print, Bookmark, Attachment, Layout-Analysis, plus an AI package. **pdf.js and pdf-lib are dropped** (EmbedPDF covers viewer + create/modify/save); the standalone `pdfium-render` binding is likely unnecessary (the engine runs PDFium-WASM in Node). (2) **AGPL/SSPL is a hard prohibition** — including **transitive** deps and **isolated-service** use — because the platform is proprietary SaaS and AGPL §13 treats network use as distribution; categorical, to avoid relying on the contested process-isolation boundary (consistent with Documenso/DocuSeal in ADR-0045, self-hosted-Sentry in ADR-0038, Jasmin-as-fallback-only in ADR-0047). A **license-scanning CI gate** (permissive allowlist; AGPL/SSPL denied; reads the _as-used_ license of dual-licensed packages) joins the ADR-0048 OSV/audit pipeline. (3) **OCR is Tesseract-direct, not OCRmyPDF** — OCRmyPDF requires **Ghostscript (AGPL)**, transitive copyleft one import deep; OCR = Tesseract (Apache-2.0) + EmbedPDF/PDFium or qpdf for rasterize/reassemble, refining ADR-0030. (4) **Redaction un-deferred, Adobe parity largely off the shelf.** EmbedPDF's **Redaction plugin is true content removal** (destructive, alters the content stream, original unrecoverable, optional fill of any color) — the vector-level capability the first pass planned to _build_ on PDFium, now an MIT plugin, removing the maintenance burden the owner was wary of and giving Adobe-class text-native output. Two thin shells stay ours for legal defensibility: a **sanitize pass** (confirm Metadata+Redaction strip every hidden vector; keep **qpdf** as belt-and-suspenders if not) and a **verification gate** that re-extracts and **refuses to emit** if any content survives under a mark (most real-world leaks are operators who only boxed text — a pipeline that cannot emit an unverified redaction is a defensibility edge competitors lack). The raster-rebuild flatten is retained as a documented _fallback_ only. (5) **Placement = client-first by default.** The one WASM engine runs in browser, Worker, Container, and Tauri; interactive single-document user-present work (view/annotate/redact/fill/stamp/extract/metadata/export) runs **on the user's device** (browser WASM / Tauri native), privileged bytes never leaving it — ADR-0045's client-side-by-default sharpened into the default, matching the ADR-0023/0024 parity model; the result still uploads to R2 (ADR-0081, compute client-side / artifact server-stored). **Only four cases are irreducibly server-side:** no-user-present generation; bulk/batch over a corpus; native engines (OCR/LibreOffice); and trust/authority (the PAdES seal, and the authoritative redaction+verification for an evidence/discovery production). That server tier is **mostly Container with the Worker as orchestrator** — the light-Worker-PDF-compute tier largely collapses into the client. **Device variance for interactive work is the user's concern** (owner, 2026-06-13 — no required server fallback for large interactive docs; batch remains server-side regardless). **Shared review/markup and its coordinating Durable Object are DEFERRED** (owner, 2026-06-13) — v1 markup is single-user, client-side, persisted as firm-scoped annotation data on save (no real-time coordination, no markup DO in the near-term design); collaborative _document_ editing stays Collabora on the source `.docx` (ADR-0010), not the PDF. (6) **Upstream-first engine-extension policy (scoped to metadata and signing).** Generic PDF-engine capability we need but EmbedPDF lacks is built to be **contributed upstream** (fork + PR under EmbedPDF's MIT terms — verify any CLA/DCO at first contribution), **shipped locally in parallel** via `pnpm patch` or a pinned fork so no slice blocks on the merge, and **collapsed to upstream — dropping the external dependency — if/when accepted** (rejected/stalled → keep the patch indefinitely, which MIT permits). Two named targets: **signing** — EmbedPDF/PDFium _verifies_ signatures but cannot _create_ a PAdES seal (spike #2), which otherwise forces the external **`@signpdf`**; build seal-creation for upstreaming and drop `@signpdf` if merged (the textbook dependency-elimination case). **Metadata** — read/write/strip is _already_ in-engine via EmbedPDF's `EPDF_*` extensions (spike #3), so there is **no dependency to eliminate**; the contribution is gap-filling (most likely full-document _sanitization_ of non-metadata hidden vectors for redaction defensibility). **Boundary:** only the _generic engine primitive_ is upstreamed; the proprietary product layer stays in-tree — the **ESIGN/UETA attribution workflow + Certificate of Completion** (ADR-0045) around the signer, and the **redaction verification gate** around sanitization. Designing for upstreamability also forces a clean engine-vs-product seam (ADR-0051), and becoming an active contributor **upgrades this ADR's bus-factor mitigation from "we could fork" to "we know the codebase well enough to fork."** Generalizable to other strategic OSS dependencies later. **Considered & rejected:** **MuPDF / `mupdf.js`** (the only single-library rival; does everything incl. true vector redaction — but **AGPL**, excluded entirely, not even a fallback; EmbedPDF is the permissive equivalent); **OCRmyPDF** (transitive Ghostscript AGPL); **paying Artifex** for commercial MuPDF/Ghostscript (owner prefers not to pay); **the first pass's five-library hand-composition** (PDFium+pdf.js+pdf-lib+qpdf — fragmented vs. a single SDK, and pdf.js/pdf-lib are now redundant); **building Tier-2 vector redaction ourselves** on PDFium (the EmbedPDF Redaction plugin already provides it); **commercial suites** Apryse/Nutrient/Syncfusion (paid). **Consequences:** the stack collapses to one engine in two runtimes (EmbedPDF/PDFium-WASM, browser + Node/Container) plus orthogonal services (LibreOffice convert, Tesseract OCR) and an optional qpdf sanitize backstop — uniformly permissive (MIT/BSD-3/Apache-2.0/MPL-2.0); Adobe-parity redaction arrives off the shelf with our defensibility shell on top; a license gate makes a future AGPL dependency unmergeable; cost is a single-org dependency (bus-factor — mitigated by MIT + thin-over-PDFium, escape hatch to raw `@embedpdf/pdfium`/`pdfium-render`) and the open verifications below. **Status Accepted** — validated since the proposal: the four-vector document-sanitization contribution (the §J metadata target) was built CI-green and submitted upstream (issue embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer#673; PRs embedpdf/pdfium#27 + #28 and embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer#674 + #675), the item-2 license gate is implemented (`tooling/lint-licenses.mjs` + a CI governance step; weak copyleft MPL/LGPL allowed, AGPL/SSPL denied), and fork consumption is settled in ADR-0089. The five build-time questions were **spiked 2026-06-13** (evidence on file): render CPU ~2.9–6.6 ms/page (single-page Worker-feasible, bulk → Container; WASM ~2.05 MB gzip under the Workers paid limit); **signature = verify only → `@signpdf` stays for the PAdES seal** (the one firm constraint); **metadata read+write/strip confirmed in-engine** via EmbedPDF's `EPDF_SetMetaText` extensions + a working verification-gate text-extraction primitive (qpdf needed only for non-metadata hidden vectors, if at all); **`FPDF_SaveAsCopy` round-trips** a valid PDF headless in Node; Bates feasible (APIs present, save round-trips — drive content mutation through the high-level `@embedpdf/engines` API, as raw FPDF string marshaling is inconsistent). No blocker found. Refines **ADR-0045** (PDF services + redaction-deferral; pdf.js/pdf-lib/`@signpdf` re-scoped), **ADR-0030** (OCR = Tesseract-direct); realizes **ADR-0024**/**ADR-0039** (one WASM+native engine, placement); builds on **ADR-0010** (Collabora source editing), **ADR-0048** (CI gains the license gate), **ADR-0083** (placement measurement), **ADR-0074** (Workers paid limit); competitive gap in [`docs/research/competitive-analysis.md`](research/competitive-analysis.md). Note: [`docs/research/pdf-tooling.md`](research/pdf-tooling.md).
- **ADR-0089** · Accepted · 2026-06-13 · Documents / Tooling — **The PDF-engine fork is consumed as `pnpm pack` tarballs of our built `@embedpdf/*` packages (vendored, `file:` deps) — not `pnpm patch` and not a live git dependency — and collapses to the published npm version once the upstream PRs merge.** **Context:** ADR-0088 §J set the upstream-first policy (ship locally in parallel; collapse on merge) but left the _consumption mechanism_ open. The sanitization contribution ships a custom-built **PDFium WASM binary** (~4.6 MB) plus TypeScript living in pnpm-workspace subpackages, which rules out the casual options. **Decision:** consume the fork via `pnpm pack` tarballs of our built `@embedpdf/pdfium` + `@embedpdf/engines` + `@embedpdf/models` (each `.tgz` bakes in the built `dist` **and** the rebuilt `pdfium.wasm`), vendored into the repo and depended on via `file:` (or a private registry) — reproducible, self-contained, and the only clean way to carry the binary. **Collapse** to the published `@embedpdf/*` version once the upstream PRs merge (issue embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer#673; PRs embedpdf/pdfium#27 + #28, embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer#674 + #675). **Fork-sync cadence:** on a PDFium bump, re-pull upstream, rebuild the wasm via the fork CI's `rebuild_wasm` path, re-pack; the ADR-0088 item-2 license gate covers the vendored tarballs (all permissive / weak-copyleft). **Considered & rejected:** **`pnpm patch`** (it diffs files; a multi-MB binary patch is unworkable); a **live git dependency** (the `@embedpdf/*` packages are workspace subpackages, not root-installable). **Consequences:** no live external git dependency at build time; a small vendored-tarball + fork-maintenance tax until the PRs merge, after which it disappears. **No consumption code lands until the first PDF product slice needs it** — this records the mechanism so that slice does not stall on it. **Status Accepted.** Refines **ADR-0088** (§J upstream-first); first exercised by the first PDF slice; signing follows its own path ([`docs/specs/2026-06-13-embedpdf-signing-options.md`](specs/2026-06-13-embedpdf-signing-options.md)).
- **ADR-0090** · Accepted · 2026-06-15 · Architecture / Distribution & IP protection — **Distribution & IP-protection posture: treat everything distributed to a client as readable by an adversary; the moat is the server and the legal perimeter, not the bytes. Client hardening is minification only (no obfuscation as a primary defense; no production source maps); the open/closed line is drawn explicitly; entitlement enforcement is server-side and deferred.** **Context:** asked ahead of distribution (web MVP first, Tauri desktop after — ADR-0002) how the program's internals are protected from actors who might extract the code or recreate the product. Four threats named (all in scope): competitor cloning, code/IP theft from the shipped artifacts, license/entitlement circumvention, tenant-data exfiltration. This records the _posture_ only — nothing is built now; every mechanism below is a named future seam (owner, 2026-06-15: posture ADR only). **Decision, seven points.** **(1) The moat is the server, not the bytes.** Anything embodying competitive value or correctness secret-sauce runs server-side (Workers / Containers / Durable Objects), never in the distributed client. The distributed surfaces — the Svelte SPA JS bundle, the Rust→WASM shared core, and the Tauri native binary + webview — are **treated as public** in the threat model: assume an adversary reads them. This is largely descriptive of where value already lives — the trust/ledger engine (ADR-0008/0009), conflicts (ADR-0013), billing/costs, and document-automation workflows are database-bound through the firm-scoped data-access layer (ADR-0003) and were never client candidates; it sharpens, not reverses, the ADR-0088 client-first-by-default placement (privileged bytes stay on the user's device, but the engine logic shipped there is the _generic_ PDFium engine, not proprietary product logic). **(2) Client hardening = minification only.** No heavy obfuscation / anti-tamper as a primary defense — rejected: it buys little against a determined actor while costing debuggability, performance, and clean observability/CSP (ADR-0017). **No production source maps are shipped to clients** (binds now — cheap, expensive to walk back; also trims shipped bytes). Seam: per-hotspot obfuscation may be revisited at distribution time _only_ if a concrete sensitive client-side routine appears; the default is no. **(3) Legal perimeter (named; owned outside engineering).** Proprietary closed-source SaaS license (already the platform's stance — ADR-0088); EULA / Terms of Service prohibiting reverse-engineering, decompilation, and scraping; trade-secret hygiene — the white paper, specs, ADRs, and research are internal trade secrets and are **not** published; trademark the brand (`magnacarta.esq`); register copyright; patents case-by-case and disfavored (a patent publishes the method). This is a business/legal owner track — engineering names it as a dependency and does not design it; its only real cost is legal/one-time (registration + drafting). **(4) The open/closed line is explicit** (reconciles ADR-0088 §J). Generic engine primitives are _deliberately_ open — upstreamed under MIT (the PDFium sanitization/signing contributions) — which is intentional, not a leak. What is protected is the **proprietary product layer** (trust/ledger correctness, the conflicts engine, document-automation workflows, the RBAC + ethical-walls model, the redaction verification gate), the **firm data**, and the **brand**. Rule: open the generic primitive; protect the product layer + data + brand. **(5) License/entitlement circumvention → server-side enforcement only (deferred seam).** When distribution arrives, entitlements/licensing are checked server-side in a Worker; the client is never trusted to enforce its own license, and the Tauri desktop app is a thin client to the same server entitlements — no offline crack surface for paid features (the ADR-0023 parity model: desktop adds optional local accelerations with a server fallback, never platform-exclusive entitlement logic). Nothing is built now; named as a distribution-time slice. **(6) Tenant-data exfiltration is already governed** by the tenancy/security posture — the firm-scoped data-access layer (ADR-0003), ADR-0052 absence-not-existence responses, sandbox-hardened Containers, and the separate cookieless preview origin (security conventions). This ADR adds nothing there; it references those as the answer to that threat. **(7) Honest non-goal.** Clean-room reimplementation of the product _concept and UX_ is not technically preventable and is explicitly out of scope for technical defense — it is countered only by the legal perimeter (point 3) and the operational moat (trust-accounting/regulatory correctness, data gravity and switching cost, integrations, reputation, feature velocity). **Considered & rejected:** a hardened/obfuscated-client posture (cost > value; the server is the real lever); building license/entitlement enforcement now (YAGNI — distribution is far off; recorded as a seam); treating _all_ internals as secret (contradicts the deliberate ADR-0088 §J openness — the open/closed line is drawn instead). **Consequences.** _Binds now:_ no production source maps; "does this value belong server-side?" becomes a design check on new client-shipped logic; internal process docs are treated as trade secrets. The now-binding rules are recorded in [`docs/conventions/security.md`](conventions/security.md) (_Distribution & IP-protection posture_); everything else is a named future seam surfaced in [`../ROADMAP.md`](../ROADMAP.md) at distribution time. _Cost:_ no material infrastructure cost — value placement is mostly descriptive of existing server-side logic (DB-bound, low-CPU; Workers bill CPU time, not I/O wait), and the ADR-0088 client-first default is preserved; "protected" never means relocating heavy _client-feasible_ compute onto an inline Worker — heavy + protected work goes to a Container/Queue (golden rule 10, ADR-0039, ADR-0083). Real costs are legal/one-time (trademark, copyright, EULA/ToS); the deferred entitlement check is a session-cached per-request lookup. **Status Accepted** (posture only — no implementation). Builds on **ADR-0003** (tenancy isolation), **ADR-0023** (web/desktop parity), **ADR-0052** (absence-not-existence), **ADR-0088** (proprietary-SaaS stance + §J open/closed line); composes with golden rules 3 and 10.
- **ADR-0091** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Plugin / Add-on system — **Integration port-system architecture & contract — the ports-and-adapters foundation in one pure `@magna-carta/integrations` package: a typed category _port_ (interface in our domain vocabulary, not a vendor's); _adapters_ implementing it; a _capability descriptor_ per adapter so callers degrade gracefully; a canonical-model contract (normalized record per category + vendor-native `remoteId` + opt-in untouched `raw` + zod-validated boundary + two passthrough tiers + additive-only versioning); a registry resolving `{firm, principal, category} → adapter` with a typed `Dispatch` sum (`not-connected` / `capability-unsupported` / `adapter-error`, never a bare throw); RFC 9457 problems extended with `retryable`/`category`; and framework-owned cross-cutting machinery (retry/backoff, circuit breaking, idempotency, rate-limit, redaction) behind interfaces, thin now. Isolation = in-process first-party modules now, with the in-process → service-binding/Worker-RPC → Workers-for-Platforms split as the documented, contract-stable marketplace seam. Proven by retrofitting the credential-less `parcels` resolver as the first adapter behind a `ParcelLookupPort` (zero procurement, zero slice-1·2 dependency); the same port later carries credentialed vendor adapters (Regrid/ATTOM) — one port, many adapters across the credential boundary.** **Context:** integrations are the highest-leverage parallel-to-UI backend work, and ADR-0015 fixed only the posture (uniform plugin model, Worker-per-integration behind typed ports). Owner reframing (2026-06-16): integrations _now_ are first-party features Magna Carta builds that reach out to external platforms — not outside vendors providing services, not actors bringing credentialed services in (those are seams, not built). Five-stream research (2026-06-16) found strong industry convergence (Merge/Apideck/Nylas/Cronofy/Paragon/Finch) on the canonical-model + `remoteId` + raw + passthrough + capability-descriptor + additive-versioning shape, which also matches the house code-as-contract style (ADR-0022). **Considered & rejected:** separate deployed Worker per integration _now_ (per-integration deploy/scaffold overhead before a marketplace justifies it — deferred to the seam); pure TS interfaces with no runtime boundary validation (external data is untrusted; cheap zod validation guards the domain and sets the marketplace seam); schema-first generated contracts (only worth it for third-party authors who don't exist yet); forcing every pure utility behind a port (the white paper's "pure utilities are libraries, not plugins" stands — `parcels` becomes an adapter because its port will carry credentialed siblings, not as dogma). **Consequences:** a real, tested integration framework lands parallel to the UI track with no gate on unlanded slices; calendar is the first _credentialed_ adapter, designed at the seam level and built later (ADR-0092, §11 of the spec); the connection table joins the load harness when that adapter lands (accretion rule, ADR-0083). Concretizes **ADR-0015**; builds on **ADR-0022** (code-as-contract), **ADR-0039** (async-capable dispatch / compute placement), **ADR-0071** (package seam), the RFC 9457 problem module (ADR-0066). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-integration-port-system-design.md`](specs/2026-06-16-integration-port-system-design.md).
- **ADR-0092** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Plugin / Add-on system — **Connection model, per-functionality scoping & credential seam — connection identity splits into provider-config (the adapter's first-party app/OAuth registration; one per adapter, Worker secrets, not per-firm) vs grant (one authorized account, recorded per-firm in `integration_connection`, handle `integrationConnection_…`); _scope is declared by the port_ — `firm` or `user` — not a global default, so a user-scoped port (calendar/email: each attorney authorizes their own account, the ethical-wall-correct answer) keys on `{firmId, userId}` and a firm-scoped port (e.g. firm accounting) keys on `{firmId}`; each connection carries a health state machine `connected | degraded | needs_reauth | disabled` driven from dispatch outcomes plus a capability snapshot; the `CredentialStore` is a framework interface whose real backing is slice 1·2's per-firm envelope-encrypted `firm_secret` store (`getSecret`/`putSecret`, ADR-0084); token refresh is framework-owned and serialized by a Durable-Object-per-credential lock (providers silently rotate refresh tokens — concurrent refreshes race), surfacing `invalid_grant` as `needs_reauth` after one retry, never a silent loop.** **Context:** the white paper said "per-firm scoped secrets" blanket, but research (2026-06-16) confirmed calendar/email connections are per-user in practice (and for ethical walls), while other categories are firm-wide — so scoping must be a per-port property. Storing OAuth refresh tokens is exactly the envelope-encryption job ADR-0084 already specified (it names "plugin credentials" as an encrypted-unindexed protected payload), so the credential store is that store, not a new mechanism — which makes _live credentialed_ adapters gate on slice 1·2 while the framework and its credential-less proof adapter do not. **Considered & rejected:** a single global per-firm (or per-user) scope (wrong for half the categories); a bespoke credential store separate from `firm_secret` (duplicates ADR-0084's envelope/AAD/shred machinery); per-adapter token refresh (each adapter reinventing the rotation race inconsistently — the framework owns it with a single-writer DO lock). **Consequences:** corrects the white-paper _Plugin / Add-on system_ wording (per-functionality scoping, not blanket per-firm); credentials inherit ADR-0084's per-firm isolation + crypto-shred for free; the OAuth/refresh machinery is designed here and built with the first credentialed adapter (gated on 1·2 + the Google CASA / Microsoft verification compliance review). Builds on **ADR-0091** (the framework), **ADR-0026/0084** (the credential store), **ADR-0003** (per-firm isolation), **ADR-0068** (decrypt/manage-secrets permissions), **ADR-0039** (the DO refresh lock). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-integration-port-system-design.md`](specs/2026-06-16-integration-port-system-design.md).
- **ADR-0093** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Plugin / Add-on system — **Property external-links are a pure, registry-driven, credential-less utility (`@magna-carta/property-links`), distinct from credentialed property _data_: a `LinkProvider` registry + a total `resolvePropertyLinks(reference) → PropertyLink[]` that turns a property reference (address; optionally APN/jurisdiction) into the external "view this property on X" links available for it. Zillow and Redfin are the first two providers; Google Maps/Realtor/Trulia and a county-assessor provider wrapping `@magna-carta/parcels` are one-row seams. Delivered as plain `https` hyperlinks (Universal/App Links open the native app when installed, else the website — no custom `zillow://`/`redfin://` schemes).** **Context:** the owner wants one-click property lookups (reduce clicks, increase access to information). Load-bearing constraint: **Zillow retired its public API (~2021) and Redfin has none; scraping violates ToS** — so for these services the deep-link is the ceiling (no Zestimate/listing/ownership/history programmatically). That richer data is a _separate, credentialed_ concern (ATTOM/Regrid/HouseCanary — [`docs/research/property-data-integrations.md`](research/property-data-integrations.md)) behind the integration framework's future credentialed adapters, which Zillow/Redfin cannot feed. This package is the **external-links half**, mirroring `@magna-carta/parcels` (the county-assessor deep-link resolver — the same capability, folded in via the assessor provider seam). **Decision:** a built-in pure utility, not a framework adapter (white-paper build-in-vs-plugin rule: "pure utilities are libraries, not plugins") — a credential-less framework-adapter wrapper behind a `propertyExternalLinks` port (the `parcels`-as-adapter precedent, ADR-0091) stays a named seam. URL construction only; the anchor attributes (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`) and buttons are the consuming UI's concern, gated on the Locations UI. **Considered & rejected:** wiring through the integration registry now (heavier — registry resolve + firm context — for a pure URL builder with no credentials, and the one-adapter-per-category model can't express "show all providers at once"); custom URL schemes (undocumented, fragile, no web fallback); a Zillow/Redfin-only widget (the unified external-links concept absorbs Maps/Realtor and the existing assessor link, surfacing anywhere an address lives — Location now, Directory property entity / matter subject-property later); pulling listing/valuation data from Zillow/Redfin (no API; ToS). **Consequences:** one-click external lookups at near-zero cost with a clean growth path (more providers, more surfaces, keyed by more identifiers); the credentialed property-data subsystem stays cleanly separate. Mirrors the `parcels` pattern; relates to **ADR-0091** (optional adapter seam), **ADR-0031** (Location address), **ADR-0040** (future per-firm provider config). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-property-links-design.md`](specs/2026-06-16-property-links-design.md).
- **ADR-0094** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Integrations / Postage — **Shipping & postage subsystem: adopt EasyPost as the first credentialed shipping adapter, with the `ShippingPort` as the category port, because EasyPost offers a documented REST API (rate/buy/track/refund), supports certified mail (USPS Certified Mail with return receipt and signature), and the EasyPost Forge child-user model centralizes postage billing to our account — whereas Pirate Ship has neither a public API nor certified mail support. The subsystem lands as `@magna-carta/shipping` (canonical model + `ShippingPort` + EasyPost adapter + zod boundary), the `integration_connection` per-firm table + migration `0006`, and firm-scoped `ConnectionStore` and `CredentialStore` seams in `@magna-carta/data-access`. The UI, vertical-slice routes (connection-management, `POST /v1/shipments`, rates, purchase, label, tracking, refund), and the live `firm_secret` credential backing are Plan 2 (gated on slice 1·2).** **Context:** the white paper names postage/shipping as a Phase 7 integration; the integration port system (ADR-0091/0092) landed the framework. Legal practice produces three shipping use-cases — certified-mail service-of-process (deadline-critical, USPS Certified Mail only), deposition/subpoena/evidence transport (tracked), and general document delivery — all requiring a credentialed provider. **Provider evaluation:** Pirate Ship rejected on two hard blockers: no public API (web-only UI — no programmatic rate/buy/track), and no certified mail capability. EasyPost selected: a comprehensive REST API (`/shipments/rates/buy/track/refund`), USPS Certified Mail with return receipt and signature, the Forge child-user model (charge-to-parent) so every client firm's postage bills to our EasyPost account (no per-firm billing setup needed), multi-carrier support, a generous free tier. **Design decisions:** `ShippingPort` (`validateAddress` / `rateShipment` / `buyLabel` / `getTracking` / `refundLabel`); canonical types (`packages/shipping/src/canonical.ts`) — `CanonicalAddress`, `Parcel`, `Rate` with `amountMinor` money encoding, `RatedShipment` with `remoteId` (EasyPost shipment id, consumed by `buyLabel`), `Label`, `TrackingEvent`, `RefundResult`; request-input types `AddressInput`, `ParcelInput`, `ShipmentOptions`, `RateRequest`, `BuyLabelRequest`; capability descriptor gates certified-mail usage; `ShipmentOptions` models certified mail + return receipt + signature + insurance; EasyPost mapping in `packages/shipping/src/easypost/map.ts` — `mapRatedShipment`, `mapLabel`, `mapAddress`, `mapTrackingEvents`, `mapRefund`, `mapShipmentOptions`, `dollarsToMinor`; EasyPost HTTP errors mapped into the framework's `IntegrationProblem` taxonomy (`auth`/`rate_limit`/`validation`/`upstream`/`network`) via `classifyError(status, title)` — errors surface as the dispatch `adapter-error` outcome. **Connection and credential seam:** `integration_connection` (migration `0006`, `integrationConnection_…` handles from `@magna-carta/identifiers`) and firm-scoped `ConnectionStore` (`getConnection` over the `integration_connection` table) land in `@magna-carta/data-access` as the connection persistence layer; the `firm_secret`-backed `CredentialStore` seam (`createFirmSecretCredentialStore` over a `SecretStore` interface, tested with an in-memory fake) is the ADR-0092 seam — the live `firm_secret` backing is Plan 2, gated on slice 1·2. **Out of Plan 1 (seamed):** shipment/label/tracking tables, the shipping service, all worker routes, EasyPost webhook ingress, R2 label storage, and the live credential backing. **Considered & rejected:** Pirate Ship (no API, no certified mail); stamps.com / Endicia (legacy APIs, no certified mail at API level); a multi-provider abstraction built now (one provider first, the port generalizes by construction). **Consequences:** legal-grade postage (certified mail) joins the platform; the `ShippingPort` is a first-class integration port carrying all ADR-0091/0092 framework semantics; Forge child-user billing removes per-firm credential management for postage; Plan 2 wires the live routes and credential store. Builds on **ADR-0091** (port framework), **ADR-0092** (connection model, `CredentialStore` seam), **ADR-0095** (api-key credential kind). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-shipping-easypost-design.md`](specs/2026-06-16-shipping-easypost-design.md); Plan 1 implementation plan: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-shipping-easypost-implementation-plan.md`](specs/2026-06-16-shipping-easypost-implementation-plan.md); Plan 2: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-shipping-easypost-plan-2-vertical-slice.md`](specs/2026-06-16-shipping-easypost-plan-2-vertical-slice.md).
- **ADR-0095** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Integrations — **API-key credential kind added to the integration credential model, amending ADR-0091 and ADR-0092: a new `api-key` `CredentialKind` joins the existing credential shape with `{ kind: "api-key"; key: string }` typed as `ApiKeyCredential`, carried through `@magna-carta/integrations` alongside the previously assumed OAuth-flow kind. The decision amends ADR-0091/0092 because those ADRs implicitly assumed the first credentialed adapter would be OAuth (calendar/email) — the shipping adapter (EasyPost) uses a static API key per firm account, so the `api-key` kind must be modeled explicitly.** **Context:** EasyPost authenticates with a static per-account API key passed in the `Authorization: Bearer` header; the Forge child-user model provides a child API key per authorized firm user. The original credential model in ADR-0092 described the `CredentialStore` interface in terms of token refresh and `invalid_grant`, which presupposes OAuth flows — but EasyPost's static key requires neither rotation machinery nor a Durable-Object refresh lock. **Decision:** extend the `Credential` discriminated union with `ApiKeyCredential` (`kind: "api-key"`, `key: string`); credential-kind branching in the EasyPost adapter reads the `api-key` branch and injects the key as the `Bearer` token; the `CredentialStore.get()` contract remains unchanged (the store returns the appropriate credential shape; the adapter narrows). OAuth kinds remain the seam for calendar/email adapters. Rotation: API keys do not rotate automatically (unlike OAuth refresh tokens), so the DO refresh lock is not exercised by this kind — the credentials module carries the `api-key` kind as a no-rotation branch. **Considered & rejected:** coercing the EasyPost key into an OAuth-shaped token (a category error — forces meaningless `expires_at`/`refresh_token` fields onto a static credential); a separate per-provider credential interface (violates the typed-discriminated-union contract that callers narrow over — an adapter must receive a `Credential` and narrow, not a bespoke type). **Consequences:** the framework supports both static-key and OAuth-flow adapters from the first credentialed adapter; future adapters (accounting APIs, PACER, court-filing services) can declare the `api-key` kind without further model changes. Amends **ADR-0091** (credential model, `Credential` union) and **ADR-0092** (adds `api-key` to the `CredentialKind` enumeration; rotation machinery is kind-gated, not universal). Realized in `@magna-carta/integrations` (`src/credentials.ts`). Serves **ADR-0094** (EasyPost adapter).
- **ADR-0096** · Accepted · 2026-06-17 · Plugin / Add-on system — **Integration-platform decoupling: each integration package exports one declarative `IntegrationManifest`; the core holds a central registry that enumerates manifests and drives all wiring (route mounting, per-request adapter resolution) from them — inverting the previous model where the core contained direct knowledge of each plugin. `app.ts` stops naming individual plugins; routes are mounted via a manifest-driven loop through the unchanged ADR-0067 chokepoint. `PluginRuntime` is introduced as the uniform dependency seam (firm-scoped capabilities handed to every plugin's route handler), replacing bespoke per-plugin `CreateAppOptions` fields. Shipping (credentialed) is migrated as the proof of correctness; parcels and property-links (credential-less) gain catalog-only manifests establishing both integration types in the registry from day one. Intrinsic shared-resource concerns — migrations, permission registrations, and identifier prefixes — remain core-side: they cannot be plugin-private. Accidental coupling (route wiring, dependency injection, catalog knowledge) is removed. A well-formedness + decoupling fitness guard (unique `id`/`category` per manifest; `permission` in the access-control registry or null; `app.ts` imports no individual plugin route module by name) runs in CI, making regression a build failure.** **Context:** the shipping slice (ADR-0094/0095) landed the first credentialed adapter, at which point the core accumulated bespoke wiring in roughly a dozen places per plugin. Adding a second plugin at the same cost would compound that coupling exponentially; the manifest model was the obvious inversion. The ADR-0091 port-system spec already anticipated this: "Isolation = in-process first-party modules now, with the in-process → service-binding → Workers-for-Platforms split as the documented, contract-stable marketplace seam." The manifest is exactly that contract. **Decision:** `IntegrationManifest` is a pure-data struct carrying `id`, `category`, `displayName`, `summary`, `vendorUrl`, `credential` (kind/scope, reusing `CredentialRequirement` from ADR-0091), `capability` (reusing `CapabilityDescriptor`), `permission` (a known `KnownPermission` or null), `defaultEnablement`, `connectSchema`/`settingsSchema` (Spec-2 marketplace fields declared now so the contract is whole), `registerRoutes` (the plugin's own Hono route wiring, optional), and `adapter` (the Port adapter, for credentialed resolution). The Spec-2 fields are declared now but read only by the marketplace (Spec 2), not by the current core. The central registry (`listManifests()` / `manifestFor(category)`) is the single place the core learns a plugin exists; adding a plugin = write its manifest + add one line. `PluginRuntime { httpClient, secretStoreForFirm }` is the uniform seam: built once in `createApp`, injected into every `registerRoutes` call, and — crucially — this is the exact seam the C boundary (ADR-0097) later replaces with RPC. The emitted OpenAPI contract and committed snapshot are regenerated; routes are byte-identical to before (only mounting became data-driven). **Intrinsic boundary:** migrations, permission registrations, and identifier prefixes stay in the core because they are genuine shared-resource concerns: a per-firm migration lineage is applied by the provisioner (one lineage, not per-plugin); a permission key lives in the one access-control registry and is referenced by name in the manifest; identifier prefixes live in the one identifier registry. Moving any of these to a plugin package would require cross-package coordination at provisioning time. **Considered & rejected:** a per-plugin Worker now (deferred to ADR-0097 — the trigger condition is not met; the manifest is the contract that survives that move); a plugin options bag growing per plugin (the status quo — inverting it is the decision); pulling permission/migration registration into the manifest (these are shared-resource registrations, not plugin-private declarations — they cannot be owned by the plugin package). **Consequences:** adding or removing a plugin touches only that plugin's package plus one registry line; the core enforces the chokepoint for every plugin route without naming them; the fitness guard keeps the decoupling from regressing; shipping, parcels, and property-links all live under the same manifest-driven model from day one. Builds on **ADR-0091** (port framework), **ADR-0092** (connection model), **ADR-0067** (chokepoint), **ADR-0071** (package seam); sets the stage for **ADR-0097** (C-transition seam). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-17-integration-platform-foundation-design.md`](specs/2026-06-17-integration-platform-foundation-design.md).
- **ADR-0097** · Accepted · 2026-06-17 · Plugin / Add-on system — **The C-transition seam: `PluginRuntime` is the boundary contract that makes the in-process integration model transport-agnostic. The transport progression under one unchanged manifest is: (C0, current) in-process — plugin `registerRoutes` runs in the same Worker, `PluginRuntime` is direct function calls; (C1) service bindings — a per-plugin Worker, `PluginRuntime` becomes RPC over a service binding, sub-millisecond, same-DC; (C2) Workers for Platforms — per-untrusted-plugin Workers in a dispatch namespace, the full third-party marketplace isolation model confirmed as Cloudflare's supported path. Authors code against `PluginRuntime` identically at every stage. The trigger for advancing past C0 is the first untrusted/third-party plugin, or the first plugin needing an independent deploy cadence or sandbox — not a calendar date. This is recorded now, not built — the hard problems below are documented so they are not improvised at trigger time. Hard problems C must solve: (1) per-firm D1 across Workers — bindings are set per-Worker by `database_id` at deploy time; a plugin Worker cannot receive the core's runtime-resolved firm DB connection across the boundary, so the resolution mechanism (capability token, proxy Worker, or similar) must be designed; (2) firm-key / credential custody — the `CredentialStore` and per-firm `firm_secret` encryption root live in the platform Worker, not the plugin; crossing the boundary requires a defined, audited handoff; (3) distributed migration ownership — the current model has one per-firm migration lineage applied by the provisioner; an isolated plugin Worker cannot safely manage its own migrations against the firm D1 without coordination; likely resolution is plugins _declaring_ schema fragments that the platform applies to the single lineage, not per-Worker migrations; (4) chokepoint-across-boundary — C1: the platform enforces tenancy/authn/RBAC then forwards a trusted context header to the plugin Worker; C2: per-Worker sandbox plus a re-check inside the plugin Worker (the platform cannot trust the plugin's enforcement in the untrusted model); (5) aggregated OpenAPI — the single committed contract must aggregate operations from Workers that are separately deployed; (6) multi-Worker dev/test/deploy complexity and Workers-for-Platforms dispatch-namespace pricing. What stays platform-owned at every stage regardless of transport: tenancy resolution, the single ADR-0067 chokepoint, the per-firm migration lineage, and the encryption custody root. The manifest (`IntegrationManifest`) is the stable interface all three stages share; it is defined whole in ADR-0096 (including the Spec-2 marketplace fields) precisely so the C boundary does not reshape it.** **Context:** the in-process model (ADR-0096, ADR-0091) is deliberate and correct for first-party integrations where trust is inherent and inter-Worker overhead would be gratuitous. The C seam documents why isolation is deferred and exactly what must be solved before it is triggered — avoiding both premature complexity and improvised architecture at trigger time. ADR-0091 named the seam ("Workers-for-Platforms split as the documented, contract-stable marketplace seam") without specifying it; this ADR specifies it. **Considered & rejected:** building C1/C2 now (no trigger condition met; the manifest and `PluginRuntime` seam cost nothing today and preserve the option); leaving the boundary unspecified (risks improvising it under time pressure when a third-party plugin first appears); per-plugin D1 databases (isolation at the cost of cross-plugin queries and the provisioner complexity — not adopted; the shared per-firm lineage is the invariant). **Consequences:** the hard problems are named and searchable before they become urgent; the trigger is explicit (untrusted/third-party plugin or independent-deploy need) so advancement is event-driven, not calendar-driven; the platform retains its invariants (tenancy, chokepoint, migration lineage, encryption root) in all three stages. Builds on **ADR-0096** (manifest + `PluginRuntime` seam), **ADR-0067** (chokepoint), **ADR-0003** (tenancy + per-firm isolation), **ADR-0084** (encryption custody), **ADR-0091** (port framework). Not built — recorded only. Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-17-integration-platform-foundation-design.md`](specs/2026-06-17-integration-platform-foundation-design.md).
- **ADR-0100** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Operations / Communications layer — **Company email & account-of-record on Google Workspace (`magnacarta.esq`) — the firm's operational mailboxes and the identity that _owns_ third-party accounts run on Google Workspace: one paid human seat with role addresses (`ops@`/`billing@`/`support@`/`legal@`/`privacy@`/`security@`, plus `postmaster@`/`abuse@`) as free aliases forwarding into it.
