Two invariants bind: (1) recovery stays off the domain it depends on — the Cloudflare account and the `.esq` registrar keep an independent break-glass recovery mailbox _outside_ `magnacarta.esq` (their mail only resolves while Cloudflare DNS is up, so a same-domain recovery is a lockout loop), while accounts that don't serve this DNS (GitHub, EasyPost, Google Cloud, npm) move fully onto `ops@`; (2) infra/API logins use role addresses, never a personal `name@`. Distinct from the product's transactional sending (`no-reply@`, Cloudflare Email Sending, ADR-0074) — the two co-tenant the one DNS zone and are coordinated so neither clobbers the other's MX/SPF/DMARC.** **Context:** as the program acquires third-party services, the founder needs durable, recoverable, role-based logins plus a professional correspondence address; today those accounts sit on a personal Gmail (`trevordgoldsmith@gmail.com`) — a bus-factor and account-recovery risk. `.esq` is a real Google-Registry TLD (legal-profession eligibility), already the canonical app domain; `magnacarta.esq` is owned. **Decision:** Google Workspace, over Microsoft 365 and over Cloudflare Email Routing + Gmail Send-As — chosen for compliance/audit maturity (enforced 2FA, audit log, retention, BAA — material to a legal-records product's future attestations, ADR-0090 perimeter) and a unified Google dev identity (the `.esq` registry, Google Cloud / OAuth verification, and the deferred Gmail/Calendar adapter of ADR-0092 all live in an owned Workspace org). **Considered & rejected:** Microsoft 365 (equal capability, no ecosystem alignment unless Microsoft Graph becomes the dominant integration); Cloudflare Email Routing + Gmail Send-As (free, but receive-only forwarding into one personal Gmail — weakest exactly for account-of-record: re-creates the single-human bus factor and risks the same-domain recovery loop); Migadu / Zoho / Fastmail (flat-cost unlimited real mailboxes, fully viable and product-neutral, but forgo the audit/BAA maturity and the Google dev-identity convenience — recorded as the rational budget alternative). **Consequences:** a small recurring per-seat cost (tracked in [`docs/operating-costs.md`](operating-costs.md)) buys a clean recovery/handoff story and an attestation-ready identity; the company mailbox choice is **decoupled from the product's email features** — the product sends via Cloudflare Email Sending (ADR-0074) and integrates with each firm's _own_ Gmail/Outlook (ADR-0092), never routing through the company Workspace; the only seam is the convenience of an owned Workspace org when building/testing the Google adapter's admin-consent path. Runbook + the two invariants + the §2 DNS-coexistence steps live in [`docs/conventions/operations.md`](conventions/operations.md) (_Company email & account-of-record_). The product email architecture ("what email means for users" — the per-user mailbox-connect adapter and a candidate inbound email-to-matter capture) is a separate, deferred design thread. Relates to **ADR-0074** (product email sending), **ADR-0092** (the per-user email/calendar adapter), **ADR-0090** (legal/attestation perimeter; trademark of `magnacarta.esq`), **ADR-0084** (secrets posture). **Status Accepted** (operational decision; setup is an owner runbook, not engineering work).
- **ADR-0101** · Accepted · 2026-06-16 · Tools area — **a presentation/IA catalog above the integration framework.** A top-level Tools area surfaces first-party utilities both generally (a sidebar grouped by subject) and contextually (queried by context tag), from one pure `@magna-carta/tools` catalog. **Decision:** the Tools layer is a thin presentation layer _above_ the existing packages, not part of the integration framework — `library`-backed tools (link generators and interactive widgets) are self-contained; `integration`-backed tools reference an integration `category` and reflect its `Dispatch`/health, while the Tools layer itself never touches credentials. Honors the framework's "pure utilities are libraries, not plugins" boundary. **Consequences:** the pure package (types + catalog + selectors + the `tools:` permission namespace) builds independently of the UI; the sidebar/contextual rendering, the id→component map, the integration health branching, and role-seeding land with the design-system shell. Concretizes ADR-0015 on the presentation side; builds on ADR-0091/0092 (integration port system) and ADR-0068 (access control). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-16-tools-area-design.md`](specs/2026-06-16-tools-area-design.md).
- **ADR-0103** · Accepted · 2026-06-17 · Tools / Diagnostics — **Firm usage snapshot: a read-only, per-firm usage view assembled from our own D1.** **Decision:** storage (summed from the `storage_meter` rollup) + entity counts (matters by status, non-deleted files, clean versions, folders, locations), read per-firm from D1 with no API token; each domain package owns the read of its own tables (no cross-domain query). **Deferred seams:** per-firm compute/activity (Workers CPU time, request volume) needs the Cloudflare Analytics Engine query + an API-token credential — designed as a seam, not built; the aggregate `FirmUsageSnapshot` type + worker read route land with the consumer; the UI rides the design-system shell. Builds on ADR-0101 (the Tools layer), ADR-0078/0083 (storage metering + load-profile accretion), ADR-0003 (tenancy), ADR-0068 (access control). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-17-firm-usage-diagnostics-design.md`](specs/2026-06-17-firm-usage-diagnostics-design.md).
- **ADR-0104** · Accepted · 2026-06-18 · Dev guidelines — CHANGELOG and ADRs move to per-entry fragment files assembled on main; ADR numbers are assigned at merge (contiguous, stable); supersedes the prior changelog-automation open line.
- **ADR-0105** · Accepted · 2026-06-18 · Dev guidelines — Parallel-worktree coordination — an active-work registry (`vp run worktrees` + per-stream `docs/active-work/` notes), a main-sync cadence, and a worktree/branch lifecycle rule.
- **ADR-0106** · Accepted · 2026-06-18 · Observability — Product-usage analytics — always-on opt-out reached/completed events on the one telemetry path; content-free, HMAC-pseudonymous identifiers; cross-firm aggregate-only with small-n (k=5) suppression; Analytics Engine for live counts, R2 Data Catalog Iceberg (Logpush→Pipelines) for full-fidelity retention/BI.
- **ADR-0107** · Accepted · 2026-06-18 · Matters / Directory — Custom-fields engine realization (slice 1·4) — the shared typed-EAV `@magna-carta/custom-fields` on three per-firm tables (`custom_field` / `picklist_option` / `custom_field_value`), stable-handle field identity (a rename is a one-column UPDATE, never a cascade), five typed value columns under an exactly-one-non-null CHECK, append-and-flip full-temporal value history, audit split (who/when in `audit_log`, what-it-was in the value rows), and a two-target extension of the directory merge with a new `archive-to-history` collision mode for field-level survivorship.
- **ADR-0108** · Accepted · 2026-06-18 · Matters / Directory — Custom-field lifecycle & governance (slice 1·4) — rename is label-only and free; retype is a non-destructive guided Convert (preview classifies clean/needs-rule/parked, apply flips originals to history — reversible); value history is full-temporal; delete is archive-first with a hard-delete only on a zero-value/zero-binding field and a separate gated, audited Purge erasure seam; load-profile-calibrated anti-bloat caps; conformity templates deferred behind a before-first-client gate (only the reserved `template_field_key` ships now).
- **ADR-0109** · "Proposed — owner decision pending" · 2026-06-19 · AI / Inference — "Client-consent model for third-party-frontier processing: the recommendation is recorded (yes — but only under ZDR, gated by the firm-level eligibility setting plus a recorded consent decision; strictest firms default to the on-Cloudflare Workers-AI path; privilege must never ride on an implicit default); the choice is not yet made."
- **ADR-0110** · Accepted · 2026-06-19 · AI / Inference — "Credential and confidentiality posture: Unified Billing + ZDR-on + payload-logging-off; ZDR is a diligence obligation backed by named Cloudflare/Anthropic procurement artifacts (which gate the first real AI feature, not this plumbing slice); a fail-closed per-request ZDR guard is built now; the residual classifier-retention disclosure is required in the firm-facing language."
- **ADR-0111** · Accepted · 2026-06-19 · AI / Inference — "`@magna-carta/inference` is a standalone pure package, not a registered integration adapter — the integration framework is reused at the type level only (`Dispatch` sum + problem taxonomy), and the BYOK credentialed adapter is a named later slice."
- **ADR-0112** · Accepted · 2026-06-19 · AI / Inference — "Multi-tenant fairness and availability: one gateway per environment, off-hot-path Queue dispatch required, a single firm-scoped spend rule, account-level credit alarm, a named BYOK/Enterprise scaling switch point, and the `firmRateGate` per-firm back-pressure seam built now with the token-bucket machinery deferred until measured."
- **ADR-0113** · Accepted · 2026-06-19 · AI / Inference — "Tenant-isolation hard constraints: caching disabled for all firm-data inference, firm metadata derived not caller-supplied, authenticated gateway, authoritative response-captured token-usage metering in the firm-scoped data-access layer (never gateway logs)."
- **ADR-0114** · Accepted · 2026-06-19 · File preview / File Manager — The `inline_playable` verdict is homed AT INGEST (per the white paper) but slice 2·3 lands ONLY an additive, nullable, two-axis column on `file_version` (`playable`|`download-only`|`indeterminate`, NULL = never-sniffed), on `main` ahead of the worktrees, matching the `storage_encoding`/`artifact_role`/`pinned`/`storage_class` seam precedent; the codec-sniff PARSER, the `runIngest` compute hook, and the corpus backfill are ALL DEFERRED to whichever slice first READS the value (the 2·1 ingest rebuild, the 0·7a/0·7b player, or Reports); `ingest-core.ts` is off-limits in 2·3 (the live, built-but-unmerged 2·1 worktree is rewriting it — hard-conflict surface); the migration number is not pre-reserved.
- **ADR-0115** · Accepted · 2026-06-19 · File preview / Security — Slice 2·3 inline-preview safe delivery — untrusted bytes are served from a separate cookieless `files.*` host (the existing Worker on a second hostname, outside the chokepoint) and consumed as a native `<video>`/`<audio>` SUBRESOURCE on the trusted app origin, so the bytes reach only the browser's media decoder, never an HTML/script parser; `Content-Disposition: attachment` is KEPT verbatim (a no-op for the subresource, honored for a top-level navigation), so `security.md` is amended ADDITIVELY (a second sanctioned inline-render mechanism) rather than deviated from; inline bytes flow through a Worker route under a first-party HMAC token (over `AUTH_SECRET`, NOT an R2 presign, which cannot carry the headers); the grant is `audited:false` (no per-view logging); seekable Range/206 is the slice's primary acceptance criterion; CORS is ungranted at launch; browser media-decoder RCE is a knowingly-accepted platform residual whose fix is the deferred Container rendition.
- **ADR-0116** · Accepted · 2026-06-21 · File conversion — "In-app conversion stays in-house only with confidentiality absolute and one engine per edge; a pluggable external-engine model (Microsoft 365/Adobe behind an egress policy + per-content-class classification) was considered and rejected because in a law practice nearly all content is privileged, so any classifier permitting egress is a one-mistake leak of privileged material, and the fidelity gain is marginal. The ConversionPort/adapter + format-registry + multi-hop-pathfinding architecture is retained (it has in-house value and leaves a future deliberate seam); the confidentiality axis, egress policy, and content classification are not built. Affirms and extends ADR-0030."
- **ADR-0117** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Worker / Delivery — Every surface the application Worker serves — the SvelteKit SPA shell, the API, Better Auth, and every branded per-firm subdomain — is a private, authenticated, multi-tenant surface that must never be indexed by a search engine, so the Worker stamps `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` on every app-origin response via a global response-header middleware. The middleware is registered AFTER the cookieless preview route so the sealed `files.*` edge (attachment bytes + bare 404s, never an indexable page) keeps its exhaustive payload-derived header set untouched. The directive lives in code (version-controlled, CI-tested by a standing fitness guard) rather than as a Cloudflare dashboard Transform Rule; the existing dashboard-managed `robots.txt` is kept as complementary (it governs crawling, the header governs indexing). The public marketing site is deliberately a separate future app on its own host (apex/www) that does not run through this Worker and so does not inherit the directive.
- **ADR-0118** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Security / Integrations — "Every user-supplied outbound target (the WHOIS query subject, the safe-browse URL, and every redirect hop) must pass assertPublicTarget (@magna-carta/integrations/safe-target) before any fetch or render. Rules: http/https only; resolved IP must be public — loopback, RFC1918 private ranges, link-local (169.254.0.0/16 incl. cloud-metadata 169.254.169.254), unique-local IPv6, and all other reserved ranges are rejected. The check is on the resolved IP, not the literal string, to defeat DNS-rebinding. The investigated URL/host is never logged. This is a platform security invariant: every future feature that performs an outbound fetch to a user-supplied target must use the same guard."
- **ADR-0119** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Safe Website Viewer — "The Safe Website Viewer renders a suspect URL via Cloudflare Browser Rendering (the env.BROWSER Workers binding — no API token) and returns a static snapshot: screenshot, final URL and redirect chain, response headers, page title, extracted text/markdown, and outbound links. The non-interactivity of the snapshot is itself the strongest safety property — the live page never loads in the user's browser. Interactive pixel-streaming (Browser Sessions + Durable Object per session + WebSocket frame streaming) is a documented future seam, not built. The redirect chain and response headers come from a guarded fetch redirect-probe; Quick Actions (the snapshot binding) do not expose these."
- **ADR-0120** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Tools catalog — "The @magna-carta/tools catalog gains a new 'web-domains' subject group (label: Websites & Domains) inserted before 'diagnostics', and a new 'website' context tag. Both the WHOIS Lookup and Safe Website Viewer tools declare this context tag, enabling any future surface that holds a URL or domain (e.g. a Directory contact's website field) to call toolsFor('website') and get both tools for free. Builds on ADR-0101."
- **ADR-0121** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · WHOIS Lookup — "WHOIS lookups use RDAP (HTTP/JSON, via the IANA bootstrap registries) as the primary path and fall back to a direct port-43 TCP socket (Workers connect()) for ccTLDs that publish no RDAP server. No third-party WHOIS vendor is introduced. The port-43 response is returned as labelled raw text; the long tail of free-form WHOIS formats is not parsed. Builds on the integration framework's http transport seam and the shared SSRF guard."
- **ADR-0122** · Accepted · 2026-06-23 · Platform architecture — "Heavy Container-backed subsystems that process untrusted bytes — malware scanning, file conversion, document editing, and the future PDF engine — execute in their own dedicated Cloudflare Workers, not in the main app worker, each reached over an internal service binding with no public surface and outbound networking disabled. The app worker keeps the orchestration (Durable Objects, queues, routes, firm-scoped state) and selects the engine behind an existing port boundary, so the split is transport-only and the orchestration does not change. Search (Tantivy) is the explicit exception — its index is durable per-firm data, not stateless compute. Scan (apps/scan-worker) and conversion (apps/convert-worker, slice 2·4c) are the first two; editing (2·2) and the PDF engine follow the same pattern."
- **ADR-0123** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Tools — "All-TypeScript Metadata Inspector & Scrubber with document and image work behind swappable engine interfaces; a stateless MetadataPort seam with a size cap; v1 formats .docx/.xlsx/.pptx and .jpg/.png/.tiff; .heic, PDF, large-file Container engine, and Rust→WASM image engine are named measured-trigger seams."
- **ADR-0124** · Accepted · 2026-06-23 · File handling / malware scan — "The scan engine's YARA layer is sourced from YARA Forge (externally maintained, self-updating) rather than hand-curated rules: the image build fetches the latest YARA Forge release, drops the false-positive-prone heuristic `_SUSP_` pocket, compiles the kept set with yara-x (skipping + logging the rare incompatible rule, failing the build only on a systemic collapse), and serializes it into the image; the runtime deserializes at boot. The `extended` tier is the default (its ~4,686 high-confidence malware rules over `core` justify the small filtered FP pocket), tier + score floor are build ARGs. The engine-version `yara-rules` component is the Forge release tag, so the fleet re-scans on a real rules change but not on an unchanged nightly rebuild. A YARA hit is treated identically to a clamd signature hit (`infected`) — one quarantine path. Extends ADR-0081."
- **ADR-0125** · Proposed · 2026-06-18 · Documents / Editing — **Collabora editing — WOPI host, per-document hosting topology, and access-token auth.** **Decision:** in-app `.docx` editing runs Collabora Online (coolwsd) in **one isolated Cloudflare Container per open document**, coordinated by a **per-document Durable Object** (`DocumentSession`, addressed by `fileId`, durable state in `ctx.storage` SQLite). We are the **WOPI host**, Collabora the client; the host is built to the **standard `X-WOPI-*` contract as the baseline** with Collabora's `X-COOL-WOPI-*` extensions **additive**, keeping both Word futures (Office-for-web client; desktop-Word check-out) reachable. WOPI callbacks are a **token-authenticated surface off the cookie chokepoint**: a short-lived HMAC-signed capability (per-firm-derived signing key + `kid`, `sessionId`-bound, constant-time verify) whose firm-scope **and** capability are **re-derived/re-checked at use on every callback** (a firm-A token against a firm-B `WOPISrc` → 404; a view token cannot `PutFile`/`Lock`, server-side, independent of the advisory CheckFileInfo flags). Near-instant revocation rides a sub-ms DO `member.status` check **scoped to genuine permission-change** (in-flight `PutFile` allowed to complete; routine expiry via `access_token_ttl`). coolwsd runs **least-privilege**: `enableInternet` stays **false** (untrusted document bytes get no general egress), no R2/D1 credentials or key material, ephemeral per document. It is still a WOPI *client* that must call the host, so the WOPI callbacks ride Cloudflare **container outbound interception** (`static outboundByHost`, GA 2026-03-26): coolwsd's `WOPISrc` targets a fixed internal host that the `DocumentEditorContainer` class intercepts and forwards to the app worker over an `APP_WORKER` **service binding**, entirely inside the Workers runtime — so the container reaches the token-authenticated WOPI host and nothing else (default-deny). All object I/O flows through that host. The editor iframe is served from a **dedicated cookieless content domain — a separate REGISTRABLE domain (`magnacartausercontent.com`), not a subdomain of the app** — with a `postMessage` origin allow-list. Each open document gets a first-level subdomain `<documentKey>.magnacartausercontent.com` (`documentKey` = a SHA-256 prefix of `fileId`, opaque, leaking neither firm nor `fileId`; the edit worker reads it as the container key so co-editors converge). A separate registrable domain makes the origin cookieless **by construction** — the browser never attaches a `magnacarta.esq` session cookie to a different site, independent of app cookie-scoping — which a subdomain (`*.edit.magnacarta.esq`, same registrable domain) cannot guarantee; it is the googleusercontent.com model (separate-domain sandboxing that subdomains cannot provide). First-level keys are covered by free Universal SSL (`*.magnacartausercontent.com`), avoiding the Advanced-cert cost a two-level `*.edit.<app>` would incur. The domain is the **one home for all browser-served untrusted content**: file previews/downloads (`FILES_ORIGIN` → `files.magnacartausercontent.com`, a more-specific route on the app worker) share it. Tenancy is never carried by the hostname — it rides the per-firm-keyed WOPI token re-checked at use — so a firm-agnostic content origin is safe. The ADR-0029 preview-sandbox does not apply (a trusted derived renderer; raw bytes never reach the browser). Container liveness while a document is open is carried **natively by the editor's WebSocket** — `@cloudflare/containers` (≥0.3.7) counts an open WebSocket as an in-flight request and renews the activity timeout without any alarm or heartbeat (the historical WS-frame-doesn't-renew bug #147 no longer reproduces; verified in the measurement spike). We deliberately do **NOT** run an alarm-driven keep-alive: it is unnecessary and rides the still-open alarm-scheduler spinloop (PR #191). On disconnect the container sleeps after `sleepAfter` (tuned to minutes for reconnect tolerance) so provisioned-memory billing stops when no document is open; durable reconnect (state in DO SQLite + R2, version-cut-on-reap) keeps lost work at zero. See the measurement-spike findings folded into [[container-subsystems-extract-to-dedicated-workers]] (also: idle floor ≈ 1.16 GiB → a ~2–2.5 GiB custom instance, single pre-spawned kit). **Rejected:** pooled/shared coolwsd (one process serving many documents — blast-radius + cross-firm-in-one-process, owner red-flag; the ~$8–100/mo amortization does not justify it); a hand-rolled per-`fileId`-claimed warm pool (collides with `getContainer(binding, fileId)` keying — use native `min_instances` pre-warming instead); a second native CRDT/structured editor (ADR-0010, fidelity). Builds on ADR-0010 (editor choice), ADR-0067 (chokepoint), ADR-0066 (OpenAPI contract; WOPI routes via the conformance allow-list), ADR-0046 (key-versioning), ADR-0075 (resource naming), the security-conventions untrusted-file bar, and ADR-0029 (preview origin). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-18-slice-2-2-editing-versioning-storage-tiering-design.md`](specs/2026-06-18-slice-2-2-editing-versioning-storage-tiering-design.md).
- **ADR-0126** · Proposed · 2026-06-18 · Documents / Editing — **Document editing concurrency & reconciliation — optimistic, never-block, human-reviewed.** **Decision:** the per-document Durable Object is the single authority over three access intents, under three invariants — **reading is never blocked**, **no work is ever silently lost or merged** (every merge is human-reviewed), and **one isolated container per document**. **Online** editing is real-time co-editing: connected editors share one Collabora session and **one** WOPI session lock (first editor locks, co-editors join, last leaver unlocks); a second editor **joins**, never blocked. **Read** is always available — a viewer joins read-only (write hard-rejected server-side) or gets the last committed version. **Offline** editing is optimistic and never blocked: checkout hands back a short-TTL **presigned GET** (key server-derived, ADR-0081/0082); check-in returns through the **ADR-0081 quarantine ingest path** (untrusted bytes — scanned before any container opens them); on a cleared check-in, divergence (by base-version-id + hash) either fast-forwards or is kept as a **conflicting copy** + a `needs reconciliation` flag, resolved by a human `documents:reconcile` decision (**no automatic `.docx` merge**; the conflicting copy is retained forever). An **opt-in exclusive lock** lets a member prevent divergence (others still read). **Stale locks:** a dead co-edit session is reaped fast by an alarm heartbeat (~60–90 s; 30-min WOPI expiry is only the backstop); a deliberate exclusive lock gets a **generous, firm-configurable idle auto-release + manual force-release** (authority matter-team-with-edit + firm admins/owners, **matter-scoped** so an ethical-wall exclusion denies it even for a firm admin; force-release is always safe). The §4 revocation explicitly **does not recall** an already-downloaded offline copy — a revoked member's check-in is refused and the checkout force-released. **Rejected:** pessimistic exclusive-lock-by-default (blocks people; the owner red-flagged the "locked out by a ghost" failure mode); automatic merge of diverged `.docx` (not line-mergeable — reconciliation is human-reviewed); a decomposed/structured doc model to enable merge (ADR-0010, fidelity). Builds on ADR-0035 (versioning), ADR-0081 (quarantine ingest), ADR-0068/ADR-0052 (access control; within-firm 403 vs absence 404), ADR-0049 (deleted-file lifecycle). Spec: [`docs/specs/2026-06-18-slice-2-2-editing-versioning-storage-tiering-design.md`](specs/2026-06-18-slice-2-2-editing-versioning-storage-tiering-design.md).
- **ADR-0127** · Accepted · 2026-06-21 · Documents — hayro is an accepted Cargo dependency for PDF rendering; it is consumed as a versioned crate (not vendored), pinned in Cargo.lock, and treated as a black-box render library — the codebase does not fork it or patch its internals.
- **ADR-0128** · Accepted · 2026-06-21 · Documents — The PDF render engine for inline preview and safe-delivery is written entirely in pure Rust (hayro), compiled to WASM for the web runtime and native for the desktop runtime, with no C binding, no vendored binary, and no server round-trip for rendering.
- **ADR-0129** · Accepted · 2026-06-21 · Architecture — Rust crates live in a single Cargo workspace at crates/ beside the pnpm workspace; the two build systems are independent and share no package manager — Cargo owns the Rust graph, pnpm owns the TypeScript graph, and the WASM artefact is the only artifact that crosses the boundary.
- **ADR-0130** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Financial Core / Time tracking — Rates are effective-dated, append-only-versioned rate cards. Resolution is per-entry override → matter → client → timekeeper → role → card default; a time entry snapshots the rate in force on its work_date and keeps it forever (never re-resolved). Multiple named cards (e.g. "Insurance-capped"). Retroactive corrections insert a new dated version — billed entries never change, draft entries keep their captured rate with an opt-in reRateUnbilledDrafts action (gated by time:manageRates). Closed-interval non-overlap is enforced in the service (SQLite has no range-exclusion constraint); a partial unique index backstops the open row. rate_e4/amount_minor are nullable (null = non-billable; flat-fee/contingency matters still capture the standard rate for realization); currency is stored alongside.
- **ADR-0131** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Financial Core / Time tracking · Architecture — The single active live timer is a per-timekeeper Durable Object (the codebase's first DO), firm-namespaced via idFromName(`${firmId}:${userId}`). It holds only transient timer state ({startedAt, pausedIntervals, matterId?, label?, narrative?, lastActivityAt}) in DO storage — it is NOT a business-data store; committed entries flow through the data-access chokepoint into per-firm D1. It is idle-friendly (hibernates between interactions; elapsed is computed client-side from startedAt, so the DO never ticks), making cost negligible. Stop-commit is idempotent on a DO-minted live_run_id (compare-and-set) to close the cross-device double-commit race; abandoned-timer detection uses an Alarms-API alarm cancelled/rescheduled on commit/discard. Scratch/unbound timers are allowed; binding to a matter is required only at commit.
- **ADR-0132** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Architecture / Settings — Slice 3·1 lands the first realization of the white-paper settings-precedence chain — a scoped_setting (key, scope_tier, scoped_id, value) table + a resolveScopedSetting(key, context) resolver in @magna-carta/data-access that walks most-specific-first: matter → client → user → location → firm → in-code default. The existing flat `setting` seam is firm-level key/value only and cannot express the white-paper's own per-client/per-matter (and the owner's per-timekeeper) overrides that the time increment/rounding settings need. This is net-new shared infrastructure, kept minimal (only the tiers time needs) and reusable by later settings, generalized when a second consumer arrives. Firm-level-only use behaves exactly like the flat seam.
- **ADR-0133** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Financial Core / Time tracking — The time-entry lifecycle is Draft → (optional Submitted → Approved) → Billed. The Submitted/Approved review gate is firm-configurable and OFF by default, preserving the white-paper frictionless Draft→Billed for the default firm; this revises §17.1's "no approval workflow" line to "an optional, firm-configurable review gate, default off." On entering Billed or Void a row freezes (a column-level freeze contract enforced by the service — distinct from, and weaker than, the ledger's never-mutate append-only rule); corrections to a billed entry are new adjusting entries. The intermediate Submitted state is also the host for the deferred passive draft-approve queue.
- **ADR-0134** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Documents — PDF redaction uses rasterize-and-reassemble (render the page to a pixel image, burn the redaction region onto that image, replace the original page content with the image) rather than content-stream surgery, and enforces a hard verification gate that fails closed on any text-extraction anomaly.
- **ADR-0135** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Architecture / Data — Before the first real alpha firm is onboarded, collapse the PER-FIRM migration lineage into a single genesis-baseline migration equal to the current drizzle schema; leave the control-plane lineage incremental (it is already deployed). Gated on (1) no durable per-firm database to preserve and (2) all parallel slice branches merged. A one-time pre-alpha cleanup, not a now-task; it absorbs the accumulated per-firm migration cruft (renumbers, hand-edited ALTER/PRAGMA table-rebuilds, mid-slice snapshot amendments).
- **ADR-0136** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Documents — PDF document sanitization removes seven document-level surfaces by default with per-surface opt-outs; a five-layer hard gate including logical-content decompress-and-scan closes compressed string survival; OCG content-level operator excision and selective metadata retention are deferred.
- **ADR-0137** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Documents — The bad-redaction detector is built on a shared observing-Device text-position primitive (GlyphMark + CoverMark + PageObservation) and a high-recall divergence model — extractable ∧ not-visible — covering all seven categories (CoveredText, InvisibleText, WhiteoutText, RemovableAnnotation, HiddenLayer, MetadataLeak, OrphanedText); none deferred.
- **ADR-0138** · Proposed · 2026-06-22 · Platform / Deployment topology — **Container-backed subsystems extract to dedicated Workers (not modules in the app Worker).** **Decision:** a subsystem whose compute runs in a Cloudflare Container is deployed as its **own Worker**, reached from the app Worker by **service binding** (or, for a browser-fronted renderer, called back over its existing token-guarded HTTP surface) — never bundled into the main `magna-carta` Worker. The malware scanner set the precedent (`apps/scan-worker` → `magna-carta-scan`: its own Container DO, image, deploy/refresh cron, `max_instances`, no public surface, outbound disabled). The rubric a subsystem must clear: ① heavy native / Container runtime, ② independent deploy cadence, ③ distinct scaling profile, ④ containment / blast-radius (processes untrusted bytes), ⑤ **the Container compute holds zero firm state** (state stays behind the data-access layer / in a coordination DO in the app Worker). The split is along the **compute ↔ coordination seam**: the Container (stateless compute) extracts; the coordinating Durable Object and all firm-scoped data-access stay in the app Worker.
- **ADR-0139** · Accepted · 2026-06-23 · Documents — PDF redaction gains an opt-in text-preserving surgery mode that excises targeted glyphs from the content stream with a per-page outcome gate, falling back to the existing rasterize-and-reassemble path for any page it cannot confirm clear.
- **ADR-0140** · Accepted · 2026-06-23 · Tenancy & isolation — The per-firm database moves from a per-firm D1 database (reached by a static Worker binding, which capped firms at the ~5,000-bindings-per-Worker ceiling and required a config-commit + redeploy to onboard each firm beyond a pre-declared pool) to a per-firm SQLite-backed Durable Object addressed by firmId — implicitly created on first access, no per-firm binding, no redeploy, scaling to millions of instances; the control-plane D1 and all per-firm R2 are unchanged. The domain layer is already written against an abstract SqlDatabase/BatchDatabase interface (not D1Database), so two ~100-LOC adapters (doSqliteBatchDatabase over ctx.storage.sql with a SELECT changes() mapping for meta.changes, and doSqliteMigrationTarget using transactionSync) carry the unchanged statement modules; a throwaway spike proved the real 18-migration per-firm lineage self-migrates on wake and a real unmodified statement module runs with faithful meta.changes, UNIQUE-conflict detection, and batch rollback. Supersedes the per-firm-D1 half of ADR-0003; the design is docs/specs/2026-06-23-per-firm-database-durable-object-design.md.
- **ADR-0141** · Accepted · 2026-06-23 · Dev tooling — "Regenerable build artifacts and dangling Docker cache are reclaimed automatically when a branch lands in main, via a tracked post-merge git hook that runs a `vp run clean` script. The cleaner deletes only git-ignored artifact directories (target/, .svelte-kit, .wrangler, .vite, dist, build, coverage) inside worktrees whose branch is merged into main, and prunes dangling Docker builder cache and image layers. It deliberately never removes worktrees, branches, or node_modules — those stay manual because a worktree may hold uncommitted or foreign work. Installed once per clone with `vp run setup-hooks` (sets core.hooksPath to the tracked tooling/git-hooks)."
- **ADR-0142** · Accepted · 2026-06-23 · Documents — Bates stamping is implemented as an additive text overlay using base-14 Helvetica with rotation-aware corner placement, non-destructive resource merge, and continuous set numbering; single-PDF merge and from-scratch page generation are explicitly deferred.
- **ADR-0143** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · Tools / File Manager — The File Hash & Integrity tool is built as a pure streaming multi-digest core (one read → five digests) with server-side computation on every surface, verification against the ingest SHA-256 baseline recorded to audit_log, and a new "file" context tag — with async large-file verify, client-side standalone hashing, and signed/PDF receipts deferred as measured-trigger seams.
- **ADR-0144** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · Dev tooling — "The build/deploy pipeline is Cloudflare Workers Builds, not GitHub Actions. Workers Builds is connected to the GitHub repository and, on push, builds each Worker (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && vp run build-all`) and deploys it via Wrangler (`wrangler deploy`); deploying the scan Worker builds and pushes its Container image. This records the as-built reality and supersedes the deploy-model choice in ADR-0075 (which named GitHub Actions). GitHub Actions is retired (the `.github/workflows/ci.yml` workflow is removed); the quality gate (`pnpm check`), tests, the OpenAPI contract toolchain, and the scan EICAR acceptance test run pre-push (locally and/or as a Workers Builds build step) rather than in a standalone CI runner. Operator runbook in docs/conventions/operations.md."
- **ADR-0145** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · Dev tooling — "Build-time performance is a standing foundation: anything that builds repeatedly in the cloud is made fast by default (caching, path-scoping, build-time baking), added up front rather than gated on measurement. Codified in a new docs/conventions/build-performance.md, hooked into the root CLAUDE.md 'Build when in the area' principle (ADR-0078), distinct from runtime performance (golden rule 10 / performance.md) and from the auto-clean disk-hygiene sweep. The build surface is Cloudflare Workers Builds (ADR-0144), so the primary levers are its native Build Caching and Build Watch Paths; the scan Container additionally gets BuildKit cache mounts on the Cargo registry/target."
- **ADR-0146** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · File handling / File Manager · Architecture — The matter bulk-download ("bundle") path gains user-chosen output options — format (ZIP and 7z, via an extensible encoder registry), compression (store/normal/maximum), AES-256 encryption, and format-native multi-volume splitting — plus quality-of-life options (named output, wrapper folder, flatten, a CONTENTS manifest). This EXTENDS ADR-0027 (which kept bulk download as store-mode ZIP64) rather than replacing it: a two-lane build keeps the existing in-Worker client-zip path for plain ZIP (store, no encryption, no split) and routes everything heavier to a dedicated Rust Container (containers/bundle, mirroring containers/scan), per golden rule 10 and the "Container subsystems extract to dedicated workers" standing rule. The encryption password is user-supplied and NEVER persisted (in-memory only — not in the durable queue, not in logs, not in the cache key); encrypted bundles therefore bypass the durable queue and the content-addressed R2 cache and build single-flight. This is general-convenience download tooling, not a legal-production feature, and it is NOT a new Tool (the Tools area is matter-grade work done without a matter; bundling only acts on files already in a matter). "Bundle" is the deliberate term — "archive" means retired/retention items in this product.
- **ADR-0147** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · Untrusted-file pipeline / Malware scanning — The malware-scan Container reports raw engine facts (a RawScanResult — clamd state, structural heuristic, YARA matches each tagged {tier, score, isSuspicious}, per-leg timings) instead of deciding a verdict. The full YARA corpus is baked and scanned once; the per-firm file verdict (clean | flagged | unscanned) is computed app-side by computeVerdict from a firm scan policy, so a firm can re-tier its policy with no container re-scan and cross-firm content dedup stays global. The content-dedup scan_cache stores the raw result keyed on the engineVersion of that same result; a clean ingest commit repopulates it. The four prior verdicts collapse to one scan-flagged state. clamd-unavailable is a raw fact (HTTP 200), raised to a retryable fault app-side; the only container 503 is an in-process engine panic.
- **ADR-0148** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · Platform / Delivery — Cloudflare Flagship is the feature-flag engine for the release-channel system, wrapped in a thin `@magna-carta/feature-flags` package that centralizes evaluation-context construction and safe-default handling; the evaluation context carries only non-privileged identifiers; every evaluation supplies a default equal to stable behavior so a Flagship outage or a missing flag can never expose unfinished features.
- **ADR-0149** · Accepted · 2026-06-25 · Platform / Delivery — Release channels gate behavior only, never schema; feature code ships dark to the single fleet; the one-fleet-one-schema model is a correctness invariant that channels must not break; beta is a firm self-serve door (RBAC + explicit recorded consent, rejects alpha); alpha is a secret-guarded platform door.
- **ADR-0150** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Foundational data & runtime conventions — Cloudflare Workflows is adopted narrowly as the durable-execution substrate for the few multi-step external flows that must be compensated on partial failure — payment capture (charge → ledger post → receipt), trust disbursements, and firm provisioning — composing ON TOP of the existing transactional outbox and idempotency primitives rather than replacing them. ADR-0064 already named "sagas via Workflows with compensating actions" as the path and deferred it ("not expanded until a flow needs it"); two things converge now — Cloudflare shipped first-class saga step-rollbacks (2026-06-25), turning hand-rolled catch-block compensation into a durably-replayed, reverse-ordered, idempotent-retry handler, and Phase 3 (payments/trust/costs) plus firm provisioning are the first flows that genuinely need cross-system compensation — so the deferred decision graduates to a scoped adoption. Forward-only pipelines (ingest, scan, bundle, inference) are explicitly NOT migrated: they are retry-forward and already well-served by Queues + Durable-Object coordinators, so the rollback primitive buys nothing there. Firm provisioning is the planned pilot (high value, lower blast radius than client money) and proves the new runtime surface and crash-replay compensation before payments/trust depend on it. The Workflow's step history is never a system of record — D1 + ledger + audit stay authoritative. Separately, ADR-0064's "Cron-Workflow relay" wording is corrected by amendment: the built relay is a plain Cron scheduled handler, not a Cloudflare Workflow, and this adoption is the product's first real use in the codebase.
- **ADR-0151** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Data / Operations — Control-plane D1 migrations apply at runtime via the single-flighted scheduled (Cron) handler, mirroring the per-firm runtime-bundled model; reads of new additive columns fail-safe to stable defaults; a cold-start migrate-before-serve gate was rejected (concurrent isolates race the atomic-batch + PRIMARY KEY constraint and the fail-loud runner would throw for all but one — requires a coordinator Durable Object not worth the cost); deploy-time application via the Workers Builds command is the documented future zero-window hardening.
- **ADR-0152** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Documents — AcroForm read/fill/flatten with engine-generated Helvetica appearances (no NeedAppearances reliance) and flatten-into-content; AcroForm-only, XFA/signature/rich-text out of scope; forms is not redaction.
- **ADR-0153** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Financial Core / Billing core — Slice 3·3 extracts the shared work-in-progress billable lifecycle out of `@magna-carta/timekeeping` into a new `@magna-carta/billing-core` package, so time entries and costs share ONE state machine and freeze contract instead of two copies drifting apart. `billing-core` holds `BillableStatus` + the gate-keyed transition tables + `isFrozen`/`canTransition`/`assertTransition` (+ `IllegalTransitionError`) and the `billing.approval_required` review-gate definition. Timekeeping's `lifecycle.ts` becomes a thin re-export that aliases `BillableStatus` as `TimeEntryStatus`, so the rest of the time package compiles unchanged; the extraction is behaviour-preserving and re-verifies 3·1's suite green.
- **ADR-0154** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Financial Core / Costs & expenses — Slice 3·3's `cost_entry` is the per-firm disbursement WIP store (migration `0022_burly_piledriver`): integer-minor `amount_minor`, a `disbursement_class` of hard|soft (the column is `disbursement_class`, renamed from `cost_class` to clear the money-naming conformance gate — never `cost_class`), a `source` of manual|grid|recovery, the shared billing-core lifecycle (draft → submitted → approved → billed → void, with billed/void frozen), and a partial-unique `recovery_ref`. Permissions are `costs:track` (capture/read/receipts) and `costs:approve` (the review-path transitions). E-BILLING DECISION: a billable cost on an e-billing matter normally requires a UTBMS expense code at capture, but a SYSTEM-recovered cost (`source=recovery`) is EXEMPT at capture — it lands as uncoded draft WIP rather than being dropped (which would lose the fronted disbursement); the coding requirement re-enforces at the 3·4 submit/bill gate. OPEN 3·4 QUESTION: how recovered costs get LEDES-coded.
- **ADR-0155** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Financial Core / Costs & expenses — Slice 3·3 lands the codebase's first per-firm queue consumer — a topic-dispatch over the single bound `env.QUEUE` (queue `magna-carta-production-platform-jobs`), the symmetric analogue of the control-plane `CONSUMERS` map but keyed on `message.topic` and resolving the firm DB by a two-hop (`firmId` → control-plane `database_identifier` → bound D1). It ACK-AND-IGNOREs unknown topics — a deliberate divergence from the control-plane sweep's throw-on-unknown, because the shared per-firm bus carries `shipment.created`/`shipment.refunded`/future topics this consumer does not own. The shipping adapter maps `shipment.purchased` to recovered disbursements. Idempotency has two floors: the per-line `runIdempotentEffect` key `${shipmentId}:${category}` and the DB `cost_entry` `recovery_ref` unique index. DEFERRED: the wrangler `consumers` declaration for `platform-jobs` (a deploy-activation step — the `index.ts` branch is dormant until then) and the `cost.recorded` emission (3·4).
- **ADR-0156** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Financial Core / Costs & expenses — Slice 3·3 lands a generalized recoverable-charge seam — a canonical `RecoverableChargeEvent`/`RecoverableChargeLine` a producer emits instead of reaching into the cost-entry tables. A line's `kind` is the closed distinction that drives recovery: `pass_through` (a third-party charge the firm fronted for the client) is ALWAYS recovered as a client disbursement; `platform_fee` (a Magna-Carta-absorbed fee such as e-signature, messaging, or shipping handling) is recovered only when a per-category firm policy `cost.recover_platform_fee.<category>` (default off) is on. The seam's logic is `shouldRecover(line, policyOn)` + `chargeToCaptureInputs(event, policy, incurredDate)`. 3·3 builds only the firm→client recovery CAPTURE (a WIP cost appears); the firm→client BILLING is 3·4; the MC→firm recoupment of absorbed platform fees is not designed yet.
- **ADR-0157** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Platform / Support access — Help-desk access to a firm's instance is pull-not-push — a firm member's request mints a time-boxed, audited support_grant (control-plane), and a named platform_staff principal redeems it for a short-lived signed X-Support-Session token. The "temporary key" is a temporary GRANT, not handed-over crypto: decryption stays server-side and the firm data key is never given to help desk. Two tiers slot into the existing route chokepoint as an alternate principal — diagnostic (a fixed diagnostics:* allow-list carrying no decrypt capability, so privileged content is unreachable by construction) and privileged with scope:self (exactly the requesting member's own effective permissions, no firm-level escalation). Backend rails only; staff self-service auth+MFA, UI, notification transport, and AI-agent principals are deferred named seams.
- **ADR-0158** · Accepted · 2026-06-27 · Untrusted-file pipeline / Malware scanning — Per-firm scan-policy settings (tier + suspiciousScoreFloor compound, flagged-file treatment, step-up re-auth flag) stored in the generic setting table; computeVerdict applies the resolved policy at ingest and rescan; a treatment menu (block/informed-access/allow-and-flag) is enforced at both ingest and access; one persisted flagged verdict replaces the prior infected/suspicious distinction; step-up re-auth enforcement is deferred to a future auth slice.
- **ADR-0159** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Documents — Compose lopdf's Standard Security Handler for PDF unlock/protect; read every revision but write AES-256 only; treat permission bits as advisory.
- **ADR-0160** · Accepted · 2026-06-28 · Tools / Scan — The File Type Verifier identifies a file's true content type by running the Magika ML engine in the sealed scan Container, returning raw identification facts; the app Worker assembles the extension-match verdict and recommendation via `@magna-carta/file-type`. Key decisions: Magika over a signature table or `file-type` (text/source coverage); co-located in the scan subsystem (shared containment and deployment cadence); raw-facts-in-Container / verdict-in-app split; probabilistic-with-confidence framing; `ort`/ONNX Runtime (C++) accepted as a contained golden-rule-14 exception.
- **ADR-0161** · Accepted · 2026-06-28 · Architecture / Rust document engines — Extract the shared runtime seam (wasm32 JS edge, axum container skeleton, magic-byte Format detection, pure-Rust feature discipline) from the pure-Rust document engines into a magna-carta-engine-host crate; the hosting pattern is shared, the deep format logic stays per-engine; detection/ML containers may adopt the container half but are not dual-target; engines are retrofitted incrementally behind green tests after the harness is proven on the File Repair & Salvage tool.
- **ADR-0162** · Accepted · 2026-06-26 · Documents — Merge / split / rotate / watermark in the pure-Rust PDF engine; merge carries interactive structure; watermark does text and image; all additive, never redaction.
- **ADR-0163** · Accepted · 2026-06-28 · Tools / AI — "AI-text detection ships as a vendor-agnostic core on the integration ports-and-adapters framework (ai-detection category): the result is a calibrated likelihood band with structured reliability caveats (never a boolean verdict); every §0 accuracy-collapse vector (short, non-target-language, paraphrase/humanization, mixed/edited authorship) forces inconclusive and per-segment bands inherit that override; external detection is opt-in / off-by-default under the AI-inference confidentiality posture; no concrete vendor is wired (a deferred adapter returns capability-unsupported)."
- **ADR-0164** · Accepted · 2026-06-29 · Delivery / Performance — Magna Carta adopts a forward-binding network-resilience standard for slow, high-latency, metered, and intermittent connections — because its users work from courthouses, rural offices, hotels, and the road against hard filing deadlines, where a transfer that aborts and restarts is a missed filing, not a nuisance. The standard ratifies four commitments and lives as a cited convention (`docs/conventions/network-resilience.md`); behaviour-vs-measurement is split so each fact has one home (this file and the convention own how to BEHAVE; `performance.md` owns how to MEASURE). (1) Resumable transfer is a required seam NOW — file bytes move only through the established R2 seam (multipart-resumable upload, presigned-GET download, HTTP Range serving), never a monolithic non-resumable in-app fetch; the backend already realises it, so this codifies the reference rather than retrofitting. (2) A slow-network / transfer-weight profile is added to the load-profile harness. (3) A shared client transport seam (one utility owning timeout, backoff, retry, and stale-cache exposure) is required so "no hardcoded timeout" and "render last-known value" are implemented once, not per call site. (4) A per-route transfer budget is a fitness function — mechanism decided now, numeric gate triggers once (2) lands and the SPA route baseline is measured. The standard binds all NEW work immediately; existing surfaces are audited once (the backend is conformant; the single bounded retrofit is the inline-ZIP stream) and migrated from findings, not reworked en masse. Commitments (2) and (3) are decided-but-deferred: the SPA is a one-page skeleton with nothing to route or measure, so building the machinery now would be speculative (ADR-0039). Builds on ADR-0078 (build expensive-to-retrofit seams up front), ADR-0039 (off the hot path / measure-first), ADR-0083 (load-profile harness), and respects ADR-0003 / ADR-0067 at the transfer seam.
- **ADR-0165** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Tools / AI — "Pangram is wired as the first vendor behind AiTextDetectionPort, platform-managed (a single Cloudflare-secret platform key, credential: none — not the per-firm credentialed-adapter infrastructure); firm consent is a per-firm setting (aiDetection.externalDetectionEnabled, default off) enforced at the worker chokepoint before any provider call; usage is recorded in a dedicated per-firm ai_detection_usage table (word-native, matching Pangram's billing unit); BYO-key, billing pass-through, document text-extraction, a bulk endpoint, and the UI are deferred seams."
- **ADR-0166** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Compliance — Design to SOC 2 / GDPR / PCI DSS / ISO 27001 controls from day one; the engineering-facing control-to-framework map lives at docs/conventions/compliance.md; formal attestation/certification stays an owner/legal track (legal-perimeter.md), deferred to distribution. Compliance is not inherited from Cloudflare.
- **ADR-0167** · Accepted · 2026-06-22 · Architecture / Delivery — A subsystem built on a heavy native runtime in a Cloudflare Container extracts into its own dedicated Worker (no public surface, reached only by service binding, owning the Container Durable Object, outbound disabled, with its own deploy/scaling/warm-poke cadence) rather than being wired into the main `magna-carta` app Worker — realized first by malware scanning (`apps/scan-worker` → `magna-carta-scan`) and adopted as the standing pattern for the Container family (editing/Collabora, conversion/LibreOffice+OCR, the pure-Rust PDF engine), gated by a five-property rubric plus a compute-vs-coordination seam: only stateless compute crosses into the dedicated Worker; firm-state-bearing coordination (the session DO, the job DO, the registry/pathfinding logic) stays in the app Worker behind the data-access layer; a subsystem whose state is a durable per-firm dataset (full-text search/Tantivy) fails the rubric's statelessness test and is a different design (a stateful service), not this pattern; the rubric and the worker template are the living standard in `docs/conventions/operations.md`, which specs and code cite by path.
- **ADR-0168** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Tenancy & data isolation — Introduce a per-firm region/jurisdiction attribute now as a designed seam so a firm's D1/R2 can be pinned to an allowed jurisdiction (EU/UK/Canada) even though only one region runs at launch — cheap now, migration-hostile later. Resolved 2026-07-02: the column is firm.data_region (us | eu, extensible; canada reserved), us being the primary-market default; the eu path uses Cloudflare's eu jurisdiction for the R2 bucket and the per-firm database Durable Object; set once at provisioning, no cross-region moves. Spec: docs/specs/2026-07-02-data-subject-attribution-erasure-residency-design.md.
- **ADR-0169** · Accepted · 2026-07-03 · Tools / File unlock & password recovery — A Tools-area utility to unlock encrypted PDF/ZIP/OOXML files (strip-with-known-password) and recover unknown passwords, built on ONE in-house pure-Rust core (`crates/decryption-core`) behind a `FormatAdapter` trait, compiled to WebAssembly for in-Worker use and to native for the search shards — no C/C++ dependency (golden rule 14). The confidentiality keystone: for recovery only the extracted password HASH crosses any boundary, never the file bytes; the password/plaintext never enters an audit row, log, or problem detail (the sole egress is the cleartext to the unlocking caller or the recovered password to the initiating requester). Every operation is button-initiated, `tools:decryption`-gated, per-file-attested, and redacted-audited. Escalation to heavier compute is a `RecoveryBackend` port seam.
- **ADR-0170** · Accepted · 2026-07-03 · Tools / File unlock & password recovery — Password recovery is orchestrated by a `RecoveryJobCoordinator` Durable Object (state in DO SQLite, not a D1 table) that splits the keyspace, enqueues one shard message per shard on a recovery Queue, dispatches each to a distinct search-container instance via a service binding, aggregates progress, and cancels the rest on the first hit. A tier router runs CPU-crackable formats and reports slow-key-derivation formats as `needsGpu` (a later rung) rather than to a futile timeout. Recovery is the platform's FIRST fan-out consumer, so the generic cross-domain fan-out primitive is deliberately NOT built here — the next non-recovery domain that needs container fan-out extracts it from this coordinator. Password-confidentiality lifecycle: surfaced once to the initiating user (retrieval bound to that identity), never audited/logged, purged from DO storage after a retention window; at-least-once queue delivery is made safe by per-shard report dedup and a time-since-last-progress stuck-job watchdog.
- **ADR-0171** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Dev tooling — "The Workers Builds production branch moves from `main` to a dedicated `release` branch on every connected Worker, and non-production branch builds stay off. Pushing or merging to `main` therefore publishes code to GitHub without building or deploying anything; a deploy is the explicit fast-forward `git push origin main:release`. This decouples source-control cadence from the live environment, which ADR-0144's as-built pipeline had fused together. Runbook in docs/conventions/operations.md."
- **ADR-0172** · Accepted · 2026-07-14 · Maintenance agents — Fragment assembly moves to per-merge CI; ADR-0058's propose-only docs-maintenance agent ships as a weekly cloud routine owning the judgment half; the roadmap joins the rendered pipeline; public roadmap/changelog become audience-filtered JSON projections.
- **ADR-0173** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Communications — The email parse core is a pure-Rust engine (crates/email-core) on the engine-host seam; parse-only — rendering and DKIM verification live outside it.
- **ADR-0174** · Accepted · 2026-07-03 · Tools — Put the engine-host R2 client (reqwest/rustls) behind an off-by-default `r2` cargo feature so an engine container that does not egress to R2 links no HTTP/TLS stack — and therefore no `ring` C/assembly — keeping the default harness pure Rust and honoring golden rule 14 by removing an unused C dependency rather than consenting to it.
- **ADR-0175** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Core domain / File Manager — Expose ingest outcome on files.list rather than a separate quarantine route — files.list gains an opt-in includePendingAndQuarantined flag and FileSummary gains status + quarantineReason, while currentVersionId stays clean-only. The clean-only download gate is not weakened: every read path independently carries its own status = 'clean' predicate, and current_version_id only ever advances to a clean version, so a pending or quarantined first upload carries currentVersionId null and structurally cannot render a download link. A separate /matters/{id}/quarantine route was rejected because it puts the answer on a page nobody opens.
- **ADR-0176** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · File Manager / Worker — "The local development upload path is a Worker route, not a production flag or a wrangler binding. Off production (no real R2 S3 credential set) the upload presigner points its PUT/part URLs at the Worker itself; a development-only route accepts those writes to the Miniflare R2 binding and enqueues the ingest message directly, standing in for the R2 event notification. It registers on exactly the credential-absence condition that already selects the dev presigner — a fail-closed signal, not a togglable binding — and is further guarded by the session + files:upload chokepoint and by validating the object key against the firm's upload session. It is same-origin, so it exercises no CORS and is explicitly NOT the acceptance gate; the first R2 bucket CORS configuration is checked in and applied out-of-band."
- **ADR-0177** · Accepted · 2026-07-03 · Tools — Run file repair on the native Container lane only — drop the WASM lane the engine-host seam offers by default — because users tolerate an upload-and-wait and a second runtime is pure cost; and rename the `documents` tool subject group to `files` (it was already the file-utilities bucket) rather than split it.
- **ADR-0178** · Accepted · 2026-07-03 · Tenancy & data isolation — Replace sequential `00NN` schema-migration numbers with monotonic-unique (timestamp-based) migration IDs so parallel branches never collide on a number, and — while still pre-production with no firm onboarded — collapse the existing migration chain (control-plane + per-firm) to one clean baseline per lineage so the new scheme starts fresh rather than retrofitting timestamps onto 35 legacy migrations. Executed as a dedicated, coordinated main-level pass, never on a feature branch.
- **ADR-0179** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Discovery / Documents — "Amends ADR-0060: the OCR crate resolves to oar-ocr (Apache-2.0, ort backend) without a spike, the model generation moves from PP-OCRv5 community ONNX exports to the official first-party PP-OCRv6 ONNX artifacts, the small tier is baked in over medium on a measured benchmark (ties on character error rate, leads on word-box overlap, a quarter of medium's size), and the cross-architecture determinism claim is narrowed to server-canonical — the x86-64 container is the single evidentiary source of truth, a future desktop path is a provisional accelerator, and every result stamps three separable version fields (engineVersion, modelVersion, platform)."
- **ADR-0180** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Tools / AI — "The Pangram adapter supports both the async task-poll flow (text.external-api.pangram.com, POST /task → poll GET /task/{id}) and the sync endpoint (text.api.pangram.com/v3), selectable by PANGRAM_MODE switch (async default); the poll delay is injected, keeping the pure package timer-free; provenance uses version→vendorVersion mapping, never the stage lifecycle field."
- **ADR-0181** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Payments — To hold PCI DSS SAQ A, card capture uses Helcim's hosted payment modal (the HelcimPay.js iframe) returning a cardToken — not the in-DOM Helcim.js tokenizer (SAQ A-EP) and not the raw card API (SAQ D); store only the cardToken (never PAN/CVV) and keep the payment surface free of third-party scripts per PCI v4.0.1. Refines ADR-0032.
- **ADR-0182** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Documents — Bound every pdf-core operation against decompression bombs with a parser-agnostic raw-bytes pre-flight (input cap + bounded discarding deflate scan) rather than forking lopdf/hayro; 256 MiB input / 256 MiB per-stream / 1 GiB cumulative caps, deflate-rigorous with LZW/RunLength and image-codec expansion as disclosed residual.
- **ADR-0183** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Platform / Authorization — Introduces the first platform-level (global, cross-firm) authorization axis — a control-plane `platform_operator` allow-list on elevated Better Auth users, MFA-required, with time-boxed (nullable-`expires_at`) grants — because status authoring is a repeated, attributed, human action that a shared secret cannot fit, and the existing `platform_staff` support identity is deliberately firm-scoped with no global session. Membership is hand-seeded (no self-service grant UI). Migrating firm-provisioning's `PLATFORM_ADMIN_KEY` onto this principal is named as a later cleanup, not done here.
- **ADR-0184** · Accepted · 2026-07-13 · Dev tooling — "The whole-repo quality gate (full `pnpm check` + `vp test`, governance lints, contract-drift checks, changed-container acceptance) moves into a GitHub Actions workflow (.github/workflows/gate.yml) that runs on every pull request into `main` and every push to `main`, and the `pre-push` hook is narrowed to a fast diff-scoped subset (`pnpm check:scoped` + audit + governance + migration integrity). This supersedes ADR-0144's decision to run all gates pre-push with no CI runner; it does NOT change the deploy model (Cloudflare Workers Builds off `release`, ADR-0144/ADR-0171 remain in force). Runbook in docs/conventions/operations.md."
- **ADR-0185** · Accepted · 2026-07-14 · Discovery / Documents — "The scanned-to-searchable PDF writer lives in pdf-core beside the Bates content-append path, writing recognized OCR text as an invisible (3 Tr) layer that is strictly additive, deterministic (byte-identical re-runs), and self-verifiable through the existing page observer. The font is a single non-embedded Type0/Identity-H CID font with a constructed ToUnicode CMap — chosen over an embedded glyphless font because an invisible glyph is never drawn, so extraction rides ToUnicode and no font binary need be vendored; this covers everything the engine reads (Latin, CJK, Greek). Placement is per word where the recognized word count matches its boxes, per line otherwise. Delivery wiring, the conversion-orchestrator edge, ingest, and the UI are deferred seams."
- **ADR-0186** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Product updates / Service status — Extends ADR-0048's single live-status KV banner into a full service-status system — a component matrix for our own primitives (exception-based health, default Operational), upstream-provider aggregation via one Statuspage-summary adapter, and operator-authored service events with a rolling update timeline — served by a new isolated read-only status worker (`apps/status-worker`) over the first `STATUS_KV` namespace. Two writers (a cron and the operator endpoints) own separate keys merged at read time, removing any lost-update race without a lock; attribution is audience-scoped so an operator's identity never reaches the public feed. UI is deferred; this slice builds the datastore, the read/write paths, and the machine-readable feed.
- **ADR-0187** · Accepted · 2026-07-03 · Dev guidelines — STATUS.md becomes a build output assembled from persistent per-slice status.d/* fragments (+ a machine-readable STATUS.json), extending the fragment workflow to the last hand-edited shared tracker.
- **ADR-0188** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Product updates / Service status — Upstream provider status never sets this platform's verdict — a troubled provider raises a distinct advisory surface instead, and `overall` is computed from our own components and events alone, amending the rollup rule in the service-status ADR. Adds a third cron-owned KV document, `status:history`, holding sample-counted per-component daily status tallies over a 90-day window, from which the page renders per-component uptime bars and a windowed incident archive. Maintenance is excluded from the uptime ratio; a day with no samples renders as no data, never as green. One shared `effectiveComponentStatus` resolves probe, override, and active-event impact for both the published component rows and the history sample, so a row and its uptime bar cannot disagree.
- **ADR-0189** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Product updates / Service status — Enables Workers Cache on `apps/status-worker` — the only worker in this repo whose responses are byte-identical for every reader, so the cache key (entrypoint + path + query + version) fully determines the body. The edge absorbs the traffic spike an incident brings without invoking the worker or reading STATUS_KV, strengthening the fate-isolation the worker exists for. Every response now carries an explicit `Cache-Control`, because with a cache in front, a directive-less response is cached anyway under heuristic freshness — the `FALLBACK_HTML` stand-in, the 404, and the 405 all return `no-store` so a failure's stand-in cannot outlive the failure. Purge is scoped to the issuing worker and every writer lives in `apps/worker`, so the 30-second staleness on a newly published incident is accepted rather than engineered away; responses carry `Cache-Tag`s so a purge route on the status worker remains available later. `cross_version_cache` stays off.
- **ADR-0190** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Compliance — Design a subject-level erasure path (GDPR Art. 17 / CCPA deletion) beyond the existing firm-level crypto-shred. Recommended: a data-subject attribution model plus a cascade that hard-deletes/anonymizes live data across per-firm D1 + R2, relies on per-firm-key crypto-inaccessibility + retention-window expiry for backups, honors legal holds, and instructs sub-processors. Resolved 2026-07-02 as Option A (structured-PII erasure with a legal-retention boundary): a declarative PII/erasure registry + coverage fitness function is the seam built now; the eraseDataSubject orchestrator is designed but deferred; matter-corpus data is retained under Art. 17(3). Spec: docs/specs/2026-07-02-data-subject-attribution-erasure-residency-design.md.
- **ADR-0191** · Accepted · 2026-07-02 · Wellbeing resources — "Lawyer wellbeing resources vary in shape per jurisdiction (a state carries a list of resources — bar programs, affiliated nonprofits, courts, outsourced EAP vendors — at statewide or metro scope), so the keyed deep-link model of `@magna-carta/government-links` (`PortalSite`/`deepLink`) does not fit, and a validated research corpus with per-resource provenance already exists. Ships a dedicated `@magna-carta/wellbeing-resources` package with a resource-centric model (`WellbeingResource`/`JurisdictionEntry`) whose data table is generated from the research corpus `docs/research/wellbeing-resources/profiles.json` (single source of truth) rather than hand-authored per state — the generator projects research resources to the shipped model, dropping research-only provenance (confidence, sourceUrl, sourceTier) and folding no-program jurisdictions into a nationalFallbackOnly flag, with the national safety net (988, SAMHSA, ABA CoLAP) unconditionally present. One home per fact — the corpus and the shipped data are meant to stay in lockstep; a verify:data guard catches drift between them when run, though it is manual and not yet wired into CI; diverges from government-links' hand-authoring, justified by the upstream corpus; the in-app UI and any public directory are deferred to a later vertical slice."
- **ADR-0192** · Accepted · 2026-07-09 · Conflicts — "The conflicts engine has no scorer: every candidate the blocking channels surface is shown, ranked by a deterministic evidence tier (exact identifier > exact normalized name > phonetic code > trigram overlap) plus the graph path that reached it, and nothing is ever suppressed by a threshold; strictness is redefined from a score cutoff into a recall knob (which channels run, which name rows they see); what a check searches is a typed source registry — Party (similarity, always on), Graph (edge traversal to depth), Imputation (identity joins only, never name similarity), Content (registered, unavailable) — governed by a firm-set floor a per-run selection may only add above, with the scope in force recorded verbatim in every log entry; firm personnel become a source, not a Directory node type, resolving the ADR-0085 reservation; the immutable log is three append-only tables (conflicts_check / conflicts_hit / conflicts_disposition), dispositions appended never updated."
- **ADR-0193** · Accepted · 2026-08-03 · Communications layer — "The forward/CC capture entrance routes on a single zone-level catch-all (`<token>@in.magnacarta.esq`), not a per-firm subdomain. The whole local part is an opaque, server-minted token; the capture Email Worker resolves it to a firm against a new control-plane `email_capture_route` (token→firm, with an active flag) index, then hands the token to the consumer, which resolves it to a candidate matter against that firm's per-firm `email_capture_address` store. Cloudflare Email Routing supports exactly one zone-level catch-all — not per-subdomain wildcards — so the firm must live in a control-plane token index, never in the subdomain."
- **ADR-0194** · Accepted · 2026-07-16 · Communications layer — "The matter-email record is five per-firm tables: email_message (one row per unique message, the unit of truth, keyed for dedup by Message-ID+firm with a normalized-header+body content-hash fallback, message-id provenance never flattened), email_thread (the conversation as a DAG carried on message edges, not a re-saved blob per reply), email_matter_link (the multi-matter junction, unique on (message, matter), per-matter access), email_participant (each from/to/cc/bcc frozen as-at receipt with a nullable directory_entity_id resolved via the Directory reverse lookup), and email_attachment (ties a File Manager file back to the message; bytes ride the existing quarantine chokepoint, decorative inline cid images filtered). Metadata in per-firm D1, the verbatim original and attachment bytes in per-firm R2; a matter's email view is reconstructed from links + the thread DAG, never a per-reply copy."
- **ADR-0195** · Accepted · 2026-07-16 · Communications layer — "Firm-user email is captured into matters as first-class, thread-aware records over one convergent pipeline and one record, never a mailbox the product hosts; the capture posture is add-in-led and CASA-free (a native Save & File add-in reads the open message with no full-mailbox scope), with a forward/CC Cloudflare Email Worker as the universal any-client fallback and an authenticated `file this MIME to matter` API as the shared server target for the add-in and manual .eml/.msg upload; every entrance parses through the one pure-Rust email-core engine and terminates on the existing ingest/quarantine chokepoint, staging staff-accept by default; full mailbox sync is the later CASA-gated credentialed tier feeding the same record, not a competing integration. This slice realizes the forward/CC and API entrances."
- **ADR-0196** · Accepted · 2026-07-16 · Communications layer — "Filing an email calls a filing-time conflict/ethical-wall hook at the link site — a no-op returning 'no conflict' now, the seam the Phase-4 conflicts engine drops in behind without touching the capture path. The 'already filed' read that prevents cross-user double-filing is wall-safe by construction: for a message the firm has captured it returns only the matters the caller can access, and a caller who can see none of them receives a generic 'already captured by your firm' with no matter identity — the signal can never become an oracle for a walled matter's existence. The same absence-not-existence rule governs the multi-matter 'also on ...' list. This ships the indicator and the hook seam now; the conflict engine and the DKIM-authenticated-sender guardrail are later."
- **ADR-0197** · Accepted · 2026-07-16 · Documents / Conversion — "Realizes ADR-0030's reserved scanned-PDF→searchable-PDF edge: the OCR engine joins the conversion graph as a new engine `ocr` behind a distinct `searchable-pdf` format node (same application/pdf media type, distinct id so a target can differ from its pdf source), reached through a per-engineId resolver that routes the ocr hop to the already-live OCR service binding rather than the dedicated conversion worker. A scanned image reaches searchable-pdf by composition (image→pdf→searchable-pdf), not a direct edge, because the searchable writer requires a PDF to append onto."
- **ADR-0198** · Accepted · 2026-07-17 · Supply-chain security — Pin the osv-scanner CI binary by SHA256, remove the pre-push hook's npm-only pnpm audit step (rather than replace it with a local osv-scanner), and drop package.json auditConfig so osv-scanner.toml is the single accepted-advisory home.
- **ADR-0199** · Accepted · 2026-07-16 · Supply-chain security — The CI dependency-audit gate moves from npm-only `pnpm audit` to osv-scanner over both the npm lockfile and every Rust Cargo.lock, keying on CVSS >= 7.0; it immediately surfaced four real high-severity Rust/npm advisories, accepted-with-tracking so the gate protects against new ones now.
- **ADR-0200** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Compliance — "Classify the six email-filing tables in the PII/erasure registry. email_message (frozen sender), email_participant (each frozen from/to/cc/bcc address plus its resolved Directory entity), and email_matter_link (which member filed a message to a matter) are personal data with a retain-under-matter action — a captured message is correspondence on the matter file, and the subject-side scrub happens at the Directory entity, as with matter_party. email_thread, email_attachment, and email_capture_address are explicitly non-PII structure. Participants and senders are keyed by frozen address VALUE as well as by resolved entity id — the first value-keyed subject keys in the registry — because a participant the Directory never matched carries no id and would otherwise be invisible to erasure."
- **ADR-0201** · Accepted · 2026-07-17 · Supply-chain security — Remediate RUSTSEC-2026-0187 by upgrading lopdf to 0.42.0 everywhere; keep the three remaining osv-tracked advisories (quick-xml x2, npm uuid) accepted with documented upstream blockers rather than force risky version bumps.
- **ADR-0202** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Communications — "A forward/CC capture address spans two databases with no shared transaction — the control-plane email_capture_route token index the inbound Email Worker reads, and the per-firm email_capture_address store the capture consumer reads — so the two directions take deliberately opposite write orders. MINT commits the per-firm row and a transactional-outbox row in one atomic per-firm batch and lets the relay register the control-plane route, so the worst intermediate state is an address that exists but does not yet route (mail bounces, nothing is lost, the relay converges it). REVOKE and ROTATE write the control plane FIRST, so routing stops before anything else can fail — revocation fails closed. Tokens are server-minted CSPRNG lowercase-alphanumeric 20-character local parts, unique-claimed against the global index with bounded retries, and the token value is disclosed exactly once."
- **ADR-0203** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Matters — "The matter-number draw's uniqueness backstop moves from the rendered string to the drawn counter value. A new per-firm matter_number_allocation table keyed by {scope_key, drawn_value} records every counter value a draw consumed; the losing batch of a concurrent draw aborts on that key and the existing bounded retry re-reads the advanced counter. The matter_number UNIQUE index stays, but as the narrower guard it actually is — it catches a rendered number colliding with a manually assigned court/legacy one, and cannot see two draws that share a counter value while rendering different strings because the scheme mixes in a variable segment that is not marked scoping."
- **ADR-0204** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · File conversion — "The LibreOffice conversion engine runs as a standalone network-isolated Container whose supervisor manages a pool of long-lived, single-threaded LibreOfficeKit worker child processes — one conversion at a time per worker, recycled by process replacement on conversion count, resident-memory ceiling, deadline, or crash. The binding is the pure-Rust jacobtread/libreofficekit crate (dlopen2, no build.rs/cc/bindgen/-sys), pinned exactly, with vendoring as the scoped fallback. The LibreOffice C++ runtime is owner-consented under golden rule 14; the consent covers the dlopened suite only, and every layer we write around it stays Rust."
- **ADR-0205** · Accepted · 2026-07-18 · Documents / E-signature — The PAdES platform seal runs in a dedicated sign-worker (WASM engine + pkijs CMS + the platform key), reached through a typed SealPort service binding, not in-process in the app worker.
