The platform seal uses a single self-signed Magna Carta certificate with the private key held as a Worker secret on the sign-worker; the app worker route resolves the port with a deferred adapter so it works before the worker is deployed.
- **ADR-0206** · Accepted · 2026-07-18 · Documents / E-signature — The PAdES platform-seal CMS is built with pkijs over WebCrypto in the Worker (crypto host-side, key a non-extractable CryptoKey), not with a Rust CMS crate in the engine; the pure-Rust pdf-core engine owns the PDF structure and takes the CMS as an opaque blob. Proven end-to-end on workerd.
- **ADR-0207** · Accepted · 2026-07-17 · Discovery — "The discovery search index is a stateful service, not a stateless Container: the per-firm Tantivy keyword index is stored as immutable splits (self-contained segment bundles with a footer) under a per-firm R2 prefix, queried by footer-metadata pruning plus HTTP ranged reads so per-query I/O scales with query selectivity not corpus size, warmed by an NVMe cache in a dedicated always-warm search Worker (apps/search-worker, the ADR-0167 stateful-service shape), with a per-firm split catalog in the FirmDatabase DO as the sole authority the query path enumerates against; the Vectorize semantic half is namespace-partitioned per firm (1024-dim bge-large-en-v1.5 via the existing InferencePort.embed, never flat, ~5M-dim/index ceiling designed against) and fused with BM25 by reciprocal rank fusion; both halves sit behind one swappable SearchIndexPort; the index is a derived cache rebuildable from source, deletion is provable, and large-scale (Container sharding, parallel OCR) is a widening of the same design, not a rewrite."
- **ADR-0208** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Discovery — "The discovery search write pipeline builds ONE split per document version, with the split id derived (content-addressed) from documentId + version so the split's identity and its R2 key are both reproducible from the inputs alone — making a retry overwrite its own object and replace its own catalog row rather than duplicating; publishing is a delete-then-insert of the document's catalog rows in one atomic batch, backed by a partial unique index over the live per-document rows, with the many-document splits a later compaction publishes left outside that constraint; and because neither engine (text extraction, index build) is deployed yet, an engine-not-implemented outcome PARKS the job in a distinct `deferred` state that is acked and re-startable rather than retried, so an undeployed engine never burns a message's attempts and dead-letters a document that was merely early."
- **ADR-0209** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Documentation / Decision register — "ADR-0098, ADR-0099, and ADR-0102 are permanently vacant and are never reissued. All three were reservations that were vacated before anything was written: 0098 and 0099 were the tail of an ADR-0097-0099 block the email-filing draft design reserved, renumbered to ADR-0104-0106 when that line collided with main's 0096/0097; 0102 was the tail of an ADR-0100-0102 block the slice 2-1 design reserved a day before the same renumber independently claimed 0100 and 0101, and its subject — the async bulk-download build job — was built without an ADR of its own and is now covered by ADR-0027, ADR-0082, and ADR-0146. No file for any of the three was ever committed, so nothing was lost; the register is non-contiguous by history, not by defect, and the gap is recorded here rather than closed by renumbering."
- **ADR-0210** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Document Automation — "The document-automation merge is built in-house as a pure TypeScript package, `@magna-carta/document-assembly`, over fflate and fast-xml-parser — the substrate the OOXML metadata inspector and scrubber already use — running in a Worker queue consumer behind a `DocumentAssemblyPort.assemble(templateBytes, mergeContext)` seam, so the substitution layer is replaceable without touching the dictionary, the resolution pipeline, the job model, or the filing path. Rust compiled to WebAssembly is rejected for this slice but not designed out (no candidate crate carries templating semantics, and the workload is not CPU-bound); adopting docxtemplater's MIT core is the real alternative and is declined — a second zip stack beside fflate, image and HTML insertion behind paid modules, and expressions evaluated from template content — while remaining the documented, scoped fallback behind the same port; driving the consented LibreOffice container's mail merge is rejected outright as the only memory-unsafe route and the only one that would push privileged merge values through the one C++ dependency for no fidelity gain, so LibreOffice keeps DOCX-to-PDF and nothing else."
- **ADR-0211** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Document Automation — "Template placeholders are `{{key}}` double-brace text delimiters, and that is the one canonical form once a template is registered. Word MERGEFIELD constructs are always DETECTED and REPORTED back to the person registering the template, but conversion to the brace form is OPT-IN — it runs only on an explicit request, never silently as a side effect of registration — and each confirmed MERGEFIELD-name-to-key mapping accumulates in a per-firm field-mapping library so later imports reuse earlier confirmations instead of re-asking. Content controls (`w:sdt`) stay a named, deferred seam. Normalization runs once at registration, not once per assembly, and stores the normalized package as the version's bytes. A macro-bearing upload is ACCEPTED AND STRIPPED — registration removes `vbaProject.bin` with its content-type entries and relationships and then re-verifies that the stripped package sniffs as a clean macro-free `.docx`, refusing only if it does not — deviating from the recommendation to refuse macro-bearing packages outright. Templates live in their own catalogue versioned exactly like files: `document_template` plus an immutable `document_template_version` mirroring `file` / `file_version`."
- **ADR-0212** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Custodian — "A packet is the unit of custody and an item the unit of description: `custody_packet` carries the number, location, status, owners, depositor, and optional matter link, and a movement always moves a packet; `custody_item` describes one thing inside it and never moves between packets by an edit. Ownership anchors at a Directory entity and is MANDATORY — at least one `custody_owner` link at creation, enforced in the write path — because the catastrophic outcome in safe custody is an unattributable packet, not a misplaced one; the matter link stays nullable. One recursive `custody_location` tree roots optionally at a firm Location, so a bank vault or offsite vendor participates without becoming a `location` row; capacity is out of scope with no column reserved. `POST /movements` is the only state-change path and the ledger is append-only behind BEFORE UPDATE / BEFORE DELETE / replace-guard triggers, with corrections as compensating rows and `occurred_at` separate from `recorded_at`. Custody numbers reuse the matters allocation-ledger idiom, and scanned copies are ordinary File Manager files, with a matterless packet filing onto a per-firm `system` holding matter rather than relaxing `file.matter_id`."
- **ADR-0213** · "Proposed — owner decision pending" · 2026-08-04 · Custodian — "Custodian ships the retention MECHANISM and supplies no policy. What ships: `review_on` and `review_interval_months` as dates a firm sets; `retention_until` as a nullable column a firm sets per packet or item, with no default, no derived value, and no code path that computes one; a `custody_hold` table with kinds legal / discovery / client_instruction / dispute / unclaimed, blocking release and destruction while permitting a location move; a two-person destruction request/approval workflow where requester and approver must differ; and no destruction sweep. One hard refusal is encoded with no API override — an item flagged `intrinsic_value` cannot be destroyed — because no jurisdiction's rules produce 'destroy the client's original will because a timer expired'. Retention periods, will-deposit deadlines, release authority, notification, and escheat treatment are flagged as jurisdiction-specific and therefore owner or verified-catalog decisions, never defaults this design invents; a future catalog would follow the trust-rules pattern, verified against primary legal text, shipped one jurisdiction at a time, with an explicit not-encoded state and never a silent generic fallback. Whether that catalog is wanted at all, and which jurisdiction is encoded first, remains an open owner decision."
- **ADR-0214** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Product posture / Data conversion — "Conversion of user data into the platform is CONSENSUAL AND GUIDED, everywhere it happens. Wherever a conversion of a firm's data takes place or may take place — template import, migration from another practice-management system, format normalization, encoding or schema translation — three rules hold: DETECTION AND REPORTING ARE ALWAYS ON (the platform inspects what it was given and tells the firm what it found, whether or not anything is done about it); CONVERSION IS OPT-IN (a rewrite of the firm's data runs on an explicit request, never as a silent side effect of uploading or importing); and the product OFFERS INPUT, GUIDANCE, AND FEEDBACK at the decision — what will change, what it will become, what cannot be carried across, and the result once it has run. Confirmations a firm gives during a conversion are worth keeping: where a conversion asks the same question repeatedly across a set, the answers accumulate as reusable per-firm mappings so a bulk migration converges on one pass of human confirmation rather than one per item. The posture binds the whole product, not one importer; the document-automation template model's opt-in MERGEFIELD conversion with its per-firm field-mapping library is its first instantiation."
- **ADR-0215** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Documentation / Decision register — "Decision files are validated rather than parsed leniently: the assembler now REFUSES a malformed file instead of rendering a degraded register bullet, and the same validation runs as a governance gate (`pnpm run lint:decisions`) on every pull request so an author finds the problem in their own gate. A file must open with a frontmatter fence, carry only known frontmatter keys, name a non-empty slug, a status beginning with a lifecycle word, and an area, date as YYYY-MM-DD when present, and must yield register text that is neither empty nor a Markdown heading. The whole pending set is read and validated BEFORE any fragment is numbered, so a bad one is a clean no-op rather than a half-consumed assembly, and already-numbered files are validated on every run because a corrupt one would otherwise re-render its damage forever."
- **ADR-0216** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Documents & automation — "The merge-field input interface is built against a typed catalog contract in its own package (`@magna-carta/merge-field-catalog-contract`) with a mock provider over a realistic fixture catalog, and every consuming surface takes a `MergeFieldCatalogProvider` rather than importing the mock — so switching to the live dictionary and template-fields endpoints is an edit to one composition root; the design-system components stay free of domain types, taking structural option shapes that a per-app adapter maps into; and a placeholder detected in an uploaded document is modelled as a PROPOSAL with no applied state, so no surface can render a conversion as something that already happened."
- **ADR-0217** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Document Automation — "How template registration realizes the consensual-guided-data-conversion posture, and where it deliberately differs. Word MERGEFIELD constructs are always detected and reported (name, occurrences, part, the merge-field key each would map to, and whether that key exists for this firm) and converted to the canonical brace form only on an explicit convertMergeFields request; a template that uses them WITHOUT the opt-in is REFUSED rather than registered with the fields left inert, because an unconverted merge field is invisible to the assembly engine and renders Word's frozen cached result — a filed document that looks complete and is wrong, which the absence model cannot catch because there is no placeholder to be absent. Macro removal takes the OPPOSITE default under the same feedback rule: a macro-bearing template is accepted, its VBA project and every declaration referencing it are stripped, and the removal is reported — acting without asking is right there because removing code cannot make a document assert something untrue, while leaving it would run code against privileged data. Macro detection is driven by the content-types map and relationship types rather than file names, and the stripped bytes are re-verified by an independent scan before the version may register, because a stripper vouching for itself is the failure mode the gate exists to catch. The Word-name-to-key mapping is an injectable resolver, the identity mapping today, and is the seam the per-firm field-mapping library plugs into."
- **ADR-0218** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Document Automation — "OWNER DECISION, superseding the document-automation design's recommendation and answering its owner flag F6: the template expression ceiling GROWS from presence/negation/literal-equality to COMPUTED EXPRESSIONS — arithmetic over money and numbers, date math, comparisons, and a catalog of formatting and grammar helpers. The non-negotiable line that survives untouched is that an expression is NEVER executable template-supplied code: not code we sandbox, not code we lint, but code the grammar cannot express. The language is total and closed by construction — no user-defined functions, no property traversal (a merge key such as matter.number is ONE atom, not a traversal, resolved by exact match against a Map, so the __proto__ family is a missing key rather than a reachable prototype chain), no indexing, no assignment, no recursion, no template includes, no regular expressions, no author-written iteration ({{#each}} iterates data; expressions do not iterate), and no ambient authority — no clock, no randomness, no environment, no I/O, no database handle. Money arithmetic is integer-exact over amount_minor with NO floating point anywhere and no currency conversion; DIVISION IS NOT AN OPERATOR, replaced by three named helpers whose rounding is explicit — divideExact (refuses when inexact), divideRounded/applyRate/percentOf (banker's rounding, half to even, through the money package's roundLedger), and allocate (parts that sum exactly to the input). Date math is over calendar dates in the MATTER'S OFFICE time zone through the existing timeZoneForLocation seam; there is no instant type and therefore no time-of-day arithmetic, and no business-day or court-deadline helper ships. The helper catalog is a FIXED ALLOWLIST compiled into our source. Static type checking happens AT REGISTRATION: a type error is a registration error with a part, paragraph, and character offset — never an assembly surprise. Resource bounds (expression length, tree depth, step budget, numeric magnitude, loop and expansion caps) live in one limits module, and the security claim carries a standing FUZZING OBLIGATION: structure-aware and byte-level generation, a committed denied-construct corpus that must fail at PARSE OR CHECK rather than at evaluation, an arithmetic oracle, and one bound-hitting test per limit."
- **ADR-0219** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Supply-chain security — "Replace the office-crypto dependency with an in-house MS-OFFCRYPTO package decryption loop in crates/decryption-core, retiring both quick-xml advisories (RUSTSEC-2026-0194 and -0195) from the tree rather than accepting them for another review cycle, and closing three panic-on-untrusted-input paths the carrier carried. decryption-core already owned the agile verifier, the iterative hash, block-key derivation, its own descriptor parser and the compound-file validation; office-crypto's remaining contribution was the segment loop and the session-key unwrap, roughly 120 lines against primitives already in the workspace. ECMA-376 standard encryption is ported alongside agile so no capability is silently dropped; its legacy RC4 CryptoAPI variant is refused by a named error exactly as before, and office-crypto's Word-97 binary RC4 path is confirmed to have been unreachable from this adapter. Sixteen crates leave the graph and none are added."
- **ADR-0220** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Document Automation — "Owner decisions amending the two registration behaviours ADR-0211 recorded and ADR-0217 realized. (1) A template carrying UNCONVERTED Word MERGEFIELD constructs is REGISTERED, not refused: each construct is recorded on the field manifest as an unconverted-merge-field entry — the Word name, the part it sits in, its occurrence count, the merge key it would map to, and the FROZEN CACHED TEXT it will render — which makes it visible to the absence model rather than invisible to it. Registration reports the list, the field inspector shows it, and every assembly against such a template reports it again in the response and on the provenance row (names only, never the frozen text) and completes `succeeded_with_absences`, so the choice is informed at both registration and assembly. Conversion stays opt-in exactly as merged. (2) MACRO STRIPPING BECOMES OPT-IN, restoring consent parity with the merge-field conversion: detection always reports, the strip runs only on an explicit stripMacros request flag, and a macro-bearing upload WITHOUT the opt-in is refused with guidance naming both remedies — opt in to the removal, or upload a macro-free file. A template is never registered carrying live macros and never stripped silently. The adversarial strip machinery and its independent re-verification over the bytes that will be stored are unchanged; only the trigger moves. (3) The per-firm FIELD-MAPPING LIBRARY behind MergeFieldNameResolver is built rather than deferred: a firm-scoped `document_template_field_mapping` table of confirmed Word-name-to-merge-key mappings, written when a conversion confirms them and read once per registration to resolve later detections, listed and deletable under templates:manage."
- **ADR-0221** · Accepted · 2026-08-04 · Custodian — "Custody labels are rendered IN-HOUSE as Code 128 barcodes on an SVG label sheet, and the barcode carries the object's IDENTIFIER HANDLE rather than its custody number — a number is a string a firm can re-render, and a scan must resolve through no lookup rule that can change under labels already stuck to boxes in a safe. QR is deliberately not shipped: a linear symbology is a published table plus a modulo-103 checksum, while a QR encoder is Reed-Solomon over GF(256) plus mask evaluation for capacity a one-handle label does not need. Separately, custody REMINDERS resolve through ONE collection point that serves both review dates and overdue check-outs, and it is a QUERY, not a scheduler: no cron entry, no queue, no per-firm timer. The register's reports read it today; when the Notifications registry lands, delivery subscribes to that same function rather than growing a second definition of what is due."
- **ADR-0222** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Document Automation — "Realization decisions taken while building the template expression language, each an OWNER-REVIEWABLE DEFAULT applied as the design recommended rather than a new direction: an unresolved CONDITION fails the assembly naming the field and the total form to write instead, never treat-as-false (G1); banker's rounding is the only mode a template author sees, through the money package's roundLedger, with roundTax and roundInvoiceLine deliberately absent (G2); no business-day or court-deadline helper ships (G3); no aggregate over a repeat collection (G4, superseded the following day by `template-expression-aggregates`, which admits them on the owner's answer); no pronoun or verb-agreement helper, because choose covers it visibly and it would require recording a gender attribute on a party (G5). Plus six choices the design left open: an {{#each}} over allocate binds each part as loop.value, since a split's parts have no field names of their own; a conditional closes with either {{/if}} or {{/unless}} while a repeat closes only with {{/each}}; a marker in a table row's FIRST CELL makes the ROW the unit even when the same marker would otherwise read as a paragraph block; the declared tree-depth bound counts NODES, guarded separately from the parser's own recursion, so that pretty-printing an expression cannot change whether it is legal; braces are not expressible inside a text literal, because the placeholder delimiters own them; and matter.parties — role plus Directory display name, one batched read joined across directory_person and directory_organization — is the first list-valued dictionary source, answering the design's open question about where a collection comes from."
- **ADR-0223** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Worker / job coordination — "Live job progress is pushed over the Durable Objects hibernation WebSocket API on the coordinator objects themselves — not the Cloudflare Agents SDK, whose addition over the native primitive is ergonomic (setState broadcast, typed RPC decorators) and does not justify a pre-1.0 dependency in the coordination layer of a privileged system. The polling endpoints REMAIN, and push and poll resolve a job through one shared function per subsystem so the fallback cannot drift from the pushed answer. The upgrade traverses the chokepoint like any other route and the object is addressed by the SESSION firm id, so subscribing is scoped exactly as polling already is. A pushed signed URL is minted against the bucket the SUBSCRIBER's session resolved, carried on the socket attachment rather than re-derived from a queue message, so a woken object signs through the same seam a poll would have."
- **ADR-0224** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · CI / build cadence — "While the account's GitHub Actions minutes are at their budget limit, the gate's post-merge tiers are suspended — the nightly whole-repo sweep, the push-to-main run, and the whole-repo lint tier — and the merge bar moves forward onto the branch: full local verification by the agent (whole-repo `pnpm check` plus `vp test`, stated in the pull request) plus orchestrator review. The pull-request tier keeps running in full at one-hop lint scope, so no change merges unverified; what is given up is the repeated post-merge proof and the unbounded lint closure. Every suspension carries the marker `TEMPORARY CI AUSTERITY 2026-08-05` so the reversion is one grep, and the window ends with one dispatched whole-repo gate plus sweep on `main` to catch what it missed."
- **ADR-0225** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Trust accounting — "The trust sub-ledger is a THREE-LEVEL tree — the trust account's client-liability root, a client node per trust client, and a matter LEAF under it — and postings land on matter leaves only. Both readings the work needs are then a direct read rather than a derivation: the bar's client ledger is the client node's roll-up, and a matter's balance, which the close-precondition hook asks for, is its own leaf. The tier order is the least reversible decision in the subsystem because createAccount refuses a parent that already carries postings, so a client tier cannot be inserted above matter leaves that have already posted — the tree is chosen once, before the first row, or rebuilt. And trust is built NOW, before the pre-alpha per-firm baseline collapse and before the first alpha firm, because that window is the only period in which a wrong tree shape is absorbed into a baseline rather than migrated against live client-money data; the same gate makes a long-lived trust branch a blocker on the collapse, so the schema lands in few, large migrations that merge promptly."
- **ADR-0226** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Worker / API contract — "A route whose request body is raw bytes declares `idempotency: \"optional\"` and honours the Idempotency-Key in its own handler, against the bytes it received — it must NOT declare `idempotency: \"required\"`. The chokepoint's required-idempotency gate fingerprints a request by reading its body through Hono's body cache, which caches the decoded TEXT; a later `arrayBuffer()` is then re-encoded from that text, so a `.msg` or any 8-bit message would reach the handler UTF-8-mangled and be stored as the firm's verbatim original in that corrupted form. The handler-side form keeps the same guarantees (replay returns the stored response, a key reused with different bytes is refused) with the fingerprint taken over the received bytes, and the derived job id makes every layer below converge on a retry whether or not a key was sent."
- **ADR-0227** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Email filing / capture — "DKIM verification for captured mail is built as a pure-TypeScript RFC 6376 verifier over WebCrypto inside the platform, resolving selector keys over DNS-over-HTTPS — NOT inside the email-core parse engine, which keeps its parse-only, network-free boundary. The verdict is INFORMATIONAL and never a gate: no message is dropped, quarantined, refused, or auto-filed on the strength of it, which follows the subsystem's conversion-consent posture that the platform records facts and a person decides. The three-value verdict enum is not widened; every inconclusive outcome (unsigned mail, a .msg export with no signable original, a rotated-away selector, a resolver outage) stays `unverified` and is distinguished by a separate reason column, and a key that cannot be found is recorded as unverified rather than as the RFC's PERMFAIL, because archived correspondence routinely outlives the selector that signed it. A pass additionally records the signing domain; a fail records none, since a claimed d= the cryptography did not support is not an attribution."
- **ADR-0228** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Custodian — "Gated release ships as a precondition on the existing movement write path plus four columns on the append-only ledger row, enforced by BEFORE INSERT triggers rather than by a CHECK constraint. A `released` movement must name who collected the property and carry the authority the release is made on, how the collector was identified, and which member made that check; two-signatory release is a per-firm policy, off by default, and the two signatories must differ. The trigger, not the CHECK, is the load-bearing choice: a CHECK would require rebuilding the table and copying every existing release through it, and every release recorded before the gate existed was recorded honestly under the rules that then applied and carries no capture — making those rows pass would mean writing an assertion nobody made into a ledger whose only claim is that nobody could do exactly that. The authority itself is free text the firm writes and no platform vocabulary, because what document suffices to release an original is jurisdiction-specific and remains an owner decision."
- **ADR-0229** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Document Automation — "The document-automation design's open question 3 is answered — THE ASSET DECLARES THE BOX: a letterhead logo carries the printed width the office set, on the office, and the height is derived from the image's own proportions at render time, so no template restates a measurement in a unit no word processor exposes and no document can print a stretched mark. The image-format policy is PNG and JPEG only, decided by the FIRST BYTES rather than by a declared content type or a file name, enforced at the write and re-checked at assembly; SVG is refused because an image format that is a document is a markup and script surface inside a package we assert carries neither, EMF and WMF because they are replayed record streams and the richest parser surface in the format family, and GIF/BMP/TIFF/WebP because they widen the content-type set for worse reader compatibility than a printed mark needs. A firm's own fields bind as `custom.<customField_ handle>` derived per firm at read time, never by name, and — because a handle's alphabet includes the hyphen, which is also the subtraction operator — the expression lexer matches a custom key by its EXACT FIXED LENGTH rather than greedily, so `custom.customField_a-b…c - 5` is a subtraction and `custom.customField_a-b…c` is one atom with no case where an author's spacing decides which. An image is a value type with no rendering and no expression type at all: `{{key:format}}` on one is a registration failure and `{{#if key}}` over one is an unknown merge field."
- **ADR-0230** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Document Automation — "Owner flag G4 is answered: aggregates over a repeat collection are ADMITTED, on exactly the terms the original exclusion named. `sum`, `minimum`, and `maximum` take a LITERAL FIELD PATH of a list-valued merge key — one lexical atom, resolved by exact match, never an expression — and their cost is bounded by the data on the same terms a repeating block's is: the collection arrives from the batched read it already cost, the items-per-repeat cap applies unchanged, and every element is charged a step against the budget the whole document shares. `count` is deliberately NOT repeated among them, because `count(matter.parties)` already answers it and a field-path spelling could differ only by skipping items whose field is absent, which is a filter. Element types are checked at REGISTRATION — `sum` folds number and money, `minimum` and `maximum` fold number, money, and date — and the result carries the element's type, with money folding through the same addition the `+` operator uses. RECOMMENDED AND FLAGGED for owner review: an aggregate over an EMPTY collection is an ABSENCE (unresolved, naming the collection) rather than a zero, uniformly across all three helpers; an item carrying nothing for the field likewise leaves the answer unresolved rather than being skipped. Named `minimum` and `maximum` rather than `min` and `max`, because the repository spells names out."
- **ADR-0231** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Trust accounting — "A trust movement writes a second row beside its ledger entry — an append-only `trust_movement` record carrying the source or payee, the purpose, the payment METHOD as a modelled value, the reference, who entered the record, and who authorized it — because the per-client ledger the trust rules require shows facts the double-entry ledger has no column for, and because a jurisdiction rule cannot forbid a payment method the engine cannot see. Its column combinations are a database CHECK rather than composer discipline, so a record cannot misdescribe the movement it sits beside. And a reallocation between two matters of the SAME client posts NO bank line: the money never moved at the bank, both leaves sit inside the client-side total, so the book and client sides still agree by construction — while the alternative, an offsetting pair of bank lines, would manufacture two items that could never clear a statement and would make every future reconciliation of that account wrong."
- **ADR-0232** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Custodian — "A custody stocktake observes and reports; it never writes the register. The design's own words for slice 4·2·4 were that every discrepancy resolution is a movement, never an edit — this decision settles what that means at the boundary: the reconciliation names the corrective movement a person would record as a PROPOSAL on a report, and a person records it through the existing single movement write path or does not. Nothing on the stocktake path writes `custody_movement`, `custody_packet`, or `custody_item`, and a source-scan fitness function holds that boundary against call sites nobody has written yet. Sighting rows are evidentiary and therefore append-only at the database, with the same three triggers the movement ledger carries plus a fourth refusing an observation against a count that has already closed. A closed count freezes its findings on the stocktake row so the number a firm reports cannot drift as the register is put right; a count begun and never finished closes as abandoned, freezing nothing. No new movement kind was added, so the ledger table was not rebuilt."
- **ADR-0233** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Document automation / merge dictionary — "Directory role merge sources are a small PLATFORM-DECLARED set of roles (opposingParty, opposingCounsel, guardian, witness), matched to the free-text `matter_party.role` by normalizing both to lowercase alphanumerics — not derived per firm from `directory_party_role`, whose names are firm-typed free text that cannot be trusted to lex as a merge key. A role that matches more than one link resolves to NOTHING with a reason naming the candidate count, rather than picking one; the many case is addressed by a `<role>.all` collection walked by the repeat machinery that already exists. Every role is answered from the single party-collection read the merge context already issues, so cost tracks sources rather than roles. Resolution's reason travels as a new `MergeContext.absenceReasons` / `AssemblyAbsence.reason` channel and is deliberately NOT persisted on the assembly provenance row."
- **ADR-0234** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Trust accounting — "A matter whose client trust ledger still holds money, or carries an active disputed-funds hold, does not close — a hard block with no override, because a closed matter holding somebody else's money is the condition that produces unclaimed funds and the firm that closed it is the one that answers for them. Two shapes follow from making that block honest. The matter shell does not learn what money is: the close hook takes rules from the composition root rather than importing the subsystem that owns one, so the matter lifecycle keeps no dependency on the financial core. And because a read cannot be atomic with the write that follows it on a platform with no interactive transactions, a rule may also contribute a GUARD that rides the close batch and re-asks its question where the answer is the committed one — a statement that either matches nothing or violates a constraint, never one that stores a row. Trust's guard aims at its own node table's kind CHECK, so a deposit landing between the check and the commit aborts the entire close, its audit row, its event, and its idempotency record together, instead of closing a matter over money that arrived a moment earlier."
- **ADR-0235** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Custodian — "The owner ratified all seven custodian shape flags as recommended, and answered the eighth: C4 — the location tree depth cap is eight, enforced as a write-path refusal. C5 — ownership form on the owner link is a firm-configurable label validated against a per-firm catalog, not a platform enum of tenancy forms. C6 — the matter close path gets an advisory-only notice when custody packets are linked; close is never blocked on custody. C7 — the platform never ships default retention periods; a firm sets retention per packet or item, and the outright refusal to destroy an intrinsic-value item stays absolute. C9 — declared item values are ordinary business numbers, not protected columns. C10 — two permissions stay two; destruction approval is gated by the two-person workflow, holds, and the intrinsic-value refusal rather than by a third key. C11 — moving an item between packets does not re-assert the destination's owner set; ownership is a packet-level fact and the move itself is on the append-only ledger. C8 — the will-on-death statutory engine proceeds design-first: the jurisdiction-keyed catalog shape ships so deadlines refuse rather than guess, and Nevada is the first jurisdiction, gated on verified primary-source research (NRS) before anything is encoded."
- **ADR-0236** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Trust accounting — "The owner ratified T12 as shipped: a matter whose client trust ledger holds a non-zero balance or an active disputed-funds hold refuses to close, with no override path. The refusal names the amount, the account, the held portion, and the movements that clear it. An unclaimed-funds workflow, if built later, becomes a real exit rather than an override. In the same review the owner directed that two-person disbursement authorization be built now: a disbursement requires a second member's approval before the movement posts, with the approver recorded as the authorizer — the recorded_by / authorized_by split the movements slice laid down in anticipation. Design specifics the owner did not state (threshold versus all disbursements, single-trust-manager firms) ship as recommended defaults, flagged."
- **ADR-0237** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Document automation / merge-field catalogue — "The merge-field catalogue scales to hundreds of fields as a CROSS PRODUCT of two small registries — subjects (the things a document talks about) and facets (the facts about a subject of that kind) — materialized into the same flat, exactly-matched key list every consumer already takes, rather than as hundreds of hand-written rows. A facet is a different FACT and gets a key; a format is the same fact rendered differently and gets a suffix, which is what keeps a catalogue of hundreds from being a catalogue of thousands: structured `name` and `address` value types carry the renderings, formats stay a closed named set with no arguments and no chaining, and composition is the expression language's job. Presentation metadata (label, group, subject, prominence, synonyms, examples) moves ONTO the dictionary entry so the merge-field catalog contract becomes its projection rather than a second catalogue. Keys are permanent, defaults are frozen, absence may only become more visible, and withdrawal is deprecation with an immutable per-version field-binding index making impact answerable. Facts the platform does not record — matter staffing, court/case identity, estate identity, party capacity — land as their owning AREA'S records with merge fields as a consumer, each costing exactly one batched read, so the source count goes from six to twelve while the field count goes from twenty-nine to roughly two hundred and sixty."
- **ADR-0238** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Documents & automation / Encryption — "A protected merge field resolves through the existing protected-field chokepoint and nothing else: the assembling member must hold the same permission a direct decrypt requires, and every resolution commits the same decrypt-audit row a direct decrypt commits — same event, same subject, same table, no assembly-specific reason code. A member without the permission gets a declared absence naming the field and the permission, never a value, never a silent blank, and never a failed assembly. The permission is re-derived from the firm's database when the job runs rather than captured on the queue message, so a revoked grant bites; the decrypt count is bounded by the manifest rather than batched, because a sealed value is bound to its own row by its AAD and commits its own audit record, and any bulk form would have to bypass the chokepoint to exist. The protected merge sources are the client's Directory identifiers — the values the platform actually holds encrypted; a protected CUSTOM field is a separate decision needing schema that does not exist."
- **ADR-0239** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Core domain / Matter assets — "A matter-attached asset register: an asset row is ONE MATTER'S ASSERTION about one thing the client or estate owns, so it roots at the matter (NOT NULL) where Custodian roots at the Directory entity — the same house in a divorce and in an estate are different records with different dates, values, and privilege, and no cross-matter thing-identity exists. Typing is three layers with one job each: a CLOSED platform class enum (real property, financial account, vehicle, firearm, business interest, insurance policy, receivable, digital asset, personal property, other) because the class decides which structured columns, which merge subject, and which index exist; a per-firm type catalog UNDER a class following the shipped custody-item-type shape; and the custom-fields engine widened by one target value for the firm's own facts. Structured attributes live in one one-to-one table per class that has structure, and none for a class that does not. Every number is dated: valuations are an APPEND-ONLY history with a basis, corrected by compensating rows and enforced by RAISE(ABORT) triggers, with no current-value column anywhere — an encumbrance's balance is a valuation row naming the encumbrance, so net equity as of any date is one read. Identifiers split on a principled line: public-record ones (parcel number, vehicle identification number, firearm serial) are plaintext and searchable, credential-adjacent ones (account, policy, certificate, tax) are ciphertext through protected() with only the last four readable and no merge facet at all — and credentials themselves (seed phrases, private keys, combinations) are REFUSED outright in any column, because a protected column is decryptable and a seed phrase IS the asset. Firearms are recorded like cars and carry no transfer law: a standing non-jurisdictional handling notice, a regulated-category field that is asserted and never derived, and a jurisdiction catalog that stays empty until primary-source research fills it. Documents are a many-to-many link over ordinary matter files (no holding matter needed, unlike custody scans); a custody item link joins the two registers without either copying the other's facts. Asset classes enter the merge catalogue as ELEVEN COLLECTION-ONLY subjects riding ONE new source, because a scalar slot on a matter with two houses would be an absence every time. Permissions are matterAssets:read / matterAssets:manage bound to the matter."
- **ADR-0240** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Calendar and docketing — "The case calendar is DERIVED WHERE THE FACT ALREADY LIVES AND RECORDED WHERE THE FACT IS BORN — a hybrid drawn on that one line. The eleven per-firm tables that already carry timestamped business events (email filing, document assembly, files, time, costs, custody movements and review dates, trust movements, conflicts checks, and the matter's own lifecycle) are FEDERATED at read time through a source registry, not projected into a matter_event table: no write cost, no backfill, no drift, and no second copy of a privileged fact — the timeline cannot disagree with the custody register because it IS the custody register. Litigation events and deadlines, which exist in no table today, get their own two recorded tables. The audit log is explicitly REJECTED as the substrate because it records when a row was written, not when the thing happened, and the owner asked what transpired. Federation is affordable because the read count scales with the REGISTRY (twelve declared sources, one batch, LIMIT pageSize+1 each, a k-way merge in memory) and not with the firm's data; one cursor serves every source because lexicographic order over canonical ISO-8601 IS chronological order, which makes a civil date and an instant directly comparable as text and is guarded by a fitness test asserting canonical instant precision. Deadlines are first class and separate from events — a due instant, a satisfied/vacated/superseded state with a reason, and a source (member, court order, or a verified statutory catalog) — and OVERDUE IS DERIVED AT READ TIME, never written: the platform reports what it can see and never asserts that a firm missed anything. The platform REFUSES deadline arithmetic outright — no business-day math, no court-holiday calendars, no service-method offsets, no cascade when a trigger moves — until a verified jurisdiction-keyed catalog exists, and even then the catalog PROPOSES and a member CONFIRMS, with the entry key and version frozen on the row so an amended statute surfaces as a review item rather than silently moving a date. Time zones are per ROW and required, not per office: a Nevada firm's hearing in a California court is in the court's zone, which is also why location.time_zone finally lands and timeZoneForLocation stops returning UTC. Ethical walls do not exist and are not invented here; every source's statement builder instead takes a required visible-matter predicate composed into SQL BEFORE the LIMIT (today: exclude the platform holding matter), so a wall is one clause in one place when it is built, and a post-filter — which under-fills pages and turns page size into an existence oracle — is structurally impossible."
- **ADR-0241** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · CI / proof strategy — "The gate's type-aware lint scope is now bounded by measured COST rather than by graph distance. Every trigger walks the full transitive dependent closure; the pull-request tier additionally defers `fan-out sinks` — packages whose type program is at least half the workspace's lintable files, and which are in scope only as a dependent — to the post-merge tier. On this repository the rule selects exactly `@magna-carta/worker` (1,261 of 1,669 lintable files, 75.6%), the job that measured 43% of a pull-request gate run's bill. The one-hop depth cap it replaces is retired as a cost lever: the worker depends directly on 39 of 61 packages, so it sat one hop from nearly every change. Soundness is asserted mechanically, not argued: `pnpm run lint:affected-scope` proves, for a simulated change to every workspace package, that the changed package is always linted on its own pull request and that the pull-request and post-merge scopes together cover the full dependent closure."
- **ADR-0242** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Email filing / capture — "Auto-file rules are a TYPED, MATTER-BOUND, REVOCABLE DELEGATION over the shipped capture pipeline — three rule kinds (thread continuation, correspondent-to-matter, exact matter number in subject), no expression language, no regex, no keyword, and no sender-domain kind (a domain is one-to-many by construction, so its own condition guarantees the ambiguity guardrail suppresses it exactly where a firm has the volume to want it). A matter-bound capture address is recognized as ALREADY an auto-file rule and is deliberately not re-expressed as a row: it proves that ROUTING and PROMOTION are separable questions the platform has already answered separately, since even a hand-minted credential naming a matter still lands its mail staged. The consent verdict follows ADR-0214: an auto-file rule lands a STAGED link with the matter chosen and its reasons attached, per-rule opt-in to direct filing, behind a firm policy that is off until an administrator enables it — a ceiling, so no member can opt the firm into unattended filing; on the catch-all path a staging rule is not toothless, because it converts 'captured, unrouted, someone must find the matter' into 'staged on the right matter, one click from filed'. The guardrails are the design and fail closed: ambiguity is computed STRUCTURALLY (how many distinct matters the deciding evidence names) rather than from the ranking's score-tie `ambiguous` flag, so a future tuning change to how a suggestion list is ORDERED for a human cannot silently change which messages file themselves; promotion traverses the shipped filing-time conflict hook, so the conflicts engine will protect automatic and manual filing identically with no second integration; a rule fires only into a matter its creator could access at creation AND still can at fire time (suspend, never delete), while the 'the mail's recipients could see it' test is REJECTED as unsatisfiable and directionally wrong — correspondents are external parties the platform has no account for, and inbound mail from opposing counsel is exactly the mail worth filing. The DKIM precondition is a RULE INPUT, not a change to the informational verdict posture: the verdict gates the robot, not the record — nothing is dropped, hidden, or degraded, only the machine's permission to act unaccompanied — and it binds asymmetrically, requiring an intact original plus a `pass` with a matching domain only for the one kind whose condition is an identity claim, which means correspondent rules structurally never fire on forwarded captures. A rule never contradicts a human (no firing on a message already filed or previously unlinked by a person), never fires into a closed matter, and trips a per-rule circuit breaker on firing rate or undo rate. Every outcome including suppressions is recorded in an evaluation ledger holding identifiers and enums only; undo is the existing unlink route unchanged, and undoing an automatic link is the feedback that suspends the rule."
- **ADR-0243** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Billing — "A DRAFT INVOICE IS A WORKING DOCUMENT AND A FINALIZED INVOICE IS AN EVIDENTIARY FINANCIAL RECORD, and the transition between them is the only interesting moment in the subsystem: in one batch a number is drawn from an allocation ledger, the accounts-receivable entry posts, every claimed time and cost entry locks to billed, and per-(invoice, matter) balance rows are created with a bounds CHECK that makes over-application structurally impossible. That CHECK is the answer to the trust subsystem's unanswered T4: an earned-fee transfer requires a FINALIZED invoice with sufficient UNPAID BALANCE ATTRIBUTABLE TO THAT MATTER, named as an explicit application, with no override — enforced not by the pre-read but by the balance CHECK riding the same batch as the trust entries, exactly the layering trust already uses for its no-negative floor. The precondition is implemented in billing and requires NO CHANGE to the trust package, which is the test that the seam sits in the right place; billing never composes a trust entry, never writes a trust table, and never reimplements a trust rule. Claims and lines are SEPARATE TABLES — a partial unique index on the active claim is the concurrency control (a guarded UPDATE cannot be, because a zero-row UPDATE is not an error in SQLite, the same lesson the matter-number allocation row encodes) — which also makes a flat fee representable as N claims to zero lines, and a consolidated bill as N claims to one. SENT IS NOT A STATUS: delivery is append-only rows, because a re-send is not a change to a financial record, and making the frozen row mutable for its least load-bearing field is how an append-only table stops being one. Finalization POSTS to the ledger rather than deriving accounts receivable, and that is settled empirically rather than by preference — the shipped trust transfer's second leg already credits accounts receivable, so a derived model would leave a growing negative asset equal to every fee ever taken from trust; the invoice tables are the subledger and a fitness query proves the tie-out. TAXES TAKE THE JURISDICTION-REFUSING POSTURE: mechanism only, per-firm configured rates, no taxability catalog and no default, because the trust analysis's own three-bucket asymmetry classifies a tax rate as a DATA FACT and a wrong silence states a wrong fact. The genuinely hard case is stated rather than solved: a credit note after the fee has already left trust is a FIRM-FUNDS question — the trust ledger is not rewound, the credit beyond what is applied becomes an open client credit, and the platform names the three real exits (refund from operating, return to trust as two linked entries, apply to a future invoice) and moves no money on its own."
- **ADR-0244** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Discovery — "THE OBJECT DISCOVERY WAS MISSING IS THE CORPUS, NOT AN ENGINE: search-core, the split format, the SearchIndexPort, the whole enqueue-coordinator-consumer write pipeline, OCR, email parsing, PDF glyph reading, and five-algorithm hashing all shipped dormant, and every deferred discovery feature — filters, tagging, threading, near-duplicates, privilege log, production — is a function over one object nobody had built: the REVIEW SET, a matter-scoped corpus filled by recorded LOADS whose REVIEW DOCUMENTS are pointers, never copies, attributed to a RECORD CUSTODIAN. The naming collision with the shipped Custodian register of physical originals is resolved mechanically rather than by convention: nothing anywhere is a bare `custodian` — the physical register keeps `custody*` and its `custodyOwner`, discovery gets `recordCustodian` / `record_custodian` in two words always, and a source scan enforces both directions, because two words read naturally to a litigator and cannot be confused by anyone reading either the code or the screen. DEDUPLICATION NEVER DESTROYS ATTRIBUTION — the duplicate row is kept, marked non-primary, and carries an occurrence row naming its custodian and its load, because 'which custodians held this document' is asked under oath and 'we deleted that' is not an answer; what dedup buys is that only the primary is extracted, indexed, and embedded. EVERY DOCUMENT ENDS IN EXACTLY ONE OF TWO TERMINAL STATES, processed or excepted, with a named code for every non-success and a reconciliation assertion that loaded equals processed plus excepted — the honest exception report is the artifact that converts 'we produced everything we could read' from an assertion into a document. A new pure-Rust dual-target `crates/discovery-core` owns format identification, native extraction, normalization, the passage chunker moved out of TypeScript, hashing and the near-duplicate fingerprint, so web and desktop produce byte-identical passages and therefore identical splits and reproducible evidentiary hashes; OCR and LibreOffice stay Containers, and NO NEW C/C++ DEPENDENCY IS INTRODUCED. The opaque-archive rule is narrowed inside the discovery load path only, under five guards checked before the first decompressed byte, because a production received from an opposing party arrives as a zip and a discovery subsystem that cannot open one cannot ingest an external production at all. The index scope key widens from matter to CORPUS now, while the engine is dormant and no split has ever been published, because the split's R2 key is derived from it and a rename later is a rewrite of every object; the two corpora index the same document twice on purpose, because provable wholesale deletion of a clawed-back production is worth more than the storage on a derived cache. SEMANTIC INDEXING IS OFF BY DEFAULT per review set, and the earlier spec's Vectorize ceiling is CORRECTED — 20,000,000 vectors per index, not ~5,000 — which changes the arithmetic but not the partitioned-never-flat conclusion. Scale is stated in numbers rather than adjectives: one small production is 10,000 documents, the review-document table becomes the largest in the firm database by an order of magnitude, and the per-review-set ceiling is REFUSED ABOVE BY NAME rather than degraded, because the alternative is a firm discovering at 400,000 documents that its 10 GB database is full, which is an outage with no good exit."
- **ADR-0245** · Accepted · 2026-08-05 · Matters — "The matter close path gains a second, separate extension hook — a close ADVISORY that returns notices on the successful close response and has no arm on which it can refuse — rather than reusing the close-precondition hook with a non-blocking severity flag. Custody registers the first advisory: closing a matter surfaces the physical originals the firm still holds against it and never blocks. The advisory carries a count and nothing identifying, because closing a matter requires no custody permission."
- **ADR-0246** · Proposed · 2026-08-05 · Trust accounting / Reconciliation — "The three-way reconciliation ships as a STORED, IMMUTABLE ATTESTATION over a HUMAN-CONFIRMED matching, and it is allowed to be red. Flags T9, T10, and T11 of the trust-accounting analysis ship as its recommended defaults: the proof is stored and frozen at the close rather than re-derived at read time (T9); the bank side is member-entered, with a CSV import added as a consensual guided conversion under ADR-0214 rather than a bank API integration (T10, extending the recommendation, which named import only as a seam); and 'signed' means an attestation by a named authenticated member recorded on the row, behind a seam a hash chain or the signing engine can be bound to later (T11). Four structural choices carry the design. (1) NOTHING MATCHES AUTOMATICALLY — the matcher ranks bounded candidates and prints the reasons, and a match row exists only because a member named both ends; a wrong automatic match makes the totals tie out while the ledger and the bank disagree about which events happened, which is the discrepancy the exercise exists to find, so there is deliberately no composer that takes a suggestion. (2) The book side of a match is a LEDGER ENTRY, not a trust_movement: an earned-fee transfer out of trust records a trust_transfer and no movement row at all, so a movement-keyed match could never clear one and every such transfer would sit outstanding forever. (3) A PROOF THAT DOES NOT BALANCE IS RECORDED, NOT REFUSED — the close freezes which leg disagrees and by how much, with no plug figure and no rounding to agreement, because a close that refused an unbalanced period would leave firms with a year of periods open (the same as no record) and would make the platform the judge of when a firm is allowed to have a problem. (4) The freeze runs ONE WAY: a closed reconciliation refuses every further write, so a backdated reversal moves the live proof and never the attestation, and a period redone later is a new reconciliation superseding the old. Both ledger legs are computed from dated ledger rows rather than from the stored account_balance, so a period reproduces; book and client therefore agree by construction while the composition guard holds, which makes a book-to-client disagreement a report that something reached the ledger without going through the guard."
- **ADR-0247** · Accepted · 2026-08-06 · Cross-cutting — "The owner ratified the four highest-gating flags from the five merged subsystem designs, each as recommended. H1 — matter staffing, court/case records, and estate identity become platform tables with structured columns, not per-firm custom fields, unlocking the merge-field catalog's remaining build slices. D3 — the search-index scope key widens from matter to corpus now, while the index engine is dormant and no split has ever been published, so discovery review sets can later take their own index scope without a migration. L3 — the platform never computes a deadline from a court rule without a verified per-jurisdiction rules catalog, and even then proposes for a person to confirm; the calendar shows what a person recorded. F1 — auto-filed email lands as a staged link by default, with per-rule opt-in to direct filing behind a firm-level switch that ships off. B1 is ratified by the owner's stated silence-as-ratification: an earned-fee transfer hard-requires a finalized invoice with sufficient unpaid matter balance, which is already the shipped trust posture and the billing design's assumption."
- **ADR-0248** · Accepted · 2026-08-06 · Matter assets — "A matter asset's full account, policy, certificate, or tax number is sealed under the EXISTING sensitive-identifier decrypt permission (`directory:viewSensitiveIdentifiers`) rather than under a third `matterAssets:*` key. The asset design's permission answer is a two-key pair — `matterAssets:read` and `matterAssets:manage` — with the protected read 'separately gated by the existing decrypt permission through the protected() chokepoint'; it does not name which existing key that is, and the two candidates (reuse the directory key, or mint a third asset key) are a real choice. Reuse wins: it is the same class of secret about the same people, a firm that has decided who may open a client's account number has not decided that twice, and a second key would be seeded carelessly or left unseeded — which is how a gate stops meaning anything. No blind index is declared on the column, deliberately."
- **ADR-0249** · Proposed · 2026-08-06 · AI / Inference — "Magnus — the assistant a firm member talks to — is built as the FIRST CONSUMER of the existing inference chokepoint and gets NO PORT OF ITS OWN: a MagnusModelPort is refused because the confidentiality guarantees are four literal fields in one function, and a second port is a second place they can silently be wrong (caching on, logging on, firm metadata caller-supplied). The single change to the shipped package is ONE ADDITIVE operation, generateConversation, taking a platform-vocabulary message array plus a structurally separate platform-authored systemPrompt — flattening a transcript into the existing prompt string is refused because it destroys the role boundary and puts provider formatting in the caller. A TRANSCRIPT IS A FIRM RECORD: per-firm D1 through the firm-scoped data-access layer, retained like correspondence, author-and-administrator visible, never client-visible, member-deletable with an audited delete, registered in the PII registry with conversation-level deletion as the erasure instrument and free text inside a turn explicitly out of the structured registry's scope — and there is deliberately NO per-conversation off-the-record mode, because the question why was that one off the record is worse than any answer. Turn content stays in D1 under HARD CAPS (16 KiB member, 64 KiB Magnus) that are what make the not-bulk claim true rather than a tuning knob, and the conversation window sent to the model is capped at 20 turns / 128 KiB with truncation SURFACED to the member, because silent truncation produces a confident answer built on a premise the member believes was in scope. Consent is THREE LAYERS, all fail-closed: the shipped inference.aiProcessingEligibility gate inherited not re-asked, a NEW magnus.enabled feature gate re-read in the consumer so a firm switching it off stops in-flight turns, and a magnus:converse RBAC permission plus a VERSIONED per-member disclosure acknowledgement — a per-member on/off setting is refused as a second authorization mechanism the chokepoint does not know about. Transport reuses the shipped push-progress hibernation WebSocket rather than server-sent events, because ADR-0112 REQUIRES off-hot-path queue dispatch, and the Durable Object holds NO conversation content — it reads turn state from the firm database and pushes it, which is exactly the constraint that made AIChatAgent unusable. Gateway logging stays fully off, so the AI Gateway dashboard shows NOTHING for Magnus by design and the in-repo token_usage_meter is the only cost record; the metadata-only middle ground is a cf-aig-* header the binding does not read, and GUARDRAILS MUST NEVER BE ENABLED because they log user prompts and model responses — a retention surface behind a dashboard toggle, outside everything the code makes fail closed. Magnus starts with NO platform context and NO tools: retrieval attaches at a MagnusContextPort whose only implementation returns nothing, because the disclosure what you type is what is sent is one a firm can actually evaluate and what our retrieval layer happened to select is not."
- **ADR-0250** · Accepted · 2026-08-06 · Magnus — "The owner answered three Magnus flags. F3 — context is explicit per-conversation grants: a member attaches specific documents or matters to a conversation deliberately, nothing implicit, every grant visible on the transcript record; firm-wide retrieval never arrives without its own consent decision. F2 — once a firm opts in, magnus:converse seeds to Owner, Admin, Attorney, and Paralegal. F4b — the owner reframed transcripts from the design's correspondence default to WORK PRODUCT: protected, deletable, archivable; discoverable like email, but any search or discovery surface that could sweep transcripts in must carry an explicit include-or-exclude control — a transcript enters a review set or a search scope by a person's visible choice, never by default inclusion. That control requirement binds the discovery review-set load design and any future search scope that could reach transcripts."
- **ADR-0251** · Accepted · 2026-08-06 · Notifications — "The Notifications subsystem's delivery half SUBSCRIBES to the transactional outbox rather than growing a second emission path: thirty-one topics already commit a durable row in the same batch as the write that produced them, so the notification consumer becomes a second non-exclusive pass on the per-firm queue and no emitting write path changes. The comparison-shaped emission points — the calendar's deadline horizon/due/overdue and the custody register's one collection point — are materialized by the existing cron pass with a CIVIL-DATE-bucketed dedupe key, because a continuously-true comparison must raise once a day and not once a sweep. A notification row carries identifiers, integers, instants, and declared enum labels, and NO STRING A PERSON TYPED: no matter title, no filename, no subject line — the reader's card is assembled at read time from the kind plus a visibility-filtered matter join, which is both the strongest redaction posture and the only one that cannot go stale. An email digest states the SHAPE of the queue and never its contents — a fixed matter-free subject, per-category counts, one deep link — because an email is the one channel that cannot be re-filtered when access changes; account and security kinds, which are about the recipient rather than a client, keep full content. Matter visibility binds at DELIVERY time through the one visible-matter predicate composed in SQL before the LIMIT, and the feed, the unread count, and the digest resolve it through ONE shared function so 'the digest respects the wall' names one implementation rather than two that can drift."
- **ADR-0252** · Proposed · 2026-08-06 · Search — "Cloudflare AI Search is adopted for one shape only — a firm-facing, retrieval-only natural-language search over a firm's own working documents, opt-in per firm and off for everyone on merge. One instance per firm (the only allocation that scales past the 100-namespace and 5,000-instance ceilings), fed not from the firm's bucket but from a derived prefix holding already-extracted text, so what can enter the index is an allow-list of exactly one object class and Magnus transcripts, review-set originals, and mid-edit working copies are absent by construction rather than by rule. The metadata filter is explicitly NOT the ethical wall: every hit is re-authorized against the caller's entitlement through the firm-scoped data-access layer before any of its text is returned, and a filter's semantics are proved by a fenced synthetic experiment before the retrieval route ships at all. Slice 1 is matter-scoped only; firm-wide waits on a proven disjunction and a measured recall figure. No generation, no public endpoint, no MCP, similarity cache off, and no firm enabled until Cloudflare takes the product to general availability with final pricing."
- **ADR-0253** · Accepted · 2026-08-06 · Discovery — "THE TRANSCRIPT CONTROL IS A REQUIRED FIELD WITH NO DEFAULT IN EITHER DIRECTION, and that is the whole of the ratified work-product ruling made structural: every discovery load declares `transcriptScope` as `include` or `exclude`, the column is NOT NULL with no default, and a declaration that omits it is refused — because a silent exclusion decides as much for somebody as a silent inclusion would, it just fails in the other direction, and a firm that believed transcripts were in scope would find out after producing. The scope is stored on the LOAD rather than inferred from what arrived, so the manifest can state what the corpus was DRAWN FROM and not only what landed in it, and it is carried on EVERY lane's declaration — not only the two lanes that could reach a transcript — because a record that sometimes carries the scope and sometimes does not is a record whose silence means two different things. `include` is refused BY NAME until the lane that resolves transcripts exists, the same device the unbuilt direct-collection lane uses, because the two honest alternatives are both worse: silently dropping an include produces a corpus that does not match the declaration, and pretending to honour it produces a manifest claiming a sweep that never happened. DISCOVERY IS ITS OWN PERMISSION NAMESPACE, and the namespace IS the access rule the design asked for: a review set is reachable from a `discovery:*` grant and from nothing else — never from matters:*, never from files:*, never from being on the matter team — because material produced by another party may be attorney's-eyes-only under a protective order. Three keys rather than the house read/manage pair, because loading is genuinely a third capability: it brings another party's untrusted material into the firm's own corpus, consumes the review set's ceiling, and is the one discovery operation whose cost is set from outside the firm; a contract reviewer working under a protective order holds discovery:read and neither of the others, and that configuration has to be expressible before anyone needs it. The per-review-set access LIST the design recommended is NOT built here and is named for the review surface: a list nothing renders is a list nothing enforces, and the namespace separation is the part that has to exist before the first review set can be created. The occurrence table's columns are renamed from the design's `duplicate_of_review_document_id` to `primary_review_document_id` + `duplicate_review_document_id`, because 'duplicate_of' on the row that IS the duplicate reads backwards to half of everyone. And the platform-wide widening of the custodian-vocabulary gate that the tool's own comment scheduled for this slice is REFUSED as written: the shipped Custodian register legitimately owns the bare word as its subsystem name in prose, its package specifier (@magna-carta/custodian), and its route (/custodian), so the widening would have required either renaming a shipped subsystem for no defect or a gate riddled with exemptions, which is a gate that stops biting."
- **ADR-0254** · Accepted · 2026-08-06 · Storage / tenancy — "Every object a firm's bucket holds carries two dimensions in its R2 custom metadata: `matter` (the matter it belongs to, on matter-scoped objects only) and `object_class` (a closed vocabulary saying what the object is). The object KEY stays scope-free — `files/{fileId}/{versionId}` is opaque precisely so a rename or a matter reassignment moves no bytes — so the scope travels in the metadata instead, written at put time by the path that mints the object. On the one write path whose bytes never touch the Worker, the browser's presigned PUT, the metadata headers are covered by the signature, so an upload that omits the stamp is refused rather than landing an unscopeable object. Firm-level objects (download bundles, templates, letterheads, conversion artifacts, staged mail, and the content-addressed email original that one message filed onto three matters shares) carry the class and deliberately no matter, so `no matter` is a recorded answer rather than an omission. The `client` dimension named by the natural-language-search design is RESERVED and not written: the matter-to-client link has no reader and no populated data yet, and a dimension absent on every object is worse than no dimension because a filter on it matches nothing while appearing to work. Objects written before any of this are repaired by a nightly convergent sweep whose candidate query is an R2 listing rather than any new database state, and future write paths are held to the rule by a source-scan fitness function."
- **ADR-0255** · Accepted · 2026-08-07 · Governance — "The owner answered the complete open-decision docket — all 92 items. Sixty-seven ratified their shipped defaults, closing the open flags across the merge-field catalog (H2, H4-H6), matter assets (all five), calendar (L2, L5-L7), auto-file rules (F2-F6, F8), billing (B2, B3, B5-B8, B10-B14), discovery 5-1 (D2, D5-D7, D9, D10), AI Search (A1-A5), template expressions (G1, G3, G7), Magnus (F4a folded into the work-product ruling), trust (T2, T5, T6, T8, T9, T11, T13-T15, T19-T21), document automation (F3-F5, F9), and the standing postures (aggregates-as-absence, DKIM informational, custodian release authority as free text, and the custodian-vocabulary lint scope judgment). Nine overrides carry build direction: the Magnus model default becomes an evergreen platform-maintained tier that tracks model advancement rather than a pinned identifier; notification email content becomes a member choice under a firm ceiling, and retention becomes firm-configurable in slice 1 with unread loss surfaced honestly; AI Search enablement waits only on the firm's versioned disclosure, not on Cloudflare general availability; the trust jurisdiction corpus is Nevada-first with the rest explicitly not-encoded; two-person disbursement becomes a per-firm policy (off, threshold, or always, with an approver-authority option) and the earned-fee application follows the same policy; bank-statement import gains a PDF path through the extraction engine; the calendar gains a designed slice for firm events, room and resource availability, event links, and honest continuance semantics; billing builds all fee models, and multi-matter invoices render per-matter pages plus a summary at the firm's choice; the directory gains optional owner-entered pronouns feeding a template helper; and a small provenance-tracked Nevada starter court-form library is curated. Two items went to research before build: unclaimed-funds escheat obligations, and the firm-funds-in-trust carve-out (the owner holds firm money never enters trust; the recognized bank-charge exception is being verified against Nevada RPC 1.15 primary sources and returns to the owner with the rule text). Eight items were clarifying questions answered in the orchestration channel with their defaults standing pending re-answer."
- **ADR-0256** · Proposed · 2026-08-07 · Trust accounting / Jurisdiction compliance — "The trust-accounting jurisdiction corpus ships NEVADA FIRST and REVIEW-GATED, answering flags T7 and T8 of the trust-accounting analysis as owner-ratified. T7: one jurisdiction, researched from the state's own published rule text, with every other jurisdiction explicitly not-encoded and refusing exactly as before — no baseline profile, no fallback, no 'most states require' default. T8: attorney review is a hard shipping gate, and it is a gate rather than a flag — an encoded-but-unreviewed jurisdiction surfaces as not-verified and refuses every requirement with its own code (jurisdiction-not-verified), distinct both from the unrecorded-office refusal and from the never-researched one. Nevada ships NOT VERIFIED. The review record names who reviewed, when, and WHICH REVISION of the profile they read, so amending a value re-closes the gate rather than silently inheriting an attestation about superseded text. The research turned up three corrections to facts this repository had already written down, all traceable to the State Bar's own 2019 handbook rather than to the rules: retention is five years and not seven (the seven-year wording predates the 2021 amendment), the account-mechanics rule is SCR 78 and not the repealed SCR 78.5, and Nevada mandates no reconciliation at any cadence (the string does not occur in either rule set, and the guidance asserting a monthly duty miscites a subsection about interest remittance). The approved jurisdiction schema design's golden test for Nevada — '7 years, monthly three-way' — would have locked both errors in. The firm-funds bank-charge carve-out is encoded as a CITED RULE and deliberately NOT WIRED: NRPC 1.15(b) permits it, but states no cap, is nearly inapplicable to a pooled account under SCR 217(3), and enabling it would require changing both the no-commingling composition guard and the three-way proof — so the permission is recorded beside a platform that does not act on it."
- **ADR-0257** · Proposed · 2026-08-07 · Email filing / capture — "Wiring the auto-file guardrail engine into the capture consumer settles five questions the rules decision left to build time. Rules are consulted ONLY where no entrance routed the capture — the firm catch-all and the unrouted forward — because an entrance that already resolved a matter answered the routing question with better evidence than a rule has (a credential naming that matter, or an authenticated member choosing it), and a rule second-guessing it would be the wrong-matter mass-file with extra steps. The filing-time conflict hook reaches the evidence gatherer as a CLOSURE supplied by the capture core rather than as an import, so `checkFilingConflict` keeps exactly one call site and the promotion gate and the recorded reason are the same answer: a gatherer cannot report 'no hold' for a matter the hook holds, and the capture core re-applies that memoized answer before it commits, so no automatic path reaches `filed` without traversing it. On a DEDUP HIT the signature is re-adjudicated rather than read back from the message row, because the shipped row records the verdict but not whether the signature covered the whole body — and a partially-covered `pass` is exactly what a sender-identity rule must decline — so the choice is one cached DNS lookup on a duplicate capture where rules could fire, against a schema column that would have to be right for every row written before it existed. `matterNumberInSubject` ships as AMBIGUITY EVIDENCE ONLY and its firm toggle is deliberately NOT built: the shipped engine has no routing path for a subject number (a firing requires a matched rule), so the toggle would be inert and an inert switch in an administrative screen is worse than an absent one. Undo feedback is derived from the LINK's own state — a link a rule made that somebody unlinked — rather than from a reversal row written by the unlink route, so the unlink contract is untouched and a reversal cannot be missed by a path that forgot to record it."
- **ADR-0258** · Accepted · 2026-08-07 · Notifications — "Notification retention is FIRM-CONFIGURABLE within [30, 365] days (default 90) rather than a fixed platform ninety, and the nightly sweep RECORDS WHAT IT TAKES: before deleting, it tallies per member how many of the rows it is about to remove were still unread and how far back they reach, and the unread-count read reports that tally beside the live count. The reasoning is the same one that makes sweeping defensible at all — a notification is a pointer and never a record, so the timeline, the deadline, the ledger entry and the audit_log row it named all survive it — but 'nobody needs the pointer' is not the same claim as 'nobody missed anything', and returning a silent zero to a member back from three weeks' leave is a claim the platform is not in a position to make. Clearing the tally is the same act as marking the live feed read, bounded by the same caller-supplied cursor, so a member has one gesture for one backlog and no badge they cannot clear."
