- **ADR-0259** · Accepted · 2026-08-07 · Matters / Document automation — "The three H1 platform records land as the Matters area's own tables with the merge-field catalog as one consumer: matter_assignment carries staffing beyond the responsible attorney (whose one home stays matter.responsible_member_id — the write path refuses an assignment role that normalizes to it), matter_court_case carries the case identity with the court as a Directory organisation and the judge as a Directory person (resolving the catalog design's open question in favour of Directory references, so a court's address and a judge's name formats are recorded once), and matter_estate carries the estate identity with STORED styles (a court-fixed designation is never composed), the decedent as a Directory person, and the estate's own optional Directory organisation as the home of its encrypted tax identifier. matter_party gains a nullable capacity column — a fact about the link, never inferred. A member's professional profile is one whole-record upsert keyed by the member's own user id; the bar number is deliberately NOT a profile column and lives encrypted in directory_identifier on the member's mapped Directory person, read only through the protected-field chokepoint (bar_number joins the identifier-type catalog, never standalone-indexed). One deliberate deferral: the member's self-maintenance door for the profile, because the chokepoint structurally refuses authenticated permission-null mutations and the platform has no self-scoped mutation concept anywhere — the profile is written through firm:manageMembers until the Members area designs self-service writes."
- **ADR-0260** · Accepted · 2026-08-07 · Trust accounting — "Two-person disbursement authorization becomes a per-firm policy (owner override of T12's shipped default): off, threshold (a second person for amounts strictly above a stated minor-unit amount), or always, plus an independent approver-authority option requiring whoever lets money go to hold the new trust:authorize capability — a capability predicate, never a role name, seeded to Owner/Admin and Attorney and deliberately narrower than trust:manage. The shipped default is the strict posture (always two-person, authority off), so no firm's controls weaken on migrate. The policy is an append-only, densely versioned table; changing it takes trust:authorize and writes an audit row naming from and to. Every request and transfer stamps the version current at submission and is decided under that stamp — an in-flight authorization keeps the policy it was created under, in both directions — with the stamp, the basis exactness (second_person / sole_trust_manager / policy_exempt), and the authority requirement all held by database triggers, whose policy CASEs whitelist the exemption so an unrecognized kind requires two people rather than zero. The lone-holder exception belongs strictly to the counted member: when the second person is absent, the sole actor must BE the firm's one trust:manage holder, in the composers and the triggers alike. The earned-fee application follows the same policy through the same queue: an application is a trust_disbursement_request of kind earned_fee_application whose invoice lines billing keeps beside it, approved through billing, posting a transfer that records submitter, approver, request, and policy version — one transfer per request ever (UNIQUE), matching the request's amount, claiming only a pending request. Known limitation stated: the threshold is per-authorization, with no per-payee or per-day aggregation, so split payments can stay under it. Open questions flagged for the owner: whether one person should be able to dismantle the two-person control (a single seeded attorney can relax the policy to off and then move money alone, audited), and whether matter-to-matter reallocations should fall under the policy."
- **ADR-0261** · Accepted · 2026-08-07 · Calendar and docketing — "Calendar slice 2 executes the owner's two docket overrides (L1: firm events and room availability must exist; L4: a continuance is a real-world reschedule the record must show honestly) with five new per-firm tables and one additive column, and no change to any shipped row's meaning. FIRM EVENTS get their own firm_event table rather than a nullable matter_id — rejected once on the domain ground slice 1 stated (every matter_event read keys off the matter, and the visibility predicate's value is that it has no branches) and once mechanically: dropping NOT NULL on a shipped SQLite table is a rebuild, which the additive-only lineage forbids, so the loosening is not merely undesirable but unshippable. A firm event shares the civil-date/time/zone shapes and the three-shape participant model (its own participant table, same rebuild reason), reuses the matter_event_type catalog (its category column already carries firm; the matter_ name prefix is an accepted, recorded wart cheaper than a second home for one vocabulary), and adds audience = all_members | participants so a firm holiday is on everyone's calendar without enumerating members — a list that decays with every hire. Visibility is an HONEST DECLARATION, not a silent exemption: the source contract gains visibilityAnchor = matter | firm; matter-anchored sources compose the predicate exactly as today (fitness-enforced), firm-anchored sources are a reviewed allowlist whose rows are firm-visible by construction under the trusted-insider posture — ethical walls are matter screens with nothing to bind to on a firm meeting. RESOURCES (rooms, vehicles, equipment) are their own firm_resource table — NOT Directory entities (the Directory is the conflicts corpus; a conference room beside opposing counsel is the forced-judge problem with less dignity) and NOT custody_location nodes (that tree answers where the original will is, and bookability is not custody). A BOOKING is its own row attached to EXACTLY ONE event (matter or firm; a bare 'book the car' is a Reservation-type firm event), with its own window defaulted from the event (setup time is real) and a zone frozen from the resource's office at the write; its LIVENESS IS DERIVED from the event's status — no released flag, no second write, a room freed by cancellation cannot drift because releasing is not an action. Overlap is a two-stage bounded check (coarse civil-date SQL widened a day each side, precise instant intersection in application code through each row's own zone), and DOUBLE-BOOKING IS WARN-AND-ALLOW WITH VISIBLE CONFLICTS: refusal would make the platform referee rooms it does not own (tentative holds and shared rooms are real intentions), could not be held by the schema anyway (SQLite has no interval exclusion constraint, so a refusal is a race), and must never be the reason a hearing cannot be recorded — the overdue-deadline posture applied to rooms: report, never adjudicate. EVENT LINKS are one calendar_event_link table with four end columns (exactly one per end), kinds continuance_of | related plus a free-text note, real foreign keys because the calendar linking to itself can afford integrity. CONTINUANCE dissolves slice 1's edit-or-new-row dichotomy instead of picking the other horn: a CORRECTION is still an edit (the row misdescribed reality; slice 1's mutable-event rule stands), a CONTINUANCE is a new setting — the old row keeps every historical column untouched, its status becomes cancelled (accurate: that setting will not occur; also the only option, since widening the status CHECK is a rebuild) with a new nullable status_reason, a new row carries the setting as it now stands, and one continuance_of link points new to old, constrained to a CHAIN by two hand-written partial unique indexes (at most one predecessor and one successor per setting — the schema, not a service invariant, refuses a fork). Slice 1's every-consumer-hides-one objection assumed the old row would still read scheduled; it does not, so consumers change not at all. The continue operation is one atomic batch whose response lists open deadlines whose trigger names the continued event (a lookup, never a cascade — the ratified no-arithmetic position binds in full) and the bookings the status change just released by derivation. Continuance binds matter events only; a moved staff meeting is not procedural history. Four notification emission points are declared and wired to nothing (calendar.firmEvent.scheduled/rescheduled/cancelled, calendar.booking.conflict); a continuance raises the existing calendar.event.rescheduled. Three owner flags, all confirmations: the warn-and-allow posture, the closed resource-kind enum, and no freestanding bookings."
- **ADR-0262** · Proposed · 2026-08-07 · Discovery — "REVIEW IS A FUNCTION OVER THE CORPUS, AND ITS RECORD IS THE PRODUCT: discovery 5·2 designs tagging and coding, privilege review, and productions out — tagging first by owner direction. CODING IS GROUPS CONTAINING TAGS, two levels and no deeper: a single-choice group is the mutually exclusive choice review coding is actually made of (flat tags cannot say 'responsive XOR non-responsive', arbitrary trees buy depth nobody at this scale uses), and MACHINE LOGIC READS SEMANTICS, NEVER NAMES — a closed semantic vocabulary on tags drives the privilege log, production membership, and QC, so renaming a label can never change behavior. TAG STORAGE IS ONE ROW PER DOCUMENT PER APPLICATION, ALWAYS — family and bulk are recorded gestures that write per-document rows, never inherited at read time, because inheritance resolved years later at production time is where a bug produces a privileged document. Rows are NEVER DELETED: removal is a state with an actor and an instant, single-choice is a partial unique index rather than a guard, and bulk operations freeze their selection to R2 at initiation and undo by exact compensation — remove precisely the rows your event applied that still live, touch nothing anyone else did. Coding attaches to the dedup PRIMARY and reads through to occurrences, and the load file carries every occurrence's record custodian, which is 'which custodians held this' answered at the moment it is asked under oath. PRIVILEGE IS A TAG PLUS A CLAIM, deliberately both: designation stays uniform with all coding (one panel, one bulk path, one audit), while the claim carries what a tag cannot — multiple bases and the log description — and the LOG GENERATOR IS STRUCTURALLY UNABLE TO READ DOCUMENT CONTENT: its inputs are the claim, the tag rows, and the extracted metadata, never the text object. A PRODUCTION IS EVIDENCE OF WHAT WAS PRODUCED: membership frozen to explicit rows at staging, Bates drawn as a guarded BLOCK from a per-(matter, prefix) register by widening @magna-carta/sequence-numbering with an increment-by-N (one mechanism, not a drifting second counter), rendering on its own DISCOVERY_PRODUCE lane with the rasterize-route burn and a fail-closed verification gate, five-digest hashes on every artifact, and a cut production IMMUTABLE FOREVER — clawback is a new recorded fact on a member, never an edit, and the Bates numbers stay burned because they were served. PRODUCTION TEXT FOR A REDACTED DOCUMENT DERIVES FROM THE REDACTED ARTIFACT OR IS OMITTED — the pre-redaction extraction never reaches the production writer, because handing original text beside a redacted image is a redaction failure by sidecar. Withheld-privileged documents appear on the frozen per-production log and never in the volume. Production artifacts are RECORDS UNDER THE DISCOVERY PREFIX, not File Manager files — which is also what keeps them structurally outside the AI Search feed, whose only producer remains committed working-document text; 5·2 adds no producer, asserted by a fitness test. The per-review-set ACCESS LIST that 5·1·2 named ships with the first surface that renders it, every content read re-authorizes at read time through the existing file-manager and email paths, and walled review sets are absent, not hidden. Four owner flags stay open: the privilege-coding permission tier, Bates and slipsheets for withheld documents, the family-split default, and the privilege-log delivery format."
- **ADR-0263** · Accepted · 2026-08-07 · Billing — "ALL FOUR FEE MODELS BUILD NOW, on the shipped invoice spine, per the owner's docket override of the staged B9 default — and the FEE AGREEMENT IS A PER-MATTER, APPEND-ONLY, VERSIONED RECORD: a fee change is a new version whose insert closes the prior version's valid_to in one batch, versions are dense per matter by trigger, exactly one version is in force by partial unique index, and the only UPDATE a version ever takes is that one-time close — because invoice lines record the agreement version that composed them (a new invoice_line.billing_arrangement_id), so an edited agreement would rewrite what a finalized bill was computed under. Absence of an arrangement means hourly, so no matter needs a backfill and the shipped hourly path is untouched except that a flat-fee or contingency matter now REFUSES hourly time claims by name (costs stay claimable — advancing a filing fee is not a fee model, and a retainer is a funding arrangement whose time still bills hourly). The flat fee is one line plus zero-amount claims exactly as the invoice-model decision anticipated, with instalments representable and over-billing REPORTED by fitness query rather than blocked. The CONTINGENCY FEE IS ALWAYS DERIVED, NEVER STORED AS A DECIDED NUMBER: matter_recovery records the settlement/judgment/award (append-only, void-with-reason its only edit), and the fee is computed at composition from the agreement's share against the recoveries as they stand — under the recorded costs-before-or-after election, where net-after-costs deducts the matter's advanced (hard) costs and never its soft costs — landing as an ordinary reviewable fee line that snapshots the share in the rate position. Settlement disbursement statements and lien handling remain deferred exactly as designed. The EVERGREEN RETAINER records target and floor on the agreement (the floor is business configuration, never the trust leaf's structural min_balance), reads trust's own AVAILABLE figure, and records a retainer_replenishment_request (observed balance + shortfall, one open per matter by partial unique index, resolution its only edit) emitting billing.retainer.replenishment_requested — the notifications seam, with delivery and the below-floor automation belonging to slice 3·7. NO NEW TRUST WRITE SHAPE EXISTS ANYWHERE IN THIS: an advance flat fee or retainer deposit sits where the agreement's recorded advance-deposit destination says, and money leaves trust only through the existing earned-fee application against a finalized invoice. And per owner override B4, THE MULTI-MATTER RENDER SHAPE IS A FACT ON THE INVOICE MODEL: a firm-level default (per-matter pages plus a summary is the shipped default) overridable per finalize and SNAPSHOT onto invoice.render_shape with the letterhead — a later change to the default must not re-shape a document already given to a client — with the per-page/summary derivation shipped as a read and the PDF tail explicitly separate work. Hybrid arrangements (e.g. capped hourly, hourly-against-contingency) are NOT built and not schema'd: the design names no semantics for them, and inventing any would decide fee-agreement law the owner has not been asked about."
- **ADR-0264** · Proposed · 2026-08-10 · Documents — "Translation services are adopted as a document-and-text capability that generates translations and describes how it generated them, and never certifies them. A certificate of translation accuracy is a named human being's signed declaration of competence in the language pair and of the completeness and accuracy of the specific translation (8 CFR 103.2(b)(3); 8 CFR 1003.33; the authentication practice behind Fed. R. Evid. 604 for documentary exhibits; NRS 50.054 and NRS 1.510 for Nevada interpreters, the Eighth Judicial District Court Rules containing no express rule on translated exhibits), and no engine can hold either half of it. The platform therefore emits a Translation Provenance Record — engine, model, pinned snapshot, date, operator, matter, source hash, per-page native/OCR extraction route, conversion engine, segment counts, and whether any human edited the output — carrying a verbatim statement that it is not a certification, with a prohibited-claim vocabulary (certificate, certified, sworn, accurate, complete, court-ready, USCIS-accepted, any quality score, any signature line or seal) enforced by a fitness function over the renderer's own strings. Engine posture: Cloudflare Workers AI m2m100-1.2b is the default behind its own fail-closed firm gate and does NOT require the third-party frontier consent axis; the frontier route through the shipped inference chokepoint requires all three gates; DeepL, Google Cloud Translation, and Azure Translator are refused for now in favour of the already-named BYOK deferred seam. A member acknowledgement is required for both routes, because the sentence the member needs is engine-independent. Translations are derived sibling documents, never new versions of the source, stamped with the source's own matter and object-class dimensions."
- **ADR-0265** · Proposed · 2026-08-10 · Trust accounting — "Authority-based trust authorization, owner-directed: a member holding trust:authorize releases client money alone below the firm's threshold, and a member without it routes to one who has. The shipped machinery already expresses this — no new policy kind, no widened CHECK, no new column, no edit to any of the four exemption mirrors: the owner's posture is the stored triple (kind, threshold, require_authorized_approver = 1), because that flag binds whoever lets money go and therefore turns an amount-only exemption into an actor-conditional one. Peer approval above the threshold falls out rather than being added: the decider is the second person, and the flag binds the decider. The shipped strict default changes by SEEDING a version-1 row (policy off, authority required) in a new per-firm migration rather than by re-reading the absent row, so the migration stays purely additive, the NULL stamp keeps describing exactly the history it describes, and the new default is auditable data in each firm's own posture history; safe because no firms exist, and in-flight requests keep their stamped policy by construction (the request carries policy_id, the resolution loads that stamp, and the mirrors join it rather than MAX(version)). Routing needs no schema: trust_disbursement_request is already the kind-tagged, policy-stamped pending queue for both money-out paths, so the only addition is a shared resolveTrustAuthorizationDisposition read that lets callers choose route-versus-solo before composing — the composers' refusals stay guards and never become routes. The four-cell matrix is the cross product of two predicates already implemented (the decider holds authority; the decider differs from the submitter above the threshold), so the two above-threshold cells collapse into one enforcement path and no mirror grows. The audit vocabulary does not grow either: basis states the relation between actors, the stamped policy version states the rule, and widening the basis CHECK would cost a second rebuild of trust_disbursement_decision. Open: whether the lone-holder exception should count trust:authorize rather than trust:manage (cheap now, expensive after the first firm), whether above-threshold means two people or three, whether solo-restricted-to-holders should be separable from peer approval, and whether a platform sentinel is acceptable as set_by on the seeded row."
- **ADR-0266** · Proposed · 2026-08-10 · Tenancy & data isolation — "Firm creation becomes self-serve behind a two-stage registration — one control-plane row at sign-up, resources provisioned only by a queue job after email verification — and a free evaluation tier is enforced as a MASK over the existing permission registry rather than as a second authorization system: the entitlement catalog names the same permission strings routes already declare, the withholding happens inside the chokepoint's existing loadPermissions port, and the single permissions.has(required) check gains one denial branch (402 upgrade-required beside the existing 403 permission-denied) rather than a second gate. Cloudflare's account limits do not bound self-serve tenancy — the per-firm database is a Durable Object, not D1, and R2 allows a million buckets; the binding constraints are ours (the serial fleet-wide cron loop) and one feature's (AI Search caps at 5,000 instances per account, one per firm). The provisioner's resume identity moves from the firm's globally-unique NAME to its id, because a unique name column on a public endpoint is a tenant-enumeration oracle and a squatting surface. Abandoned free tenants are reaped on a warned, recorded ladder that recovers the recurring cost at dormancy and destroys nothing for a further four months; destruction follows the custodian precedent — the sweep proposes, an operator approves, holds and non-zero trust balances refuse outright, and an undelivered warning stops the clock. A lapsed PAID firm is never reaped and never downgraded to free: it goes read-only with export, indefinitely, keeping every byte it has."
- **ADR-0267** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Authentication, access control & encryption — "Network and device controls over access to Magna Carta are enforced by the FIRM's identity provider, not by us: we become a SAML 2.0 / OIDC service provider (Better Auth's SSO plugin) so a firm's Cloudflare Access, Okta or Entra can apply device posture, conditional access and its own tunnel before issuing an assertion — the firm's account, the firm's seats, the firm's logs, and nothing held here. A 'VPN to the platform' is a category error for this architecture: we are four public Cloudflare hostnames over Workers, Durable-Object-fronted SQLite and R2, with no private network for a tunnel to reach, and Cloudflare's own documentation confirms protecting a public hostname requires neither a tunnel nor a client install. Answering the owner's actual goal — a lawyer working safely from a hotel — is a package, because the top three real threats (credential phishing, session-cookie theft, device compromise) are defeated by passkeys, Device Bound Session Credentials and firm-enforced posture, and by none of them a tunnel. Per-firm IP allowlisting ships as an opt-in setting with blunt warnings, never a default; mTLS with Cloudflare-issued client certificates is a named backlog seam."
- **ADR-0268** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Authentication, access control & encryption — "Magna Carta will not operate a VPN for firms, will not resell Cloudflare Zero Trust, and will not ship a VPN client in the Tauri shell. The stated goal — helping lawyers reach their files safely over insecure wifi — is already met by TLS 1.3 plus the .esq TLD's top-level HSTS preload, so a tunnel wraps an encrypted channel in a second encrypted channel for a marginal gain measured at close to zero. The disqualifying objection is confidentiality, not cost: Gateway's own logging reference shows a VPN operator holds a per-lawyer, per-device, timestamped record of every hostname and full URL a firm's staff reach, which reveals unfiled matters and undisclosed representations the firm never gave us; that record is non-content under the Stored Communications Act and therefore reachable on a SUBPOENA rather than a warrant, making us the cheapest route to a law firm's confidences and moving the fight from the party who holds the privilege to a third party who does not. It is also the one dataset our confidentiality architecture cannot cover — protected() encrypts declared fields written through the data-access layer, and per-firm crypto-shred destroys a key, but neither reaches operational routing telemetry generated continuously by the act of forwarding traffic. Reselling is separately forbidden by Cloudflare's Zero Trust service-specific terms on pain of material breach of the account the whole platform runs in."
- **ADR-0269** · Accepted · 2026-08-10 · Service status — "An upstream provider's severity is read from the components this platform actually depends on, not from its whole-page Statuspage rollup. Cloudflare publishes ~470 components and the great majority are individual edge locations; several dozen are re-routed or under maintenance at any moment, so its page-level `status.indicator` sits at `minor` more or less permanently while every service component is operational and Cloudflare's own headline reads `All systems operational`. Consuming that indicator made our public status page report Cloudflare as degraded on days Cloudflare said it was fine. A provider definition may now name the top-level component groups we depend on; the severity is the worst of those components and of any unresolved incident touching them. A scope that matches nothing falls back to the whole-page indicator rather than reporting health, so a group renamed upstream degrades to the pessimistic reading and never to a fabricated green. Provider rows also carry the human status page explicitly, so the destination a reader is sent to no longer has to be the origin of the machine feed we poll."
- **ADR-0270** · Proposed · 2026-08-08 · Discovery — "The per-review-set access list is fail-closed and composed as a SQL predicate. A review set with no access rows is reachable by nobody rather than by everybody holding a discovery permission, so a bug in the seeding path hides a corpus instead of disclosing one. The predicate is a required argument to every review-set statement builder and is composed into the WHERE clause before the LIMIT — never applied in the Worker after rows return — proved for every registered builder by a table-driven fitness pass modelled on packages/calendar/test/source-fitness.test.ts. The list is seeded with the creating member alone rather than the matter team, widening is a recorded act requiring discovery:produce, the last member cannot be removed, and existing review sets are backfilled with their creator by the migration. Coding writes no generic audit row: the coding journal is the audit."
- **ADR-0271** · Accepted · 2026-08-11 · Directory — "Pronouns become an optional, owner-entered field on a Directory person, and document assembly gains the pronoun() and pronounVerb() helpers that render them — reversing the shipped no-pronoun-helper default on the owner's direction. Five decisions carry the weight. PRONOUNS ARE NOT GENDER and no gender field ships: a pronoun set is what a person states and what prose needs, while the gendered legal terms a decree occasionally requires (widow against widower, husband against wife) are already served by the platform's stored-designation pattern — matter_estate's styles, matter_court_case's designations, and matter_party.capacity are all recorded text printed as recorded, never composed — so a gender attribute would add a second, guessable source for facts the firm already records verbatim; the question is FLAGGED for the owner rather than settled unilaterally. THE WHOLE DECLENSION IS STORED — subject, object, possessive determiner, possessive pronoun, reflexive, plus a verb-agreement number — rather than a label resolved through a lookup table, because a label determines only two of the five forms and any label-to-forms table is a closed enum in disguise whose gaps fall on exactly the people most often rendered wrong; six columns render any set, including a neopronoun set invented after this ships, with no code change. A PARTIAL SET IS UNREPRESENTABLE: a table CHECK admits all six columns or none, the write path exposes no per-form update, and the read projection treats anything short of a whole set as no set, because a document mixing one set's subject with another's object is invisible on the page and is the single failure the field exists to prevent. THE UNRECORDED CASE ANSWERS THEY/THEM: pronoun() is the only helper in the expression language that never goes unresolved, because the unstated case is the common case and a marker mid-clause would turn every ordinary letter into a document needing repair — the same neutral answer covers an organisation party and a role the matter does not resolve to one person, since all three mean the same thing and they/them is wrong about nobody. Verb agreement is NOT inflected: pronounVerb(person, singularForm, pluralForm) picks between two forms the author wrote, using the agreement number the set declares, which is total for every English verb including the irregular ones and keeps the choice visible in the template. Nothing anywhere derives a pronoun from a name, a courtesy title, or any other column."
- **ADR-0272** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Trust accounting — "The build of authority-based trust authorization, and the three calls the design left to it. (1) The seeded version-1 posture is INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS: a firm that has already written a posture keeps it, both because its choice must not be appended over and because version 1 over an existing version 1 would abort the dense-version trigger and leave that firm unmigrated. (2) A solo release is ONE atomic write — the request, the movement, and the decision recording the footing ride one batch through composeTrustDisbursementSubmission — rather than a proposal followed by a self-decision, because between those two calls the request is pending and another member can settle it as a two-person approval nobody asked for; the solo arm composes nothing new, reusing the same request composer, the same composeTrustDisbursement, and the same decision statement in the order the triggers need. (3) The route-versus-solo choice for earned fees lands on the SUBMIT endpoint rather than the direct-apply one, so no request body has to be widened and the direct endpoint keeps its role as the last guard; billing's applyEarnedFeesUnderAuthorizationPolicy resolves the invoices to learn the amount, asks the shared disposition, and delegates to the direct path or the queue. resolveTrustAuthorizationDisposition is a read in front of the guarantees and adds no fifth mirror: it calls the existing predicate and the same two membership reads the triggers count, asks the authority question FIRST and unconditionally of the ACTOR, and every path out of it is a refusal or a route. The lone-holder exception is extracted into one helper both the disposition and the approval resolution call, so the count-plus-membership pair cannot drift. Open question 1 from the design — whether the exception should count trust:authorize rather than trust:manage — is NOT taken: the design flags it for ratification, and the change would relax a firm with three managers and one authority holder from two-person to solo. No UI in this slice."
- **ADR-0273** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Documents — "An exhibit is three facts that are never the same fact: a matter-scoped identity (`matter_exhibit`, carrying a symbolic `reference_name` minted once from the title and thereafter independent of it), a position in an ordered `exhibit_set`, and a label that is a pure function of position and the set's scheme — computed on read while the set is a draft, never stored. In-text references cite the identity and render the label, through a fourth placeholder form `{{@referenceName}}` that is deliberately neither a merge field nor an expression: a merge key is a platform-declared fact of the matter checked at template registration, and an exhibit is a firm-authored instance created during drafting, so binding one as the other would either make the subject registry firm-derived or make every reference a registration error. A multi-exhibit citation enumerates its members (`{{@leaseAgreement,@rentLedger,@noticeToQuit}}`) and the renderer computes the phrasing, because a two-endpoint range survives an insertion syntactically and dies semantically. Word's SEQ/bookmark/REF machinery is rejected on its own documented behaviour — fields do not update automatically, and an attached exhibit offers no in-document target to bookmark — while its cross-reference dialog is adopted as the insertion model; LaTeX's symbolic \\label/\\ref is the mechanism taken, because a number that never appears in the source is a number an author cannot make stale."
- **ADR-0274** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Documents — "An exhibit set has exactly two states. A DRAFT set is a plan: reorder it and every label recomputes and every citation follows. An ISSUED set is a record of an act that happened — filed, served, exchanged, or lodged — so at issue every member's label, title, and file version are materialized onto the row and frozen by a trigger, the set admits no new members and loses none, and its numbering can never be recomputed. Because the platform has no court e-filing integration and will not pretend to observe a filing, issuing is a recorded human assertion carrying a disposition and a date, and it is irreversible. The line is the calendar's correction-versus-continuance line transplanted: a CORRECTION means the row misdescribed what was actually filed (edit in place, audited, structurally unable to renumber because it composes no statement touching label or position), and a SUPERSESSION is the continuance (an amended filing is a NEW set, the old one preserved byte-untouched, one `supersedes_set_id` link held to a chain by a partial unique index so a fork is refused even under racing writes). An issued exhibit is never removed but WITHDRAWN, and its label is retired and never reused — two documents both correctly cited as Exhibit C is a collision no reader can resolve — so a continuing set starts past the retired label. Freezing on download or on PDF conversion was rejected: both punish drafting, and neither is what actually happened."
- **ADR-0275** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Community / Identity — "Community identity reuses Better Auth as a `community_profile` keyed to the account, and the model is REAL DISPLAY NAME BY DEFAULT with an account-level pseudonym permitted, the FIRM NEVER DISPLAYED, and a verified badge for staff — a move from the first draft's forced pseudonymity, caused by scoping the board to product usage and by the observation that ClickUp itself shows real full names and NO company affiliation. Firm was always the sharper edge than name: firm plus practice area plus question is the inference path. Account-level choice does not carry the per-post choice's defect, where non-disclosure becomes a signal. The VOTER LIST IS NEVER RENDERED — ClickUp publishes theirs and it is findable by web search, which is the demonstration of the harm, not a hypothetical. Eligibility: firm staff participate fully including paralegals, a firm's CLIENT is excluded STRUCTURALLY rather than by a permission check because a client in a cross-firm list discloses the firm-client relationship, and a prospect reads only. Requiring a PROVISIONED FIRM to write is the anti-astroturf control and beats real names, which do not stop a competitor's employee using their own. `firmId` on every vote, ranked by DISTINCT FIRMS with a display floor of five. The identity model DEPENDS on the absence of a general category and that dependency is written down rather than remembered."
- **ADR-0276** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Community / Moderation — "Two failures, two postures. SPAM AND ABUSE DEGRADE — a read-only kill switch in KV, one operator write and no deploy, built with the FIRST write endpoint because it is twenty lines then and a retrofit across every route later, under pressure; plus provisioned-firm writes, first-post pre-moderation, Turnstile, rate limits, and an N-distinct-firm report threshold. CONFIDENTIALITY REMOVES FIRST AND ASKS LATER, which is a deliberate inversion of normal moderation because the asymmetry is extreme: a false positive costs a lawyer an eight-hour wait for a template question, a false negative is a permanent disclosure of a client relationship. Four detectors in descending order of real work done: the AUTHOR, whose self-service delete is one click and one write with no queue and no operator; ANY READER, where a SINGLE confidentiality flag hides the post pending review rather than the distinct-firm threshold that is right for spam and wrong here; a FORMAT-ONLY detector on every post that never consults firm data, because checking a post against the poster's own Directory would cross the boundary the package fitness function exists to hold; and the moderator at 9am. So the honest answer to `what happens at 3am` is that the post is ALREADY HIDDEN. Attachments are off at launch because a screenshot of legal software carries a client name, matter number and balance and defeats every text-based control at once. The 511R notice is KEPT and REWORDED for a product-usage context: the operational rule leads, the citation stays as one line."
- **ADR-0277** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Community / Delivery model — "The community board is PUBLIC TO READ and login-to-write, as ClickUp's is — a change from the first draft, which put it entirely behind the login and under-weighted the acquisition value of a prospect seeing an active board with staff replying in-thread. But public and INDEXED are different things: the board is `noindex` permanently, because ClickUp's public voter list with real names was located through a web search, which is the harm demonstrated rather than theorised. The board becomes its own deployable, `apps/community-worker`, because it is the only surface in the estate serving unauthenticated public reads of user-authored content, its availability is not load-bearing for billable work, and the `@magna-carta/community` package's no-per-firm-binding isolation is far stronger as a property of the deployment than as a lint rule. The public changelog needs no compute at all — static assets, no `main`, no bindings, more fate-isolated than the status worker, which at least reads KV. No Durable Object anywhere: vote counting is a unique constraint plus a counter, and the no-preview-URLs constraint is already priced in repo-wide since all thirteen workers in `apps/` set `preview_urls: false`. Domain settled by the owner on 2026-08-11: `magnacarta.esq` is the URL and `magna-carta.com` stays marketing only. The changelog takes up the apex reservation the white paper's hosts table ALREADY holds for the public product-updates pages — `magnacarta.esq/changelog`, indexed, on the one hostname search authority should accrue to — while the board gets its own host at `community.magnacarta.esq` because it is permanently noindex, serves user-authored content from its own worker, and is the surface most likely to need turning off. `community.` rather than `feedback.` because the board carries questions as well as requests and is already the word the package and the app's ecosystem-site registry use. A free consequence, re-verified live rather than assumed: `.esq` is HSTS-preloaded at the TLD by Google Registry, so both surfaces are HTTPS-only with no header from us and no configuration step to forget."
- **ADR-0278** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Community / Product updates — "Public changelog first and alone, then the community board — ONE board carrying two post kinds (votable requests with a status pipeline, and threaded questions with votable answers), which wakes the dormant `discussion` seam rather than replacing it. The owner directed the design at ClickUp, and studying ClickUp's live surfaces resolved the earlier recommendation against a forum rather than merely overruling it: ClickUp has NO first-party discussion forum, closed the 2023 request for one as `Not on the roadmap` in August 2025, and points users to Facebook and Reddit — so what ClickUp does under the word `forum` IS a category-structured, status-tracked, publicly readable board with long-lived comment threads. That shape sits outside ABA Formal Opinion 511R because the subject is the software rather than a representation, and the scoping is held by STRUCTURE rather than by rules: every category is a product area and there is NO general category, so a practice-advice question has nowhere to go. Three failure modes are named rather than absorbed — the bivalent question, screenshots (the serious one, because our screenshots carry client names, matter numbers and balances), and the long tail of specificity. A general discussion space is still not built, which is also what ClickUp concluded."
- **ADR-0279** · Proposed · 2026-08-11 · Product updates / Changelog — "The public changelog renders from `changelog.d/` — the same fragments that assemble `CHANGELOG.md` — rather than becoming a second thing to maintain, but only after the fragment contract gains a REQUIRED `audience:` key with NO DEFAULT in either direction and a `## Public` block carrying the user-facing prose of a public fragment. The filter is necessary and not cosmetic: of 87 fragments today roughly a quarter are design documents, at least fourteen say in their own words that they ship no product code, and one is a competitor build-platform evaluation — publishing those would advertise features that do not exist, which breaks version-accuracy-by-construction in the opposite direction from the one ADR-0048 guarded. Both defaults are wrong (public publishes research notes the first time someone forgets; internal fails silently and the public changelog quietly dies), so the key is required and a new fitness function fails the build without it. The 87 existing fragments migrate to `internal` in one mechanical pass now, which is an hour today and a judgement call per file at 800. Entries publish DATED, not versioned, because no releases have been cut."
- **ADR-0280** · Accepted · 2026-08-11 · Worker / Authentication — "Joining the platform is three anonymous routes and one authenticated one. Registration forks on a role the visitor declares (client of a firm, works at a firm, founding a firm); only the third writes anything, and it writes one control-plane row and sends one email, with the firm built by an outbox consumer after the emailed link is followed. Invitation redemption takes a code plus the invitee's surname and date of birth, and the three facts are stored as ONE combined digest — so the handler is structurally incapable of reporting which was wrong, and every failure is one identical refusal. Every credential attempt across the password, the two-factor code and the recovery code spends ONE shared budget keyed on the caller's address, published on the response so the sign-in screens can render a count the server agrees with; the chokepoint's 429 gained the same two fields."
- **ADR-0281** · Proposed · 2026-08-12 · Authentication — "A neutral refusal may carry facts about the caller or constants, and nothing read from the row it looked up. Invitation redemption published the invitation row's remaining attempts, and because the row is found by the code alone that number answered 'does this code exist' to anyone submitting a guessed code with invented facts — the combined three-fact digest guards the verifier comparison, not the lookup. The budget is now read from a caller-keyed attempt ladder of the same kind the credential surfaces use, the lockout countdown likewise, and the refusal is built at exactly one call site so nothing row-derived can re-enter it. The invitation's own counter and lockout are kept and made silent, and the attempt-spending write now runs on every refusal path — against an id no row holds when there is nothing to spend — so the removed difference does not survive as a timing difference. The budget the caller reads is enforced (five tries per address, then a pause), because a count the server does not agree with is worse than no count."
- **ADR-0282** · Accepted · 2026-08-12 · Trust — "Owner flag T3 is answered: the firm's own funds go into a trust account, for bank service charges only, and 'only' is made structural in five independent places rather than stated as policy. A separate `trust_firm_funds_movement` table carries no column for a client, a matter, a node, or a payee, so a row of it cannot describe a movement of somebody else's money. A second composition door (`composeTrustFirmFundsEntry`) admits exactly the firm-funds node paired with its own account's bank leaf; the client-money door still refuses a firm-funds line outright and was NOT relaxed. The firm-funds node carries the same `account_balance` zero floor a matter leaf does, which is how 'a bank charge is not borne by client funds' becomes a database guarantee rather than a comparison. A distinct `trust:firmFunds` capability, seeded to Owner/Admin alone, gates opening and funding the carve-out. And the three-way proof gains a fourth figure so a cushion is never folded into the client total and never reads as a `book-to-client` disagreement. Opening the carve-out is refused outright on a pooled interest-bearing account. No withdrawal-of-surplus movement was built: the rule text authorizes the deposit and is silent on taking it back out."
- **ADR-0283** · Accepted · 2026-08-12 · Trust — "Owner flag T16 is answered, and the answer is shaped by the escheat research finding that reframes it: in Nevada, designated client trust accounts are exempt from the unclaimed-property presumption outright (NRS 120A.500(6)(f)), nothing escheats, and the one onward destination the rules name is permissive (NRPC 1.15(f) 'may remit'). So this is not an escheat feature and there is no deadline to count down to. What ships is a dormancy READ computed at query time against a window the reader states — no stored flag, no sweep, mirroring the calendar's overdue posture — plus an append-only diligence file recording each locate attempt with the contact actually used, plus a closure that attaches a disbursement WHICH ALREADY POSTED through the ordinary authorized path. The closing composer receives no amount, payee, date, or method, so there is nothing it could assemble into a ledger entry: the manual-confirmation gate is the absence of the inputs, not a check. Deferred to a later slice: the Clients Security Fund remittance packet and certification, the jurisdiction profile's `UnclaimedFunds` type-fit repair, the separate NRS 120A regime for the firm's non-trust property, and the route and interface layers."
- **ADR-0284** · Accepted · 2026-08-12 · Product updates / Changelog — "The customer-facing prose of a public changelog fragment is folded into `docs/public-changelog.md` in the SAME pass that folds its developer half into `CHANGELOG.md`, because the fragment is deleted when it is consumed and the `## Public` block lives nowhere else — a publish step deferred to later would silently destroy the prose it deferred. That gives the public changelog one assembled home, the exact sibling of `CHANGELOG.md`: never hand-edited on a branch, but unlike `CHANGELOG.md` it is CURATED on `main`, where a dated section that starts as one bullet per fragment may be edited into the thematic roll-up a reader wants. Curation is editing what was published, never authoring a change with no fragment behind it. The surface holds NO firm data of any kind and therefore sits outside the firm-scoped data-access rule by having no business data in play rather than by taking an exception to it — a distinction worth recording, because the two community surfaces that follow are cross-firm and will need a real exception argued on its own terms. A committed generated artifact was rejected: consumers build it from the document at their own build time, so it cannot drift and needs no gate."
- **ADR-0285** · Accepted · 2026-08-13 · Document automation — "An assembly asked for a PDF converts through the registry's own `docx` to `pdf` edge inside the same job, using `runConversion` and the same per-engine-id resolver the file-converter tool executes with — no engine of document automation's own, no new registry edge, and no second answer to what a firm's document looks like on paper. The requested format is a column on `document_assembly` rather than something inferred from the filed object, because a job that FAILED still has to say what it was trying to make. A conversion that cannot be done fails the assembly instead of filing the word-processing package, on the reasoning that a member who asked for a PDF and silently received a Word document does not find out until the seal refuses it. That same recorded format is the whole of the signature hand-off on the backend: the poll answers it beside the file and version handles, so a caller knows before the document lands whether it is in the one shape `POST /api/v1/documents/seal` accepts."
- **ADR-0286** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Billing — "Every correction to a finalized bill is a SECOND permanent record and the bill's own total never moves: a credit note lands as an application against applied_minor, and what it cannot absorb becomes an open client_credit whose 'open' is derived from a frozen amount and a delta-updated discharged total under a bounds CHECK, never a status column. Which posting an application rode with is now RECORDED on the row that owns it and held by trigger — none for a trust transfer (trust's two linked entries already credited the receivable), none for a credit note (one posting per note, across however many per-matter applications it produces, including none), exactly one for every other kind — which makes the no-double-credit rule a database guarantee and lets a new fitness query prove the mirror condition in the other direction. A credit-note line may NAME the invoice line it credits, and that attribution is load-bearing: a contingency fee is billed as the increment over what earlier bills already charged, that comparison read the gross charge, and a finalized line cannot be edited — so before this the one record that resolves a shrunk basis had no effect on the report that found it. Both the composer's offset and contingencyBilledBeyondEntitlementStatement now read billed NET of attributed credits, and readContingencyResidual states the residual as an amount against the bills that carry it while issuing nothing. A reversal refuses a trust transfer (billing composes no trust statement) and a credit note (a permanent document is answered by a further correction). A matter does not close over an open client credit — hard block, no override — and deliberately does not block on an unpaid receivable. Credit-note NUMBERING is not built and is an open owner question."
- **ADR-0287** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "Ethical walls are a deferred seam (ADR-0068 point 6) and nothing in this repository implements one, so this slice cannot honor a mechanism that does not exist; what it can do is refuse to design a shape that makes honoring one later impossible, and answer the one part of the question that does not wait. The part that does not wait: the bar-audit pack is NEVER screened. It is evidence produced for a regulator about a firm's handling of client money, and a partial one is not evidence — so the access answer to a screening concern here is a narrower grant (reports:trustCompliance, seeded to Owner and Admin alone) rather than a quieter number. The part that waits is genuinely binary and both arms are bad: a complete-but-undrillable firm-wide total ties to the books while leaking the walled matter's balance by subtraction from what is itemized, and an excluded-and-untying total leaks nothing while showing a member a firm-wide figure that disagrees with the reconciliation, the receivable control account, and another member's copy of the same report. That choice changes what the platform asserts about a firm's books and is flagged for the owner rather than settled. So that the later answer is cheap, every report response carries a completeness declaration from the first sub-slice — complete: true today, shaped to carry an excluded-matter count — so a consumer can never render a screened total as an unscreened one by omission. Location stays a view and reporting filter and never becomes an access gate, per ADR-0031."
- **ADR-0288** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "Producing a bar-audit pack is asynchronous and leaves a permanent record: a POST validates and writes one append-only report_production row plus an outbox message in a single batch, a queue consumer assembles the pack, renders it through the assembly-to-PDF conversion edge that already exists, files it as a File Manager version — which computes and stores the SHA-256 content hash at write — and writes an append-only completion row. Completion is DERIVED from the presence of that row rather than from a status column, the same arrangement that keeps a status column off trust_hold and off invoice_application. Filing the artifact is what makes the surface capable of producing evidence at all: the reproducibility contract covers figures for a stated period and knowledge instant, while display names, office letterhead and jurisdiction citations are mutable captions, so the only way to retrieve the exact document a specific examiner was handed is to have kept it. The pack is bounded to one trust account and one period; heavy assembly is off the request path per golden rule 10, and a read route whose period would exceed the measured budget refuses by name and points at production rather than degrading. Whether a produced pack is undeletable and inherits the jurisdiction profile's trust-record retention, and whether it carries its own named attestation the way a closed reconciliation carries closed_by, are open owner questions recorded in the design rather than answered here."
- **ADR-0289** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "Every quantity the reporting surface prints is classified, and the class is a property of the read rather than of the presentation. Class 1 — owned elsewhere (the three-way proof, an invoice total, a matter's unpaid receivable, a trust account's client-ledger total as of a date) — is read through the owning package's own function or statement, never re-implemented, never rounded a second way. Class 2 — a decomposition of a class-1 aggregate, such as a per-client breakdown of a client-ledger total or a journal whose closing balance is a book balance — is new SQL, and each one ships with a fitness test asserting it sums to the aggregate its owner produces over the load-profile fixture book; a decomposition that cannot be proved equal to its aggregate is not shipped. Class 3 — counts, ratios, hours, distributions — is genuinely new and is the only class allowed to be original, and it is deliberately the class that touches no money the ledger has an opinion about. The load-bearing corollary is that nothing recomputes the three-way reconciliation: where a period contains a CLOSED reconciliation the pack prints the frozen attestation verbatim from trust_reconciliation, and where the live proof at the pack's knowledge instant differs it prints both, labelled, with the difference explained as ledger movement since the attestation — never a third number and never a silent preference. A negative-space regression test fails if the reporting package ever grows its own SUM over ledger_line restricted to trust node accounts, which is the exact shape that would quietly become a second client-ledger total."
- **ADR-0290** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "Every ledger-backed report reads two date axes, not one: an effective-date period and a knowledge instant (`recorded_at` / `applied_at`). Both columns already exist, both live on append-only tables, and neither is ever updated — so a report parameterized by the triple (periodStart, periodEnd, knownAsOf) is a pure function of rows that cannot change, and re-running the triple in five years returns the same integers. A single-axis read filtered only on effective_date would let a reversal posted next month with a backdated effective date silently change last month's answer after somebody signed it, which is the exact failure ADR-0246 froze the three-way reconciliation to prevent; reintroducing it in every report would undo that decision by the back door. Omitting knownAsOf is legal and means 'now', which produces a LIVE result stamped computedAt and explicitly not claimed to reproduce — the two are labelled differently in the payload and on the produced artifact, because a reader cannot tell them apart from the figures. Reproducibility is a claim about FIGURES only: display names, entity merges, office letterhead and jurisdiction citations are mutable captions carried beside stable ids, so a re-run may render different labels against identical amounts, and the audit pack states that in its own words rather than implying more than it can deliver. Reads whose underlying stores are mutable (work in progress, productivity) do not accept knownAsOf at all — the parameter is absent rather than accepted and ignored."
- **ADR-0291** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "No rollup table, no cached total, no period snapshot. The invoice derives its total rather than storing one, a trust balance is a sum over dated ledger lines, dormancy is a comparison rather than a column, and overdue is derived at read time and never written — the whole financial core is organized against stored derived figures, and a reporting layer that materialised one would reintroduce exactly the drift the rest of the subsystem was built to prevent, in the surface a regulator reads. Every aggregate is computed in SQL rather than by pulling rows into the Worker (the shape the existing aging read already documents and defends against), and per-row sections stream in effective-date order rather than buffering. The reads are bounded by account and period and served by ledger_line_account_id_index and ledger_entry_effective_date_index, which already exist. The one honest weak point is named rather than hidden: a journal over a trust bank account drives from the account index and filters dates as a residual, so a long-lived account reads more lines than the period needs. If the load profile says that matters, the remedy is a DENORMALIZATION rather than a materialisation — a copy of effective_date onto ledger_line, written by the ledger package inside the posting batch so a composite (account_id, effective_date) index can drive the read. That duplicates an immutable column of the same row's parent and can never drift, which is categorically different from storing a computed balance; it is not built now, and measuring it is part of the first sub-slice."
- **ADR-0292** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "Read-only is made a checkable property rather than an intention, through four independent mechanisms. (1) `@magna-carta/reporting` is a pure package that composes nothing: a purity fitness function modelled on packages/custodian/test/purity.test.ts scans its own sources and rejects any INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/REPLACE literal, any import of EffectWrite, composeEffectBatch, run-domain-effect or commitEffectBatch, and any import from a domain package whose name begins compose/issue/post/place/apply/open/close/finalize — with a non-vacuity assertion so it cannot pass by scanning nothing. (2) Every read route under /api/v1/reports/* is a GET through the existing chokepoint, and the conformance test asserts the namespace declares exactly one non-GET route. (3) A new reports:* permission namespace grants reads only, with a registry assertion that no reports: key appears in any write path's guard, so a future route cannot borrow one; the keys are seeded to Owner and Admin alone, on the reasoning that split trust:firmFunds off trust:manage — an aggregate across every matter and client is a different exposure from a per-matter read. (4) The one write is named and outside the package: producing an audit pack writes an append-only report_production row, an outbox message, and a File Manager version holding the artifact, all composed by the route and the queue consumer through the ordinary chokepoint with the ordinary audit trail. Stating mechanism 4 is the point — a reporting surface that could not file its own output could not produce evidence, and 'read-only' without that sentence would be a slogan."
- **ADR-0293** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Reports — "Work in progress, productivity, and realization's standard-value leg are LIVE ONLY and never accept a knowledge instant, because the stores behind them are mutable by design. time_entry and cost_entry are working documents until they freeze at billed or void — bill review is exactly when a firm fixes a narrative or a duration — and they carry a status column, no billed_at, no voided_at, and an amount_minor that is recomputed on an explicit re-rate. So an entry voided today disappears from last month's work-in-progress figure when that figure is re-run, an entry re-rated today changes it, and an entry created today with a backdated work date appears in it: 'unbilled work in progress as of 30 June', asked in August, is not answerable from stored data. Rather than approximate it, these endpoints omit the knownAsOf parameter entirely — accepting it and ignoring it would be the worst of the three options — and stamp computedAt with an explicit non-reproducibility flag that the user documentation explains in one sentence. The named remedy belongs to the slice that owns the stores, not to a reporting layer: billed_at and voided_at stamps on both work-in-progress stores, and re-rate recorded as an append-only adjustment rather than a column rewrite. A period-close snapshot is rejected as the alternative because it is a materialised figure. Profitability is separately not computable at all: no timekeeper cost rate exists anywhere in the schema, rate_card and rate being billing rates only, and it is deferred with its reason rather than approximated from what a client is charged."
- **ADR-0294** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "Beider-Morse is adopted now as a third phonetic scheme at over-inclusive strictness, meeting the flip condition ADR-0192 stated ('a measured recall gap on cross-origin surnames') by argument rather than by measurement — and saying so. The gap is structural, not statistical: Double Metaphone is an English-pronunciation encoder and Beider-Morse was built for surnames across origins and transliterations, which is the population a real Directory holds. Waiting for a measured miss means waiting for a firm to miss a conflict, and measuring the miss requires already knowing the answer the engine exists to supply — a flip condition that cannot be reached without the harm it guards against is not a gate, it is a deferral. The seam is already paid for: the scheme CHECK is length > 0, so this is additive rows plus a resumable backfill, with no schema change on the name table, and over-inclusive-only keeps the strict-superset invariant true by construction. Beider-Morse is a WRITE-time cost, so the Rust/WASM port's flip condition is a measured write-path or backfill regression rather than a matching one — and golden rule 14 binds the encoder choice: a C/C++ or compiled-to-WASM implementation stops and asks the owner rather than being adopted silently."
- **ADR-0295** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "Automatic re-checking creates a new problem the shipped engine never had: a periodic re-scan re-surfaces last month's hits, and a reviewer buried under answered questions stops reading — the reports-nothing failure arriving by a longer route. The fix is NOT to let an evidence-identical re-surfaced hit inherit its prior disposition: that writes a conclusion into the record that the named reviewer did not make on that day about that check, and the record's whole value is that every conclusion has a person behind it. Instead a hit's lifecycle is three DERIVED states (undisposed, disposed, answered-before) with nothing stored and nothing updated in place; answered-before requires BYTE-IDENTICAL evidence — same entity, tier, matched-field set, path and expiry facts — and any strengthening at all makes it a fresh question, because strengthening evidence is exactly when last month's conclusion is worthless. Bulk affirmation makes clearing the answered-before group cheap and writes one REAL disposition row per hit, each naming the AFFIRMING member and each carrying a new nullable carried_from_disposition_id — added by ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN, with no DISPOSITION_KINDS value added and no kind CHECK widened. Two structural layers stop the machine ever concluding anything: a source-scan fitness function fails the build if either re-check consumer imports disposition.ts, and a BEFORE INSERT trigger refuses a disposition whose reviewer is not a live member."
- **ADR-0296** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "Search depth stops being one integer. Traversal gains a budget per EDGE CLASS — corporate family transitive at zero cost, control/family/representation one hop each by default — plus a per-run closure cap, because the shipped unbounded corporate closure is both an availability risk and an O(entities x channels) query fan-out. Every stop is recorded: budget exhausted is on the hit, and reaching the cap writes expansion_truncated with the entities reached and the unexplored frontier, surfaced in the posture read, so a truncated expansion is NEVER reported as a completed one — the depth analogue of the shipped rule that unavailable is never searched-and-clean. Expired edges (divorced spouse, sold subsidiary) ARE traversed with the expiry carried on the path, because Rule 1.9 is about former relationships. Degree-based automatic hub suppression is refused outright — a bank on five hundred matters is where a positional conflict lives — and replaced by an attributable firm marking that stops traversal THROUGH an entity while still matching it as a party. The check row records the resolved channel set verbatim (schemes, name roles, match keys, identifier types probed and skipped, per-class budgets, cap, marked entities), so a log stays legible after the code moves under it. The N+1 goes in the same slice: one query per node table and one per blocking channel over the whole expanded set, with an equivalence test against the per-entity matcher as the refactor's correctness proof."
- **ADR-0297** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "The frozen name normalization strips '&' as punctuation, so 'Smith & Jones LLP' normalizes to 'smith jones llp' and 'Smith and Jones, L.L.P.' to 'smith and jones llp' — the same firm, no tier-2 match, and a trigram hit buried under every other Smith. Rather than change normalize-name.ts, which is stored corpus-wide and load-bearing for merge, entry-time duplicate detection and tier-2 semantics, two derived columns are added BESIDE normalized_display: normalized_match_key unifies '&' with 'and', canonicalizes legal-entity forms to one token each and drops a leading article while PRESERVING the legal form (a match is tier 2 — the same name written two ways); normalized_match_key_bare removes the legal form entirely (a match is tier 3, because LLP versus LLC may be one firm re-formed or two unrelated legal persons, and the engine's job is to surface the question). Both are computed at write time like the phonetic codes, indexed, and backfilled by a resumable sweep with a fitness probe mirroring everyNameHasPhoneticCodes. The evidence_tier CHECK is deliberately NOT widened — that is a rebuild of a growing append-only table — so the new signals map into the existing four tiers and name themselves in matched_fields_json."
- **ADR-0298** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "Conflicts re-checking becomes event-driven and durable: seven corpus write points (party linked, entity merged, entity renamed, edge created, identifier added, matter created, matter opened) plus personnel change and a cron cadence each commit ONE outbox row in the batch they were already composing, and nothing heavy ever runs in a request handler. The row is relayed to the per-firm queue, a request consumer resolves the affected matter set in one bounded indexed query and writes rows into a new conflicts_recheck_request ledger, and a separate run consumer drains them with a wall-clock budget — coalescing every cause accumulated for one matter into ONE check that names them all, never discarding a cause. The ledger exists because a pending re-check must be VISIBLE before it is satisfied, because it bounds work without dropping it, and because the interval between a corpus change and the check that answered it is the evidence a reasonable-procedures defence actually turns on. The non-triggers are decided too and stated as decisions: entity creation alone, party removal, disposition, matter close, and a policy raise all deliberately fire nothing. The trigger catalogue widens additively (the column CHECK is length > 0) and two nullable columns record trigger_source and cause_json, so an automated run is never attributed to a person who did not make it."
- **ADR-0299** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "The conflicts subsystem may assert facts about its own behaviour and never a fact about the world: it reports what it searched, what it found, and what it could not search, and it never states that a matter is conflict-free. The read side gains one derived posture model — latest check with its scope verbatim, the shortfall between that scope and the firm's CURRENT policy, pending re-check causes, undisposed counts by tier, unavailable sources, and a never-checked state distinct from checked-and-nothing-surfaced — with no boolean, no aggregate, and no field named clear, clean, ok, passed or status. Staleness is derived at read time against live policy and the live re-check ledger, never written onto a record, following the posture the calendar holds for an overdue deadline and trust holds for a dormant balance. A zero-hit check renders as a statement of the search (channels run, entities reached, sources unavailable, 'Nothing surfaced'), not a verdict on the matter. The discipline is enforced by a source-scan fitness function over the package, the routes, the OpenAPI descriptions, the web surfaces and the user docs, modelled on the shipped custodian-vocabulary lint, with 'cleared' allowlisted only as a disposition kind carrying a named reviewer."
- **ADR-0300** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "A one-time sign-in link and a typeable emailed code establish a session at `mailbox` assurance and no higher, so they can never satisfy a step-up gate — enforced by the lattice rather than by a list of endpoints somebody has to keep current. Two Better Auth defaults are inverted and PINNED BY TEST because both are wrong for an invitation-only platform: `disableSignUp` must be true (the default auto-registers an unknown address, which would be a second unguarded door into the `user` table beside a signup route that always refuses), and the plugin's documented treatment of an existing UNVERIFIED account — stripping its password and revoking its sessions on the reasoning that mailbox ownership is the source of truth — lands on exactly the state an invited member occupies between redeeming and setting a password, so an inbound link must not be able to mutate a credential. Account linking is set explicit for the same class of reason: the library links accounts sharing an address by default, and an identity provider asserting an address it does not control would otherwise inherit a password account, which is the highest-value takeover in a federated design. Requesting a link answers the same body in the same time for an unknown address, a known one, and a federated domain — the federated case mails the ADDRESS saying the firm signs in through its provider, never telling the caller."
- **ADR-0301** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "A session stops being a boolean. It carries an ordered assurance level — `mailbox` (a link or emailed code alone) < `credential` (a password, or a federated assertion with no multi-factor claim) < `multiFactor` < `phishingResistant` (a user-verifying passkey, or an assertion whose method claims are phishing-resistant) — plus `authenticated_at`, which a step-up re-verification moves WITHOUT minting a new session and which activity extension does not move. That is deliberately not Better Auth's `freshAge`, which compares against `createdAt` and is therefore the wrong clock for step-up; `freshAge` stays configured as the coarse backstop. Step-up then becomes a DECLARED property of an operation on `RouteMeta` (`stepUp: { assurance, maxAge }`), enforced at the chokepoint after RBAC and before idempotency, answering 403 with the `stepUpRequired` field the problem contract ALREADY carries for the malware-flagged download — reuse rather than a new shape. The counter-rule is stated so the gate does not metastasise: step-up is for acts that are IRREVERSIBLE or that CREATE A NEW WAY IN, and everything else is RBAC's job, which the trusted-insider model already answered. The platform does NOT claim NIST AAL conformance; saying AAL2 would assert verifier-impersonation-resistance evidence nothing here produces."
- **ADR-0302** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "Every way a principal may prove who they are becomes one row in a control-plane `authentication_method` register — kind, status, label, assurance ceiling, provenance, enrolment and last-use instants — because Better Auth spreads enrolment across `account`, `passkey`, `twoFactor` and `ssoProvider` and three of the four questions this platform must answer are asked ACROSS those tables: whether an account MAY use a method is a policy question about a principal rather than a row in `account`; whether this is the LAST method is a count over four tables; and what was enrolled in the ninety minutes after a suspicious sign-in is the takeover investigation, unanswerable without one ordered list. The register is a PROJECTION maintained by database hooks, never a second store of credential material — passwords, credentials, seeds and providers stay where the library puts them — and a Miniflare reconciliation test asserts exactly one live register row per library credential in both directions, because two sources of truth about credentials is how an account ends up holding a method nothing can see. `provenance` records what created the row (self-service, administered recovery, a domain-verified provider), which is the first question asked after a takeover and the one `account` cannot answer. Which methods an account may use resolves most-restrictive-wins over three layers: a platform floor that is CODE beside the permission registry (a floor that can be written is not a floor), firm narrowing through the existing settings registry, and actual enrolment."
- **ADR-0303** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "When a member of a federated firm types a password, silently refusing and silently allowing are both wrong — the first sends them to a password reset that either does nothing or leaves a live second door, the second gives the firm a revocation point that does not revoke. Discovery therefore resolves ON SUBMIT, on the DOMAIN of the submitted address, and answers one of exactly two shapes: `continue` (into the ordinary neutral credential path) or `redirect` (carrying the provider's display name and start URL, the password discarded UNREAD, no ladder rung spent because nothing was tested). That is not an enumeration oracle only because the map is keyed on a domain the firm VERIFIED by publishing a DNS TXT record — a fact anyone can already resolve — which is why the map may only ever be populated by domain verification and never by an administrator pasting a list of addresses. Two consequences are stated rather than discovered: the answer must be identical for a verified domain with zero live accounts, and a claim on a consumer mail domain is refused outright against a deny-list. Enforcement is `optional | preferred | exclusive`, and `exclusive` REMOVES the password from the register rather than ignoring it, because an ignored credential can still be reset and phished and later found working. `exclusive` cannot be turned on until the firm's Owner holds a passkey — the one credential that survives an identity-provider outage without being the password the firm asked the platform not to hold."
- **ADR-0304** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "A client of a firm and somebody who works at one become different kinds of principal in the schema rather than the same user row distinguished by invitation history. `user.principal_kind` is NOT NULL and immutable (a BEFORE UPDATE trigger raises), and `CHECK ((principal_kind = 'portalClient') = (firm_id IS NULL))` ties firm tenancy to the kind — which is the load-bearing half: `user.firm_id` is the column every firm-scoped surface reads to place a caller, so leaving it populated for a portal client makes a forgetful surface fail OPEN inside that firm, while emptying it makes the same surface resolve no firm and answer the reserved cross-firm 404. The kind rides the session, `RouteMeta` gains `principals` defaulting to `[\"firmMember\"]`, and the chokepoint gains a surface-admission gate ahead of tenancy that refuses an unlisted kind as absent; a route registered without the field fails at startup, exactly as an unregistered permission does. A portal principal holds no per-firm `member` row at all, so RBAC grants it nothing anywhere under any bug, and its authority comes from PortalGrant through a resolver that cannot read `member`. Portal multi-firm membership is a purpose-built `portal_membership` table naming a Directory entity, NOT the Better Auth organization plugin — which would put a second membership model in the control plane and give the session an active organization the design specifically must not have. The cost is stated: `firmId` becomes nullable and the compiler forces every reader to say what it does with null."
- **ADR-0305** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "Every recovery path is an authentication method with the ceremony removed, so it is enrolled, budgeted against the shared ladder, audited and rate-limited as one — otherwise it is the account's weakest method and the strength of every other one is decoration. A passkey defeated by 'email us a link' is a mailbox credential wearing a passkey's clothes. The mechanism is a HOLD: a recovery that would admit somebody at an assurance the enrolled methods would not have granted is recorded as pending, announced IMMEDIATELY to every channel of record plus the firm's Owners and Admins (never one channel — the attacker holds one), delayed by a window, cancellable by any valid existing session or one click from any notified channel, and on effect revokes every session AND every method enrolled since the request, so a recovered account does not silently keep the attacker's new passkey. Its converse closes the commonest takeover shape: enrolling a method is itself a notified event. The primary path is FIRM-ADMINISTERED — an Owner or Admin reuses the shipped three-fact invitation machinery — because an attributable human at the firm is a stronger control than a delay, which is why that path's hold is zero. Platform-administered recovery is bounded to the sole-Owner case, two-person, fully held, and deliberately NOT routed through the support-grant framework, which is consent-anchored and cannot stretch to somebody who by definition cannot consent. Four paths are refused outright: knowledge-based answers (a firm's data is in public court records), identity-proofing by voice or document scan, a link that mints a full-assurance session, and removing the old method before the new one is proven."
