- **ADR-0306** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Auth & access control — "The invitation fix established the rule for one route; four methods multiply the failure paths, so it becomes a test rather than a habit. Four checks bind every refusal on an unauthenticated surface: ONE refusal per surface built in exactly one function with nothing row-derived in it; any number in it is the CALLER's own and never a count belonging to the thing being probed; the work does not vary with what was found (the unmatchable-digest comparison, the balancing write, the same hashes on every path); and the difference goes to the CHANNEL rather than to the caller. `lint:refusal-neutrality` asserts that every handler reachable from a `surface: \"public\"` route touches at most one refusal constructor and that no such constructor takes an argument derived from a database read — it cannot prove neutrality, but it can prove a SECOND CONSTRUCTION SITE was added, which is how a neutral answer stops being neutral. Each method's specific oracle is closed by shape: a passkey assertion is DISCOVERABLE with an empty allowCredentials and takes no address, because an allow-list for a typed address publishes how many passkeys that address holds and their credential ids; a link request answers identically in the same time for unknown, known and federated addresses; an identity provider's own errors are never relayed; and which firms hold grants for an address is rendered only after authentication."
- **ADR-0307** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Conflicts — "The conflicts-vocabulary fitness function carries exactly one escape hatch, and only on one of its two rules. A line whose purpose is to STATE the prohibition rather than to break it — the comment on the posture read explaining why no verdict exists, the banned-phrase list in the lint's own fixture test, the invariant in the area CLAUDE.md — is exempted from the assertion-phrase rule by an inline `conflicts-vocabulary:allow — <reason>` marker on that same line, following the shape of the bucket-dimension scan's inline allowlist. The verdict-shaped-FIELD rule has no marker and no exemption at all: a field name has no legitimate meta-use in a scanned tree, and an escape hatch there would be the one that mattered."
- **ADR-0308** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Search — "The search and discovery read path gets its own standing benchmark, as a `search-eval` harness crate beside the engine on the `pdf-eval` pattern, rather than an extension of the TypeScript load-profile harness — the load profile measures per-firm database reads through node:sqlite and cannot reach a Rust Tantivy engine at all, and the two meet by sharing a percentile convention rather than a runtime. The binding rule is that the benchmark measures only paths that genuinely execute and states the rest as unmeasured: it runs against crates/search-core itself so no engine behaviour is simulated, and it refuses to time the deferred SearchIndexPort, the Container hop, R2 range GETs, the warm cache, Vectorize, or reciprocal rank fusion, printing that list before any figure and storing it inside the baseline file so a number cannot be quoted apart from its caveats. Every figure carries the corpus size, index size, hit count, and query count behind it, and the target beside it. Two axes are measured separately because they scale on different variables — corpus size with the corpus compacted into one split, and split fan-out with one split per document version as the pipeline publishes today — and their meeting point at equal document counts quantifies compaction: 45 ranged reads compacted against 186,293 for the same 5,000 documents uncompacted. The benchmark stays out of every gate as a deliberate command, under its own cargo profile because the workspace release profile optimizes for size and would understate the engine."
- **ADR-0309** · Proposed · 2026-08-13 · Search — "Index compaction is triggered by a live-split threshold, not by every write and not by a bare schedule: the write pipeline counts a corpus's live splits in the read it already makes and enqueues a bounded merge when the count crosses the threshold, and the daily sweep is the backstop for corpora that cross it through deletion or stop being written to. A pass merges at most a bounded number of splits and bytes at a time and re-enqueues itself while the corpus is still over threshold, so a matter with thousands of splits converges over several passes instead of attempting one merge no Container can finish. Publication is ordered object-then-catalog-then-retirement: the merged split is written to R2 first, one atomic batch then inserts its row and its membership and marks every source split superseded, and the superseded objects are deleted by a later sweep after a grace period — so a query holding split keys resolved a moment before the swap still finds every object it named, and a query resolving a moment after sees the merged split and none of its sources. Because a merged split covers document versions that a later re-index can supersede, the catalog records per-split membership and the query path carries a per-split exclusion list; the exclusion is what makes a superseded version stop matching immediately, and the next compaction is what physically removes it from the bytes. The trigger constants are per-firm settings rather than code, because the right threshold is a cost trade between R2 Class B reads and Class A writes that only a deployment can price."
- **ADR-0310** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Product updates — "The roadmap is a rendered output of the roadmap.d/ feature ledger rather than a document anyone maintains, on the precedent of changelog.d/ to CHANGELOG.md and status.d/ to STATUS.md, and what it renders internally is the COMPLETE register: every one of the 702 capability nodes, at the status the ledger actually holds, led by the counts across all nine statuses, with nothing collapsed and nothing curated. Statuses are never banded. Collapsing nine into five horizons was built and withdrawn because it put partial (a shipped backend with no screen on it) under the same heading as in-build (being written right now), which is the largest loss of signal this source can suffer and precisely the distinction the owner needs; the register therefore carries one column per status, a done/part-built/ahead/parked roll-up, a per-area matrix, and the shipped-versus-shipped-plus-partial gap named in prose as the program's approximate interface debt. Subtrees are printed whole rather than collapsed to their shallowest node, because a parent's status is a roll-up and its children are the actual units of work. The document states plainly that the ledger holds no history and draws no trend line from it, naming CHANGELOG.md as the only dated record of what shipped, because an invented trend is worse than a stated gap. The customer-facing rendering keeps its whole design — a human authors the durable half of a public entry and the assembler derives the volatile band from status, four optional additive ledger keys carry it, public-summary is required and never defaulted from the internal scope line, and there are NO DATES with no date, target or confidence field anywhere in the schema so there is nothing to populate under pressure — but the document itself is NOT written to disk while the product has no users, because a document nobody reads is a document nobody keeps honest; the renderer, the ledger keys, the public-copy lint and the artifact schema all stay under test so publishing is one line in the assembler on the day there are readers. Drift is held by the same two mechanisms as before: the status cannot be typed into the rendered document, and a fitness function re-derives it and fails when what is committed differs."
- **ADR-0311** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Search — "The keyword engine's RangeReader is bound to real per-firm R2 through container outbound interception rather than through storage credentials held by the engine: the Tantivy Container issues an ordinary HTTP range GET against a non-routable internal host, SearchIndexContainer.outboundByHost forwards it over a service binding into the app Worker, and a cookieless token-guarded split-object host there resolves the firm's bucket through the existing single home and performs exactly one R2 operation per call — so the engine reads a firm's index without ever holding a key to it, and enableInternet stays false. The capability token is HMAC-signed per firm from AUTH_SECRET, minted per operation, read-only for a query and write-capable only for an index or compaction pass, and authorizes ONE corpus: the allowed key prefix is derived from the token's corpus rather than carried, so a token cannot name another corpus's splits even though the request line chooses which split inside it (a query reads several, so the key cannot come from the token the way a WOPI file id does). The host refuses an unranged GET outright rather than serving an object whole, making the read count the engine was measured on structurally the read count a deployment bills. The engine keeps no split cache between calls: a cached searcher would answer from bytes authorized on a previous call, and residency is deferred to the slice that has a real resident corpus to size it against. apps/search-worker and containers/search are built; activation (image build, service binding, warm cron) stays a deploy step."
- **ADR-0312** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Search — "Discovery flag D4 — the Vectorize semantic half — stays deferred, and its trigger is a measured recall gain on a judged legal corpus rather than a deployment. The cost side is now answered: against the keyword half's measured read profile, the semantic half's marginal recurring cost for a 250,000-document review set is roughly 490 times the keyword half's, because Cloudflare's queried-vector-dimension metric counts the resident corpus once per month at twenty times the storage rate — so a queried Vectorize index costs about 21 times what pricing its storage alone suggests, a correction the earlier sizing missed. On top of that sits a one-time embedding charge of roughly $261 to $522 per review set at the platform-pinned model's rate. The convergence side is not answered and cannot be from here, and the blocker is not the deployed-only binding: the binding is substitutable (open-weights model run locally, exhaustive cosine as the upper bound on approximate nearest neighbour), while the missing piece is a legal-prose corpus carrying relevance judgements, which no deployment supplies. The experiment that settles it is specified in full — the TREC Legal Track Enron collection, its paired Boolean and request-for-production queries, overlap at k and rank-biased overlap as descriptive metrics, and recall at fixed review effort as the deciding one, with a drop/keep/flag rule written before the run. Two things are done now because they stop being cheap the moment a vector exists: the embedding model pin is re-opened, and the namespace-versus-index billing question is recorded against the partition-key decision."
- **ADR-0313** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Email — "The auto-file rule circuit breaker's thresholds become a per-firm setting on the existing firm-setting idiom, with the shipped numbers — twenty-five firings per rule per rolling hour, three undos within the last ten firings — as the platform defaults, so a firm that never touches them sees no change. The owner's answer directed that the thresholds be settable and said nothing about whether a firm may set one that disables the protection; because a circuit breaker exists to stop runaway automation, a floor is enforced rather than left open. Every threshold is a whole number inside a finite range, and the undo threshold may not exceed the recent-fire window it is counted over — a threshold above its own window could never be reached, which would leave the breaker reading as configured while never tripping. The fire ceiling is finite for a second, independent reason: the activity read walks up to the threshold plus one row per candidate rule per captured message, so an unbounded threshold would be an unbounded read on the capture path. The bounds live in the domain package beside the guardrail that applies them, the request and stored-value schema is built from those bounds, and the pure decision function re-checks them and treats a violation as unevaluable evidence, so a stored value nothing can act on stages the capture instead of filing past a guardrail that cannot fire."
- **ADR-0314** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Documentation — "The roadmap.d/ feature ledger gains a second fitness function, pnpm run lint:ledger-staleness, checking the ledger against the repository where lint:roadmap checks only the rendered documents against the ledger. It detects staleness exclusively through what a node NAMES — a path, a decision number, a child node — and never through what its prose says, because every prose-reading rule was measured and every one of them fired overwhelmingly on correct nodes. Six rules are kept: a pre-code node may not name existing code that no has-code node accounts for; an absence claim about a named path fails when the path resolves; a pre-code parent may not have a has-code descendant; every named path must resolve; every adrs entry must be an ADR-NNNN with a file; and not-started may not carry a spec. Three are rejected with their measurements — the slice-tag rule at 13 hits and 13 false, the affirmative-build-claim rule at 22 hits and 22 false, and diff-to-ledger coupling because it needs a diff base and is satisfied by touching a node rather than by making it true. The kept set produces zero false positives on the ledger and on a pre-fix checkout, catches the first of the two known stale nodes and not the second, and found four further stale nodes on its first run."
- **ADR-0315** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · AI / inference — "Model-pin rot is treated as one defect on two surfaces. The MECHANISM is built and changes no model: reasoning selection resolves through a platform-maintained evergreen tier (most-capable / balanced) whose occupant is catalog data and can be re-pointed by deploy configuration without a build — but only ever onto a model already curated, because a deploy-time string cannot carry a human's finding that a model is zero-data-retention eligible, and the catalog being exhaustive is what makes a non-ZDR model unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged. Each entry now carries TWO verification dates, verifiedOn for the confidentiality facts and availabilityVerifiedOn for the lifecycle facts, because they come from different provider pages and a single date would let the cheap check silently refresh the claim that matters; beside them sit the provider's published retirementNotBefore and supersededBy. A pure freshness pass grades findings expired (fails the gate) or due (reported only), and on 2026-08-14 reports three true findings and zero failures. It cannot detect a successor released since the last check — supersession is not derivable from in-repo data and a gate-time provider call needs network and credentials in CI — so that gap is bounded by the re-read interval rather than closed. For the embedding pin the honest finding is that NO mechanism makes it safely changeable once an index exists, so the design protects the pre-index window instead: the entry records pinState open and a fitness scan fails the moment any code reaches a vector index, forcing the choice to be made before the first vector rather than discovered after. THREE CHOICES ARE LEFT TO THE OWNER and none is taken here: whether the reasoning tiers move onto claude-opus-5 and claude-sonnet-5, what replaces claude-haiku-4-5 before its published retirement, and which embedding model the first index is built with."
- **ADR-0316** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Performance — "Custodian and calendar get load-profile volume profiles sized from the numbers their own designs state — two thousand packets over a twenty-year history span for custodian, and a decade of unevenly-spread litigation events with one matter deep in every federated source for calendar — and the branch that adds them changes no query. Both subsystems scale as designed except for one shared root cause, which the numbers make unmissable: the `custodyDueBack` predicate plans through `custody_movement_item_index` for `item_id IS NULL` rather than through the partial due-back index built for it, so a read whose answer is the 91 packets currently out walks 32,723 movements with a correlated most-recent-check-out subquery. It costs 16.2 ms of a 17.3 ms matter timeline page — every other source on that page costs between 0.03 ms and 0.21 ms — and 15.3 ms of the standalone overdue sweep. The reviews-due sweep has the same shape through the status index. Two smaller findings: the member-filtered calendar read is slower than the unfiltered window it narrows, because the participant EXISTS resolves through a single-column `member_user_id` index and filters `event_id` inside it, so its cost tracks how many events the member is on; and the register list has no index covering its `(created_at, id)` sort, so it materializes and sorts. The firm-wide window's nine scanning sources — the question the calendar design left explicitly to this measurement — are real but second-order at this corpus. A fix in the same change would have made every number in the committed baseline unattributable."
- **ADR-0317** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · AI / inference — "The inference catalog's snapshotId — the value written into every per-request provenance record — holds the provider's own published pinned identifier copied verbatim, in whichever form the provider publishes it, and stays a required field. It is not made optional or absent for models the provider names without a date, because on that provider's newer models the undated id IS the pinned snapshot rather than a pointer to one: recording absence there would assert that no snapshot exists when one does, and would null out the only field that answers which model produced a discoverable work product. Because whether a provider publishes a dated form for a given model is not derivable from anything in the repository, each entry additionally records that form (provider-dated / provider-dateless / platform-composed) as a read fact, and the entry schema refuses an id that contradicts it — which is what turns a fabricated date suffix from a plausible string into a malformed entry. That check lives at the data boundary and fails at module load rather than in the staleness pass, because an id that disagrees with its form is wrong when written rather than wrong as of a date; what does decay — whether the recorded form is still what the provider publishes — is dated by availabilityVerifiedOn and reported by the existing freshness pass alongside the other lifecycle facts."
- **ADR-0318** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Performance — "The four findings the custodian and calendar volume profiles named are answered, each against the baseline that change committed. The largest was diagnosed as a wrong index choice and is not one: `custodyDueBack` and the register's overdue sweep read the movement ledger first and joined the packet to it, so the planner's only choices were the partial due-back index and `item_id IS NULL` — and 32,723 of 38,711 movements matched the latter, each paying a correlated most-recent-check-out subquery, to answer the 91 packets that are out. Driving both statements from the packet instead costs no index at all and takes the standalone sweep from 15.30 ms to 0.24 ms and a matter timeline page from 17.34 ms to 1.74 ms. Making the partial index reachable would have been the wrong fix: it would have narrowed the outer scan from 32,723 rows to 7,312 and left the correlated subquery running once per row."
- **ADR-0319** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Trust accounting / Reconciliation — "A bank statement received as a PDF becomes rows on the bank side of the three-way reconciliation through one new producer of rows that already have a home — nothing about the statement table, the matcher, the proof, or the close changes. Four things carry the design. (1) THE CONFIDENCE MODEL IS ARITHMETIC, NOT A SCORE: a statement states its opening balance, its closing balance, and the items between them, so parsed rows that foot to the parsed closing balance are corroborated by two figures read independently from a different part of the page, and a statement that does not foot is REFUSED rather than stored — a reconciliation records a disagreement between the firm and the bank and must never record a disagreement between the bank and our reading of the bank. A recognizer's per-line confidence is retained and used only to order a member's review and to explain a footing failure; it gates nothing, because a threshold on a confidence score is the silent-and-usually-right default ADR-0214 exists to refuse. (2) AN IMPORTED LINE NEVER CAUSES A MOVEMENT, on four independent grounds: it carries no client and no matter, and trust attribution is derived from the matter's own client party rather than accepted from a caller; the bank leg is the only independent input in the proof, so letting it post would make the reconciliation compare the bank statement with itself and destroy the one signal that means something reached the ledger without going through the composition guard; a movement caused by a parse has no authorizer, and `trust_movement.authorized_by` is a table CHECK rather than a convention; and it is a strictly larger authority than automatic matching, which is already refused. Nothing needs building to enforce this — `trust_bank_statement_line` has no relation to the ledger, and the only join is a match row a member created. (3) PROVENANCE IS RETAINED, NOT RECOMPUTED: recognition is server-canonical rather than cross-architecture deterministic (ADR-0179) and model pins move, so re-running extraction next year is a fresh reading and not a reconstruction — the original PDF, its digest, the extraction artifact with its geometry and engine provenance, the parser version, the resolved layout profile, the parsed rows with a locator back into the artifact, and the confirmed preview fingerprint are all kept, which also repairs the delimited importer, whose confirmed file text is dropped after parsing today so that an already-imported statement cannot be re-derived from anything the platform kept. (4) PARSING RUNS WHERE THE PLATFORM ALREADY PARSES: the native text layer in pure Rust as WASM in the Worker over `crates/pdf-core`, pages that are scans in the OCR Container that is already deployed and network-isolated, and table reconstruction in TypeScript beside the money rules it must obey. No new dependency is introduced and no new C or C++ surface — the mature PDF table libraries are Python or sit on a large C++ parser reading untrusted privileged bytes, and reading a statement with a model is refused because its output is not reproducible, because a model that produces both the rows and the balance can produce a self-consistent fiction that no arithmetic check would catch, and because a client's bank statement through a model is a consent question rather than a parsing detail. Nine flags (S1-S9) are left to the owner, four of which decide what gets built."
- **ADR-0320** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Dev tooling — "The build register's \"where we have been\" is reconstructed from version control rather than accumulated by hand: for every first-parent commit that touched a `roadmap.d/` node file, the ledger at that commit is parsed by the same parser the live build uses, and the counts are persisted one record per commit — keyed by the full commit hash, dated by committer date — in `docs/register-history.json`. An ordinary `vp run register-build` reads only the commits that file does not already hold (normally one), so the expensive pass runs once; `--rebuild-history` recomputes the series from scratch, which is how a change to the parser or the status vocabulary reaches the past. A commit whose ledger does not parse under the current schema is recorded as unread with the parser's own reason and rendered as a hole: nothing is interpolated across it, the changes either side are stated across the hole rather than apportioned to it, and the document says how many commits are missing and why. No completion date is projected from any of it, because the ledger puts no size on a node."
- **ADR-0321** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Document automation — "A shipped court form carries the court it belongs to as VALUES — state, court level, and reach (statewide, judicial district, or county) — plus the proceedings it covers, so a wrong-court filing is expressible as a refusal rather than left to a naming convention. The check lives on the assembly path, not on adoption, and produces THREE distinct refusals rather than one: no court recorded on the matter, a court whose forum is unknown, and a court that disagrees with the form's. The middle one refuses rather than passes, which is the load-bearing half — a fallback there would make the binding decorative on exactly the matters where the data is thin. That refusal is only useful once courts carry a typed identity, which nothing in the platform records today: `matter_court_case.court_entity_id` points at an untyped `directory_organization`. So the court identity is build work scheduled AHEAD of the forms rather than a seam named beside them. A form whose merge keys the dictionary cannot supply is likewise not shippable — it declares them and stays blocked, listed."
- **ADR-0322** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Document automation — "A court form the platform ships is compiled reference data in a pure package, on the `@magna-carta/trust-jurisdictions` model — not a row in any firm's database and not something provisioning writes — so a correction is a package release rather than a migration against every tenant. A firm ADOPTS a form, which registers an ordinary template through the existing registration pipeline and ingest chokepoint; from that moment the copy is the firm's and the platform never edits it. Two provenance columns on `document_template` record where it came from, and a later corrected revision SUPERSEDES rather than replaces — the firm is told and offered a new version, because overwriting a template somebody edited is the data-loss shape the assembly design already refused. The platform authors its own template from prescribed statutory or rule text and cites the authority, rather than redistributing a court's published document: of the six Nevada publishers surveyed, the two whose forms a firm most wants both assert copyright expressly, one of them a private nonprofit, while the Nevada Revised Statutes assert nothing and prescribe several forms' operative words verbatim."
- **ADR-0323** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Document automation — "A shipped court form whose `attorneyReview` is null cannot be adopted at all — the route refuses `court-form-not-reviewed`, and the whole Nevada starter set ships NOT REVIEWED, exactly as the Nevada trust profile does. Review is per form rather than per library, because forms are revised one at a time by different authorities and a library-wide review would be invalidated by any single amendment; and a review binds a content-derived revision, so amending an entry re-closes the gate. An unreviewed form is LISTED with its state rather than hidden, because 'do you have a Nevada notice to creditors' should be answered 'yes, awaiting review' rather than 'no'. Staleness is our obligation expressed as a check that fails: no Nevada source emits a machine-readable currency signal, so each entry declares a re-verification interval and a CI fitness function fails when one is past due. `Provenance`, `SourceTier` and `AttorneyReview` move to a shared `@magna-carta/legal-provenance` package rather than being written a second time."
- **ADR-0324** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Reports — "Two rules the reporting design holds simultaneously turn out to collide in the bar-audit pack: every ledger-backed figure is bounded by the report's knowledge instant, and no figure another package owns is ever re-implemented. The three-way proof, the outstanding-item list, and the ledger's stored-balance fitness query are each owned elsewhere and each take no knowledge instant, so a pack cannot both call them and bound them. The pack calls them, groups them into two blocks that carry their own `computedAt` and an explicit `boundedByKnowledgeInstant: false`, and states on its own face that those blocks are the only part of the document that may differ between two runs of the same period. Every other figure reproduces, and the reproducibility test strips exactly those two blocks and holds the rest to byte-identical integers across a backdated correction. The rejected alternatives were both worse: a bitemporal copy of the proof would be a second definition of the reconciliation a firm attested to, and omitting the live comparison would remove the surface on which a firm sees that a backdated posting moved its books."
- **ADR-0325** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Document automation — "A legacy Word form field — a w:fldChar complex field whose instruction is FORMTEXT, FORMCHECKBOX, or FORMDROPDOWN and which carries a w:ffData block — is detected at template registration, recorded on the field manifest as its own legacyFormFields list, and never bound or converted. Before this it contributed no merge key at all and nothing reported its absence, so a template built entirely from them (the appellate court forms, and a great deal of legacy firm precedent) registered clean, declared no keys, warned only no-placeholders — 'every assembly of it produces the same document' — and filed carrying the form's own 'Click here to enter caption'. The reporting half follows the unconverted-MERGEFIELD precedent exactly: recorded rather than refused, because an unregisterable template is one nobody can inspect, and surfaced at registration, in the field list, and by every assembly, which lands on succeeded_with_absences. The conversion half is refused on the engine's existing name-guessing rule with worse evidence than that rule already turns down: a MERGEFIELD carries a name the firm chose and can confirm through the field-mapping library, while a form field carries a Word-minted bookmark (Text1) beside a sentence of help text, so a derived merge key would be a silent binding decision on privileged content inferred from an identifier nobody chose. The list is kept separate from unconvertedMergeFields rather than widened into it, because blending them would offer 'register again asking for conversion' as the remedy for a construct conversion does not answer. Whether the product should ever offer such a conversion is left open as an owner decision, and w:documentProtection surviving the metadata scrub is recorded as a related, unfixed surprise."
- **ADR-0326** · Proposed · 2026-08-14 · Tooling — "A source-scan fitness function, pnpm run lint:control-characters, rejects every raw C0 byte and DEL in tracked source other than tab, newline, and carriage return, and requires the escape form instead. The argument is legibility, not correctness: the raw byte is invisible in a file read, in git diff, in a review pane, and in most editors, so the source reads as one thing and means another — a separator written as a literal NUL read as .join('') to two separate readers on the same day and produced a wrong diagnosis of a real defect. Ten files carried eighteen bytes and every one was converted rather than exempted, including the tests whose subject IS the byte, where the escape is clearest. The scan covers Markdown, which the process-reference scan deliberately exempts, because a code sample in a document is copied into source and one spec already carried a NUL in a cache-key sample. It self-tests on every run, on the ledger-staleness idiom: over invisible bytes, a check that has quietly stopped firing is indistinguishable from a clean tree. The escape hatch is a same-line control-characters:allow marker with a required reason, and nothing in the repository needed it."
- **ADR-0327** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Document automation — "A content control (w:sdt) that the document itself declares unfilled — one whose properties carry w:showingPlcHdr, or one carrying w:dataBinding — is detected at template registration, recorded on the field manifest as its own unboundContentControls list, and never bound or converted. Before this nothing in the package looked at w:sdt at all, so a template whose blanks are content controls registered clean, declared no keys, warned only no-placeholders — 'every assembly of it produces the same document' — and assembled into a document printing 'Click here to enter text.' with zero absences reported. The reporting half follows the legacy-form-field precedent exactly and reaches the same five surfaces: registration warning, no-placeholders suppression, the version field list, the refusal, and every assembly, which lands on succeeded_with_absences with the control named in frozen_field_names. The conversion half reaches the same answer on a DIFFERENT argument, which is the thing this decision settles that the form-field one did not: a content control carries w:alias and w:tag, names an author typed rather than a Word-minted Text1, so the objection is not the quality of the evidence — it is that a name means something only once a human has confirmed it through the field-mapping library, and that every rewrite of a firm's uploaded document here is opt-in. A control that is neither placeholder-showing nor data-bound is deliberately silent, because Word wraps ordinary content in controls constantly and a filled one is content. The list is a third one rather than a widening of the form-field list, because the two constructs stand for different futures: one is dead and replaced, the other is what this engine intends to bind. An opt-in that maps an alias through the mapping library is left open as an owner decision."
- **ADR-0328** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "A trust-destined payment is two independent ledger entries with no link between them at the entry level — the gross deposit through composeTrustEntry, and the processor fee as an ordinary operating expense entry that touches no trust account. They cannot be joined by an entry, because an entry joining them is exactly the commingling the composition guard refuses, so they are joined by a payment record instead. The processor fee for a trust-destined payment therefore lands on the firm's operating account, borne by the firm, always. The firm's cushion inside the trust account may not bear it: the second door's category is closed at firm_funds_deposit and bank_charge, and a card processor's discount rate is not the institution's service charge. Netting is refused by assertTrustComposition; a firm depositing its own money onto a client's matter leaf to cover a fee is refused by name, because that shape IS expressible through composeTrustDeposit's free-text source and is bare commingling wearing a client deposit's face."
- **ADR-0329** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "A payment carries no status column: state is derived from an append-only payment_event log, the same way a pending disbursement is a request no decision names and an active hold is a hold no release names. A status column and an event log disagree the first time a webhook arrives out of order, and the column is the one that gets believed. The webhook is the platform's first inbound one, so the idiom is specified: signature verification over the RAW bytes before any parsing, with a constant-time compare and a replay window; the firm named by an unguessable per-firm path segment, because the signing secret is per-firm and the body is attacker-controlled until the signature verifies; runIdempotentEffect keyed payments:{adapterId}:{remoteEventId} rather than the Idempotency-Key header a processor does not send; and UNIQUE (adapter_id, remote_event_id) as the commit-time guarantee rather than a read-then-write pre-check. idempotencyMiddleware must never be mounted in front of a webhook route — it reads the body through context.req.json() and consumes the raw stream the signature is computed over. A scheduled settlement pull re-asserts the processor's truth, because webhooks are lost."
- **ADR-0330** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "A refund is never a deletion. A full operating refund is an invoice_application of kind `reversal`, which already exists, already requires a stated reason by trigger, and is already UNIQUE on reverses_application_id so a double reversal loses at commit. A PARTIAL refund cannot be a partial reversal for exactly that reason, so it is a full reversal plus a fresh smaller payment application in one batch — the shape trust already uses, where a partial release is a release plus a new smaller hold. A trust refund is a disbursement composed and authorized in the trust package, and payments composes none of it: the same boundary billing holds when its reversal refuses a trust transfer by name. And a chargeback's ledger entry carries the date the chargeback happened, never the original payment's — backdating would leave the LIVE proof for an already-closed reconciliation disagreeing with the frozen attestation for the same period, with nothing recording why."
- **ADR-0331** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "The jurisdiction fact that would settle whether a processor fee may reduce a client's trust principal is unresearched on the only jurisdiction the platform ships. trust-jurisdictions already carries the typed requirement — processorFees.mayChargeClientPrincipal, in the `data` bucket, which may never resolve from silence — and Nevada records it as GAP: four sources were searched for `credit card`, `processing fee`, `merchant`, `surcharge` and `processor` with no hit, and the ethics-opinions index was never worked through. Rather than research it, pick a default, or block the subsystem on it, the design implements only the arrangement that is safe under EVERY possible answer: the fee is borne by the firm on its operating account and never touches client principal, so the permission the field would grant is never exercised. Every arrangement that WOULD need the field to resolve — netting a fee out of a deposit, a merchant-fee cushion inside trust on the Arizona model, a transit account with a transfer window on the Texas or Maine model — is refused by name until the field resolves and an attorney reviews the profile. The corpus's refusing-rather-than-defaulting posture is inherited, not softened."
- **ADR-0332** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "An operating card or ACH payment posts against a payments-clearing account at capture and clears against the operating bank at settlement, so one settlement entry corresponds to one bank line. Capture is Dr Payments Clearing / Cr Accounts Receivable, named by the invoice_application of kind `payment`; settlement is Dr Operating Bank (net) plus Dr Merchant Fees (the fee) against Cr Payments Clearing (gross). Posting gross straight against the operating bank and the fee as a second entry would make one bank line correspond to two entries in opposite directions — the shape a one-line-clears-one-entry reconciliation cannot express, and the shape trust already records as unexpressible. Two new firm_account_role members, payments_clearing and merchant_fees, resolved through the existing map, which refuses by name when unmapped. The trust rail has no clearing account and cannot: a clearing account is not a trust account, so an entry pairing it with a matter leaf is refused."
- **ADR-0333** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "Recording that money arrived is never gated on the money fitting an invoice. The invoice_matter_balance bounds CHECK aborts a whole D1 batch when an application exceeds what a bill can absorb — which is correct and must not be weakened — so composing the receipt and the application in one batch would mean an over-payment aborts the record of money the firm has already received. Receipt and application are therefore two acts: the ledger entry posts, and the invoice_application of kind `payment` is composed in the same batch when it fits and not at all when it does not. Money received and unapplied is a visible reportable state, which is what it actually is. This resolves partial payment (ordinary), over-payment (received, unapplied — a client credit is unavailable because client_credit.credit_note_id is NOT NULL and SQLite cannot relax it without a rebuild the schema policy forbids), and payment against a bill that can absorb nothing (refused by name, money kept)."
- **ADR-0334** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Payments — "A processor serving the trust rail must deposit each payment to the trust bank account gross and individually, and an adapter that cannot declare it in its capability descriptor returns capability-unsupported for that rail. The binding reason is reconciliation, not compliance: composeTrustReconciliationMatch ties one statement line to one ledger entry, so a batched net deposit covering ten clients' payments leaves nine entries outstanding forever and one bank line unmatched, and the three-way proof reports a difference every month until a firm learns to ignore the one report that would also catch a real misappropriation. The gross-deposit rules want the same thing, but the reconciliation is the reason that will be forgotten first, so it is the reason recorded. The operating rail is unaffected and settles through a clearing account instead."
- **ADR-0335** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Client intake — "A lead and a prospective matter are one record, not two: the matter shell already carries a responsible attorney, a title, a type, parties, documents and calendar events, and the custom-fields engine extends it, so a separate lead entity would duplicate all of it and then need a migration at conversion that moves documents — the specific harm avoided by making each consultation its own matter. Conversion is the shipped open transition and intake adds only the preconditions in front of it; it draws the number inside the matter's own atomic batch and there is no second write path to an open matter. Conversion is one-way and stays one-way: un-converting would either release a drawn number back to a counter that a primary key exists to prevent, or leave a gap a firm's numbering audit reads as a missing file. The correct undo for a mistaken conversion is closing the open matter. The engagement letter and the fee agreement are hand-offs to the packages that own them, in that order, after the number exists."
- **ADR-0336** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Client intake — "A prospect is not a record — it is a reading of two records the platform already has: a Directory entity, and a matter that has not drawn a number. No prospect table, no prospect status, no prospect category. The Directory placement is forced rather than chosen, because the conflicts engine's four blocking channels are Directory tables and there is no narrower home a prospect could occupy and still be checkable from first contact. The distinction between a prospect and a client is carried by the absent matter number, which is structural rather than a status column: the number is the join every money subsystem reaches a matter through, so a prospective matter has no invoice, holds no trust money and carries no portal grant — not because a permission check refuses it, but because there is nothing to join on. What a prospect can therefore do to the rest of the platform is exactly one thing: join the conflicts corpus, permanently."
- **ADR-0337** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Client intake — "The platform ships the shape of a lead pipeline and the firm defines the stages — per-firm ordered rows, soft-deactivated rather than deleted, seeded at provisioning — on the same pattern as matter types, party roles, Directory categories and custom fields. The binding rule is that a stage carries no authority: it is a label and a sort order, it gates nothing, permits nothing, and is never read by a precondition or a permission check. The moment a stage gates conversion, a firm renaming a stage moves an ethics gate by editing a text column and a firm deleting one removes the gate entirely; the things that gate are platform-owned and unrenameable. A stage change is append-and-flip so a lead's progress survives, and nothing about a stage survives conversion — an open matter has no stage, because the funnel ended."
- **ADR-0338** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Client intake — "An inbound enquiry lands as an `intake_submission` — a record that is not a Directory entity, not a matter, and not in the conflicts corpus — and only a named member's audited act turns one into an entity plus a prospective matter. The boundary is drawn at triage rather than at conversion because the Directory is effectively permanent: it soft-deletes only, and a merged subordinate deliberately stays in the corpus so a merged-away alias still surfaces a hit. Anything an anonymous stranger can write into the Directory is in the conflicts corpus forever, and a corpus full of bot submissions is a corpus a reviewer stops reading. Nothing automated promotes a submission — no queue consumer, no cron, no rule engine — and a submission may not carry a protected identifier at all, enforced by the form field vocabulary having no kind that maps to a protected field declaration rather than by validation."
- **ADR-0339** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Client intake — "Opening a matter out of intake is gated on four conditions the conflicts posture read already computes — no check has ever run, the latest check's scope falls short of the firm's live floor, a surfaced hit carries no disposition, or a re-check request is open — and on nothing else. Every one is a fact about the firm's own procedure rather than a conclusion about the client, so the gate concludes nothing and the engine's refusal to return a verdict is preserved intact. A matter with fourteen tier-1 hits, each disposed by a named partner with a rationale, passes; a matter with zero hits and no check does not, and that asymmetry is the whole argument. The scope condition costs nothing because the shortfall is derived against the live policy row on every read, so raising the firm's floor fails the gate on every affected matter immediately with no sweep and no write. Whether the gate is a hard refusal or an override is the owner's call; an override is a named member, a mandatory rationale and an audit row either way."
- **ADR-0340** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Client intake — "The public intake submission path performs no lookup against firm data — it writes one row and returns — so its answer cannot depend on the content of the submission. One response for a name the firm already represents, a name adverse to a current client, an unknown name, a repeat submission and a switched-off form. The rule is enforced by construction rather than by answering identically after a lookup, because a form that consults the Directory answers measurably slower for a name with candidates and a probing script gets its oracle from the clock. No duplicate detection, no entity resolution, no conflicts check, no matter-type routing that could refuse an unsupported area of law; every one of those is available a moment later on the staff side of triage, where the caller is a member and the reveal is authorised. A rate-limit refusal may report the caller's own budget, which is a fact about the caller and not about the content."
- **ADR-0341** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Tasks and workflows — "The platform ships no matter-plan templates and every plan is firm-authored, for the reason the calendar refuses to compute deadlines: a plan is a statement about how this firm practises in this jurisdiction, so a platform-shipped plan with a missing step or a step that is right in one state and wrong in another is a malpractice vector carrying the platform's name, trusted precisely because it shipped. What ships is an empty catalog and an authoring surface; if a curated set is ever wanted the pattern already exists in the tree as jurisdiction-keyed reference data with attorney review in the loop, and that is a research workstream rather than a seed migration. Instantiation is a one-time copy with no live link: instantiated tasks carry the template identifiers as provenance and never as a reference the read resolves, so editing or deactivating a template does not touch a task on an open matter — a template edit that silently moved a due date on a live matter would be a docketing change nobody authorized and nobody saw. Nothing reconciles: there is no drift report and no plan-has-changed prompt, because both are the live link wearing a disguise. Sequencing is a single blocked_by predecessor that renders and sorts and gates nothing, because a platform that enforces order on a checklist is a platform people stop using the checklist part of."
- **ADR-0342** · Superseded — 2026-09-01, by the owner · 2026-08-15 · Notes and activity — "A note is a chain — an identity row carrying the matter, the original author and the created instant, plus revision rows each carrying a body, its own author and its own instant. An edit appends a revision and moves the head; every prior body is retained. Retraction is a state with its own actor, instant and reason: the body stops rendering everywhere and drops out of the federated timeline entirely, while the row and its revisions remain. There is no hard-delete path, no route and no capability, and the one case where bytes genuinely must leave — an erasure obligation, or another person's protected data typed into a note — routes through the deferred erasure orchestrator under the PII registry's report-manual action, which exists for exactly this and neither auto-erases free text nor silently refuses. The reasoning is that a note is neither the ledger nor the calendar: the ledger is append-only because a third party relies on it and the firm demonstrably could not have changed it, which no rule of conduct demands of a note; the calendar edits in place because it records a plan, and a firm made to file a compensating row to fix a typo stops recording — but a note is a contemporaneous record whose evidentiary weight comes from having been written then, by that person, saying that, and editing it in place destroys the property that makes it worth keeping."
- **ADR-0343** · Accepted — built · 2026-08-15 · Notes and activity — "Two containment rules, stated as rules because both failures would look reasonable to whoever wrote them. No merge field ever resolves to a note or a comment on any record — the document-scrub engine removes document-level PDF surfaces and is structurally blind to merge-filled text, which is page content, so a catalog entry resolving to a note body would put internal work product into the body of a letter assembled under the firm's letterhead and the one machine built to stop privileged text escaping in a PDF would not see it; the guard is a source-scan fitness function over the merge-field catalog, landing in the same slice as the note table. And notes stay outside the discovery collection vocabulary — review_document.source_kind is a three-value CHECK over file versions, email messages and email attachments, so a note is structurally uncollectable today, and that default is defended rather than inherited: widening the enum would put every candid internal assessment one bulk load away from a production set with only a process-level quality gate between them, so a note genuinely ordered produced is exported as a document into the matter's file record and collected as that document, which gives it a version, a hash and a custodian of record and makes production a deliberate attributable act."
- **ADR-0344** · Accepted — built · 2026-08-15 · Notes and activity — "A note's author is a plain foreign key to member.user_id and it never nulls, never repoints, and is not anonymized when a member leaves the firm. An unattributed contemporaneous record is worth a fraction of an attributed one, the foreign key is what the federated timeline reads as the entry's actor, and every sibling subsystem here already made this choice — custody movements, trust movements, calendar events and deadlines, conflicts checks and filed email all name their actor as a member reference and none of them clears it, while matter_assignment deliberately keeps ended rows because who carried a file is the matter's record. What changes when a member leaves is presentation and not storage: a note authored by a former member renders with a former-member signal derived from a read-time join on member.status, never a stored flag, on the same reasoning that keeps overdue out of the calendar's schema — a fact that is a join cannot drift from the row it joins. The one case that genuinely moves is a member who is themselves the subject of an erasure request, where the member row anonymizes to a tombstone and the author reference correctly points at it under the PII registry's pseudonymize-keep action, with the note body handled separately as report-manual because it may name the departed member and no automated pass can safely rewrite prose."
- **ADR-0345** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Tasks and workflows — "A matter-plan template carries a due-date anchor and an integer offset rather than a date, instantiation renders a proposed civil date, and nothing is written until a member accepts — which is the propose-and-confirm interaction the calendar already reserved for the statutory catalog it has not built, reused rather than reinvented. Three things keep the offset from being the date engine this platform spends a subsystem refusing to build: the arithmetic is plain calendar-day addition in the matter's zone with no business days, no holiday calendar and no service-method offset, which is not the derivation the calendar forbids; the anchor vocabulary holds exactly one real value, matter_opened, and deliberately has no service-date, filing-date or trigger-event anchor, because each of those is the front half of a docketing rule and adding one invites the back half, so the vocabulary is a CHECK constraint that grows only by a reviewed migration; and acceptance is the write, so no obligation exists on a matter that a person did not read. This also narrows the white paper's auto-instantiated-when-a-matter-opens promise on purpose — eleven dated obligations written onto a matter nobody read is the trusted-because-computed failure again — while leaving automatic instantiation available as a per-template firm setting rather than a platform default."
- **ADR-0346** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Tasks and workflows — "A task is its own record with an assignee and a completion state, not a calendar event and not a deadline — the continuance discipline that makes a rescheduled hearing two rows is exactly wrong for a due date somebody changed their mind about, and diluting the deadline table with reminders would degrade the one surface where a missed entry is a malpractice event. A task may carry an optional link to the event that prompted it and to the deadline it serves, and both are links and never inputs to a calculation. The date finding matters more than the modelling: there is no date engine in this platform to reach. The calendar states as a capital-letter invariant that nothing computes a deadline date — no business-day arithmetic, no holiday calendar, no service-method offsets, no cascade when a trigger moves — its time-zone module states that it never derives a date from another date, and the docketing ledger node records the absence as a deliberate position rather than a gap. So a task stores the three columns a deadline stores, a civil date, an optional wall-clock time and a required zone, resolves them to an instant through the calendar's own civil-date resolver with a dateless task due at the end of its civil day, and computes overdue at read time with no column, no flag and no sweep — an argument that is stronger for tasks than for deadlines, because tasks are numerous and a nightly sweep across every firm's is precisely the scheduled work the platform keeps off itself."
- **ADR-0347** · Accepted — built · 2026-08-15 · Notes and activity — "A record's activity stream is the calendar's existing federated timeline read with one more declared source on it, not a new subsystem and not a projection table. packages/calendar already reads one page across fourteen sources under one comparable sort key, one shared cursor and one required visible-matter predicate, and its registry states that a source is a read-side declaration and never a write-side obligation on the subsystem that owns the rows — so a note joins it for one registry row, one statement builder, one projection and one label decision, with no change to any write path anywhere. The package's own invariant decides it: nothing is projected, copied or backfilled, which is what makes the class of bug where the timeline disagrees with the register unwritable, because the timeline IS the register. Three consequences are accepted rather than discovered: a note source declares no label and projects NULL, on the precedent that a filed email's subject line and a time entry's narrative are both refused; the source reads the note table directly and orders by when the thing it records HAPPENED, falling back to the day it was written, which is how every other record of an occurrence is ordered here; and a note on a Directory entity cannot join at all until a third visibilityAnchor with its own predicate is designed, because the firm anchor is a reviewed one-entry allowlist and reaching for it would be a silent loosening of the exemption the package guards most carefully."
- **ADR-0348** · Accepted · 2026-08-15 · Notes and activity — "An internal matter note and a client-visible portal comment are two tables with two identities, not one table with a visibility flag, and there is no path by which one becomes the other. A boolean would make publishing attorney work product to the opposing party's most interested reader a one-character diff, and an irreversible one — flipping it back does not unsend what the portal already rendered. Beyond that the two differ in kind: a note's author is a firm member while a comment's may be an external principal that the portal places in the control-plane database as a distinct principal type never mixed with staff access control; a portal grant's scope vocabulary is matter, folder and file, so a comment is reachable through a grant and a note is reachable through nothing, and that structural unreachability is the actual protection rather than a WHERE clause; and the two carry different retention and different discoverability postures. Sharing a note with a client is therefore a new comment authored by the member at the instant they authored it, never a state change on the note — a publication decision made in June must not alter a record made in January. Decided now because the portal is unbuilt and nothing depends on the answer yet, which is the cheapest this decision will ever be."
- **ADR-0349** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Tasks and workflows — "Tasks register a close ADVISORY on the matter shell's advisory hook and never a close precondition, and the advisory carries a count of open tasks and no titles. The matter shell ships both hooks as deliberately separate mechanisms — a precondition blocks and may contribute an in-batch commit guard, an advisory returns notices that ride the successful response with no arm on which one becomes a refusal — and the advisory file's own reasoning is this exact case: one boolean away from each other, a subsystem that only meant to inform starts blocking closes on a Friday afternoon and the firm learns to route around the close screen. Trust blocks a close because holding client funds on a closed matter is a fiduciary failure; an open task is a reminder somebody wrote three months ago, and a matter that cannot close because of one produces a firm that cancels tasks it never read. Nothing happens to the tasks themselves: they are not cancelled, completed, deleted or swept, because closed is archived-but-additive and a close that mutated a dozen unrelated rows would contradict that shipped invariant. They stay open on the matter and drop out of the default my-tasks read through a filter on matters that are not closed, so reopening a matter restores them with no backfill and no state to repair."
- **ADR-0350** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Notifications — "The matter-team notification audience resolves to the matter's responsible member unioned with every member holding a currently-valid matter_assignment row, in one clamped statement, and no longer substitutes to the responsible member alone. The responsible member is an unconditional arm of that read rather than an assignment lookup, because matter.responsible_member_id is that fact's one home and the assignment write path refuses a role that normalizes to it — the attorney who owns the file is structurally incapable of appearing in the assignment table, so resolving the team from assignments alone would omit exactly the person most entitled to hear. That is also what settles the unstaffed-matter case: a matter with no assignments resolves to its responsible member rather than to nobody, because a team of one is a correct answer where an empty one silently drops a critical notice such as a quarantined file. The two arms filter membership differently on purpose: the responsible arm is byte-for-byte the responsible-member resolver's read so the two audiences cannot disagree about the same person, while the assignment arm additionally requires an active member, because an assignment row outlives a member's departure by design and this audience reaches email where a deactivated member's feed does not. The validity window is read exactly as the document-assembly staffing read reads it — a creation bound is a civil date inclusive of its whole day, an end-assignment bound is the ending instant and exclusive — so an assignment that staffs a document also staffs its notifications rather than the platform carrying two answers to who is on a matter. substituted now reports false for this audience and no registered type declares audienceSubstitutedFrom; the field and its counter stay for the next audience that needs them, with a fitness test holding today's emptiness to being deliberate."
- **ADR-0351** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Documents — "The storage meter keeps no Analytics Engine trend, and the port declared for one is deleted rather than left standing. As shaped it could not have been implemented: it wrote a raw data point indexed by the firm id in clear, into the dataset every other firm handle is HMAC-pseudonymized before reaching, and it laid out its own blob columns beside the declared metric catalog that exists to be the only door into that dataset. The question behind it — how a firm's storage grows — is real and belongs in a periodic snapshot row in the firm's own database, where it is durable, firm-scoped, and readable by the surface that already reports current totals; Analytics Engine holds roughly ninety days and is a cross-firm aggregate store by policy, so it could not answer it even with the disclosure problem solved. A declared port nobody fills is worse than no port, because everything built on top of it assumes the data exists."
- **ADR-0352** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Communications — "Email filing now commits an `email.filed` outbox row in the same batch that puts a link in `filed`, on both paths that reach that state, and the in-process `raiseEmailFiledNotification` no-op is deleted rather than kept beside it. Nothing subscribes to a function call; a call fires for a batch that rolled back; and it fires only from whichever write path remembered to make it — so the raise was not a body swap away from working, it was the wrong mechanism. Keeping the no-op as a documented call site, which the notifications design proposed, would leave two apparent mechanisms at one call site, which is how a codebase acquires a second convention nobody chose. Writing the row also forced the emission to be honest: the accept route committed an audit row and would have committed a topic on a repeat accept whose guarded UPDATE promoted nothing, and answered 200 filed over a link somebody had unlinked."
- **ADR-0353** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Integrations — "The integration framework gains a fourth `Dispatch` failure reason, `credential-store-not-implemented`, for a deployment that has no per-firm credential read at all — distinct from `not-connected`, which is about a firm that has not connected an account. The per-firm secret-store seam stops throwing: `SecretStoreForFirm` returns `SecretStore | null`, the worker hands the registry the framework's `unbuiltCredentialStore` value when it is null, and every credentialed resolution answers the new reason instead of the registry construction blowing up on whichever request first needed a credential. Shipping maps it to `501 not-implemented` on connect, rate, purchase, track and refund; drafting a shipment deliberately still succeeds, because a draft is the firm's own addresses and parcel and needs no carrier. The seam itself stays unbuilt — this makes its absence legible rather than fatal."
- **ADR-0354** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Conflicts — "Conflicts graph traversal is budgeted per edge class (corporate family transitive at no hop cost; control, family and representation one hop each by default) rather than by one depth integer, and bounded by a per-run closure cap defaulting to 500 entities — both firm-configurable settings, both resolved per run and recorded verbatim on the check row. The load-bearing rule is that reaching the cap stops the walk and makes the check record that it was TRUNCATED, with the entities reached, the caps in force, the refused frontier, and the reached-but-unexpanded count; the posture read carries it and the report says in words that the search of the graph is incomplete, so a truncated expansion is never reported as a completed one — the depth analogue of the shipped rule that `unavailable` is never `searched and clean`. Degree-based hub suppression is refused: the bound is on the SIZE of the closure, where reaching it is recordable, never on the SHAPE of the graph, where stepping around a hub leaves nothing on the record. Expired edges are traversed with the expiry carried on the path step, judged against the check's own clock. The N+1 is removed in the same change rather than after a measurement — one subject-facts read per node table over the whole closure, one batched edge read per traversal round, and each blocking channel run once over the closure — and the batched matcher is gated by an equivalence test proving it returns exactly the per-entity matcher's hit set over a real corpus before the per-entity path is retired."
- **ADR-0355** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Conflicts — "A conflicts check gets a SECOND bound, on candidates, beside the one on the closure, because a measured corpus sweep shows hits scaling as closure times corpus rather than as closure: with the closure pinned, candidates per subject rise linearly with the Directory at roughly six tenths of the organization corpus for an organization subject and a tenth of the person corpus for a person, and about ninety-nine per cent of that volume is candidates whose only signal is trigram overlap. `candidateCap` joins `closureCap` on `ConflictsPolicy` at a default of 5,000, is applied to the similarity channels' output in run-check rather than inside either matcher (so the batched-versus-per-entity equivalence proof stays about the whole answer), keeps the strongest evidence when it must refuse, never refuses a co-party surfacing, and records candidates surfaced, written, refused, and refused-by-tier on the check row where the posture read and the report statement both carry it. A channel-level bound on the trigram query and a cheaper trigram generation were both considered and rejected. Separately, `composeEffectBatch` stops spreading its statement list — an argument-count ceiling around 125,000 that moved with stack depth — and refuses at a stated 10,000-statement ceiling naming the remedy; `candidateCap` is clamped below that ceiling so the two bounds cannot collide. The recommended closure default of 500 is unchanged; what the sweep says needs an owner's decision is the trigram channel's recall, not the cap."
- **ADR-0356** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Discovery — "Discovery processing gets its own `DISCOVERY_PROCESS` queue lane, and the platform's one text-extraction port becomes genuinely composite: the pure-Rust discovery engine reads natively, recognition is asked only about pages the engine reports as pixels, and the consented office container converts a legacy binary so the memory-safe engine can read the CONVERSION rather than the 1997 bytes. The port's three dispositions are unchanged and gain a separate diagnosis field, because what the pipeline should DO about a failure and what a firm has to be able to SAY about it are two facts — `encrypted` and `unsupportedFormat` are both terminal, and a corpus that could only record `unextractable` could not tell a court which one it was. Every extraction records its ROUTE on the review document, which is the fact that was missing when an OCR-only port recorded born-digital PDFs as unreadable and nothing in the corpus could say why. A document nothing can read is a named exception code a reviewer sees; a document an UNDEPLOYED engine has not read yet parks at `pending` with its reason on the row, because those are different sentences and only one of them is a verdict."
- **ADR-0357** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Dev tooling — "The gate's two-tier runner choice stands — `ubuntu-slim` for the short, single-threaded jobs and `ubuntu-latest` for everything that needs cores, memory, privilege or more than fifteen minutes — because `ubuntu-slim` is a generally available GitHub-hosted label requiring no plan and no enablement, and because it is provably not the cause of the outage that has kept the gate from executing since 2026-08-05. The outage is an account-side billing block that refuses to start any billable job at any label, established by three independent observations: a run on `main` that failed identically when `gate.yml` had thirteen `ubuntu-latest` jobs and no `ubuntu-slim`, a second workflow with no `ubuntu-slim` failing the same way throughout, and a live experiment that moved the root job to `ubuntu-latest` and reproduced the failure exactly. Reverting the labels would therefore triple the runner rate and fix nothing. The decision this records for the future is a classification rule, because the failure is invisible at the altitude anyone reads it: a job reporting `runner_id: 0` with an empty `runner_name`, concluding in seconds with a log endpoint that 404s, never started, and a change under it is UNVERIFIED rather than broken — a distinct state from a red build, carrying a different response (check the account, not the diff). The guard is deliberately a recognition rule in the runbook rather than a workflow job, because any in-workflow canary would be killed by the very condition it exists to detect."
- **ADR-0358** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Notifications — "A notification type declares which production path raises it — `outbox-topic`, `comparison-sweep`, or `job-coordinator` — rather than the registry inferring it from the emission point's name, and each path gets its own index so a type can only ever be materialized by the raiser that owns it. The comparison path is a per-firm pass on the daily cron that calls the calendar's shipped overdue arithmetic and the custody register's own `collectCustodyReminders` rather than restating either, buckets its dedupe key by the civil date in the SUBJECT's zone (the court's for a deadline, the platform's for a register with no zone), caps each kind at 200 findings with the clamp counted, and reports per emission point on the same telemetry metrics the queue-driven path already uses. `calendar.deadline.horizon` is deliberately left unwired because no firm setting names a lead window, and a lead the platform picked for a firm is a lead the firm did not choose."
- **ADR-0359** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Calendar — "The calendar's three matter-event write paths gain real outbox topics — `calendar.event.scheduled`, `.rescheduled`, `.cancelled` — written in the same atomic batch as the row they describe, with payloads of two identifiers and nothing else. `rescheduled` is raised only when the edit actually moves the setting (its dates, its times, or its zone), never on a title or room correction; a continuance raises exactly one `rescheduled` naming the NEW setting rather than a cancellation plus a scheduling, because one real-world thing happened. The three topics are spelled once in `packages/calendar/src/notifications.ts` and the frozen emission-point list is built from those constants, so a producer and a subscriber cannot come to disagree — which matters more than it looks, because an unrecognized topic on the per-firm bus is acked and ignored by design and a misspelling therefore stops delivery silently instead of throwing."
- **ADR-0360** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Notifications — "A job coordinator's terminal state additionally writes a durable notification for the member who asked for the job — the row for the tab that was closed while it ran — and the dedupe key carries the terminal outcome as well as the job, so a job the stuck-alarm failed and that then reported success announces both rather than leaving the timeout's row standing as the last word. It is built for `DocumentAssemblyJob`, the coordinator that records a requester, and deliberately NOT for the other four: `ConversionJob`, `SearchIndexJob`, and `BundleBuildCoordinator` record no requester at all, and `BundleBuildCoordinator` structurally cannot — it is single-flight across concurrent identical requests, so one job has many requesters and naming one would reply to the wrong person. `RecoveryJobCoordinator` does record `startedByUserId` and is registered but unwired, because its five terminal transitions sit on the platform's most sensitive read path and centralizing them is a refactor that belongs in its own change."
- **ADR-0361** · Proposed · 2026-08-15 · Authentication — "Human verification on the platform's anonymous writes is a port with a three-valued outcome — verified, refused, unavailable — resolved by the presence of a Turnstile secret and never by a construction-time throw, so the not-configured path is the default in every environment and is reachable and tested rather than theoretical. When no verifier is provisioned the protected endpoints FAIL OPEN: they serve exactly as they did before the gate existed, and every unprotected pass records a `human_verification.decided` point with outcome `unenforced`. The rejected alternative, fail-closed, is one deployment variable (`HUMAN_VERIFICATION_POSTURE`) away and is covered by its own test, because the choice is a security posture with a product consequence rather than an implementation detail. The reasoning is that these endpoints include the platform's ONLY self-serve way in, so a fail-closed default would make the act of adding abuse protection its own outage — before the owner has touched the Cloudflare dashboard — while fail-open leaves the endpoint exactly as protected as it is today and converts the gap from an unticked runbook box into a number somebody can alert on. A verifier that is live but does not answer, or that rejects our own secret, is NOT covered by this posture: it is a retryable 503, because reading an outbound failure as a pass would mean anyone who can break one call has turned the gate off, and reading it as a refusal would blame a visitor for our fault. Owner ratification is requested on the fail-open default specifically."
- **ADR-0362** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Notifications — "A member's per-category notification preferences ride one `notifications.preferences` scoped-setting key, and the two channels compose in opposite directions: in app the firm's tier is a floor a member may not lower, and by email the member's tier wins because a firm may not force privileged-adjacent metadata into a mailbox it does not administer. Two categories — security and trust — are non-silenceable in app whatever either tier stores. Because a category set `inApp: off` writes NO feed row, a suppression would otherwise be indistinguishable afterwards from a notification nobody raised: so the fan-out writes a `notification_in_app_suppression` tally (member, category, civil day, whose choice, count — and nothing else) beside the rows it did write, and reports a `notifications.suppressed_by_preference` counter. The firm's trust-compliance designation joins the recipient set of every trust-category notification without granting access, and the visibility filter still decides at read what those rows may show — which is why no delivery row is written at fan-out for a member who cannot see the matter."
- **ADR-0363** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Build & tooling — "The type pass of record stops being a statement about `src/` and becomes one about the repository: every graph member gains a generated `tsconfig.test.json` beside its `tsconfig.build.json` — `composite` so a solution can reference it, `noEmit` so it escapes the `rootDir: \"src\"` that declaration emit forces on the build project, referencing that build project so every import resolves to a declaration already built rather than re-checking source. Its `include` is DERIVED as the package's own `include` minus `src/**` and `.svelte`, so the two projects together equal exactly what the package declares and the existing `--check` diff-gate holds that equality; `vp run typecheck` builds both roots. One member is deferred and named rather than quietly skipped: `apps/worker`'s 224 test files do not finish a single check in half an hour where all 70 others together take seconds, which is the same route-hub fan-out that makes its type-aware lint a whole-repo lint, and it rejoins the solution when that shape is broken up."
- **ADR-0364** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Notifications — "The notification email digest carries a fixed matter-free subject holding a count, a line of per-category counts, and one link back into the product — and nothing a person typed: no matter title, matter number, client or party name, filename, email subject line, deadline description or date, custody number, amount, or invoice number. The boundary is structural rather than remembered: the digest read selects a category, a kind, an instant, and two handles, and the renderer's only parameters are a closed category label, integers, a cadence label, and a deployment-configured origin, so there is no argument a title could arrive in. `matter-number`, the owner-directed detail level under a firm's ceiling, stays declared and unbuilt and renders as `counts-only` — a stored ceiling is honoured at its most private reading rather than silently rewritten.
