A summary is composed per member per cadence from a keyset watermark through the SAME shared read the feed and the unread count compose, so the wall binds at delivery time in one implementation rather than two; it SENDS FIRST AND MARKS SECOND, because a duplicate summary is an annoyance where a lost one is a missed deadline; a member with nothing visible in the window gets no email at all; and every row a run did NOT mail is settled as a `suppressed` `notification_delivery` row naming a closed reason, so a member who chose not to be written to stays distinguishable from a member the platform silently failed to write to. Outbound mail is an unprovisioned seam that reports a typed not-configured reason rather than resolving to the dev logging adapter, which would have recorded `sent` for mail nobody received."
- **ADR-0365** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Document automation — "The asset-schedule starter template that closes slice 4·3·3 is NOT built, and the reason is a missing mechanism rather than missing content: nothing on `main` distributes a document template the platform authored. There is no committed template package, no template seed in either migration lineage, no template role, no adopt route, and `document_template.source_form_key` does not exist — the only `.docx` in the repository is a LibreOffice container test fixture. The document-automation design named cross-firm template distribution as an open question to settle 'before the first real client', and the answer to it was subsequently designed in the Nevada court-form library (a pure reference-data package a firm ADOPTS through the ordinary registration pipeline, with two provenance columns and supersede-not-replace), whose build is gated on unanswered owner flags N1 through N5. Building a bespoke second distribution path for one asset schedule would pre-empt that answer, duplicate the machinery, and leave two ways a firm receives platform-authored content. The register's own half is complete — eleven asset subjects with `itemFields`, one batched source, and the repeating-block vocabulary all ship — so a template a firm authors and uploads works today; what is missing is only the supplying of one. Two owner questions are named: whether shipped starter content rides the court-form adoption path once N1–N5 clear, and what an asset schedule should actually say, which is a product opinion about a legal document rather than an engineering choice."
- **ADR-0366** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Core domain — "`custom_field.target` gains `matterAsset`, and the change ships with no migration — the column is a bare `text NOT NULL` with no CHECK, drizzle's `enum` option is a TypeScript type that emits no SQL, and the lineage-head snapshot records no enum for the column, so `drizzle-kit generate` emits nothing and `check-snapshots` reports the head already current. The 4·3·3 plan asked for 'one additive migration, coordinated with the Custodian custodyPacket widening so both values land in one'; the schema says there is nothing to migrate and therefore nothing for the two widenings to share, so they are decoupled and `custodyPacket` is left to the Custodian stream to add when it needs it. What the absence of a CHECK actually means is that the database will never refuse an unrecognised target, so the whole of the safety is in the readers: the vocabulary moves to one home (`CUSTOM_FIELD_TARGETS` in @magna-carta/custom-fields), the drizzle column, the define-field request schema, and every scoped read derive from or are pinned to it, and a fitness test asserts the column's `enumValues` equals the constant. The merge catalogue's `target = 'matter'` filter is deliberately left alone and is now covered by a test written over the whole target vocabulary, so a fourth axis arrives excluded from documents by default and has to be argued in."
- **ADR-0367** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Discovery — "The discovery load manifest and exception report are generated by pure builders that take the knowledge instant as an argument and read no clock, walk every set keyset-ordered by identifier, and derive nothing from the environment — so the same load reported twice is byte-identical, which is what makes an artifact handed to another party a record rather than a snapshot. The instant is the load row's `updated_at`, the moment its last counter moved and therefore the moment the corpus stopped moving. Deduplication groups on the content hash the ingest gate recorded (or the email record model's dedup key), never on the near-duplicate fingerprint, which collides by design; primacy is taken by one guarded statement and held by a partial unique index, the duplicate row survives with its own custodian, its own load, its own terminal state and its own inherited exception codes, and an occurrence row makes it reachable. An archive expanded in the discovery load path becomes a family whose entries each re-enter the ordinary ingest gate; the archive is `processed` with no text object, entries the engine described rather than opened become an exception carrying a count and a kind but never a path, and expansion is bounded to one level per document because the engine already recursed to its own nesting ceiling. Three deliberate deviations are recorded: the manifest carries three digests rather than the design's five, engine VERSION pinning is replaced by the extraction routes the corpus itself observed, and an exception row may sit on a document whose terminal state is `processed`."
- **ADR-0368** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Build & CI — "A check that scans the whole repository is broken by a change made anywhere, so it never lives in the package the change touched — which makes a diff-scoped gate structurally incapable of running it. Three changes reached `main` red in one session through that hole while the whole-repo tier that would have caught all of them was unavailable. `pnpm check:scoped` therefore runs the repo-wide set itself, in two labelled steps: the derived governance lints always (~0.7 s, run concurrently), and a Vitest fitness sweep whenever the diff touches first-party source (~2.5 s, under its own config rather than the root workspace, which is 2.5 s against 8.5 s for the same files). Membership is derived rather than listed — the lints are enumerated from the `lint:*` scripts in the root package.json, and the fitness tests self-register with an `@repo-wide-fitness` directive in their own header — because a hand-maintained roster of repo-wide checks is the same bug one level up. Two lints are excluded with their numbers: `lint:licenses` (1.6 s, dependency-set-only, so it runs on a manifest or lockfile change) and `lint:test-collection` (26 s, CI's own job)."
- **ADR-0369** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Core domain — "Natural-language document search is built and merged with every gate closed and the retrieval route deliberately absent, splitting the feature at the line where evidence runs out rather than where the work does. Everything that does not expose a query surface ships: the consent gate behind a firm-level versioned disclosure whose acceptance the write path refuses without, the per-matter exclusion that deletes rather than stops, the derived text feed written as a second consumer of the extraction the search index job already performs, the instance lifecycle with its derived opaque name and single versioned configuration constant, catalog-driven deletion with reconciliation, the usage meter, and the audit and error-pipeline events. The adapter that calls the managed index does not, because the index's metadata-filter semantics have never been observed against a real instance and a filter that fails open or is accepted-and-ignored cannot bear even the recall-optimization weight the design assigns it. Three decisions inside that split are recorded here rather than left implicit: the not-configured seam is a typed outcome selected on the absence of configuration and reachable through the production resolver, never a throw and never a clean empty success, because an adapter that resolves with no results is indistinguishable from a corpus in which nothing matched; the two metadata fields the managed index declares but the platform's own object-dimension reservation forbids writing (client, confidentiality_scope) are declared-and-unwritten rather than written, resolving a live contradiction between the design and a shipped invariant in favour of the invariant; and the embedding pin that the first provisioned instance would freeze is still open, so the management binding stays commented out and the pin must be frozen before any instance exists. The owner's 2026-08-07 override of flag A6 is built to: enablement gates on the firm's versioned disclosure, not on Cloudflare general availability."
- **ADR-0370** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Conflicts — "Two derived match keys join `normalized_display` on `directory_name`: `normalized_match_key` canonicalizes connector words and legal-entity spellings while KEEPING the legal form, so `Smith & Jones LLP` and `Smith and Jones, L.L.P.` are one name at tier 2; `normalized_match_key_bare` removes the form, so `Smith & Jones LLC` meets them at tier 3. Both are nullable columns derived INSIDE `insertNameStatement` from the row's own normalized display, so none of the eight name-writing paths can omit one, with a resumable budgeted backfill on the daily cron and a fitness probe reporting the outstanding count. BEIDER-MORSE IS NOT SHIPPED, and the reason is measured rather than argued: the only maintained pure-TypeScript implementation (`bmpm` 0.1.1, MIT, zero runtime dependencies — golden rule 14 was never triggered) compiles its rule tables into regular expressions at module load, and inside workerd that costs 20,377 ms on the first request that touches it. Node does the same work in 16 ms, so this is a workerd property, not a library defect, and 20 seconds exceeds the Workers CPU limit outright: the channel cannot run in a request handler at all, on the write side or the read side. The recall it would buy is real and was measured before it was cut — over a thirty-pair cross-origin surname corpus it reaches seven pairs Double Metaphone structurally cannot — so it stays the named follow-on, now with a numeric flip condition rather than a hopeful one: an encoder whose rule tables cost workerd single-digit milliseconds, which in practice means the Rust/WASM port the deepening design already nominated."
- **ADR-0371** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Conflicts — "The set of name roles a conflicts check compares against stops being an implicit consequence of the strictness word and becomes an explicit firm setting, resolved with the rest of the scope and recorded verbatim on the check row. `ConflictsPolicy.balancedNameRoles` ships the deepening design's recommended default for owner flag F1 — current, former, maiden and alias — so the married-name change that was invisible at the default band is now found there, and answering F1 the other way is one setting edit rather than a release. over_inclusive always searches every role and is deliberately not configurable, which keeps the superset invariant true by construction whatever a firm configures. `aka` and `dba` stay over-inclusive-only, because the reading the default encodes is 'the same person under a name they previously held' and a trading style is not that. The check row also stops relying on the preset word for the rest of the story: `inputs_json.matching` records the name keys compared, the phonetic schemes that ran, and the resolved roles, so a reader of a years-old row does not need to know what the word meant that day. The flag stays OPEN — the mechanism is the contribution and the default is the design's recommendation, not an answer."
- **ADR-0372** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Financial Core — Billing — "A scheduled or batch billing run writes one row per target, including every target nothing happened to, with a closed outcome label plus identifiers and never a sentence — so a run that touched four hundred matters and skipped twelve can say which twelve and why, months later, to somebody who was not there. The recorded skip is also the convergence: UNIQUE (schedule, period) and UNIQUE (run, matter) mean a period is decided exactly once, so a skip that wrote nothing would draft on the next pass and produce a second bill for one period. The counterpart rule is what keeps that safe: a run row records a DECISION ABOUT THE WORLD and never a failure to read it. A firm database that threw writes nothing, is quarantined by the existing per-firm catch, and is retried tomorrow — without that branch one bad afternoon becomes a permanent skip and a firm never gets that month's bills. The item row commits in the same batch as the work it describes, which is what makes a half-completed run resumable with no stored cursor: the resume predicate is the items with no outcome."
- **ADR-0373** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Financial Core — Billing — "No billing automation moves client money, and the reason is structural rather than a scheduling deferral. The trust authorization posture is built on an actor: resolveTrustAuthorizationDisposition takes a UserId, the seeded posture is that an authority holder acts alone and anybody else routes to one, and the per-firm triggers hold the same predicate at the database. A cron pass is nobody, and minting a platform principal would mint one that can release client money and cannot be held to anything. The payments design reaches the same wall from the other side: a stored card token is a protected() column, so decrypting one is a permission check plus a committed audit row against a principal an unattended context does not have. Automations are therefore split into three tiers by what the act does to somebody outside the firm — internal and reversible (a draft, a record), becomes-evidence-or-is-told-to-a-client (finalize, deliver, remind), and money moves — and an unattended pass acts freely in the first, only under a firm setting pinned to a recorded human authorization in the second, and never in the third. A source scan over the automations module proves it, on the precedent of the scan billing already carries against trust."
- **ADR-0374** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Financial Core — Billing — "A billing automation writes a durable billing record of what it decided and emits an outbox topic; Notifications subscribes as an ordinary outbox-topic type and delivers. It does NOT use the comparison-sweep production path, for two concrete reasons. Convergence: a reminder step must fire once per invoice ever, and the notification dedupe key is a UNIQUE index on notification_event — a row the nightly retention sweep DELETES on the firm's window — so a reminder whose only convergence is that key re-raises the day it ages out. Completeness: MAX_COMPARISON_FINDINGS_PER_KIND caps a comparison at two hundred findings per firm per pass, and a capped reminder is a bill nobody chased. The comparison path stays for conditions the owning package deliberately refuses to store, which is what it was built for; these conditions ARE stored, because a firm has to see what was decided on its behalf after the notification is gone. This is Notifications' own rule — a notification is a pointer and never a record — applied where it lands."
- **ADR-0375** · Proposed · 2026-08-16 · Document automation — "A firm-supplied fillable PDF becomes a second TEMPLATE KIND rather than a second subsystem: `document_template_version.kind` is `wordProcessing | pdfForm`, defaulting to the existing value, and the two share the catalogue, the immutable version ladder, the ingest gate, the field-manifest shape, the binding index, the dictionary, the formatters, the absence model, and the provenance row while sharing no engine. The binding rule is the load-bearing decision: an AcroForm field binds to a merge key ONLY through a mapping a human confirmed, stored in the firm's existing `document_template_field_mapping` library — and the exact-name match is refused as well as the near one, offered as a `proposedMergeKey` that nothing applies. Every terminal field the form declares comes out either bound or on a fourth manifest list with one of three reasons (`no-confirmed-mapping`, `not-fillable`, `unclassified`); a field the engine cannot classify is never defaulted to text, and a form that binds nothing warns that every document from it is the blank form. The absence model is unchanged, with one honest divergence: a tick box and a set-answer box have nowhere to print a marker, so an unanswerable one is left unanswered with a reason rather than ticked. Whether a filed form is flattened or interactive is NOT decided here — it is a required, typed `FormFillFinish` parameter at the fill seam, answered in one worker constant that is `interactive` today because flattening is irreversible."
- **ADR-0376** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Billing — "Invoice delivery is an append-only `invoice_delivery` row per attempt — channel, recipient reference, a firm-supplied civil delivery date, an optional external reference, an outcome, and who recorded it — never a `sent` status on the invoice, because a bill goes out possibly several times by several channels and a status would force the finalized row to stay mutable for its least load-bearing field. The record is a RECORD, not a transmission: nothing renders an invoice or carries one to a client, so every row is a delivery the firm performed out of band and is reporting, which is what makes the compliance question answerable for the firms that hand bills over at meetings, post them, or submit them to a client's own e-billing portal. It is surfaced and never a gate — `readInvoiceDeliveryState` rides the invoice detail read so the apply-funds surface cannot forget it, and finalization alone gates an earned-fee transfer, because a hard delivery gate would be satisfied by firms recording deliveries that never happened and would convert a compliance record into a checkbox. The channel vocabulary is closed at five (`email`, `post`, `hand`, `client_portal`, `e_billing_gateway`) with `client_portal` enumerated before anything can produce it, since SQLite cannot widen a CHECK without a destructive rebuild of a financial-evidence table. Two triggers bound what a row may describe — a delivery names a finalized bill, and cannot predate its issue date — and three hold append-only, including the BEFORE INSERT guard that catches `INSERT OR REPLACE`. Whether a delivery recorded in error may be superseded by a correcting row is left to the owner, and the `outcome` vocabulary ships as `delivered`/`failed`, a floor rather than an answered set."
- **ADR-0377** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Notifications — "The UNIQUE index on notification_event.dedupe_key sits on a row the retention sweep deletes, so the guarantee it carries is duplicate suppression bounded by the firm's window and not a permanent record that a subject was ever notified about. Confirmed as the intended contract rather than a defect, on the subsystem's own evidence: the delivery design frames the key as a redelivery floor throughout, and each production path's required lifetime is far shorter than the thirty-day retention floor — an outbox row is published once and flipped out of pending so the relay never re-carries it, a comparison's civil-date bucket is derived from the pass's own instant so it can only move forward, and a job reports each terminal state once inside its own life. No shipped consumer depends on once-per-subject-forever semantics, and the platform already routes conditions that need it away from this key: billing's automations put their convergence in invoice_reminder rows and recorded why. The decision is therefore to name and enforce the boundary rather than build a durable key table: each production path declares a convergence horizon in a Record keyed on the raiser union, so a fourth path is a compile error until it states one; the rule that a condition needing once-per-subject-forever convergence carries it in the owning subsystem's own tables and points a notification at that record is written into the subsystem's own home rather than only into another subsystem's design; and a fitness test states the semantics out loud by re-inserting a key the sweep took, then proves the comparison bucket follows the clock. A durable key table is rejected: it would trade a bounded pointer store for an unbounded one to serve no consumer that exists."
- **ADR-0378** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Financial Core — "The trial balance, balance sheet and income statement refuse by name rather than degrading: a trial balance that does not foot, a balance sheet that does not balance, trust asset and client-liability sections that do not net to zero, and any account carrying postings that no section would place. An unclassified account is the interesting one — dropping it silently means the statement does not foot, and lumping it into 'other' asserts a classification nobody made, so it refuses and names the accounts that need classifying, the posture the unmapped account role and the unresolved jurisdiction already take. Classification-on-the-account is safe because an entry names its accounts by handle, so a rename or a renumber restates no historical figure; RE-SECTIONING is the hazard, because a statement is assembled from classification at read time and moving an account across statements would silently rewrite last year's profit. It is bounded by making the section vocabulary a refinement of type with a DDL CHECK per type, and by refusing a type change on any account that carries lines — so a re-section can only move an account WITHIN one statement. Statements are bitemporal in the shape financial reporting already shipped and defended, and the temporal contract moves down into a shared leaf so exactly one validation of a period-plus-knowledge-instant triple exists rather than two. This slice OWNS these figures under the reporting layer's own class-1 discipline and reporting renders them without re-deriving; nothing is materialised, no closing entries and no period snapshot, and the performance relief held in reserve is the effective-date denormalization onto ledger_line argued from measurement rather than up front."
- **ADR-0379** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Financial Core — "Period close is a single monotonic lock date rather than a table of period rows — one never closes March while February stays open, so a scalar makes the illegal state unrepresentable where period rows make it representable and then need overlap and gap validation to police it. It lives in its own table rather than a firm setting, because a setting is reachable by the generic settings route and a lock any settings write can move is not a lock. The check lives INSIDE postEntry, loaded with the account snapshots it already reads and asserted by a pure leaf: a lock only the general journal honours is not a lock, and period membership is a property of the ledger's own effective_date column — the same class of rule as no-future-dating, which validateEntry already enforces — rather than a consumer rule. The consequence reaches other slices and is deliberate: a trust movement or an invoice finalization backdated into a closed period refuses by name. A REVERSAL ALWAYS PASSES, dated into the open period, which reverseEntry's fresh caller-supplied effective date already makes possible; without that exemption a firm that closed a month with a mistake in it would have to reopen the month to fix it, which makes reopening routine and the lock meaningless. The close-versus-posting race is CLOSED rather than accepted: the posting batch aims a value the lock column's date CHECK forbids at the row only when the lock has moved since the read, so a stale read aborts its own whole batch — billing's refuse-on-frozen-work pattern — with the fitness query kept as the backstop rather than the mechanism. Retained earnings is computed as income less expense to the cutoff and never posted: closing entries would have to be reversed on reopen and re-posted on every re-close, turning a derivation into a state machine."
- **ADR-0380** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Financial Core — "A vendor bill records what the firm owes the vendor; a cost entry records what the firm may bill the client. They are different obligations with different lifecycles, so accounts payable writes both and the ROUTE commits them in one D1 batch — recordVendorBill returns its statements, the costs slice's capture returns its own, neither package imports the other, and a fitness query proves a matter-tagged hard line always carries its cost entry. The debit routes on the line's disbursement class and misrouting it is a tax error rather than a cosmetic one: a hard cost debits Client Costs Advanced with no profit-and-loss impact because an advanced client cost is a loan to the client and not the firm's deductible expense (Canelo v. Commissioner), while a soft cost debits the line's own expense account and is reimbursed as income. This closes the negative Client Costs Advanced position billing documents as open. The link lives on vendor_bill_line.disbursement_entry_id rather than as a fourth cost_entry.source value, because SQLite cannot widen a CHECK without a destructive rebuild of a table holding data — the earlier design got this wrong — and because provenance derived from a link is the same shape reversed-status already takes. The column is not named cost_entry_id: the schema conformance gate refuses that name. A bill's state is DERIVED, with no status column: an append-only payment table plus a bounds-checked paid_minor delta, the invoice_matter_balance arrangement, so two payments racing for one remainder abort rather than overpay and a guarded UPDATE — which cannot abort a batch — is never used as the guard."
- **ADR-0381** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Integrations — "QuickBooks is a sync and never a system of record, and the property is made structural in four ways rather than stated as a policy. The accounting integration category's CapabilityDescriptor declares EXPORT OPERATIONS ONLY — there is no pullJournal, no applyRemoteEntry, no fetchBalance, no reconcileAgainstRemote — and a fitness assertion rejects any operation name in the category matching an import shape, so a capability that does not exist cannot be called by a future route. There is nowhere to put a remote figure: no quickbooks_id on ledger_entry, no external_balance column anywhere, no table reconciling the two books. The only remote state stored is an append-only accounting_export_dispatch row recording what was sent, when, and what the remote said — it records that a fact LEFT and has no column that could hold a fact that came back, which is the load-bearing schema decision, because the day a remote balance is rendered beside the platform's own figure a reader has two answers and no rule about which is right. On conflict the platform does one of two things and says which: an export refusal is a dispatch outcome, recorded and retried through the outbox and surfaced through the standard error pipeline; a divergence caused by somebody editing the remote is NOT DETECTABLE and is not simulated, because detecting it requires reading remote balances, which is exactly the read that would make the remote authoritative — so the user documentation says in plain words that the platform's books are the firm's books and an edit made on the far side does not come back. A source scan asserts no domain package imports the adapter port and that the port returns no monetary figure into one. A bank feed is the one import that would be legitimate — a bank statement is evidence rather than a competing book — and it is reserved with no table, no port method and no column, because reserving a shape for an importer whose reconciliation surface does not exist would be designing the retrofit in."
- **ADR-0382** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Financial Core — "Matter and location dimensions go on ledger_line rather than ledger_entry, because one vendor bill carries lines for two matters and a rent expense has an office but no matter at all. This amends the ledger's account-only account model without replacing it: ACCOUNTS ENFORCE BOUNDS — the per-matter trust leaf stays a child account row, because the no-negative invariant is the account_balance bounds CHECK, which is per balance row and has no dimension equivalent — while DIMENSIONS ENABLE SEGMENTATION. Practice area is deliberately not a column: the referent is matter.matter_type_id, reachable by join once the line carries the matter, and denormalizing it is a cache under the measure-first rule. Timekeeper is excluded because the grain is wrong — at invoice finalize many timekeepers' work aggregates into one receivable line — and realization reads the work-in-progress store where that grain lives. The honest half is a scope-out rather than a caveat: the general journal and vendor bills populate the columns and the trust, billing and cost paths are NOT retrofitted, so they write null, which is additive and breaks nothing. A per-office or per-matter income statement assembled from a dimension the income-producing paths do not write would be silently partial, so those statements are NOT DESIGNED in this slice. The columns land anyway, because a nullable column on the line is cheap now and expensive to retrofit into every posting path later, and because the paths this slice owns populate them from the first day."
- **ADR-0383** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Financial Core — "The firm's operating books are metadata on ledger_account — a code, a statement section, a system-account marker — over the one append-only ledger, not a second ledger; there is one posting primitive and a source scan asserts the general-ledger package composes no entry, line, or balance write of its own. The general journal is a third door onto that primitive and is the shape that would defeat everything trust built, because the ledger is account-agnostic by design and a balanced entry naming a matter leaf and an income account would post client money into fee income. So the journal is made incapable of naming a trust account rather than taught the trust rules: every line resolves through trust's OWN loadTrustAccountRoles and the whole entry refuses by name unless every line is outside trust, with a fitness query re-proving it over stored rows (it catches the account registered as trust after a journal posted to it, which no pre-check can). The same refusal binds the vendor-bill and bill-payment composers. A chart MAY and must classify a trust-registered account for statement presentation — a trust bank account is an asset and the client-trust liability is an equal liability, and omitting them hides the one relationship an examiner checks — but MAY NOT map one to any firm_account_role, MAY NOT re-section one (the two reserved trust sections are derived from trust's registration, not stored as a firm's choice), and MAY NOT re-type one. There is deliberately NO is-trust flag on ledger_account: registration is already stored in three places and a fourth would be free to disagree with them silently in the one direction that matters."
- **ADR-0384** · Proposed · 2026-08-17 · Financial Core — "The operating general ledger adopts billing's shipped firm_account_role map as THE account resolver and adds no second one. The phase-3 slice map's fourth coordination pin expected the chart and resolver to be co-designed with 3·5 landing first; billing landed first instead, with nine role keys, a resolver that refuses by name when a role is unmapped, and a configuration composer, and the payments design extends the same map with two more roles. A semantic_key column on ledger_account — the earlier design's answer — is refused because it would be a SECOND map from meaning to account, editable independently of the first: a firm could carry semanticKey 'accountsReceivable' on one account while firm_account_role pointed at another, both correct in their own surface, and one of them is where the money went. Three changes to the seam that exists: the map moves into a pure leaf package so general-ledger, billing and payments can all depend on it and none on the others (the billing-core precedent, with a no-behaviour-change gate); exactly two roles are added, accounts_payable and owners_equity, with deliberately no default expense account because a plausible fallback makes a misposting invisible; and three configuration guards land — no trust-registered account, no parent account (a role must resolve to a leaf or every posting through it aborts at commit), and the account's type must match the role's declared normal balance, because mapping fee_income to an asset account is a silent sign inversion on every future invoice that nothing downstream would notice."
- **ADR-0385** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Discovery — "The ratified widening of the search-index scope key from a matter to a CORPUS was built everywhere except the half that decides disclosure: `packages/search` took a corpus, the split's R2 key derived from one, and `SearchIndexPort`'s three operations all named one — but the split CATALOG was still keyed by matter, and the catalog is what a query enumerates its splits from. A review set indexed under its own matter would therefore have surfaced material an opposing party produced in an ordinary matter search. `index_split` gains `corpus_kind` and `corpus_id`, every scope read moves onto them, and `matter_id` is retained as provenance nothing enumerates by. On that foundation a review document reaches the index through the same coordinator and the same consumer as a committed file version, indexing the text the discovery pipeline ALREADY extracted rather than re-parsing an opposing party's bytes — with a form-feed page separator so the certified text can be read back as the passages it was, and a refusal rather than a positional guess when the text and its page metadata disagree. And a document the index has not taken is recorded on its own row rather than as a `review_document_exception`, deviating from the plan, because the exception vocabulary is a CHECK-enforced closed list of sentences about what could not be READ and every one of them is rendered into a report a firm hands to another party."
- **ADR-0386** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Discovery — "The per-review-set access list shipped with the coding surface and was composed by the coding reads alone. Every other discovery route — the review-set LIST, the review set itself, its loads, its Custodians of Record, and every write including the irreversible close — reached a corpus on a discovery permission and nothing else. A member holding all five discovery grants and on no access list could read a walled review set's name, which on a matter is itself the disclosure that a production was received, and could rename or CLOSE it, releasing its derived artifacts with no path back. Every route that names a review set now resolves it through the access list first and answers ABSENT rather than refused; the nine new member-facing reads compose the predicate in SQL ahead of the LIMIT, and the visibility fitness sweep gained a second registry so they are held to it from the same table-driven pass the coding reads use."
- **ADR-0387** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Email — "The auto-file rule management surface refuses at creation every rule the guardrails could only ever suppress, rather than storing it and letting it fail silently at fire time. Three refusals carry it: a rule asking to file while the firm's direct-filing ceiling is down is rejected outright, because storing the posture would tell the member who set it that their firm had opted into unattended filing — which is the one act the ceiling exists to reserve for an administrator; a thread-continuation rule is rejected unless its conversation is already filed to the named matter, because its whole claim is the firm's own prior decision and without a filed link that claim is false; and a rule naming a matter that is not open is rejected, because the matter-state guardrail would suppress it forever with no way for the firm to see why. The review read narrows its matter set through the single wall filter BEFORE it reads any rule and carries no cursor, since narrowing a paged set hands out short pages and calls them the end of the list; and its per-rule counts are derived from the links themselves rather than the decision ledger, so the reviewer's numbers and the circuit breaker's undo input cannot tell a firm two different stories about one rule. A partial index over the automatic links is added because the shipped undo derivation subqueries an unindexed column on the capture path."
- **ADR-0388** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Notifications / Authorization — "The firm tier of the notification settings — the per-category in-app floor, the retention window and the digest local hour — gets its own surface, GET/PATCH /api/v1/firm/notifications/policy, behind a new firm:manageNotificationPolicy seeded to Owner/Admin only. All three settings shipped with write statements and no production caller: the floor was composed into every fan-out and could not be raised, the window was read by the nightly sweep and could not be changed, and the hour was asked of every firm hourly and was 07:00 everywhere. The permission is the substance of the decision. The member preference route rides notifications:read, which is deliberately seeded to EVERY default role including Read-only because reading one's own feed is not a privilege; reusing it for the firm tier would let any member raise the floor and shorten retention for every colleague. The retention window and digest hour are refused outside their declared bounds rather than clamped. The firm's email detail-level ceiling is deliberately NOT included: it has no write statement in either tier, and shipping the firm half alone would create the one asymmetry this subsystem exists to prevent."
- **ADR-0389** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Platform / Architectural integrity — "A fifteenth repo-wide lint, pnpm run lint:unwired-writes, fails any exported write-shaped function in apps/ or packages/ that nothing outside a test references. It exists because two subsystems shipped inert and neither had a failing test: the email auto-file engine evaluated a candidate set nothing could add to, and three notification settings were read on every fan-out and every sweep and writable by nothing. Both were found by a person, and in both cases the tests wrote the rows themselves. Measured against the whole tree in two variants before it was kept. The strict form — no non-test reference anywhere outside the definition line — reports 4 of 272, every one an allowlist entry with a written reason; replayed against the tree one commit earlier it reports 10, including the three auto-file writers. The looser form — no reference outside the defining FILE — reports 9, and all five extra hits are the same correct idiom: a statement composed into an ordered batch by a helper beside it. The looser form is rejected on that measurement. A blank allowlist reason, an entry for a name that no longer exists, and an entry whose function has gained a caller are each failures, so the gate cannot go quiet by accident."
- **ADR-0390** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Platform / Architectural integrity — "A sixteenth repo-wide lint, pnpm run lint:ungranted-permissions, fails any permission that gates a member-facing surface and that no committed seed grants to any role. Demands come from two places: a chokepoint-enforced route, read out of the byte-exact OpenAPI snapshot where defineOpenAPIRoute writes x-mc-permission beside x-mc-surface, and a Tools catalogue manifest, which is what toolsByGroup filters on. Measured in four variants against main before it was kept: every declared key with no seed reports 12 with 3 false; every key named by any route meta reports 7 with 3 false and drops all five Tools cases; surface app routes alone report 5 with 1 false; the kept form — that plus the catalogue — reports 10 with 1 false, allowlisted with its reason. Replayed against 2026-06-22 it names documents:edit on the very commit that introduced the route, fifty-eight days before the gap was found by reading, and every hit in that replay was real. Excluding surface public is grounded in the chokepoint's own code rather than fitted to the data."
- **ADR-0391** · Proposed · 2026-08-19 · Access control / RBAC — "Nine of the twelve registered permissions that no role_permission seed granted are seeded: the four co-editing keys the chokepoint has enforced since 2026-06-22 (documents:edit and documents:checkout to the three content-writing seats, documents:reconcile to Owner/Admin and Attorney, documents:forceReleaseLock to Owner/Admin alone) and five Tools catalogue manifests (the four public-record link libraries to every default role, the shipping tile to Owner/Admin, matching shipping:manage). The other three are correct and stay ungranted: firm:provision and support:mintSession ride surface: public routes whose RBAC gate the chokepoint skips, and diagnostics:overview is carried by a diagnostic support session rather than by any firm role. The load-bearing fact behind all of it is that role_permission and direct_grant have no write statement anywhere in the product, so the migration seeds are the entire supply and an ungranted permission is a permanent refusal rather than a default a firm can change. Whether documents:forceReleaseLock should also reach Attorney is left as an owner question on the ledger."
- **ADR-0392** · Proposed · 2026-08-21 · Delivery — "The single-page application is proposed to move out of the magna-carta Worker onto a Worker of its own, so an interface change stops rebuilding and redeploying the API. The premise is a dependency graph read rather than a runbook claim: apps/web imports none of the three WebAssembly packages, so the interface build is separable from the three Docker Rust builds that dominate the current one, and operations.md had said the opposite since 2026-06-28. The split does not cost the single origin the session cookie depends on, because two Workers route on ONE hostname by path pattern rather than on two hostnames -- which makes a zone hostname the hard precondition, since route patterns do not exist on workers.dev and the application is reachable only there today. What is being moved is a folder rather than a program: apps/web uses adapter-static, so the new Worker is an assets host holding no binding, no secret and no reach into firm data. Accepted costs are a deploy-skew window between interface and API that does not exist while they are one artifact, an eighth Worker to keep on the release branch, and a rollback that is no longer atomic. The design gates itself on a measurement nobody has taken: whether build caching and watch paths on the existing Worker close most of the ten minutes first, in which case the honest outcome is to close the proposal."
- **ADR-0393** · Proposed · 2026-08-29 · Platform / Database & migrations — "The migration runner reconciles a known retired migration lineage instead of refusing it. When a database's applied ledger is exactly the pre-2026-07-04 sequential chain that a baseline collapse replaced (per-firm: 36 ids; control-plane: 7 ids), the runner atomically rewrites those ledger rows to the single baseline tag — schema untouched — and then applies the pending tail. The retired chains live as a committed constant beside the runner, and only the complete chain qualifies: a strict prefix, or any other unknown tag, still refuses with the existing deploy-mismatch error, because the unknown-tag guard's real job — detecting code older than the schema — must not weaken. Chosen over keeping legacy rows marked superseded (the ledger then never matches the journal and every future reader needs the map) and over a one-off operational data fix (unauditable, and per-firm Durable Objects cannot be reached one by one)."
- **ADR-0394** · Proposed · 2026-08-29 · Authentication and access control — "Registration creates a person and nothing else; which firm they belong to is settled afterwards, signed in, on a dedicated screen. A session carrying no firm is admitted by three routes and answered with the reserved 404 everywhere else."
- **ADR-0395** · Accepted · 2026-08-29 · Dev tooling — "The Workers Builds production branch moves back to `main` on `magna-carta` and `magna-carta-web`, so a merge to the trunk builds and deploys within minutes and the deliberate `git push origin main:release` no longer exists. This supersedes ADR-0171, which had moved both to a dedicated `release` branch to decouple source-control cadence from the live environment. The reason for the reversal is the UI review loop: the owner reviews screens on the deployed site, and a correction that needs a second gesture to become visible costs a round trip per round of review. The cost accepted in exchange is that a merge now reaches real users on whatever verification ran before it landed -- and the gate of record has been blocked account-side since 2026-08-05, so that verification is the scoped local gate plus whatever tests the change carries, with no undeployed queue to catch a mistake. The other ten connected Workers Builds projects were not named in the move and their branches are unverified, so the fleet may be mixed."
- **ADR-0396** · Proposed · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "Every organizational membership — employment, a directorship, an officer post, a shareholding, a trusteeship — currently collapses into the single edge type affiliation, which the conflicts engine classes as control. So the traversal spends its control budget walking from an adverse corporation to a mailroom clerk with exactly the weight it spends walking to the chief executive. The class map is right and the catalog underneath it is too coarse to feed it. The fix is catalog granularity plus a fifth budgeted class, not a new membership table: directory_edge already is the n-ary object Popolo argues for, with its own id, role, note and validity period, and a second table would split the conflicts traversal across two corpora. Rule 4.2 comment 9 draws the line the classes need — a constituent who can bind or direct the organization is a different traversal risk from one who cannot. affiliation stays in the catalog and stays classed control, so nothing already recorded is reinterpreted, and the existing coverage test means a new type cannot land without a class beside it."
- **ADR-0397** · Accepted · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "The owner's direction is that the platform must be able to denote what methods a person CAN and SHOULD be contacted by. Those are two facts and one flag cannot hold both: a person is routinely reachable at a number they would rather nobody rang, and routinely asks to be reached by post while their email address stays perfectly usable. So every contact point carries contact_permitted, defaulting to yes, and contact_preferred, of which at most one exists per entity ACROSS email, phone and address together, because reach-me-by-post is a single answer about every channel at once. is_primary stays what it already was and is neither of them: the channel's default row, where a message goes when the sender named no address. Withdrawing a permission is offered where deleting the row would otherwise be used, because deleting loses the instruction along with the value and the next member types the number back in and rings it. None of this is Rule 4.2: a represented party may not be contacted about the subject of ONE matter, which stays matter-scoped and derived."
- **ADR-0398** · Accepted — the verification half is built, the source half is deferred · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "Nothing records where an address came from or when it was last confirmed, and courts require proof of diligence before permitting substituted service — verification of a last-known address against postal forwarding records being the ordinary first step. Five columns go on every contact point and every identifier: a source on a closed catalog, a free-text source detail, a verification status of unverified, verified or failed, and who verified it when. The status that does the most work is failed, because returned mail, a bounced message and a disconnected line are the facts an affidavit of diligence turns on and there is nowhere to put any of them today. The columns go on the row rather than in a polymorphic side table keyed on table-and-id, because that would turn every contact read into a join and last-confirmed is exactly the fact a list renders inline; five columns on four tables is more DDL and less code, and the DDL is paid once."
- **ADR-0399** · Accepted · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "Date of birth is stored today as an encrypted directory_identifier row, and the codebase already flags the mismatch: IdentifierNotIndexableError exists because a date of birth is too low-entropy to blind-index, so conflicts resolves it by decrypt-and-compare. It is the one entry in that catalog nobody issued, that identifies nobody uniquely, and that the firm needs to compare with less-than and greater-than rather than equals — is this client a minor, has a limitation period tolled, is this person over sixty-five. A range query over ciphertext is not a query. Date of birth, date of death, a separate deceased flag for the real state of known-dead-date-unknown, and place of birth become first-class nullable columns on directory_person, following NIEM, which puts birth, death and citizenship on the person and SSN and passport in identification. The owner settled the contested part directly: a date of birth needs a year and needs to be a date, so the full date is stored in the clear as an ordinary attribute, not a plaintext birth year beside a retained sealed date. The dob scheme is never dropped, only closed to new writes."
- **ADR-0400** · Accepted — built · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "directory_phone is a free-text label and a free-text number, so a program cannot tell a fax line from a mobile and cannot dial either. It gains a contact_system saying what technology answers the number — voice, fax, sms, pager — and a contact_use saying in what context — work, home, mobile, other, old, temp — because the two are orthogonal and vCard's single TYPE axis, where cell and fax and work share one list, is the free-text label problem with better spelling. Fax is a system rather than a label for three concrete reasons: a document-delivery path must not answer from a column where fax and facsimile and office-slash-fax mean the same thing to a person and nothing to a program, service by facsimile is a distinct permitted method whose consent is a recorded fact, and a voice line must never be fax-dialled. A number answering both voice and fax is two rows, not a combined enum value, because they are separately verifiable facts with separately recorded verification dates. The number as entered is never lost; a canonical E.164 column sits beside it and goes null when the input does not parse, and the extension is its own column because E.164 has no extension concept and one embedded in the canonical string breaks dialling."
- **ADR-0401** · Proposed · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "A directory_name row carries five Western name parts plus a lowercased normalized display that is the FTS5 content column, and the human-readable form is reconstructed by joining the parts. So a mononym, a family-name-first name and a name with a particle all render wrong in a name history, in a search hit and in anything assembled from one. A display_text column holds the name exactly as written and is never derived, following FHIR HumanName's text element, which exists for precisely this; normalized_display and both match keys are untouched, so nothing about search or duplicate detection changes. The role catalog also gains legal and preferred, because goes-by-Bob-legal-name-Robert can only be recorded as an alias today, which is correct for conflicts and wrong for a salutation. A repeating middle-name table is rejected: NIEM and vCard both allow many, one column holding Anne Marie has cost nobody anything, and a table would be a join on every name read."
- **ADR-0402** · Accepted — built 2026-09-02 (the type is in the catalog and classed; the record screen offers it) · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "The owner's direction is that the platform must record who the point person with an organization is. The design left two options open: a new edge type, or a flag on an employment edge. The flag is wrong, and the reason is not aesthetic — the people a firm actually talks to at an organization are frequently not its employees. A court's clerk of court, an insurer's assigned adjuster at a third-party administrator, opposing counsel's paralegal, a designated agent for service, a trustee's accountant: every one of them is the point of contact and none of them is on the organization's payroll. A flag on an employment edge cannot express any of them without first asserting an employment that is false, and a false employment edge is not a cosmetic error — the conflicts traversal spends a control-class budget on it. So point of contact is its own edge type, point_of_contact_for, and it stands alone: a person who is BOTH an employee and the point of contact carries two edges, which is correct because they are two facts with two validity periods. The build is blocked on one line outside this stream's ownership: a new edge type must be classed in packages/conflicts/src/edge-classes.ts in the same change, which the existing coverage test enforces."
- **ADR-0403** · Accepted · 2026-08-30 · Design system — "Searching within a screen and searching the whole application are two different objects and the product builds them as two. The top bar keeps the command palette: it renders registries, answers Ctrl K, and goes places. Every list screen gets `ScreenSearch`, one centralized design-system component that rests as an icon in the compact action row, expands on use, collapses when left empty, keeps itself open with an accent edge while a query is live, and carries no clear button (Escape clears, a second Escape closes). The owner chose this shape over three alternatives and gave the reason: one component used by every screen gets better for all of them at once, where hand-rolled copies get better one at a time and drift in between. It is screen-dependent for now — each screen searches its own content — so the component owns the query string, the debounce and the in-flight flag, and owns no results and no transport: a screen hands over `onsearch` and says whether to run it on every keystroke (data already in the browser) or after a pause in typing (a request that leaves the machine), and the calling shape is identical either way. That is the specific defect being repaired: the previous table-bound search box could only narrow rows already in the browser, so the screens whose search ran on a server bypassed it, and it shipped with zero consumers. `DataTable.Search` survives as a thin preset over the shared control. Three future capabilities ship as built-and-exercised seams wired to no screen — a scope control at the field's leading edge, an in-field match count, and a shortcut key — because a screen search and a global search will meet once the palette gains matters and contacts as their registries ship, and the component should not need replacing when they do."
- **ADR-0404** · Accepted — built · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "directory_email, directory_phone and directory_address have existed since the baseline migration, are registered for subject erasure, and are named in the schema with careful comments about temporality and primary enforcement. None of the twenty-five directory routes touches any of them. The email table has a write path built for inbound capture and reachable only from it; phone and address have no domain module at all, so not one statement anywhere in the repository inserts a telephone number or an address for a person. A firm therefore cannot record a client's phone number. Reaching the three tables is a phase of its own, ahead of every structural improvement and depending on none of them, because the structural work is worth nothing while the capability is zero. The three tables stay three: a postal address has seven components and a telecommunication endpoint has one, a polymorphic contact-point table would put six always-null columns on every phone row, and it would break the normalized-address index inbound email capture depends on."
- **ADR-0405** · Superseded — 2026-09-20, by the owner authorising the disclosure · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "The owner asked what a mapping or geocoding provider would buy an address record. It would buy four separable things: deliverability validation, normalization to a canonical postal form, geocoding to coordinates, and a map view. Each is worth a different amount and each carries the same cost, which is that a client's home address leaves the firm's tenancy for a third party — a privacy decision the owner makes explicitly, not a dependency an agent adds. So this pass ships the seam and no provider: the address model is already the international field set with a normalized duplicate key, and a provider result would land in the verification columns that already exist. The recommendation, if one is adopted, is Google's Address Validation API at 17 USD per thousand for coverage plus Smarty for a United States-only firm; the USPS API is free and, since its January 2026 rate limit of sixty lookups an hour and its ban on list validation, is not a platform dependency. Self-hosting Nominatim or Pelias keeps every address inside the tenancy and is the only option with no privacy question at all, at the cost of an operational surface this platform does not otherwise have. libpostal is C and has no pure-Rust port, so golden rule 14 applies to the normalization half."
- **ADR-0406** · Proposed · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "An identifier type stops being a bare string and becomes a registered scheme descriptor shipped as reference data in the package: a stable dotted key, whether it applies to a person or an organization, whether it requires an issuing jurisdiction, the frozen normalize transform and blind-index policy it already had, a masked-display policy so a screen can render a value's shape without a decrypt, an entry-time format and check-digit rule, and whether name-plus-this-value meets a state breach statute's definition of personal information. The database CHECK stays open at length > 0 because a committed migration is immutable and a closed enumeration would turn every catalog addition into a fleet migration; a fitness probe over stored identifier_type values closes it instead, which is what would have caught two spellings of driver's licence on the day they landed rather than as a silently missed conflicts match. The issuing jurisdiction moves out of the sealed value and into its own column, and the frozen transform still sees identical bytes so no existing blind index is invalidated."
- **ADR-0407** · Proposed — deferred by the owner, 2026-08-30 · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "directory_organization holds a legal name, a display name, the merge-graph status and timestamps, against a directory_person carrying five name parts, a member mapping and a six-column pronoun declension. The thinness is already causing harm inside the same schema: court_entity_id and estate_entity_id both point at directory_organization and neither can filter for what it points at, so a picker that must offer the courts has no column to read. It gains an organization_kind on a registered catalog, a formation jurisdiction, formation and dissolution dates, an organization_lifecycle_status deliberately not named status because that column is the merge-graph state and overloading it would be a live bug, and the legal form as registered so the match key's legal-form strip stays recoverable. Two organizations of the same name in different states are different legal persons, and the formation jurisdiction lets a conflicts reviewer see that in a second — as an attribute on the evidence, never as a filter, because suppressing a hit converts a fast dismissal into a missed conflict."
- **ADR-0408** · Proposed · 2026-08-30 · Operations — "Operational facts the Cloudflare account owns — which Workers are connected, each one's production branch, root directory, build command, Build Watch Paths and build-caching setting, which hostnames and routes each Worker answers on, when anything deployed and whether a build passed — are read live from the API at the moment of use and are never written down in the repository. The runbook and the deploy queue record the reasoning, the procedures, the failure modes and the invariants, and replace every derived value with the runnable command that returns it. The rationale is measured, not aesthetic: docs/conventions/operations.md wrote those values down and disagreed with the live configuration four separate times, each disagreement was corrected, and each correction decayed as soon as the dashboard moved again — a snapshot in a file reads exactly as confidently when stale as when fresh, and nothing in a merge can invalidate it. A drift-detecting fitness function was considered and rejected for the same reason: it needs a committed copy of the truth to compare against, which is the same snapshot with extra steps, and it cannot run in the gate of record because that gate has no Cloudflare credentials and must not require network."
- **ADR-0409** · Proposed · 2026-08-30 · Directory — "Model Rule 4.2 forbids communicating about the subject of the representation with a person known to be represented by another lawyer in the matter — in the matter, which means a person represented on one matter may be contacted freely about an unrelated one, and an entity-level do-not-contact boolean states the rule wrongly. So the no-contact warning is derived, never stored: matter_party gains a nullable represented_by_entity_id naming counsel on this matter, and every surface that addresses a party reads it. Derived because a stored copy rots the moment counsel withdraws, and a stale warning trains people to ignore warnings. A separate small temporal table records the restrictions that are not Rule 4.2 at all — a client who asked not to be emailed, a protective order — scoped to a channel and attributed to whoever recorded it, because a firm has to be able to say who said so and when."
- **ADR-0410** · Accepted · 2026-08-31 · Integrations — "The Firm Usage screen is segmented tables of raw figures, computed when the page is opened and stored nowhere. The owner rejected the dashboard-tile treatment for this surface: no stat tiles, no percentage bars, no note columns — every fact is a labeled number in a table row, because the audience is the firm reading its own records, not a visitor being sold a summary. The one route composes per-domain reads (each domain owns the read of its own tables) and grew the rollups the tables need: matters by the firm's own type catalog, the file versioning breakdown with sizes, per-location matters and staffing, Directory and member counts, and the three AI meters that were metered but surfaced nowhere. Growth is by seams: a new meter lands as a new table row, custodian holdings are a reserved column, and per-firm compute stays a deferred credentialed read — each rendered honestly as absent rather than omitted or zeroed."
- **ADR-0411** · Proposed · 2026-08-31 · Cross-cutting — "Exporting a screen's data is one framework, not a per-page feature: a surface builds a single tabular export document (title, timestamp, table sections) from exactly what it shows, and each download format is a renderer over that document. Plain text and CSV render client-side today under $lib/export; the other asked-for formats are named seams with stated prerequisites rather than second implementations — a native spreadsheet (.xlsx) waits on a deliberate library adoption, and Word/PDF belong to document assembly server-side, because the office suite is the platform's one renderer of a word-processing package and its conversion edge is the one way a PDF gets made. The Firm Usage report is the first consumer; the owner asked whether export should be a common framework, and this records the yes and its shape."
- **ADR-0412** · Accepted · 2026-08-31 · Platform / Delivery — "The application's hostname was converted from a custom domain on the API Worker to route patterns on the zone: magna-carta-web (assets-only) serves the interface on every path, and magna-carta is routed only its own four path families (/api/*, /wopi/*, /search-objects/*, /__dev/*). Same origin, same cookie, no CORS. This answers the split design's two owner flags: W1 yes, W2 the existing apex. Interface deploys decouple from API deploys through the magna-carta project's Build Watch Paths (dropping apps/web/*), not through code: the API Worker keeps its assets block deliberately, as the local-dev vehicle (wrangler dev and the e2e suite serve the whole app from it) and the one-gesture rollback (repoint the /* route). With two independent deployables, the version overlay's page-versus-API 'Different Builds' warning described a normal state and was retired; the reload signal is the SvelteKit version poll, which compares a tab against the deployment that actually serves it."
- **ADR-0413** · Accepted · 2026-08-31 · Reports — "Word and PDF export renders server-side from the same tabular export document every client format renders, through one route that behaves as a printer: the client sends the document it already holds, the platform composes a plain WordprocessingML package through document assembly's guarded writer, and the PDF arm rides the one live docx-to-pdf conversion edge, synchronously, answering an unreachable engine with a retryable 503 rather than an Error ID. Plain is the product on the owner's direction; the themed and letterheaded treatment is a reserved seam that will fill a firm-registered report template, never a platform-composed second answer to the firm's letterhead. The composer is document assembly's second production writer and the platform's first table emitter; the wire shape lives in its own package because a worker cannot import from an app. Nothing is stored: filing an export is a later, audited decision. Spec: docs/specs/2026-08-31-tabular-export-rendering-design.md."
- **ADR-0414** · Accepted · 2026-08-31 · Reports — "The notification feed carries what happened to the firm's WORK; a file the platform generated is platform mechanics and never enters it (owner ruling, 2026-08-31). Downloads get their own register instead: a per-member shelf at /downloads listing what the platform produced for that member, held for a short retention window and downloadable again until it expires. Two row kinds share one table because the member asks one question: a kept ARTIFACT points at bytes that cannot be re-derived (a bulk bundle, a conversion result) and re-downloads as a freshly minted presigned GET; a kept SNAPSHOT stores the tabular export DOCUMENT rather than a rendered file, so every format stays available from one row and the figures are frozen rather than recomputed. The register hands out links and never moves bytes, so the browser's own download manager keeps pause and resume. It is a shelf, not a history and not the file room: filing an export to a matter remains a separate audited act. Spec: docs/specs/2026-08-31-download-register-design.md."
- **ADR-0415** · Proposed · 2026-08-31 · Tools area — "The ten file utilities get one host rather than ten screens: the File Workbench at /tools/file-workbench, where a file that belongs to no matter is dropped once and every capability addresses it. Three rules bind. A malware verdict is a FACT ABOUT THE FILE AND NEVER A GATE ON THE SCREEN — a file somebody does not trust is precisely what this surface is for, so a flagged file is marked and still gets every reading, which is the matter file manager's settled ruling for filed documents applied to unfiled ones and is held by a fitness test asserting no capability's applicability changes with the verdict. The capability set is a REGISTRY of self-contained modules, so adding one is a module plus an array entry, and the Overview is contributed by the modules through an optional summary rather than hand-written, which is what keeps the synthesis from going stale as the set grows. And the surface stays EPHEMERAL BY CONSTRUCTION: this host wires in no history source and no version source, so a record of an unfiled file cannot exist rather than merely not being written. Capabilities are not peers — the cheap reversible readings run on arrival, anything that writes a file or discloses content waits to be asked, and conversion alone acts on the whole selection so a production of sixty scans is one gesture."
- **ADR-0416** · Proposed · 2026-08-31 · Platform — "Every API request was paying two serial control-plane reads before any work started — Better Auth's session lookup and the firm-row read — measured in production at the bulk of a ~700ms request whose actual firm-database reads took ~25ms each. Per the owner's direction that a signed-in member is trusted to be here, the session now rides Better Auth's HMAC-signed cookie cache for five minutes at a time (the refresh Set-Cookie is forwarded by resolveSession so the cache renews past its first window), and the firm row — three values that never change after provisioning — is cached per isolate for one minute, provisioned rows only. What stays live on every request, deliberately: the member-active gate and RBAC against the firm's own database, the structural-absence gate for an uncached firm, and a fresh store read for any FIRMLESS session, because attaching a founder's firm is a direct control-plane write the cookie cache never sees. The accepted staleness is a server-side session revocation surviving up to five minutes on an already-issued cookie, and a hard-deleted firm's lingering session surviving up to one."
- **ADR-0417** · Proposed · 2026-08-31 · Matters — "A prospective matter draws a tentative CONSULTATION number at creation, from a sequence of its own, rather than remaining unnumbered until it opens. The two sequences are kept separate — a consultation that never becomes a matter must consume no matter number, because a gap in the firm's matter run reads as a lost file — and opening a matter ADDS its permanent number rather than replacing the tentative one, so a document filed under the tentative number still resolves and the two never contend for one column. Both draws ride the existing matter_number_counter / matter_number_allocation mechanism under scope keys namespaced with a 'consultation/' prefix, rather than a second pair of tables that would duplicate the mechanism and the subtlety that makes it correct; '/' is outside the URL-safe set every rendered segment is folded into, so no matter scope key can collide with a consultation one. numbering_scheme gains a purpose column with one active scheme per purpose, and the consultation purpose — unlike the matter purpose — falls back to a built-in default scheme when the firm has configured none, because refusing to take a consultation until somebody visits the settings screen is a worse failure than rendering a shape the firm did not choose for a number that is scaffolding rather than the firm's file spine."
- **ADR-0418** · Accepted · 2026-09-01 · Design system — "Every modal panel in the product is one of two shells and never a third: RecordWindow, already built, for reading and changing a record, and a new ActionDialog for an act asked of a reader. ActionDialog is domain-blind and failure-blind in the manner RecordWindow already is — a caller hands it a heading, a subtitle, a fields snippet, a notice snippet, one commit verb, one refusal string and one async commit returning committed-or-refused, and the shell owns the open flag, the reset, the busy flag, the footer's geometry and the order of its two controls, the dismissal verb, whether a corner dismiss shows, and the single close path every exit runs through. Its `kind` — form, confirm or destructive — is what selects the underlying primitive, so the Dialog-versus-AlertDialog split stops being decided per file. Its seams are a closed set: the fields and notice snippets, kind, a closed width union, and the commit's outcome; no class prop, no footer override, no showClose. The unsaved-work guard that record-window.svelte already implements is lifted to a shared internal both shells consume, which is what finally gives all thirty panels a dismissal that asks before it discards. Enforced on both halves. In the source, a new lint-one-ui rule with three signals — a composed Dialog/AlertDialog/Sheet panel outside a declared shell's home, a scrim written as Tailwind utilities at the modal layer, and a reach for the platform's own modal — where WHICH homes may compose a panel is declared in registry.json beside the component register rather than hardcoded in the rule, so adding a shell is a reviewed edit to the file that already answers what is in the system; being a registered component is deliberately not enough on its own. A second rule asserts the shell's own action bar the way the header set has always been asserted against the record window. In the rendered DOM, both shells stamp data-modal-shell, which is the only thing that catches a panel whose parts were aliased on import or chosen at runtime. The thirty-seven existing panels are carried as printed allowances that must be deleted as each is migrated."
- **ADR-0419** · Proposed — placement, results surface and address settled; four recommendations standing · 2026-08-31 · Core domain — "Searching within a matter is built as a registry of declared read-side sources, not as a screen. Each subsystem declares where its rows live, how to read a bounded page of them, which read capability gates them, and whether it has a non-privileged snippet — the same declaration shape the federated calendar timeline already uses, and for the same reason: a federated read cannot disagree with the register it is built from, because it IS that register. One control lives in the matter LAYOUT, so every section inherits it and no section can mount a second one; it is the design system's `ScreenSearch` with its `scope` seam filled, which is the consumer that seam was built for. One route, `GET /api/v1/matters/{matterId}/search`, narrows the registry before building any statement, so the default scope — the section the reader is standing on — is also the cheapest query. Two permission gates: a new `search:matter` primary, and a per-source gate that DROPS a source the member cannot read before it runs a query rather than filtering results afterwards. The shared statement builder composes the matter binding and the visible-matter predicate itself, so a source physically cannot search across matters. Review sets are structurally excluded, because a review set is its own corpus precisely so an ordinary matter search cannot name material an opposing party produced. Adding a fourteenth searchable section costs one source module, one registry line, one section flag and one presentation entry, and changes nothing about the route, the control or the results page. Owner-settled 2026-08-31: the control sits top right, there is one results surface with the Files group drawn by the file manager's own row machinery, and the query rides in the address; four smaller recommendations stand, each reversible and none of them reaching the registry or the route."
- **ADR-0420** · Accepted · 2026-09-02 · Design system — "The record window's frame — the header band and its quiet controls, the body, the primary pane that stops at its measure beside a context pane that takes the rest, the notice band, the action bar of fixed geometry, and the fit-first/cap-second sizing rule they all obey — is extracted into an internal recipe both modal shells sit on, so that neither shell and no panel rebuilds it. RecordWindow keeps every class name as a hook and every pixel as it was, proven by byte-identical screenshots of its four frame-bearing stories before and after; ActionDialog moves onto the same frame, gains a context pane that is the record window's right pane lent to a form, and opens at the record width whenever it has one. The decision was forced by evidence rather than taste: the largest panel in the app had rebuilt the whole frame from utilities in 647 lines, quoting the record window's own doctrine in its comments, because there was nothing to reach for. A read-only variant of the modal was considered for the same gap and rejected — it would be a second answer to reading a thing, and would make the commit optional for every panel that has one."
- **ADR-0421** · Accepted · 2026-09-02 · Design system — "ActionDialog gains a fourth kind, reading, for a panel that states a fact and seats any step it offers in the body beside that fact rather than in the action bar — the matter-status window is the model. This reverses the position taken a day earlier that a read-only variant would be a second answer to reading a thing and would weaken the type for every panel that commits. The reversal is on evidence: a panel arrived that was neither a record (no tabs, history or editing mode) nor an act asked of a reader (nothing to commit), and the alternative was a hand-composed Dialog.Content that the one-implementation rule rightly refused. The type is not weakened: the props are a discriminated union on kind, where the acting arm still requires commitLabel and onCommit and the reading arm forbids them. The reading kind renders on Dialog with the corner dismiss, is dismissed by a press outside, runs no unsaved-work guard unless told there is something to lose, and its one bar control says Close — nothing was begun, so nothing is cancelled. It is explicitly not a read-only RecordWindow; a record still opens in the record window."
- **ADR-0422** · Accepted · 2026-09-02 · Design system — "With fifteen panels on ActionDialog, what they still repeated was measured and four findings became shell or design-system changes. The primary pane is a real form and the commit is its submit button by id, so Enter commits everywhere and a disabled commit stops it, with Ctrl+Enter or Cmd+Enter from a textarea; none of the fifteen had wired Enter because each would have had to. subtitle takes a string, since twelve of fourteen were plain text. The maturity vocabulary — model, badge, dot and the Soon affordance — moves from the app into the design system as one home, and the shell gains commitSoon so an act not served yet renders in a panel exactly as it does in a menu; the two panels that composed the affordance around their own commit lose their reason for an allowance. LabelledField and FieldRow are the layout every plain form rebuilt: the field mints its id, joins hint and error into the control's description and marks an error invalid, handing the control its attributes to spread in formsnap's idiom; it is deliberately not the superforms-bound Form.Field, and the two recipes' differing gaps are stated rather than reconciled. Refused, again: a third control in the action bar."
- **ADR-0423** · Accepted · 2026-09-02 · Design system — "ActionOutcome gains a third value, done, for an act that happened and has something to show for it. The panel stays open as a panel with nothing left to ask: the commit leaves the bar, the dismissal reads Close, the unsaved-work guard stands down, and the fields and context snippets are handed done beside busy so one snippet renders the panel before and after.
