It is the reading kind's bar arrived at by acting, so it costs the shell one flag. The upload-destination panel needed it first — it saves a destination and stays while the batch lands — and two of the panels still to migrate show a reference or a made document after their commit. Without it each would hand-roll a state machine over the shell's open flag, which is the one thing a caller must never touch."
- **ADR-0424** · Accepted · 2026-09-03 · Design system — "Full-screen is a capability of the modal frame both shells sit on, not of the record window alone. The record window's expand — its corner control, its session memory and the geometry that drops the width cap — moves beneath both shells as a shared internal, and ActionDialog gains the same control on its Dialog kinds, form and reading, and never on a question. Each shell keeps its own control in its own header set, because the header set is asserted at the source; each shell keeps its own session memory, because a reader who filled the screen with one form has said nothing about the next question. Both shells take a bindable expanded, which is how a panel opens expanded by design rather than as a hand-built window. The owner's ruling that full-screen is a design choice each time is kept and made cheap: the choice becomes one prop. The three surfaces that had been excluded as full-screen — one of which rebuilt the record window's expand by hand — migrate onto the frame opened expanded in the pass that follows."
- **ADR-0425** · Proposed · 2026-09-01 · Tenancy — "Per-firm R2 bucket isolation is completed by growing `src/files/firm-bucket.ts` into an S3-API-backed `R2Bucket` when the resolved bucket is not the bound one, rather than by binding every firm's bucket in Wrangler configuration or by collapsing every firm onto one bucket behind a key prefix. R2 bindings are static configuration and no runtime API turns a bucket name into a handle, so those are the only three shapes available. The adapter wins because the transport already ships: `files/presign.ts` signs server-side S3 calls to arbitrary bucket names with `aws4fetch` under an account-scoped credential that already reaches every firm bucket, so only the object plane is new — and because it keeps firm creation off the release path, which binding-per-firm would put there on a repository where a merge to `main` deploys. The prefix option is refused outright: it turns the tenancy boundary into a string convention enforced by application code. The binding decision is second: the audit unit is the CALL SITE, not the module, because a module can resolve the firm's bucket name for a signed URL and still read bytes through `env.R2` — five such sites existed, three of them inside modules that already called the resolver. Every firm-scoped access therefore resolves through the seam, the four genuinely platform- or development-scoped modules are named in an allow-list with their reasons, and `lint:firm-bucket-seam` holds the line."
- **ADR-0426** · Accepted · 2026-09-01 · Tasks — "Opening a matter whose type carries a plan OFFERS that plan's tasks — with their proposed dates and role-resolved assignees — and writes nothing until a member accepts. This narrows the white paper's 'auto-instantiated when a matter is opened' and answers owner flag W8, decided 2026-09-01. Three things make the review worth its friction, and all three are properties of what a plan can get wrong rather than arguments from principle: a plan's offset is plain calendar-day addition with no weekend skip and no holiday calendar (deliberately, because business-day arithmetic is the docketing engine this platform refuses to build), so a template can land a task on Christmas Day; a template names a ROLE and resolves it against the matter's live team at instantiation, so opening a matter before staffing it produces tasks belonging to nobody; and assignment notifies, so automatic instantiation tells a member they own work on a matter they have never heard of, chosen by a template rather than a person. The countervailing case is real and is not dismissed: a firm opening forty matters a month of one tuned type gets forty identical confirmations and learns to accept without reading, which costs the friction and loses the benefit. That is answered by a per-template 'instantiate automatically' setting as a fast follow rather than by making automatic the default — the firm decides which of its own plans it trusts, which is not a judgment the platform can make for them."
- **ADR-0427** · Proposed · 2026-09-02 · Tasks — "A matter plan's step names a ROLE rather than a member and carries a due-date ANCHOR rather than a date, and both are resolved against the individual matter when the plan is proposed — because a template is used many times and anything it hard-codes is wrong the moment it is used twice. The role is matched against `matter_assignment.assignment_role` through the same validity window `packages/document-assembly` and the notifications matter-team audience already read (a creation bound inclusive by civil day, an end bound exclusive by instant), and a role that matches nobody produces a step that is NAMED and unassigned rather than blank or silently given to the responsible attorney — inventing an obligation for somebody is worse than showing a gap. The anchor vocabulary is `matter_opened` and `none` and is held by a SQLite CHECK, because every candidate beyond it is the front half of a docketing rule. Separately and by the same reasoning at the catalog level: THE PLATFORM SHIPS NO PLANS. Every competitor ships plan libraries and their absence is a visible competitive cost, accepted because a plan is a statement about how one firm practises in one jurisdiction, and one shipped by the platform with a missing or misordered step is a malpractice vector carrying the platform's name — trusted precisely because it shipped, which is the calendar's own argument about computed dates one level up. Owner flags W11 and W12 remain open as the reviewed route to changing either."
- **ADR-0428** · Proposed · 2026-09-01 · Notifications — "Task assignment adds a sixth notification audience, `named-recipient`, resolving to the one member a type's declared payload field names, and raises FOUR topics for two acts — `tasks.task.assigned` / `tasks.task.reassigned` for a task on a matter and `tasks.firmTask.assigned` / `tasks.firmTask.reassigned` for one on none. The audience is new because none of the five existing ones means \"the one member this row names\": the 2026-08-15 design routed task assignment to `designated`, which is the firm's TRUST-COMPLIANCE designation and resolves to nobody when unconfigured, so as designed every task assignment would have delivered to no one, silently, since an unrecognized audience is an empty audience rather than an error. It resolves from the payload rather than the subject's row so the person told is whoever was named at the WRITE — re-reading the row when the queue drains would deliver the first notification to the second person and the second one to them again. The four-topic split is forced by two fitness rules the registry already enforces (a matter-anchored type must resolve a matter; a type that is not matter-anchored must not), which a nullable matter cannot satisfy with one type; `calendar.event.*` versus `calendar.firmEvent.*` is the shipped precedent, and the producer chooses at the write where the answer is certain rather than leaving a consumer to infer it from a null."
- **ADR-0429** · Proposed · 2026-09-01 · Tasks — "`matter_task.matter_id` is nullable, so one record and one set of routes serve both a matter's tasks and the firm's own work, and the does-not-apply branch that nullability creates is confined to one composable SQL fragment (`taskVisibilityFragment`) that every read composes rather than being answered with a second table. This is the opposite of the call `firm_event` made against `matter_event`, and the divergence is deliberate on two grounds: the mechanical half of the calendar's argument — that dropping a NOT NULL on a shipped SQLite table is a rebuild the additive-only lineage forbids — does not apply to a column that is nullable from its first migration; and the design half turns on where the records are READ. A firm event and a matter event are read on two different surfaces, so two tables match two surfaces, whereas tasks have one surface either way, because \"what is on my plate\" spans a member's client work and their firm work in one list and two tables would mean unioning them back together on the read every member makes most often. `magnus_conversation` is the shipped precedent: an optional matter link on a record whose tenancy root is the firm. The two visibility rules the fragment holds are that a task on a matter is firm-visible within it under the same visible-matter predicate every calendar source composes, and a task on none is private to its assignee and whoever created it — never firm-wide, because a firm-wide default would put \"review the associate's performance\" in front of the associate."
- **ADR-0430** · Proposed · 2026-09-01 · Tasks — "A task carries both a `cancelled` state and a real delete path, and the two are not redundant. Cancellation records that the firm DECIDED not to do the work and requires an actor, an instant and a reason, because the decision is the whole content of the record; deletion is for a task that should not exist — a typo, a duplicate, a line somebody abandoned before it meant anything — and removes the row, leaving an audit entry carrying identifiers and no words. The 2026-08-15 design offered cancellation alone, on the reasoning that without it a task that should not have been created has only `done` (a lie about work) or deletion (a hole in the record) to reach for. That reasoning establishes why cancellation must exist and does not establish that deletion must not: it conflates two different mistakes. The owner's direction on notes hours earlier settled the same tension the other way for a different record — a chain that can only accumulate is a record people stop keeping — and the resolution here takes both halves. The database holds each terminal state to carrying its own evidence and neither carrying the other's, through two CHECK constraints, so a repair script cannot invent a completion nobody performed or a cancellation nobody decided."
- **ADR-0431** · Proposed · 2026-09-02 · Tasks — "A time entry may name the task it was worked on, as ONE nullable `task_id` on `time_entry` rather than a many-to-many join — answering scope-study flag T2 the way the study recommended. An entry split across two tasks needs an allocation of its minutes between them, and an allocation is a second ledger with its own rounding, its own disputes and its own audit; the honest encoding of a block of time that served two tasks is two entries, which the capture surface already supports and the billing narrative already wants. A join table remains an additive migration over this column's data should many-to-many ever be needed, and the reverse is not, so the cheaper direction is the one taken. The named task must be on the ENTRY'S OWN MATTER: a task on another matter would let one matter's time point at work another matter is paying for, and a task on NO matter is the firm's own work with no client matter to bill it to — so a null `matter_id` on the task is a refusal rather than a wildcard. SQLite cannot express either condition without a trigger, so `assertTaskOnMatter` in the timekeeping service is the enforcement and its tests are what keep it honest, under one refusal code on the `file-not-on-matter` model. The time read back is MINUTES AND NEVER MONEY, because what a task cost is a question with a different audience and a different permission; it is batched over a page of tasks by construction, and whose time it counts is the reader's `time:viewFirm` answer arriving as data, with the scope travelling beside the totals so a surface cannot render the number without saying which it is."
- **ADR-0432** · Proposed · 2026-09-02 · Tasks — "Tasks carry the four primitives an outside system needs to mirror them — an external-identity table generic over an open `system` string, a deletion tombstone written in the delete's own batch, an `updated_by` stamp for echo suppression, and a change feed of two keyset streams with INDEPENDENT positions — and the platform ships no adapter, no sync engine, no conflict policy and no field mapping, because each of those is a decision about somebody else's product. The four are built now rather than when an addon is because none can be added afterwards: a correspondence kept in an addon's own side table cannot have referential integrity to matter_task or be cleaned up on delete; a hard delete is unobservable to anything not watching at that instant, and past deletions cannot be reconstructed; without updated_by a two-way sync pushes back what it just pulled; and a feed retrofitted onto a table with no ordered cursor cannot report the changes that happened before it existed. Both uniqueness rules on the link table are INDEXES rather than service checks, because the failure they prevent — two tasks fighting over one mirror — is produced by exactly the race a pre-check loses. The feed's two streams carry separate positions because one shared cursor drops rows silently: its id half comes from whichever stream produced the last row, and a deletion handle used as the tie-break against matter_task.id excludes every live task sorting below it at that instant, permanently. Visibility is never relaxed for a sync: the feed composes the same predicate every other task read does, so an addon connected as a member syncs exactly what that member can see."
- **ADR-0433** · Accepted · 2026-09-03 · CI / build cadence — "A merge queue was the structural fix for the class of failure the gate's first full battery found on 2026-09-02 — two branches merged minutes apart, each green against the main it had seen, never tested together — and the gate's merge_group tier was wired for it. GitHub offers merge queues only in organization-owned repositories (public, or private on Enterprise Cloud); this repository is private and user-owned, so the setting does not exist for it, and the only routes to it are a transfer to an organization plus either going public or paying for Enterprise Cloud. The owner declined the transfer: it costs time the project does not have, and interleaved-merge failures are dealt with as they come. The merge_group tier stays wired, unreachable, for the day the ownership changes. The stand-in is a habit rather than a ruleset: one merge at a time, the next branch synced onto the main the last merge produced. 'Require branches to be up to date before merging' is deliberately left off because it spends a full battery per open stream per merge, which is the budget pressure the austerity exists to avoid, and it still cannot stop two branches updated from the same main from landing back to back."
- **ADR-0434** · Accepted · 2026-09-02 · Calendar — "The matter's Activity section names its rows through a `describe` declared on each timeline source, run server-side after the merge for the one matter in scope and gated by a `permission` the source now declares, rather than the client re-reading each subsystem's own listing and joining by row identity. The federated projection stays as thin as the day it shipped: a firm-wide read is never described, and a source without a describe keeps the platform's sentence. The client's half is an `ActivityContribution` that lives beside the owning subsystem's own code and is registered in one list, paired to the server's registry by a scanning test and to the section registry by a coverage lint, so a subsystem joining the timeline touches nothing in the Activity section itself."
- **ADR-0435** · Accepted · 2026-09-03 · Client intake — "What a kind of matter must clear before it opens is a firm-authored procedure bound to a matter type (or every type), holding ordered steps whose KIND decides who answers them: the conflicts posture, the billing arrangement in force, an assembly of a named template, the matter's trust position, a custom field's value, or a member's own record. The checklist is derived at the moment it is read and never stored, so a step cannot say done after the fact it stood on has changed and editing the procedure reaches every consultation of its kind at once. Readers are registered by the composition root rather than imported by the checklist, which is what lets a kind exist ahead of the subsystem that will answer it. The opening gate refuses while a required step is outstanding and concludes nothing about the client; the override is a waiver under a named member's own permission with a mandatory rationale. Every member record is append-and-flip, every write is a person's, and the platform seeds no procedure."
- **ADR-0436** · Accepted · 2026-09-05 · Directory — "A person or an organization in the directory has one surface, the entity window, and no screen of its own. The window takes the New Person screen's shape — the record's sections as a rail on the left, the chosen section's pane beside it, what stands about the record on the right — and opens over whatever the reader was doing: the directory's list, a matter's parties, the client fact on the overview, a conflicts hit. Closing it lands the reader back where they were, so no way back is owed, and the header's way back on every remaining screen keeps meaning the screen above. The record's address is the directory's, carrying which record to open, so a pasted link still lands on it; the old record route redirects there. A related record opened from inside the window swaps its subject; a record opened from another window stacks once. A host contributes only what it alone knows — a matter, the roles the record holds on it — and never describes the record itself. Chosen over an origin-aware way back and over a breadcrumb trail."
- **ADR-0437** · Accepted · 2026-09-05 · Design system — "Every loading state in the app runs through one design-system region, LoadingRegion, and the placeholder is the component itself: the real markup renders over representative content and one CSS recipe turns its leaves into bones while the frame, the headings, the labels and the column heads stay real. Nothing is measured at runtime and nothing is captured at build time, because every library that promises to derive a skeleton from the finished component needs representative content before it can measure anything, and once that content exists the measurement is the part worth dropping. The region takes the two facts only its caller knows, whether it is loading and what it is reading, and owns the rest by having no prop for it; it is inert while loading so a fixture can never be pressed or read as real. Skeleton leaves the public surface and stays as an internal box for the six design-system components that render placeholder rows of their own shape. A lint:one-ui rule fails any hand-placed loading state on either side of the line, with the homes allowed to hold one declared in the registry and every exception recorded with its reason."
- **ADR-0438** · Accepted · 2026-09-06 · Matters — "The matter lifecycle gains a hold rung beside open: an active matter the firm has stopped working without closing it, for a recorded reason such as a client who has stopped paying. A hold is reached only from open, keeps the matter number, draws and surrenders nothing, carries its reason and its instant on the row, and returns to open when released; a close from hold leaves them standing so a reopen returns the matter to its hold. The status window offers the pause beside the close and asks for the reason in place. One permission, matters:hold, in both directions."
- **ADR-0439** · Accepted · 2026-09-06 · Matters — "A matter number set by hand (a court's number, a legacy number, a correction) is accepted only on a matter that has been active: open, on hold, or closed after opening. An intake or a consultation cannot be renumbered, by the counter or by hand, because the owner's ruling is that no matter number exists before a matter is active. The renumber service refuses the unnumbered rungs with matter-not-open, and the status window offers Set Number only where the matter has opened. The numbering scheme editor ships under Settings · Matter Numbering over the routes that already existed, with the preview rendered by the server so the number a scheme produces is the number a matter gets."
- **ADR-0440** · Accepted · 2026-09-05 · Client intake — "The intake desk is a firm-wide reading of the firm's intakes and consultations — never a register of its own — and an intake is complete when no required step of the procedure binding its kind is outstanding, which is when the consultation may be scheduled from it. Every intake is resumed on its matter's own Intake section. And a field that picks a person or an organization can RECORD one from where it stands: the design system's picker offers a top-level New and a per-kind add row, and the application wraps it with the Directory's own New Person and New Organization windows so a record made from a client field is the same record, past the same duplicate gate, as one made from the Directory."
- **ADR-0441** · Accepted · 2026-09-05 · Matters — "A matter passes through intake, consultation, open and closed, stored as spelled. An intake is the walk from first contact to a consultation: the matter row exists from the first call, because the conflicts check, the files, the calendar and every intake-procedure reader are anchored on a matter and a prospective client is owed the record from the first consultation, but it carries no number of any kind and lives on the Intake desk rather than the docket. The firm's intake procedure gates graduation from intake to consultation, which draws the consultation number; a consultation opens on the firm's decision with no intake gate. The second rung is spelled consultation rather than prospective, because prospective is the conduct rules' word for the client's standing while consultation is the firm's word for the phase, and the product already said consultation everywhere but the status chip. The stored value is renamed, not mapped, because no firm holds a matter at this date."
- **ADR-0442** · Accepted · 2026-09-06 · Time tracking — "Time gains a third listing shape — a `from`/`to` span of work dates across every matter — because neither of the two that existed could answer what went on the record this week. A span naming no timekeeper is the firm's whole record and requires `time:viewFirm`; naming somebody other than the caller requires it too; an ungated caller is refused rather than silently narrowed to themselves. The period ledger at `/time` is built on it, narrows through the one grid's own column filters rather than a free-text box, and states its figures over what is loaded."
- **ADR-0443** · Accepted · 2026-09-07 · Communications layer — "The product's Messaging surface means voice and SMS/MMS and deliberately excludes email, resolving a name that had carried four different scopes at once — a destination id naming the whole three-channel Communications layer, a label naming one channel, a description naming two, and a matter section under the same word describing the client portal's message grant. The refusal of email is argued rather than assumed: mail already lives in the firm's own mailbox and is FILED onto a matter from there through the add-in, the capture address and the auto-file rules, so its verb is route-what-already-exists, where a call leaves no trace at all unless the platform makes one and the verb is cause-a-record-to-exist. One surface carrying both verbs would have to be two screens under a shared header. The place the channels do genuinely converge is a Directory contact, which is a contact-scoped surface rather than a sidebar row. Two things are displaced and named rather than dropped: portal secure messaging loses the matter section it had been reserving, and the unrouted email capture queue — mail that routes to no matter, which is written today and readable nowhere — remains without a home."
- **ADR-0444** · Accepted · 2026-09-07 · Communications layer — "A logged call or text hangs on a matter identified by its STATUS, never by its number, and the surfaces that pick one must offer every status a firm can hold a conversation under. `matter.matter_number` is null until the status reaches `open`; an intake carries no number of any kind and a consultation carries only a tentative one, and those two are precisely where a first contact lands — a firm texts a prospect before the file exists. So the routable set is intake, consultation, open and hold, checked on the status column, and a closed matter is refused a NEW filing while keeping everything already on it. The consequence that costs something is the picker: the docket's own listing deliberately excludes intakes, on the sound reasoning that a docket listing every phone call would bury the firm's files under its own front door, so the surface that files phone calls cannot reuse it and walks a third stream instead. Rejected: requiring a numbered matter, which would refuse the commonest case the feature exists to serve and teach firms to open files early to record a call; and inventing a second anchor entity for prospects, which would duplicate the lifecycle the matter shell already models across five statuses."
- **ADR-0445** · Proposed · 2026-09-07 · Reports — "The report surface is two screens over one registry, not a screen per report. Every report the platform offers is declared once in apps/web/src/lib/reports/report-registry.ts — its family, its permission key, whether it takes a knowledge instant or is live-only, which scope controls it needs, the read that fetches it and the presenter that turns the payload into titled table sections — and the catalogue and the run screen render from that declaration. The presentation shape is the tabular export document's shape, so every report exports in every format the moment it is declared. A report whose backend is not built is still declared, at stage soon with what it waits on, and renders in the catalogue visible and disabled. Adding a report is one registry entry plus a presenter; a second screen per report is refused."
- **ADR-0446** · Proposed · 2026-09-08 · Communications layer — "A call or a text may be anchored to a matter OR set aside with a stated reason from a closed vocabulary — spam, firm business, nothing came of it — and never to both. The unrouted queue is redefined as rows carrying neither, so it is a list that can actually be emptied; the set-aside rows keep their words, their number and their instant and stay in the firm-wide read, and returning one to the queue is a first-class act. The vocabulary is enforced in packages/communications rather than as a SQLite CHECK, because the column is added to a populated table and SQLite cannot add a CHECK to one without the destructive rebuild expand-contract forbids. In the same round the log form's counterparty number gains an explicit country, which makes storing E.164 honest for the first time — the server still refuses to canonicalize on its own, because it has no way to know the country, and the form does."
- **ADR-0447** · Proposed · 2026-09-08 · Communications layer — "A row in the call and text log opens into the platform's record window, and a member-produced row is correctable there through a new communications.update operation; a carrier-produced row is refused, because editing one would turn an observation into a recollection wearing an observation's badge. The window's fields ARE the entry form's, rendered by one component, so recording a call and correcting one cannot drift into two layouts. Where a record belongs stays out of those fields and lives in the window's action bar instead: a matter and a set-aside reason each move the row between queues through their own concurrency-safe act, and folding them into a field patch would let a correction to a description silently re-file a message somebody else had just filed."
- **ADR-0448** · Proposed · 2026-09-07 · Financial — "The 2026-08-26 fee-surface design ruled that Fees is NOT a work-in-progress surface: a matter's fee value was the time ledger's or the arrangement's, so the screen showed the fee AGREEMENT paired with the invoice LINES a bill had already composed. The owner then removed the arrangement from that screen on 2026-09-03 — it is a fact of the engagement and now lives in the matter's status window — which removed the ruling's own justification, because it rested on pairing agreement-as-cause with billed-lines-as-effect. What was left was the pure invoice view the design had itself rejected as too thin: a ledger of lines can show nothing that is still unbilled, which is the whole of what a work-in-progress ledger is for. So the third store is built. `fee_entry` holds the flat charge somebody decided on — neither an hourly matter's time value nor a flat-fee arrangement's amount, and so with nowhere to live — riding the same draft-to-billed chain as time and costs through `@magna-carta/billing-core`, claimed onto a draft invoice through the same `invoice_wip_claim` whose CHECK is widened to admit it. A fee is never computed from time entries."
- **ADR-0449** · Proposed · 2026-09-08 · Core domain — "Date handling splits into exactly two authorities, mirroring money: a new dependency-free leaf package `@magna-carta/civil-time` owns what a civil date, a wall-clock time, a canonical stored instant and a time zone ARE — the shapes, the guards, the zoned projection, and plain calendar-day counting — and the design system's `formatters/` own how each reads to a person. The leaf package having no workspace dependencies is the load-bearing property rather than a tidiness preference: every one of the roughly seventy duplicates this replaces sat in a package that could not reach the one already holding the function, which is why `packages/notes` and `packages/tasks` each wrote a zone check that accepted a fixed offset the calendar's refused, and why only `packages/ledger`'s day check excluded year 0000. Both stricter rules become everyone's. The split is enforced by a source-scan fitness function, `pnpm run lint:date-handling`, derived into the inner loop and CI by the existing repo-wide lint derivation; its rules match the shapes the copies actually took, its findings carry accurate file:line because comments are blanked rather than removed, and every allowance it carries is printed on each run and fails once it outlives its violation. Two package fitness tests that previously exempted named files from touching `Intl` now assert the absence outright — those exemptions were what made the neighbouring packages write their own copies. Elapsed time stays banned for a record, with one stated carve-out for the age of a live measurement. The fifteen surfaces still holding a native date input rather than the DatePicker are recorded and closed rather than migrated, because whether they should all move is the owner's design question and not a scan's."
- **ADR-0450** · Proposed · 2026-09-09 · Auth & access control — "Ending a session got a surface at both ends — the profile menu's Sign out, and an Account -> Active Sessions section over Better Auth's list and revoke routes — and two configuration decisions came out of building it. Better Auth's session-freshness dial (session.freshAge) is set to 0, because in the pinned 1.6.22 it gates exactly two endpoints, /list-sessions and the unreachable /unlink-account, and gates none of the writes anybody would want it for: revocation, password change and account deletion all run through sensitiveSessionMiddleware, which never consults it. Left at the library default of one day it would have answered 403 SESSION_NOT_FRESH to the signed-in-devices screen for any session older than a day — precisely the long-lived 'keep me signed in' session that screen exists to let somebody find and end — while still permitting the revocation itself, which is seeing less than you may do rather than a security posture. And the new logout / session.revoked audit events are decided by the endpoint a session was deleted under AND by whether the deleted row is the one that endpoint was asked to remove, because better-call resolves a nested endpoint's path as `context.path || path`: the getSession that the revoke endpoints' own session middleware performs inherits the outer path, and a caller whose session has expired has it swept there, filing a revocation for a request that then 401s having revoked nothing."
- **ADR-0451** · Proposed · 2026-09-09 · AI / Inference — "The firm-administrator AI settings screen writes exactly the two consent gates the assistant's resolver reads and nothing else, under a new `firm:manageAiSettings` seeded to Owner/Admin alone rather than riding `magnus:converse` — asking the assistant a question is ordinary work that seeds to Attorney and Paralegal, while consenting for the whole firm that privileged client content may leave the platform is not, so the work key must not be the consent key. The two gates stay two controls and are never collapsed or inferred from one another, because a firm that enabled inference for drafting has not consented to a free-form assistant and a firm that switched the assistant off has not withdrawn consent for everything else; the screen additionally states the EFFECTIVE composition, because an assistant switched on above a closed processing gate does nothing and a screen showing only `Magnus: On` would report that it works. The firm's reasoning model is REPORTED with its pinned snapshot and both confidentiality facts and cannot be selected: a control writing `inference.primaryReasoningModel` would silently convert a firm that follows the platform's evergreen reasoning tier into one pinned to today's occupant of that tier, so selection waits on a setting that can express the tier itself. The screen does not pre-empt the pending client-consent decision, because reaching a provider takes a third, deploy-level switch (`MAGNUS_INFERENCE_ADAPTER`) that is absent by default and resolves to a stub; the screen therefore claims consent recorded, never content in flight."
- **ADR-0452** · Proposed · 2026-09-09 · Authentication — "Spending a registration's emailed confirmation token now mints a session on the confirmation response, so a person who confirms lands inside the product rather than back at the front door. The proof is not weakened by this and is arguably strengthened: the token travels by email and nowhere else, so spending it demonstrates possession of the mailbox — the same possession proof a mailed sign-in link rests on, and a stronger one than the password the very next screen would have asked for. The session is minted through Better Auth's own verify-email endpoint with `emailVerification.autoSignInAfterVerification` enabled, rather than by writing a session row and signing a cookie by hand, so the session row, the cookie format, the cookie cache and the login.success audit stay whatever the installed library does across an upgrade. The one structural consequence is that the confirmation batch inserts the `user` row UNVERIFIED and the verify-email call performs the flip, because that endpoint declines to mint a session for an address already marked verified; a mint that does not happen falls back to flipping the column directly and answering `signIn`, so the account is never left in the one state it cannot sign in from. Registration correspondingly stops asking for a name — `name` is optional on the request and the address is filed as the display name — and the password is typed twice on the form."
- **ADR-0453** · Accepted · 2026-09-10 · Observability & diagnostics / Tenancy & data isolation — "The credentials that select the real provisioning and presigning adapters are required by the deploy gate, so a production deployment can no longer run the development adapters — which recorded a bucket name for every firm they provisioned and created no bucket, with nothing reporting it. Beside that, the preconditions of a browser upload — the firm's bucket exists, carries the ingest notification, the expiry rules and the CORS policy, and the presigner's own credential signs a request it honours — are verified from the server side, where a refusal has a status code, by one function run from one button on the File Safety & Scanning screen; idempotent settings are re-applied in place, and a missing bucket or a mis-scoped key is named with its fix. Deliberately absent, after a first draft carried them: a deployment-mode object, a startup refusal, a status line, a daily sweep, and a readiness cache on the firm row — each was machinery for a fact the gate or the button answers in one piece."
- **ADR-0454** · Accepted · 2026-09-11 · Conflicts — "A conflicts check follows every recorded relationship to closure. The per-edge-class hop budgets, the default depth, the closure cap and the candidate cap are removed from the check draft, the check request, the firm's conflicts policy and its settings page: a limit a firm can set is a conflict the firm does not find, and that is the failure that matters. One bound remains and it is the platform's, not a setting — a ceiling on the entities one walk may reach, held in the engine as a constant, recorded on every run's row and stated in the run's own account when it is reached, so a walk the ceiling stopped can never read as a completed one. The candidate bound that remains is likewise the most one record can carry in a single atomic batch, stated as such. Stored rows keep their key names so a row written under the old budgets still parses and still reads under the budgets it actually ran with."
- **ADR-0455** · Accepted · 2026-09-11 · Conflicts — "A conflicts check is a numbered record — CC-0001, a per-firm sequence drawn the moment the check is opened — and every search performed under it is a RUN of that check: the first run is the opening, a re-run from the check's own screen is a further run under the same number, and an automatic re-check lands under the matter's own check rather than starting a new one. Opening answers at once; the search executes after the response and reports its stage and counts on the run row, which the screen polls until the run settles; a run that stops reporting for two minutes is settled as failed on the next read rather than left in flight. The numbered check is identified by its first run's handle and every run is keyed by the handle of the record it writes, so no new identifier kind is needed and every old address still resolves. The number is the greatest sequence plus one under a unique index with a bounded retry, not a counter table. Execution rides the request's own background allowance today, with the executor written to move onto a queue when the per-firm dispatch table can take it."
- **ADR-0456** · Proposed · 2026-09-11 · Matters — "The hold and close edges of the matter lifecycle extend to a consultation: a firm parks a consultation it has not decided on, or declines one, from the consultation itself. The hold accepts `open` or `consultation` (an intake is still refused: it has nothing yet to pause), and its release returns the matter to WHERE THE HOLD FOUND IT rather than to `open`, reading `opened_at` as the one honest signal — the same signal the reopen already reads — so a parked consultation never becomes an active matter by being released. The close already accepted a consultation at the service and keeps doing so: the same preconditions run, a consultation with nothing on its ledgers passes them, and it keeps its tentative number and draws no matter number, so the firm's matter run stays dense. The refusal code for a hold on the wrong rung keeps its name (`matter-not-open`) because callers branch on it. On the screen, the status seal becomes a dropdown of the steps the lifecycle allows from where the matter stands — never a picker of statuses — with the status window as its last item; each step declares whether it is taken in the window (it needs input or has requirements worth reading) or from its own confirmation."
- **ADR-0457** · Accepted · 2026-09-11 · Documents — "A file whose SHA-256 the firm already holds is never uploaded again, and there is no setting that says otherwise: the browser hashes every chosen file, the firm is asked, and a match is made from the held bytes server-side. Because one set of bytes can then stand behind several files, the bytes belong to every file_version row that names them, live or soft-deleted, and deleting one file never destroys them. Destruction has one door — purgeableVersionObjects in @magna-carta/file-manager — which refuses an object while any other version row holds the same content hash or the same object key, and it is called by no route and no sweep today: default retention is indefinite, and the retention policy engine, when built, walks it per file and destroys exactly what it names. The reference check counts by content hash as well as by key so it holds whether held bytes are copied under a new key, as they are today, or shared under one later."
- **ADR-0458** · Proposed · 2026-09-11 · Core domain — "Firm branding is firm-wide with a per-office override, not per-office alone and not firm-wide alone. The firm holds a primary brand image and a bounded set of additional images, content-addressed by the SHA-256 of the bytes and stored in the firm's own bucket under that hash with the same facts the office letterhead logo records; an office's letterhead prints its own logo where it has set one and the firm's primary mark where it has not, resolved in one place on the read routes. The set answers whole rather than by page because a letterhead resolves it in one read, and it is bounded by construction (twelve additional images) so that stays true. Promoting a mark demotes the previous primary rather than removing it, and the rows live in @magna-carta/locations beside the office logo because the two are one family of facts."
- **ADR-0459** · Proposed · 2026-09-11 · Magnus — "A Magnus conversation can be kept on a matter's file after it was started, or taken off one, and the rule for that write is an access rule: a member may move a conversation they can read (their own, or anyone's under magnus:readAllConversations) onto a matter the firm still writes to (a closed matter and the platform's own matters are refused), and the confirmation states that which matter a transcript is kept on decides who may read it. The move is audited naming both matters. The conversation's attachments go with the move, because each is one of the old matter's files. The matter link still scopes only the record: what reaches the assistant beyond the typed question is exactly the readable text of the files a member attaches by reference, read fresh on each question and copied nowhere, which is why the disclosure moved to version 2."
- **ADR-0460** · Proposed · 2026-09-11 · AI / inference — "The roster a firm chooses its reasoning model from is the platform's curated catalog and nothing else, because nothing at runtime can list models AND carry the verified retention facts a legal platform needs: the Workers AI binding exposes run, gateway and log methods but no model enumeration, the AI Gateway's unified REST API offers run, chat-completions, responses and messages but no roster, and the Anthropic passthrough would need a provider key the platform does not hold outside the gateway and could not tell the firm what a listed model retains. So the settings page offers the catalog's ZDR-eligible reasoning entries, states each entry's verification dates and the catalog's version and effective date, and says the freshness pass is what keeps the list current. The firm setting keeps its two postures: a firm that has never chosen FOLLOWS the platform's reasoning tier, and choosing the platform's option on the page deletes the firm's row rather than writing today's default, so touching the control never silently converts following into pinning."
- **ADR-0461** · Accepted · 2026-09-14 · Database / schema migrations — A closed vocabulary held as a CHECK on a table other tables reference is never widened in place; the new vocabulary arrives as an added column and the old one is retired where it stands.
- **ADR-0462** · Proposed · 2026-09-14 · Web — "apps/web reads the API through @magna-carta/api-client and one TanStack Query cache instead of 48 hand-written modules, executing the half of ADR-0066 that was never built: that ADR chose Hey API to generate the typed client AND the Svelte Query v6 factories for the first-party app, and the app went on hand-writing 12,389 lines mirroring 459 contract types the generator already emits. Reads move from an on-mount $effect into the route's load, which is what finally makes the data-sveltekit-preload-data=hover already set in app.html buy DATA rather than only the route chunk; the (app) session guard's /api/v1/me, previously read on every navigation and again on mount by 38 surfaces, resolves from the cache. The generated factories throw, which collides with the app's deliberate three-outcome no-throw contract, so one error interceptor reassembles the Failure record and reports it centrally — every failure surface, the platform notice and the Error ID are unchanged. Enforcement is by construction first (DataTable.Grid has no empty snippet, NoResults takes no props) and by a lint:screen-data ratchet only for what types cannot see: no hand-spelled contract path, no second QueryClient, and no screen reading on mount with nothing warming it from its load."
- **ADR-0463** · Accepted · 2026-09-14 · Matters — "A matter names two offices, not one: matter.location_id is the office the matter is run out of, and a new nullable matter.physical_file_location_id is the office the firm's own paper file for it is kept at. Both point at location; the matter detail read joins location twice and answers both with resolved names; the write is the existing matters:edit patch, and null is a real answer. The status window's Physical File renders the second through the one office link and sets it through the one office picker, while Held In and Destroy After keep the Soon mark because neither has a record behind it."
- **ADR-0464** · Proposed · 2026-09-14 · Web / Notifications / Conflicts / Magnus / Calendar — "Every shipped Workspace screen searches through the one ScreenSearch control in its header's action row, or records why it does not, enforced by a new fitness function (`pnpm run lint:workspace-search`) that also refuses a second implementation of a search box and a decline left standing after its screen gained a search. Three hand-rolled search boxes (the Matters docket, the Directory, a matter's Notes) are replaced. Where a screen's rows are paged off the server, the query goes to the server rather than narrowing the page in hand — narrowing what happens to be in the browser answers 'of the forty I have read, which match' while appearing to answer 'which of yours match'. Two of those screens carry no sentence in the record at all (a notification row holds identifiers, closed labels, counts and dates; the conflicts log holds closed labels and identifiers recorded by reference), so the CLIENT resolves the term into the kinds and categories whose displayed words match and sends those with it, and the server ORs them against the one member-facing string the row carries. Magnus searches transcript content only for a member's own conversations, never on the firm-wide administrative read, because transcripts may not be swept across the firm's records until the discovery surface owns an include-or-exclude control. The Calendar narrows the window on screen and says so, because its three reads cap their horizon server-side and refuse a wider one. `@magna-carta/data-access/like-search` becomes the one home for escaping a reader's text into a LIKE pattern."
- **ADR-0465** · Accepted · 2026-09-14 · Design system — "The subtitle slot on ActionDialog and RecordWindow stops taking a bare string and takes a RecordSubject — an optional RecordReference (kind, number, and a standing of drawn, tentative or preview) plus short facts — which the design system renders. The three standings are the domain's own: a matter number is drawn, a consultation number is tentative and outlives the matter opening, and a preview is what the next record would take, shown before the save with the words that say so. Removing the string arm is the mechanism rather than a side effect: eighteen of fifty-four subtitles were the explanatory prose the owner struck from the New Check panel on 2026-09-08, four billing panels mixed a number and a sentence behind three different separators, and a slot typed string accepts all of it. A source scan, tooling/lint-modal-headers, then reads the heading's form against the panel's kind and asserts that a panel holding a record number shows it."
- **ADR-0466** · Accepted · 2026-09-14 · Matters — "An intake draws a number of its own the moment the enquiry is written down, from a third sequence beside the matter and consultation runs, rendering I2026-0001 by default. It is not an early draw on the consultation number, for the reason the consultation number is not an early draw on the matter number: most enquiries never become consultations, and a consultation run pitted with declined enquiries cannot be told apart from one with records missing. A file that comes the whole way therefore carries three numbers and keeps all three — graduation adds the consultation number beside the intake number and opening adds the matter number beside both — so a note filed under any of them still resolves, and every surface that quotes a matter falls through to the newest one it has drawn. The draw rides the same matter_number_counter / matter_number_allocation mechanism under an 'intake/' scope prefix, numbering_scheme.purpose gains a third value, and the per-purpose facts (prefix, label, retryable collision, whether a built-in default exists) move out of the service's conditionals into one table so a fourth run would be one row rather than four edits. This reverses the last of the 2026-09-06 lifecycle ruling that no number of any kind exists before a matter is active."
- **ADR-0467** · Accepted · 2026-09-15 · Architecture / Data — "A migration's TAG is the order every database applies it in — migrationsFromJournal sorts the journal lexically by tag — and drizzle's dense idx is the order migrations were integrated in, which names the meta/ snapshots. The two coincide only while every new tag sorts above the lineage head, which a merge does not preserve: a branch cut before another one lands keeps its earlier timestamp and is renumbered to a later idx, after which a freshly provisioned firm builds its schema in an order no already-migrated firm ever used. That invariant moves out of a Vitest fitness function and into tooling/migration-integrity, which the gate runs on every pull request rather than only under the run-tests label — the window in which a tag still costs nothing to change, because a migration nobody has applied can be renamed and one that has reached a database cannot. The repair is idempotent and scripted rather than a hand edit during a conflict resolution. Never renumber idx to satisfy the rule: the snapshots are named for it and describe the schema at that point."
- **ADR-0468** · Proposed · 2026-09-14 · Navigation & information architecture — "The File Workbench leaves the Tools catalogue and becomes a sidebar destination of its own, in the Firm group at /file-workbench, starred by default. The catalogue had it inverted: of its twenty manifests, eleven are the files subject group, and ten of those eleven map one-to-one onto the workbench's own capability tabs, so the listing offered eleven doors into one room with the room filed alphabetically between them. Firm rather than Workspace on a scope test rather than an importance one — Workspace holds the matter-scoped daily surfaces and this one is matter-free by construction. The manifest row stays, because the contextual surfacing path queries the catalogue by context tag and a tool absent from it is a tool nothing can offer, so the manifest gains an optional `route` and `toolRoute()` becomes the single derivation every link-builder goes through. Supersedes the placement in ADR-0101 and the Tools row in the page-archetypes canonical grouping; supersedes the /tools/file-workbench address recorded in the workbench's own pending decision. The ten sibling manifests are deleted rather than left in place: their permissions move to the workbench permission seam, which is what keeps the capability tabs from vanishing, and their search vocabulary moves onto the workbench's synonyms."
- **ADR-0469** · Accepted · 2026-09-15 · Database / schema migrations — "Three gates replace three hand disciplines around the drizzle migration lineages, all running on every pull request rather than only under the run-tests label. The worker's tag-to-SQL bundle modules are GENERATED from the journal instead of hand-kept, because the journal already decides which migrations exist and the hand-kept map lost an entry three times — a merge resolves the journal cleanly, since its entries are adjacent lines, and drops one branch's import in the same conflict, which surfaced only as a Durable Object throwing on wake. A conflicted _journal.json is resolved by a command that takes `main` as the arbiter rather than ours/theirs — main IS production here, so a tag on main has deployed and is immutable while a tag that is not can still move for free — keeping trunk entries' tags and order, appending the rest in integration order, re-stamping each above the head, renaming the .sql and regenerating the bundles. And a migration that has deployed can no longer be edited or renamed, measured from where the branch left main rather than main's tip so a migration that landed while the branch was open is not mistaken for one the branch deleted; deliberate exceptions are recorded with the reason no database could have applied them, while a baseline collapse needs no entry because LEGACY_BASELINES already reconciles those at runtime and the gate reads that list directly."
- **ADR-0470** · Accepted · 2026-09-16 · Data & migrations — A migration tag renamed after it deployed is reconciled in the applied-ledger at runtime, from a recorded registry of renames — recreating the database is no longer the remedy.
- **ADR-0471** · Accepted · 2026-09-16 · Repository workflow — An active-work note is retired in the pull request that lands its stream's work, not in a second merge afterwards — because on this repository a merge is a deploy.
- **ADR-0472** · Accepted · 2026-09-16 · Observability & diagnostics — A diagnostic row reports on its whole subsystem, not on whichever field is cheapest to compare — a row that can read healthy while the thing it describes is broken is worse than no row.
- **ADR-0473** · Proposed · 2026-09-16 · Navigation & information architecture — "The left rail carries five platform groups plus the member's own — Favorites, My Desk, Workspace, Money, Firm, Tools — where the old Workspace group held fourteen rows spanning a person's morning and the firm's evidence; Home, Notifications, Tasks, Calendar and Magnus move to My Desk because they are about the member rather than about the work. Every tool becomes a TOP-LEVEL ABILITY addressed at /<id> with a rail row of its own, and the /tools area is retired entirely — the two-sided screen, its index, the catalogue projection and the ToolManifest.route escape hatch — because a screen about tools is a stop on the way to work and the only two things the index carried, the descriptors and the search, already belong to the presentation layer and the command palette. The rail grows a SECOND LEVEL to hold nine tool rows: a cluster is a row with a glyph, a name and a chevron whose members tuck beneath it, declared in navigation/clusters.ts and available to any group; it is a fold and never a screen, nothing is reached through one, and a cluster holding a single row is seated as a plain row instead. The catalog's eight subject groups collapse to four named for what a member is doing — Records, Internet, Mailing, Files — and a cluster's tools sort alphabetically by name rather than in catalog order. Two rows change sides: the File Workbench returns to the catalog now that reaching a tool costs no navigation, and Firm Usage leaves it for the Firm group beside Reports, keeping its permission through a new optional Destination.permission, because a tool is a utility a member runs and reading the firm's own records is not one. Finally the visibility layer and the favourite layer stop sharing a mark: Customize's show/hide takes an EYE, and the STAR becomes a real favourite — any address at all, saved from the one header component every screen builds its title from, rendered as the rail's first group."
- **ADR-0474** · Proposed · 2026-09-16 · Data access — "A guarded write — an UPDATE or DELETE whose WHERE repeats the precondition its caller read — is marked `guarded(statement, label)` in the effect it rides, and a marked statement that affects no rows ABORTS its transaction rather than being reported on after the batch has committed. Before this, the WHERE clause protected the row and nothing else: `batch()` surfaces no per-statement affected-row count, `commitEffectBatch` returned void, and the route answered 200, so a member who lost the race was told their edit landed and found out by reloading — while the audit row riding the same batch, carrying no guard of its own, recorded an edit that never happened. Checking the count after the commit cannot fix the second half, which is why the check is inside the transaction: the DO-side and node-side SQLite adapters both raise `GuardedWriteRefusedError` mid-batch, `transactionSync` and `ROLLBACK` take the audit row and the outbox message with it, and the Durable Object returns it as a typed `unaffectedGuard` outcome alongside the existing `uniqueConstraint` one so nothing depends on an error message surviving RPC. An adapter that cannot enforce guards declares nothing, and `commitEffectBatch` refuses such a pairing outright with `UnenforceableGuardError` rather than committing unguarded — a silently dropped guard is the exact bug being closed. The fee and cost ledgers adopt it first, and their routes map the refusal onto the 409 conflict they already return for the sequential refusal, so no contract moves and a lost race is indistinguishable from a plainly illegal request, which is what it is. `pnpm run lint:guarded-writes` keeps it from eroding: a builder guarded at one call site must be guarded at all of them."
- **ADR-0475** · Proposed · 2026-09-16 · Testing — "Concurrency verifiers for the domain write path are written by placing a competing writer at a NAMED point inside the operation under test, not by firing two promises and hoping they interleave. The harness is `@magna-carta/data-access/interleave` — a BatchDatabase wrapper that runs a second writer before the operation's nth commit (`beforeCommit`) or once a read whose SQL matches given text has resolved (`afterRead`), placed in data-access because the thing it wraps is the BatchDatabase that effect-batch defines and every domain package already depends on it. Its primary entry point `runInterleaved` THROWS when the point was never reached, which is the reason to prefer it: a concurrency test whose competing writer never ran still exercises the operation, still asserts what it asserted, and still passes, having quietly become a single-caller test wearing a concurrency test's name — and it stays green on the day the guard it was meant to pin is deleted. Where the operation also failed, that failure is preserved as `operationFailure` so a test written around `rejects.toMatchObject` does not lose the error it was reading. This supersedes the per-package copy: `interleaveBeforeFirstCommit` in the matters tests is deleted and its three call sites migrated. The technique is now what the seven-angle review's concurrency angle instantiates to for a read-then-commit service, and the first application of it across fees, costs and timekeeping recorded two behaviours the guards do not cover — a caller told its edit succeeded when the guard refused it, and an audit row written for an edit that never landed — as pinned tests rather than silent passes."
- **ADR-0476** · Proposed · 2026-09-16 · Web — "The browser has the same race class as the write path and none of its machinery. A screen reads on a subject, the person picks another, and both reads resolve in whichever order the network chose — so the later-landing older answer writes the state and the screen shows the previous subject's data under the current subject's heading, looking exactly like a screen that loaded correctly. Five screens had each grown their own counter for this; `LatestOnly` is now the one shape, and `pnpm run lint:stale-response` fails a sixth, which is how the fifth was found (it named its field differently from the four that were known). Separately, `writeKey()` mints an idempotency key INSIDE each attempt, so a retry after an ambiguous failure is a different request and records the thing twice — the double-click it reads as protecting against is already handled by any in-flight flag set synchronously, because the event loop makes check-and-set atomic. `WriteIntent` holds one key across every attempt at one intent and rotates only after a success, so a retry replays through the idempotency machinery already there; the cost record window is the worked example and the misleading comment on `writeKey` is corrected in all four modules that carried it."
- **ADR-0477** · Proposed · 2026-09-16 · Concurrency — "A Durable Object that reads its stored state and then writes it is atomic without anybody asking, because workerd holds the input gate closed while a storage operation is outstanding — a second call is not delivered until the first has written. Nothing in the source says so, and awaiting one non-storage thing inside that window (an R2 HEAD, a queue send, a service binding) opens the gate and loses the property silently, with the code reading identically either way. `pnpm run lint:durable-object-gates` scans for the shape and found one: BundleBuildCoordinator.requestBundle HEADed R2 to decide whether a reaped bundle still existed, so two requests for the same expired bundle each enqueued a full rebuild of what can be a very large archive. Fixed with ctx.blockConcurrencyWhile around the decision, keeping the queue send outside it so a poll is not stalled behind a network round trip. The local test pool cannot demonstrate the race through the RPC path — it serializes those calls — so the verifier drives the two calls ungated, which is what an open gate is, and the fix is argued from the runtime's documented semantics rather than from a reproduction. Two more property-based verifiers land beside it, for the questions a two-caller test structurally cannot ask: N concurrent number draws (correctness absolute at every N, convergence bounded at five by the retry budget, and that ceiling now pinned) and N concurrent ledger postings checked against the domain's own fitness queries."
- **ADR-0478** · Accepted · 2026-09-16 · Dev tooling — "The whole-repo quality gate moves off GitHub Actions and into one local command, `pnpm gate` (tooling/gate/), run on the machine that made the change before it merges; the gate.yml workflow, its seed-caches.yml companion and the required `gate` status are removed. Same dimensions, same commands, same path classification; the type-aware lint covers the full dependent closure with no sink deferral, the test battery runs whole on every code change rather than on a label, and the dependency audit downloads the pinned osv-scanner by version and hash. Supersedes ADR-0184 on where the gate runs; the pre-push hook stays the fast diff-scoped subset."
- **ADR-0479** · Proposed · 2026-09-18 · Documents & automation — "A document the browser editor opens records every insertion and deletion with its author and time, and the STORED document keeps those marks for as long as it lives; every copy that leaves the platform — download, email, assembly output, the client portal — is produced with all changes accepted, by the conversion container the platform already runs. Chosen over accepting the marks at each version cut (which keeps files clean but makes the document forget its own authorship at every save, leaving attribution only in the platform's history) because a firm's record of who changed a filing and when is worth keeping in the document itself, and the risk that choice carries — redlines leaving the building — is closed at the one seam every outbound copy already passes through. Owner's decision, 2026-09-18, on the document history spec's section 3.3."
- **ADR-0480** · Accepted · 2026-09-18 · Platform — "Every number the firm issues is drawn from one register in @magna-carta/sequence-numbering: one row per purpose naming what the sequence is, when it is drawn, whether the firm shapes it and with which parts, and what renders until it does. The segment-template scheme engine that lived in matters moves into that package beside the counter mechanism it already held; conflicts checks and custody packets join the register as scheme-shaped purposes and draw through the shared planner instead of a hard-coded prefix over MAX + 1 and a fixed-shape record nothing ever configured; invoice, time-entry and call numbers are registered with their fixed shapes so the one settings screen (Settings › Practice › Numbering, the former Matter Numbering) is the whole inventory. The conflicts default becomes CC2026-00001, restarting each year with five digits, because a four-digit firm-wide run was the wrong default for the busiest sequence a firm has; numbers already drawn keep the numbers they were, and the firm-wide counter is seeded past them."
- **ADR-0481** · Proposed · 2026-09-18 · Communications layer — "Video meetings are built into the product rather than delegated to a conferencing vendor, on the owner's ruling of 2026-09-18 that a firm should run one subscription fewer. Media goes from the browser to the Cloudflare Realtime selective forwarding unit and never touches a Worker; signaling is a Durable Object per meeting that brokers each browser's session with the unit over hibernating WebSockets, keeps the roster and writes attendance through the firm-scoped data-access layer. A meeting belongs to exactly one calendar event and carries no title, time or participant list of its own, so authorization, visibility and the walled-matter question are the event's and are answered once. The record keeps who was in the room and when, and never what was said. A vendor integration, RealtimeKit's prebuilt interface toolkit and a self-hosted media server in a Container are each considered and rejected; a Zoom or Teams port may exist later as an option for firms that keep a vendor, never as the default. External guests, recording, dial-in by telephone and rooms larger than twelve are sequenced after slice 1."
- **ADR-0482** · Accepted · 2026-09-19 · Directory — "The one address form could not say a postal code read wrong or offer a state, because the per-country table it renders from is a curated shape rather than a reference dataset, and the design system depends on exactly one workspace package by design. So the constraints become their own dependency-free package, @magna-carta/address-formats, and reach the component as optional props the way the country list already does — 252 countries projected from the address-internationalization corpus into 182 postal-code formats and 62 administrative-area lists. The corpus is CHECKED IN and refreshed by a deliberate act rather than fetched at build time, because a build that reached a live service would depend on a third party being up and could change a postal rule under a release nobody made. A malformed code renders as the field's hint and never its error: the box is never marked invalid, and nothing here can refuse a commit. The administrative-area list is offered through a native datalist rather than a combobox, because the region field is not a closed choice."
- **ADR-0483** · Accepted · 2026-09-19 · Directory — "Address autocomplete needs an engine, and the engine is a build rather than a line. So the port ships first and answers honestly: AddressLookupPort has suggest and resolve, returns typed outcomes that never throw, and deferredAddressLookupPort() reports engine-not-implemented to everything — the same wired-but-dormant shape resolveSearchIndexPort takes for the discovery engine, which lets a surface be written once and degrade to exactly today's behaviour with no branch and no flag to remove. Two things about the port are the opposite of the discovery one and are stated loudly because nothing would suggest them: the index is fleet-wide public reference data, so nothing takes a firm id and the firm-scoping machinery must not be copied; and the QUERY is a client's address being typed, so an implementation logs none of it, caches none of it, and holds nothing between calls. no-coverage is a first-class failure because the open corpora cover countries very unevenly, and telling somebody their client's address is wrong when nobody has published that country's addresses is the wrong apology."
- **ADR-0484** · Accepted · 2026-09-20 · Directory — "The owner was asked directly whether a client's address may leave the firm's tenancy for a third-party service, and answered that anything may leave. That closes the question the earlier decision left open and reverses its operative conclusion: a provider is no longer refused on disclosure grounds, and the remaining axes are cost, coverage and what a provider actually buys. What this does NOT do is authorise any particular provider, any particular volume, or a call on any particular path — a grant that an address MAY leave is not an instruction that every address SHOULD, and the narrowest call that buys the thing is still the right one. The self-hosted index stops being the only acceptable Layer 2 engine and becomes one option among several, now competing on build cost rather than on privacy. Skip tracing and change-of-address lookup were raised in the same breath and explicitly NOT decided: the owner asked to look into their real-world application first, and the answer turns out to be regulatory rather than technical."
- **ADR-0485** · Accepted · 2026-09-18 · Account — "A member's photograph is stored in the FIRM's database and the firm's bucket — a member_photo row keyed by the member's user id, bytes under their own SHA-256 — rather than on the control-plane account beside the member's name. The name is an account fact a person carries between firms; a picture is what this firm's colleagues see beside this person's work, and it is already firm-keyed by its storage. Better Auth's user.image column is deliberately left unused: it holds a URL, and a URL to a firm-scoped object read through a session is not something the control plane can resolve. The row is keyed by the member rather than by the bytes, unlike firm_brand_image, because two members may legitimately hold the same image."
- **ADR-0486** · Accepted · 2026-09-18 · Account — "Account · Profile states the address the account signs in with and offers no way to change it. The address is not a profile field: it is the account's sign-in identity, the unique key on the control-plane user row, and the key the joining surface resolves a person by — a pending registration, an invitation and the queued member-provision message are all addressed to it. So a change is a mailbox-possession proof, a ruling on everything still addressed to the old address, and a window in which the person can sign in with neither; each of those is designable and none of them is a field. The row is engraved rather than shown as a disabled box beside a Soon badge, because a person comes to this page to learn which address their account is, and a dead control would claim the work was nearly finished."
- **ADR-0487** · Accepted · 2026-09-20 · Safe Website Viewer — "Interactive remote browsing is built, on the terms ADR-0119 set for it. A per-member Durable Object holds one Browser Rendering session; navigation reuses that session so a page's cookies and state survive between hops, and each hop re-runs the SSRF guard. What crosses back to the member is unchanged from the static capture — a screenshot and structured text, never live markup — so the strongest safety property survives the feature. Pixel streaming (CDP screencast plus input forwarding) is confirmed possible on this platform and is still NOT built: the decision here is the session, not the pixels."
- **ADR-0488** · "Accepted (amended 2026-10-02)" · 2026-09-18 · Integrations — "A connected integration's credential lives in its own per-firm `integration_credential` table with its own protected-field declaration, gated on a new `integrations:useConnection` permission, rather than in the `firm_secret` vault whose decrypt is gated on `firm:manageSecrets`. The two are different acts: an administrator opening the firm's vault, and the worker using a credential on behalf of a member whose route has already been authorized. They could not be told apart while they shared a protected column, because a protected column carries exactly one declaration and that declaration's permission IS the decrypt gate — so the shipping routes, which gate on `shipping:manage`, would either have refused the member their own route had just admitted, or have been fixed by granting that member read access to every secret the firm holds. The alternatives were rejected on cost: a second declaration on the same column is forbidden by a fitness test asserting one declaration per (table, column); a caller-named gate would have added a parameter to `readProtected` whose only purpose is to not use the declared permission, inside the module two fitness scans exist to protect; and lowering `firm_secret`'s own gate would have weakened it for every secret it will ever hold. A store may now raise exactly one error, `CredentialAccessForbiddenError`, which the registry turns into a `credential-forbidden` dispatch outcome, so `resolve` keeps its promise never to throw and a refused member gets a 403 naming the grant rather than a 500 or a false report that the firm has no postage account."
- **ADR-0489** · Accepted · 2026-09-24 · Safe Website Viewer — "Live mode streams the member's held remote browser and forwards their input to it, on the owner's ruling that live websites should be streaming, not screenshots. It supersedes points 4 and 5 of the per-member-session decision — that Live answers one screenshot per navigation and that pixel streaming is not built. The session, the per-member Durable Object, the firm namespace, the idle alarm and the fifteen-minute ceiling all stand. What changes is what crosses: JPEG frames of the remote screen at the rate it repaints, one in flight at a time, and input the other way as data — pointer positions, key names, pasted text. What still never crosses is markup or script, so the member's browser still executes nothing from the suspect page. The address guard moves from one navigation to the whole session: every document request in any frame pauses on CDP's Fetch domain until assertPublicTarget has passed it, because a member clicking around has handed a hostile page a whole session of navigations rather than one. Popups are folded into the visible tab, downloads denied, the file chooser intercepted, dialogs dismissed and reported. The credential risk the earlier decision named is answered with a standing warning, not a technical block: the stream cannot tell a password field on a phishing page from a search box, and refusing keystrokes would refuse the tool."
- **ADR-0490** · Proposed · 2026-09-24 · Meetings — "A meeting guest from outside the firm is admitted on a signed, expiring link for one Directory entity on one meeting, not on a session. Six decisions carry it: a guest is a Directory entity on the event, never a free address; the link proves possession of a mailbox and nothing more, so it brings its holder to the lobby and a host admits them; a guest holds no session and never will, so the chokepoint gains a step-zero guest lane that stashes a firm and the credential and never a user id; a guest's stay is a table of its own shaped like a member's, so meeting_attendance.user_id keeps its NOT NULL and its foreign key; the guest arrives on the application's own origin, outside the signed-in shell; and two names are kept, the Directory name the link was minted for and the name typed at the door. It is a stopgap with a named successor: the client portal's PortalGrant subsumes the link and absorbs the two tables when it lands."
- **ADR-0491** · Proposed · 2026-09-20 · Communications layer — "As meetings grow past their first slices, the shared wire protocol becomes one package imported by the worker and the browser alike, the signaling room is split into concern modules under an orchestrating Durable Object, every resource gets its own route file and domain trio, the browser client is split the same way, and diagnosability becomes a slice of its own: a per-meeting event stream through the standard pipeline, a connection-quality signal from the browser, an operator's inspect call, and a load harness — so the feature can keep growing without one object knowing everything and without the two ends of the wire drifting apart."
- **ADR-0492** · Accepted · 2026-09-19 · Platform — "The numbering register's `fixed` formulation is retired: invoice, time-entry and call-and-text numbers join the five already shaped by a scheme, so every sequence the platform draws is the firm's to formulate and a new one is a row rather than a renderer. Three things made that possible without changing a number any firm has issued. The segment model gains month, day and matter-number parts, so the day-scoped call number and the matter-prefixed entry number are expressible as templates rather than as special cases. The register gains `scopeSource`: a count is scoped by the scheme's own scoping parts (the general, collision-proof case) or by the DOMAIN, where the caller passes the key — a time entry counts within its matter's handle, an invoice within the letterhead chosen at finalize — and on such a run a scoping mark is refused because it would set a switch that does nothing. And `scopeDistinguishedBy` names the part a domain-scoped scheme must carry so two scopes' numbers stay apart, which is the time-entry matter number; the invoice run's is conditional and is enforced at finalize instead, where a bill drawn under a letterhead the scheme does not render is refused rather than silently issued a number the firm-wide run can issue too."
- **ADR-0493** · Proposed · 2026-09-18 · Platform / Workers API — "The api worker's route surface is reorganized from 99 flat files registered by one 1,019-line composition root into about twenty domain modules, each a self-contained OpenAPIHono sub-app built by one shared constructor (so the 422 default hook cannot be omitted), registering its routes through the unchanged chokepoint and mounted at the root by a manifest in app.ts.
